# Part III — RETYPE

**Cross-Level Functional Projection**

<a id="chapter-29-functional-projection-without-origin-type-replacement"></a>

## 29. Functional Projection without Origin-Type Replacement

Chapter 29 begins Part III — RETYPE by establishing the claim form that every later functional projection must preserve. Foundations fixed origin type, target function, transformation context, continuity, the Admissibility Band, and the Shared Transformation Record. PATH formed temporally extended source objects without assigning them a higher-level function. SUB opened eligible occurrences and composites without converting their internal constitution into a target-context role. RETYPE now addresses a different question: when may an already typed and source-traceable object perform a bounded analytical function within another declared context without being rewritten as a different kind of object?

The governing question is:

> Under what conditions may source object X, while retaining origin type T and its source reference, perform bounded target function F in declared target context C?

The answer begins from a strict separation:

```text
what X is in its source reconstruction
≠
what X may do in one target context
```

RETYPE does not repair, replace, or complete the source object by changing its name. It adds a new, independently testable source-to-context relation. The source object remains answerable to its own formation history, source typing, prior disposition, uncertainty, and Loss. The target-function claim remains answerable to the declared target scene, relative level, analytical purpose, and the difference that the source object is proposed to make there.

Sections 29.1–29.4 establish the purpose of RETYPE, the minimal typed functional-projection claim, source entry, mandatory target-context declaration, Origin-Type Preservation, the non-primitive status of operator-like functions, and the target-function burden. Sections 29.5–29.8 distinguish target function from operator type and establish Source-Object Integrity, Functional Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, and Loss. Sections 29.9–29.13 consolidate projection without replacement, result-axis and non-success-route separation, re-entry, and the boundaries to recontextualization, `COMPOSE`, and `DECOMPOSE`. Chapter 30 alone will operationalize the complete `PROJECT_AS` signature, declarations, tests, alternatives, result, and record procedure.

The local Chapter-29 entry architecture is:

```text
identified source object X
+ visible origin type T
+ preserved source reference
+ declared target context C
+ specific candidate target function F
→
typed RETYPE claim candidate
```

This is not yet a successful `PROJECT_AS` occurrence.

```text
typed projection candidate
≠ target function established
≠ projection result selected
≠ authority increased
```

<a id="29-1-purpose-of-retype"></a>

### 29.1 Purpose of RETYPE

RETYPE specifies how a source object that is already identifiable and analytically typed may be assessed as performing a new, bounded function within a declared target context. Its purpose is to make cross-level functional claims explicit enough to be inspected, challenged, reduced, stopped, or left uncaptured without changing the PMS Base grammar or the source object’s origin reconstruction.

RETYPE therefore asks:

- Which source object is entering the claim?
- Under which origin type is that source object warranted in its source reconstruction?
- Which source record, historical load, uncertainty, and prior Loss remain attached to it?
- Which target scene, target object, Frame, relative level, and analytical purpose make a new function relevant?
- What precise function is proposed?
- What difference would that function make in the target context?
- Which later tests would have to show that the function is carried by the source rather than by a label, analogy, current target structure, or analyst preference?
- Where does the claim stop?

These are functional-projection questions. They do not ask whether the source object can be redescribed more elegantly, placed in a new diagram, summarized under a broader heading, or associated with an operator-like word. A new view may change legibility without establishing a new function. A source object may also remain historically relevant while contributing no distinct function to the current target scene.

#### PMS occurrence entry where the source is PMS-typed or PMS-derived

Where the source object is PMS-typed or PMS-derived, RETYPE also asks which concrete operator occurrences and relations are already warranted in the linked source Record and which of those relations are material candidates for carrying the proposed target function. This is a conditional source-entry burden, not an operator quota. The operation does not re-derive PATH or SUB and does not infer operator typing from the desired target function.

```text
operator name available
≠ claim-relevant operator occurrence established

source Record inspectable
+ material occurrence relation identified where needed
→ PMS-grounded projection entry
```

Operators that are not material to the tested target difference need not be foregrounded. Missing or unavailable occurrence detail remains an explicit source limit rather than an invitation to invent a trace.

#### C29-PURPOSE-01 — RETYPE adds a bounded source-to-context relation without rewriting the source object

A valid RETYPE claim adds one relation:

```text
source object X
→ performs bounded function F within C
```

It does not replace the source-side statement:

```text
source object X
→ has origin type T within its source reconstruction
```

The two statements can coexist because they answer different questions. The source claim identifies and types X. The target claim proposes what X does in C. Neither statement may silently absorb the other.

```text
new contextual function
≠ retrospective source rewrite
```

This distinction is especially important after PATH and SUB. A trajectory may have been formed through a valid `COMPOSE` occurrence. Its internal historical load may later have been opened through a valid `DECOMPOSE` occurrence. Those results can supply a stronger source trace, but they do not establish any target function by themselves.

```text
successful COMPOSE
≠ successful PROJECT_AS

successful DECOMPOSE
≠ successful PROJECT_AS
```

RETYPE receives source material, not inherited projection success.

#### RETYPE is a Part, not a fourth operation

RETYPE is one of the four Parts of STRATA. It contains the conceptual discipline, operation procedure, projection families, alternatives, failure modes, boundary conditions, cases, and local audit required for cross-level functional claims. It is not itself an operation and does not enlarge the closed operation inventory.

```text
RETYPE
≠ PROJECT_AS
```

`PROJECT_AS` remains one of exactly three STRATA operations. Chapter 29 establishes the claim that the operation must later test. Chapter 30 owns the complete operation procedure.

```text
RETYPE claim architecture
≠ PROJECT_AS occurrence automatically
```

The separation prevents functional language from performing an undeclared transformation. Saying that a trajectory “acts like a frame” may be an initial hypothesis, a metaphor, an analogy, or a shorthand for relevance. It becomes a `PROJECT_AS` candidate only when the source object, origin type, target context, specific function, and bounded claim are declared separately.

#### C29-TYPEDCLAIM-01 — the RETYPE claim binds X, T, C, and F explicitly

The minimal typed claim is:

```text
Within target context C,
source object X,
while retaining origin type T,
performs bounded function F.
```

Each position is indispensable:

| Position | Required declaration | What it prevents |
|---|---|---|
| `X` | identified source object | projection from an undefined or shifting referent |
| `T` | visible origin type | function replacing source typing |
| `C` | declared target context | universal or context-free function assignment |
| `F` | specific bounded function | vague importance, resemblance, or label substitution |

The claim is typed because the source identity and origin type remain explicit while the target function is declared as a different analytical relation. It is bounded because C and F delimit where the claim applies and what it is allowed to say.

The typed claim does not by itself prove any of its positions. It makes them inspectable. A later assessment may find that X is insufficiently identified, T is contested, C is underspecified, F is elastic, or the source-to-context relation is not supported. Making those failures visible is part of RETYPE’s purpose.

#### C29-SOURCEENTRY-01 — source object, source Record, origin type, and source reference precede projection

A RETYPE candidate must enter from a source-side declaration that exists before the target-function claim. At minimum, the entry must make visible:

- the bounded source object;
- the source Record or equivalent source reconstruction;
- the origin type under which the object is currently warranted;
- the source reference and Frame;
- relevant temporal, compositional, or occurrence history;
- prior claim disposition and known uncertainty where material;
- inherited Loss, exclusions, and irrecoverable limits;
- any source-side revision that already narrows the object or its typing.

The entry is not required to reproduce the complete source archive. It must be sufficient to identify which object is being projected and which source claim remains accountable. Full Source-Object Integrity and complete Loss treatment belong to Sections 29.6–29.8. WP1 establishes the prior-order rule:

```text
source object first
→ target-function candidate second
```

A target label cannot manufacture its own source object. Nor can the target context retroactively select whichever source history best fits the desired function while ignoring failed or excluded source claims.

The stable Chapter-29 anchor enters as follows:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_source:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  source_record: linked_PATH_source_record_and_separate_SUB_record_where_applicable
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: bounded_historical_process_X
  source_frame: declared_source_reconstruction
  source_status: identifiable_before_projection
  inherited_loss: retained_from_source_record
  target_function_status: not_yet_established
```

The identifier `documented_trajectory_X` is not evidence by itself. It stands for an already bounded trajectory whose formation, historical load, source limits, and origin typing are independently recorded. Chapter 29 does not reopen or re-prove that trajectory. It preserves the source record as the starting point for a new claim.

#### RETYPE is not source-claim rescue

A target function cannot save a failed source object or a defeated origin type. If the supposed trajectory is later reduced to a limited sequence, the projection must be retested against that revised source claim.

```text
failed or revised source claim
+ attractive target function
≠ source claim restored
```

The historical result remains visible. A new function claim may still be possible for the weaker surviving object, but it is a new claim with a different source burden. RETYPE therefore increases testability, not immunity.

The purpose result of Section 29.1 is:

```text
RETYPE permits a bounded function claim
only after source identity and origin typing are visible,
without converting source history into inherited projection success.
```

<a id="29-2-functional-projection"></a>

### 29.2 Functional Projection

A **functional projection** is a bounded analytical relation in which an origin-typed source object is proposed as performing a specific function within another declared context while remaining the same source object in its source reconstruction.

```text
source object X of origin type T
+
declared target context C
+
candidate target function F
→
functional-projection claim
```

The projection is functional because it states what X is proposed to do in C. It is a projection because that function is not treated as X’s intrinsic or universal identity. It is bounded because the target context, target relation, relative level, purpose, and claim reach limit the assertion.

A functional projection does not require an ontological upward movement. “Source level” and “target level” identify positions within a declared analytical relation. A local trajectory may function within a wider configuration, while the same configuration may itself occupy a different relative position in another analysis.

```text
cross-level
≠ higher being
≠ higher truth
≠ higher authority
```

#### C29-TARGETCTX-01 — target context, target scene or object, and relative target level are mandatory

No RETYPE target function exists without a declared target context. The target declaration must identify enough of the following to delimit the claim:

- the target scene, object, configuration, process, or analytical relation;
- the target Frame or relevant contextual boundary;
- the relative target level in relation to the source object;
- the relevant roles, relations, expectations, costs, alternatives, or action corridors;
- the temporal cut or bounded duration for which the function is proposed;
- the analytical purpose that makes the function testable;
- the immediate Claim Ceiling.

WP1 does not complete the full Contextual-Boundedness procedure. It fixes the entry condition: `C` cannot remain a vague phrase such as “at the macro level,” “in the system,” “for the organization,” or “later on.” A target function must be located in a scene or relation where its alleged work can be distinguished from current structures and from mere historical relevance.

For the positive anchor candidate:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_target_A:
  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  target_scene: bounded_reopening_and_coordination_scene
  target_frame: current_review_configuration
  relative_target_level: trajectory_X_considered_within_later_configuration_Y
  analytical_purpose: determine_how_prior_history_structures_current_relevance_and_action
  proposed_target_function: bounded_frame_function
  target_function_status: candidate_not_yet_executed
```

The trajectory is proposed as performing a bounded frame-function because sedimented expectations, prior exclusions, accumulated reopening costs, and residual binding may structure what counts as relevant, which alternatives appear available, and how current actions are interpreted in `later_configuration_Y`.

This is still a candidate. The claim has not yet shown which source features are load-bearing, whether present structures carry the same function more directly, or whether material source changes alter the target account. Those burdens belong to WP2 and Chapter 30.

#### Projection changes analytical use, not source identity

A source object may be selected for a new analytical use without being altered as an origin object. In the target account, some source features may be foregrounded, some compressed, and some left outside the immediate explanation. That selectivity is unavoidable. It becomes admissible only when the source remains identifiable and the selection does not silently replace the source object with a convenient target label.

```text
foregrounded source relation
≠ complete source reproduction

selective target use
≠ source identity erased
```

Full selection and Loss accounting remain later duties. At WP1, the conceptual requirement is that the target claim points back to the retained source object rather than creating a function-shaped substitute.

#### Source relevance is weaker than distinct target function

A source object can matter historically without performing an additional function in the target scene. It may explain how the present was reached, supply background information, support chronology, or remain normatively salient to participants. None of those relations automatically establishes a RETYPE function.

```text
historical relevance
≠ distinct target-context function
```

The difference is practical and analytical. A distinct target function must change a warranted reconstruction of the target context: which differences are active, what the target Frame selects, which alternatives are available, how costs or roles are distributed, which expectations govern action, or another declared praxis-relevant dimension. If removing the proposed source-to-context relation leaves the target reconstruction unchanged, no additional target function is warranted for that claim.

#### C29-CONTRAST-01 — the same source object is tested in a function-bearing and a no-additional-function context

The Chapter-29 anchor requires a paired contrast using the same `documented_trajectory_X`.

**Context A — function-bearing candidate**

In `later_configuration_Y`, current participants confront a reopening question whose relevance, costs, exclusions, and available continuations are materially structured by the documented trajectory. The proposed frame-function may therefore be warranted if later tests show that the target account changes when the trajectory’s load-bearing features are altered or removed.

**Context B — no-additional-function candidate**

In `independent_configuration_Z`, current Frames, roles, access rules, and action alternatives already explain the scene. The historical trajectory remains part of the background record, but its inclusion or removal does not change the warranted reconstruction of relevance, cost, binding, expectation, or available action.

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_contrast:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  context_A:
    target_context: later_configuration_Y
    proposed_function: bounded_frame_function
    current_status: candidate_for_later_test
  context_B:
    target_context: independent_configuration_Z
    proposed_function: no_additional_target_function
    current_status: candidate_no_projection_result
  comparison_rule: same_source_object_different_contextual_difference
```

The contrast protects RETYPE from universalizing the source object. The same trajectory may perform a function in one context and no additional function in another without contradiction.

```text
function in C1
≠ function in C2 automatically
```

A no-additional-function result does not invalidate the source trajectory. It says only that the new contextual relation is unnecessary or unsupported in the second target context. The source object can remain valid history while the projection claim fails to add a distinct target-context difference.

#### Functional projection is not established by the target label

The expression `frame-function` does not carry its own warrant. It names the proposed work: the trajectory would structure target-side relevance, expectation, interpretation, or available action in a bounded way. The label remains provisional until the source-to-context relation is shown.

```text
function label
≠ functional load
```

A visually central source node, frequently cited history, repeated metaphor, or useful teaching device may appear frame-like. Such features can motivate a candidate. They cannot replace the later continuity and counterfactual burden.

The Section 29.2 result is therefore a declared functional-projection candidate and a paired no-projection possibility, not an executed operation or final result.

<a id="29-3-origin-type-preservation"></a>

### 29.3 Origin Type Preservation

**Origin-Type Preservation** requires that the source object’s analytical type remain visible and un-replaced before, during, and after the target-function claim. The source object may acquire a bounded contextual role; it does not become the type named by that role.

The anchor relation is:

```text
documented_trajectory_X
PROJECT_AS candidate
bounded frame-function in later_configuration_Y
```

The source-side statement remains:

```text
documented_trajectory_X has origin type Trajectory
```

The target-side statement is different:

```text
documented_trajectory_X may perform a bounded frame-function in later_configuration_Y
```

#### C29-ORIGIN-01 — origin type remains visible and is not replaced by the target function

Origin-Type Preservation requires at least:

- the source object is still identified by its source reference;
- the origin type is stated independently of the target function;
- the source Record remains available rather than overwritten;
- the target function is grammatically and conceptually marked as a function;
- any later source-type revision is explicit and does not occur through the projection label;
- the target result does not retroactively change what earlier PATH or SUB records claimed.

The canonical non-equivalence is:

```text
Trajectory X functions as a frame in C
≠
Trajectory X becomes Frame as origin type
```

This does not require the origin-type claim to remain eternally unchanged. A later source-supported revision may narrow, replace, or reject the source typing. Preservation means that such revision occurs through the source reconstruction and remains visible as a revision.

```text
origin type preserved through projection
≠ origin type immunized against evidence
```

If `documented_trajectory_X` is later shown not to satisfy the trajectory burden, the frame-function candidate must be re-entered with the surviving source object and revised type. The target function cannot stabilize the stronger origin label merely because the label is analytically useful.

#### Origin type and function language must remain syntactically separate

Phrases such as “the trajectory is the frame,” “the event became an attractor,” or “the composite is now Asymmetry” are unsafe because they conceal whether an origin-type replacement is being asserted. RETYPE therefore uses function-marked language:

```text
functions as a frame
performs an attractor-function
carries a bounded asymmetry-function
operates as a macro-event within C
```

This syntax is not cosmetic. It preserves the difference between source typing and target performance. It also keeps the function defeasible: the same object can cease to perform that function when the target context changes without ceasing to be the source object it was.

#### C29-NOPRIM-01 — an operator-like target function creates no new PMS primitive or operator type

A target function may be described with operator language because it performs work analogous to a PMS structural function in the target context. That does not create a new Δ–Ψ operator, alter an operator dependency, or convert the source object into an operator type.

```text
frame-function
≠ □ operator type

attractor-function
≠ Α operator type

asymmetry-function
≠ Ω operator type

binding-function
≠ Ψ operator type
```

The distinction has three consequences.

First, the closed PMS Base grammar remains unchanged. STRATA does not add `Trajectory-Frame`, `Macro-Event`, `Historical Attractor`, or any other projected function as a twelfth operator.

Second, the source object remains an occurrence, composite, Path, Trajectory, Event, Non-Event, or other derived analytical object. It is not elevated into a grammar element because it performs useful structural work in one target context.

Third, operator-like language supplies no inherited authority. A frame-function does not acquire every semantic implication, dependency relation, or application status associated with the abstract Frame operator. The projection claim is limited to the precise target-side work that has been declared and later supported.

```text
operator-like function
≠ operator identity
≠ dependency inheritance
≠ authority inheritance
```

#### Preservation under multiple candidate functions

One source object may later support several compatible or competing target-function candidates. That possibility does not multiply its origin types. `documented_trajectory_X` might be tested as a frame-function in one context and as a macro-event function in another. It may also support no projection in a third.

```text
one source object
+ several context-specific function claims
≠ several origin objects automatically
```

Chapter 36 will later govern compatible and competing projections. WP1 establishes the invariant: every candidate begins from the same visible origin type unless the source reconstruction itself is explicitly revised.

#### Preservation does not imply losslessness

Origin-Type Preservation is narrower than complete Source-Object Integrity. The origin type can remain visible while the projection compresses important history, excludes internal heterogeneity, or overlooks prior uncertainty. Such a projection may still fail later traceability or continuity tests.

```text
origin type preserved
≠ source-object integrity complete
≠ functional continuity established
≠ Loss adequately declared
```

This prevents one satisfied condition from standing in for the whole admissibility burden. Sections 29.5–29.8 determine what source load must remain visible and how foregrounding, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss affect the target claim.

The Section 29.3 result is therefore a protected type relation:

```text
source type retained
+ function separately marked
+ no primitive created
→ origin-type-preserving candidate
```

It is necessary for projection, not sufficient for projection success.

<a id="29-4-target-function"></a>

### 29.4 Target Function

The **target function** states what the retained source object is proposed to do within the declared target context. Chapter 5 defines target function as a bounded relational role. Chapter 29 operationalizes its entry into RETYPE: the function must be specific enough to distinguish a target-context difference, narrow enough to avoid identity replacement, and open enough to fail when the source object adds no such difference.

A target function is therefore:

- relational rather than intrinsic;
- context-bound rather than universal;
- source-carried rather than label-generated;
- claim-limited rather than semantically total;
- revisable rather than permanent;
- non-primitive and non-authorizing.

```text
target function
= what X does in C for a bounded analytical purpose

not
= what X essentially is
```

#### C29-TARGETFUNC-01 — the target function is specific, relational, and praxeologically discriminating

A target-function declaration must identify an actual target-side difference. It should state:

1. which source object performs the function;
2. within which target context and relative relation;
3. for which analytical purpose;
4. what target-side structure the function changes or organizes;
5. which praxis-relevant dimensions are expected to differ;
6. what weaker no-function account remains available.

For the positive anchor, `bounded_frame_function` means no more and no less than the following candidate claim:

> Within `later_configuration_Y`, `documented_trajectory_X`, while retaining origin type `Trajectory`, may structure the bounded selection of relevant issues, expectations, reopening costs, and available continuations for the declared review question.

This declaration is more precise than “the past frames the present.” It names the source object, target scene, type retention, analytical purpose, and the proposed work. It also permits counterpressure: present Frames may already perform the selection; the trajectory may be background only; or the target account may remain unchanged when the historical relation is removed.

A target function is praxeologically discriminating when accepting or rejecting it changes what can responsibly be reconstructed about at least one declared dimension, such as:

- relevance and issue selection;
- action corridors and blocked continuations;
- access, exposure, costs, or exit conditions;
- roles, expectations, obligations, or binding;
- temporal interpretation or bounded historical load;
- relation among current alternatives;
- target-side uncertainty, Loss, or Stop conditions.

The function need not produce an action recommendation. Praxeological discrimination concerns the reconstruction of situated praxis, not automatic intervention.

```text
praxeological difference
≠ recommendation
≠ normative verdict
≠ causal proof
```

#### Candidate function names are open and subordinate to the relation

RETYPE may later use function names such as:

- frame-function;
- macro-event function;
- attractor-function;
- asymmetry-function;
- binding-function;
- modulating function;
- integration-function;
- higher-level boundary function.

This list is illustrative, not a closed enum and not a parallel operator inventory. A function name is admissible only to the extent that it specifies a bounded relation that can be traced and tested. New labels do not create new theoretical objects by themselves.

```text
open function vocabulary
≠ open primitive vocabulary
```

Chapters 31–35 will instantiate major projection families. Chapter 29 does not establish those families in advance, and Chapter 30 will not use a target-function enum to decide semantic validity.

#### Specificity without premature Functional Continuity

WP1 requires a function precise enough to state the expected target-context difference. It does not yet complete the test that the function is materially carried by load-bearing source features.

The anchor candidate can currently identify likely source load:

- sedimented expectations;
- accumulated reopening costs;
- prior exclusions;
- residual binding;
- historically changed alternatives.

But the current chapter has not yet established which of those features are constitutive, whether they are jointly necessary, whether present target structures outweigh them, or how the function changes under bounded source variation. Those burdens are developed in WP2 and remain operation-level tests for Chapter 30.

```text
specific candidate function
≠ Functional Continuity completed
```

This boundary prevents conceptual entry from being mistaken for result.

#### No-function remains a positive analytical possibility

The target-function architecture is discriminating only if it permits the result that no additional function is warranted. In `independent_configuration_Z`, the same trajectory may remain accessible history while current Frames and relations fully carry the target reconstruction.

```text
source history retained
+
no changed target-side reconstruction
→
no additional target function warranted
```

This is not an origin-type failure and not necessarily a failed transformation record at this stage. It is the bounded no-projection possibility that later procedure and output discipline must preserve. Forcing every relevant source object into a function would make RETYPE semantically elastic and immune to contrast.

#### Target function does not increase claim or application authority

Even a later successful projection would remain an analytical relation. It would not establish empirical causality, normative legitimacy, person type, diagnosis, recommendation, sanction, or decision entitlement.

```text
function established in C
≠ action authorized in C
≠ function valid outside C
≠ source object elevated in authority
```

The PMS governance boundary remains:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### Minimal WP1 projection declaration

The four sections can now be condensed into a bounded conceptual declaration:

```yaml
chapter_29_wp1_projection_candidate:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_X
    record: linked_PATH_source_record_and_separate_SUB_record_where_applicable
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_reference: bounded_historical_process_X
  target:
    context: later_configuration_Y
    scene: bounded_reopening_and_coordination_scene
    relative_level: trajectory_considered_within_later_configuration
    proposed_function: bounded_frame_function
    analytical_purpose: test_target_side_relevance_expectation_cost_and_continuation_structure
  preservation:
    origin_type_visible: true
    origin_type_replaced: false
    new_pms_primitive_created: false
  contrast:
    same_source_second_context: independent_configuration_Z
    second_context_candidate: no_additional_target_function
  status:
    typed_claim_declared: true
    project_as_executed: false
    functional_continuity_completed: false
    final_result_selected: false
```

The declaration is prose-bound. It is not a new schema, second Shared Transformation Record, or operation result.

#### WP1 completion boundary

WP1 establishes:

- RETYPE as bounded source-to-context functional-projection discipline;
- the typed claim binding source object, origin type, target context, and target function;
- prior source entry and non-rescue of failed source claims;
- mandatory target-context, target-scene or object, and relative-level declaration;
- the stable positive and no-additional-function contrast using the same source object;
- Origin-Type Preservation without origin-type immunization;
- operator-like function without new PMS primitive or dependency inheritance;
- target function as specific, relational, praxeologically discriminating, and non-authorizing.

WP1 does not yet establish:

- the complete distinction between target function and operator type;
- full Source-Object Integrity;
- load-bearing versus associated source features;
- Functional Continuity or the bounded counterfactual test;
- complete Contextual Boundedness, validity duration, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Ceiling;
- the full `PROJECT_AS` signature or operation procedure;
- final result routing or canonical Output-Class selection;
- Chapter-29 or RETYPE lock.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 29 complete
≠ PROJECT_AS executed
≠ target function established
≠ Functional Continuity passed
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 29 provisionally locked
≠ RETYPE provisionally locked
```

The local WP1 result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

<a id="29-5-target-function-versus-operator-type"></a>

### 29.5 Target Function versus Operator Type

A target function may be named with operator-like language because the source object performs work that is relevantly comparable to an operator function in the declared target context. That language remains subordinate to the typed relation established in WP1. It does not convert the source object into an abstract PMS operator type, a new operator occurrence of that type, or a new primitive in the Δ–Ψ grammar.

```text
operator type
→ abstract function in PMS Base

operator occurrence
→ concrete frame- and source-bound instantiation

target function
→ bounded role performed by a retained source object in C
```

These three positions are non-equivalent. A trajectory may organize issue selection in a later scene. A concrete Frame occurrence may also organize issue selection in that scene. The similarity of work does not erase the difference between the source objects, their histories, or their type conditions.

```text
similar target-side work
≠ same operator type
≠ same operator occurrence
≠ same source object
```

#### C29-OPTYPE-01 — target function remains distinct from operator type and origin type

The Chapter-29 anchor must therefore be read as:

```text
documented_trajectory_X
retains origin type Trajectory
and may perform bounded_frame_function
within later_configuration_Y
```

It must not be rewritten as:

```text
documented_trajectory_X is □
```

or:

```text
documented_trajectory_X has become a Frame occurrence
```

The first invalid form collapses the contextual function into the PMS operator type. The second invents a new source typing without a separate occurrence-level reconstruction. Neither follows from the functional candidate.

The syntactic discipline is deliberate:

```text
functions as a bounded frame-function
≠ is Frame
≠ instantiates □ automatically
```

The same rule applies to every operator-like function name:

```text
attractor-function
≠ Α as origin type

asymmetry-function
≠ Ω as origin type

binding-function
≠ Ψ as origin type

integration-function
≠ Σ as origin type
```

A target function inherits no PMS dependency position. Calling the candidate a frame-function does not place the source object between ∇ and Λ, make it a prerequisite for later operators, or alter any Δ–Ψ dependency. The function name identifies bounded analytical work; it does not insert an object into the Base grammar.

#### Function language does not establish semantic identity

Operator-like language may be useful because it points to a recognizable kind of target-side work. That usefulness is weaker than semantic identity. The function may preserve only a limited aspect of the operator analogy while lacking other defining relations of the operator type.

For the anchor, the candidate claim concerns bounded selection and organization of:

- relevant issues;
- sedimented expectations;
- reopening costs;
- historically changed continuations;
- and residual binding in the named review scene.

It does not claim that the trajectory supplies every function of □, that it defines the complete target Frame, or that all target relevance is generated by the trajectory.

```text
partial operator-like performance in C
≠ complete operator semantics
```

This distinction preserves the possibility that the target scene contains one or more actual Frame occurrences, present institutional rules, current role relations, or local expectations that perform the same or stronger work. RETYPE must compare those present structures rather than absorb them into the historical source object.

#### Origin-type and operator-type questions remain separately revisable

The origin type may later be revised by a new source-side reconstruction. The target function may also be narrowed, replaced, or rejected by a new target-side test. Neither revision automatically determines the other.

```text
source typing revised
→ projection claim must be retested

projection claim revised
≠ source typing revised automatically
```

A revised source object may still support a bounded function. A supported trajectory may fail to support the proposed function. A functionally useful relation may remain only an analogy. The separate positions protect those mixed results.

#### Same function name, different source structures

Two different source objects may be candidates for the same function name in the same target scene. A trajectory, a composite arrangement, or an operator-typed occurrence could each be proposed as performing a bounded frame-function. They do not become type-identical because the target label matches.

```text
same function name
+ different source objects
→ separate typed claims
```

Each claim requires its own source trace, integrity check, continuity burden, Loss disclosure, and contextual boundary. The shared label carries no warrant from one claim to another.

The Section 29.5 result is therefore a protected three-way separation:

```text
origin type retained
+ operator type unchanged
+ target function separately bounded
→ no type replacement and no primitive creation
```

This separation is necessary for the continuity tests that follow. It is not a successful `PROJECT_AS` result.

<a id="29-6-source-object-integrity"></a>

### 29.6 Source Object Integrity

**Source Object Integrity** is the requirement that the object entering the target-function claim remain traceably the same bounded source object, with its relevant history, constitutive relations, prior limits, uncertainty, and Loss still available for inspection.

```text
source-object integrity
= identifiable source object
+ retained reference and origin type
+ traceable formation history
+ visible constitutive relations
+ retained prior limits and Loss
```

Integrity does not require complete reproduction of the source archive. RETYPE may foreground only the source features relevant to one target-function claim. It must nevertheless preserve enough of the source object to show why those features belong together, how they were formed, which prior claims remain provisional or failed, and what the projection leaves out.

```text
source-object integrity
≠ exhaustive source reproduction
≠ losslessness
≠ immunity from revision
```

#### Occurrence-level Source Object Integrity

Where concrete PMS operator occurrences or their relations are constitutive for the source object and material to the projection, Source Object Integrity includes continued access to those occurrence records, their source Frames, temporal placement, source support, and relevant canonical dependency context. A derived object such as a Trajectory may satisfy this burden by retaining an inspectable link to its locked PATH Record; Chapter 29 does not need to reproduce that Record.

```text
PMS operator label retained
≠ occurrence-level source relation preserved
```

The abstract operator type is never decomposed or altered by this requirement. Nor must every source operator be repeated. Only the occurrence relations that carry, constrain, oppose, or materially qualify the present target-function claim must remain reconstructible.

#### C29-INTEGRITY-01 — source identity, history, constitutive relations, and prior limits remain traceable

For `documented_trajectory_X`, the integrity burden includes at least:

- the bounded historical referent of the trajectory;
- the PATH source Record that established its configurations, transitions, ordering, and trajectory claim;
- any separate SUB Record used to clarify internal source structure;
- the origin type `Trajectory` and its current claim disposition;
- the temporal relation by which earlier structures remain connected to `later_configuration_Y`;
- sedimented expectations, costs, exclusions, bindings, and changed alternatives alleged to matter;
- inherited uncertainty, compression, exclusions, and irrecoverable Loss;
- prior claim reductions, Stops, Failures, or Non-Capture findings where relevant;
- the limit beyond which the source object is not warranted.

The target-function claim may not select the attractive parts of the trajectory while discarding the relations that make them historically intelligible.

```text
selected source features
without source formation and limits
→ source fragment, not intact source object
```

The same rule prevents retrospective source engineering. The analyst may not redefine the beginning, endpoint, branch structure, or central Non-Event of the trajectory solely because a different source boundary makes the target label easier to sustain. A revised source boundary is possible, but it is a new source-side claim and must remain visible as such.

#### Integrity packet for the stable anchor

The Chapter-29 anchor can be represented conceptually as:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_integrity:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: bounded_historical_process_X
  source_lineage:
    path_record: retained
    compose_occurrence: retained_where_applicable
    sub_record: separate_and_retained_where_applicable
  constitutive_relations:
    - ordered_configurations_and_transitions
    - sedimented_expectations
    - accumulated_reopening_costs
    - prior_exclusions
    - residual_binding
    - historically_changed_alternatives
  prior_limits:
    uncertainty: retained
    claim_reductions: retained
    stop_failure_or_non_capture: retained_where_applicable
    inherited_loss: retained
  target_use:
    selected_features_only: true
    source_object_rewritten: false
```

This is a prose-bound integrity view, not a new schema or second Shared Transformation Record.

#### C29-LOADTRACE-01 — load-bearing source features are distinguished from merely associated or foregrounded features

A source feature is **load-bearing** for a target function when removing or materially altering it would require the function claim to weaken, change, or fail in the declared target context. A feature is **associated** when it accompanies the source object but does not materially carry the proposed function. A feature is **foregrounded** when the target claim makes it especially visible, whether or not it is independently load-bearing.

```text
load-bearing
≠ associated
≠ foregrounded
```

The three categories may overlap, but they cannot be presumed identical.

For the anchor candidate:

| Source feature | WP2 role | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| sedimented expectation of reconsideration | load-bearing candidate | structures what counts as unresolved in the later review scene |
| accumulated reopening cost | load-bearing candidate | changes the practical burden of available continuations |
| prior exclusion of alternatives | load-bearing candidate | narrows which later continuations remain intelligible or reachable |
| residual binding carried into the later scene | load-bearing candidate | shapes obligations and the cost of re-opening or exit |
| historically changed alternatives | load-bearing candidate | differentiates the present corridor from a current-state-only account |
| exact dates not relevant to the bounded interval | associated unless the timing relation changes the claim | may document history without carrying the function |
| broad project label | associated | identifies the setting but does not establish functional load |
| analyst’s preferred metaphor | foregrounded only | may guide attention without being a source feature |
| current meeting agenda and present rules | target-context structures, not source load | may independently carry or outweigh the proposed function |

This table is provisional in the proper sense: it states which features the target-function candidate must test. It does not declare them universally necessary or causally sufficient.

#### Source integrity under selective foregrounding

Every target-function claim selects. Selection becomes admissible only when it discloses what has been foregrounded and what remains outside the target use.

```text
foregrounding relevant source load
≠ reproducing the whole trajectory
```

The bounded frame-function may foreground expectations, costs, and continuations while leaving many local events in the background. That can preserve integrity if the omitted details do not carry the function and remain recoverable from the source Record. It threatens integrity when omitted heterogeneity would materially change the target claim.

The no-additional-function context `independent_configuration_Z` is an essential pressure test. The same intact trajectory remains historically identifiable there. Source Object Integrity can therefore pass while no distinct target function is warranted.

```text
source object intact
+ target function unnecessary in Z
→ integrity without projection success
```

This mixed possibility prevents Source Object Integrity from becoming a hidden function-pass.

#### Prior limitations cannot be dropped at the target boundary

A source object may enter RETYPE with provisional edges, disputed periodization, incomplete internal access, or irrecoverable compression. The target function inherits those limitations where they matter.

```text
source uncertainty retained
→ target-function certainty cannot exceed it where load depends on it
```

A useful target relation does not upgrade an uncertain source edge. A successful source typing does not erase a prior failed stronger claim. A later projection cannot convert a mandatory source-side Stop into positive evidence.

The Section 29.6 result is an integrity condition:

```text
same bounded source object remains traceable
+ load-bearing candidates distinguished
+ prior limits retained
→ source object available for continuity testing
```

It does not yet establish that the proposed function is carried by those features.

<a id="29-7-functional-continuity"></a>

### 29.7 Functional Continuity

**Functional Continuity** asks whether the specific target function is materially carried from the retained source object into the declared target context. Chapter 5 defines the general continuity requirement. Chapter 29 applies it to RETYPE by demanding a visible relation between load-bearing source features and a discriminating target-side effect.

```text
source feature S
+ relation R into target context C
→ target-side difference supporting F
```

The function must depend on the source object’s specific structure. It is not enough that the source is relevant, narratively useful, historically prior, formally similar, or convenient for explanation.

#### PMS-grounded Functional Continuity

If the proposed target function is said to depend on a PMS-derived source structure, Functional Continuity requires a bounded answer to an additional question:

> Would material alteration of a load-bearing operator occurrence or occurrence relation require the target-function claim to weaken, change, split, or fail?

The test concerns concrete source load—for example a source-supported Non-Event relation, asymmetric cost gradient, temporal persistence relation, commitment continuity, or recontextualizing interruption—not a hypothetical change to the abstract Δ–Ψ grammar.

```text
material change in a warranted source occurrence relation
→ possible change in the bounded target-function claim

change in the abstract PMS operator definition
→ not a STRATA counterfactual
```

Any operator naming remains illustrative and source-dependent. Ordinary-language similarity alone does not establish occurrence typing.

#### C29-CONTINUITY-01 — Functional Continuity requires a source-to-context relation, not resemblance or usefulness alone

A continuity claim must answer four connected questions:

1. Which source features are load-bearing?
2. Through which source-to-context relation do they remain operative or analytically necessary in C?
3. What bounded target-side difference do they make?
4. What alteration would weaken, change, or defeat the function claim?

For `documented_trajectory_X` in `later_configuration_Y`, the candidate relation is:

```text
sedimented expectations
+ accumulated reopening costs
+ prior exclusions
+ residual binding
+ historically changed alternatives
→ organize relevance, issue selection, and continuation structure
  for the bounded reopening-and-coordination question in Y
```

The relation is stronger than resemblance because it states why the target account changes when the trajectory’s load is included. It remains weaker than causal proof because it does not claim that the trajectory alone produced the later configuration or that no other structure contributes.

```text
functional dependence for a bounded reconstruction
≠ universal empirical causality
≠ sole-cause claim
```

Present target structures remain part of the test. Current Frames, roles, rules, resources, or newly established obligations may independently organize the scene. Where they fully explain the target-side difference, the trajectory may be relevant background without performing an additional function.

#### Trace from source load to target-side difference

The continuity burden should be stated at the level of the proposed function, not through a general declaration that “history matters.” For the anchor:

| Load-bearing candidate | Target-side difference claimed in `later_configuration_Y` | Disconfirming pressure |
|---|---|---|
| sedimented expectation | changes what counts as unresolved or review-worthy | no documented expectation or expectation already discharged |
| accumulated reopening cost | changes the burden of available continuations | reopening is cost-neutral under current conditions |
| prior exclusion | changes which alternatives remain practically available | excluded alternatives remain fully available and equally reachable |
| residual binding | changes obligations or costs of exit and revision | no surviving commitment relation in Y |
| historically changed alternatives | defeats a current-state-only reconstruction | present structure is sufficient and history adds no discrimination |

A function need not depend equally on every listed feature. It must identify which are constitutive, which are modulating, and which can be removed without changing the claim. Chapter 30 will later operationalize that declaration in the complete `PROJECT_AS` procedure.

#### C29-COUNTERFACT-01 — bounded removal or alteration of source features tests target-function dependence without proving universal causality

Counterfactual Sensitivity in Chapter 29 is a bounded reconstruction test. It asks whether a relevant source change would require a corresponding change in the target-function claim while approximately holding the declared target question and current target structures stable enough for comparison.

The positive anchor is pressured by two source variants.

**Variant A — expectation and residue discharged**

```text
remove sedimented unresolved expectation
+ replace with timely completed reconsideration
+ remove residual reopening burden
```

If the proposed function is specifically a bounded frame-function for unresolved reconsideration, reopening cost, and available continuations, it cannot remain unchanged. It must weaken, change, or fail.

**Variant B — exclusions reversed**

```text
restore previously excluded alternatives
+ equalize their present accessibility
```

A function that claims to organize the later continuation space through historical closure must narrow or fail if the relevant alternatives are again genuinely available.

These variants do not prove what would have happened in the world, establish a causal law, or reconstruct an unrestricted alternative history. They test whether the analyst’s own function claim is sensitive to the source features it declares as load-bearing.

```text
bounded source alteration
→ claim-sensitivity test

not
→ universal causal demonstration
```

The same-source target-context contrast supplies a second, independent pressure. In `independent_configuration_Z`, the source trajectory remains unchanged while current target structures fully carry relevance and continuation selection.

```text
same source X
+ context Z with sufficient present structures
→ no additional target function warranted
```

The source-variation test and the context-variation test answer different questions. The first tests whether F depends on X’s specific load. The second tests whether F is needed in the declared C.

#### C29-ELASTICITY-01 — target labels that survive opposite source structures trigger elasticity and reduction or failure pressure

A target-function label is **elastic** when it remains unchanged despite source structures that should make materially different target claims.

Suppose the analyst assigns the same `bounded_frame_function` to all of the following:

```text
source A:
  unresolved expectation
  accumulated reopening cost
  excluded alternatives
  residual binding

source B:
  expectation fulfilled
  no reopening cost
  alternatives fully available
  no residual binding
```

If opposite source structures yield the same target function, the function label is no longer discriminating. It may be carried by the analyst’s vocabulary, by a generic statement that “the past frames the present,” or by target structures not attributed correctly to the source.

```text
opposite relevant source structures
+ invariant target label
→ functional elasticity
```

Elasticity creates pressure to:

- specify a narrower function;
- reduce the claim to historical relevance or analogy;
- identify a different load-bearing source feature;
- select no additional projection;
- or reject the proposed function.

WP2 does not select the final canonical Output Class. It establishes that an invariant label cannot pass as Functional Continuity merely because it remains rhetorically useful.

#### Continuity under uncertainty and partial load

A source-to-context relation may remain plausible while one load-bearing relation is underdetermined. The analyst must state:

- which source feature is uncertain;
- whether it is constitutive or modulating;
- how the uncertainty affects the target-side difference;
- which rival function or no-function account remains available;
- what evidence would narrow or defeat the claim.

```text
uncertain constitutive relation
→ bounded provisional continuity claim at most
```

Where available materials cannot discriminate whether expectations, costs, or current Frames carry the target-side difference, Non-Capture must remain possible later. Provisionality cannot be used to preserve an otherwise empty label.

The Section 29.7 result is the conceptual continuity burden:

```text
specific function
+ load-bearing source trace
+ target-side difference
+ bounded source-change sensitivity
+ live rival and no-function pressure
→ Functional Continuity candidate
```

The complete operation-level test, alternative declaration, result routing, and Record remain Chapter 30’s responsibility.

<a id="29-8-contextual-boundedness"></a>

### 29.8 Contextual Boundedness

A target function is admissible only within the target context for which its source-to-context relation is supported. **Contextual Boundedness** prevents a local functional candidate from becoming a permanent attribute, universal projection, cross-context default, or source of action authority.

```text
function F warranted in C for purpose P
≠ F warranted in every C'
≠ F valid indefinitely
≠ F authorizes action
```

The boundary must be declared before result selection. A vague reference to “the system,” “the macro level,” “later contexts,” or “the organization” is insufficient because it does not show where the function begins, what it governs, or when it expires.

#### C29-BOUNDED-01 — target scene, roles, level, duration, validity scope, and Claim Ceiling are explicit

The bounded declaration for the positive anchor is:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_target_boundary:
  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  target_scene: bounded_reopening_and_coordination_scene
  target_object: current_review_and_continuation_structure
  relevant_roles:
    - participants_with_current_review_responsibility
    - roles_exposed_to_reopening_costs
    - roles_bound_by_surviving_commitments
  relative_target_level: trajectory_considered_as_one_source_within_the_later_configuration
  temporal_reach:
    begins: entry_of_documented_trajectory_X_into_the_declared_review_question
    ends: material_revision_or_closure_of_the_reopening_and_coordination_scene
  validity_scope: issue_selection_expectation_reopening_cost_and_available_continuations_for_this_scene
  analytical_purpose: test_whether_historical_load_adds_a_distinct_bounded_frame_function
  claim_ceiling:
    permits:
      - bounded_target_side_reconstruction
      - comparison_with_current_frames_and_no_projection
    excludes:
      - universal_frame_identity
      - empirical_sole_causality
      - person_or_group_typing
      - normative_legitimacy
      - recommendation_or_sanction
      - authority_inheritance
```

Each field restricts a different route of inflation:

- the **scene** prevents global target assignment;
- the **roles** identify where costs, expectations, and binding are relevant without typing persons;
- the **relative level** prevents ontological “higher-level” language;
- the **duration** prevents indefinite persistence;
- the **validity scope** prevents semantic expansion;
- the **Claim Ceiling** prevents causal, normative, diagnostic, or authoritative transfer.

The function expires or requires retesting when the target scene changes materially, the relevant roles or commitments change, the historical load is discharged, present Frames replace the function, the analytical purpose changes, or contradictory source material appears.

```text
material context change
→ new testable claim
```

#### Same source, boundedly different context

`independent_configuration_Z` retains the same source trajectory but differs in the target-side relation:

- current Frames already determine issue selection;
- no surviving expectation makes the trajectory necessary for the question;
- reopening costs are explained by present resource conditions;
- alternatives are currently available without historical restriction;
- no residual binding from X structures the scene.

The bounded conclusion at this conceptual stage is not that X ceases to exist or loses its origin type. It is that no additional target function is warranted for this target question.

```text
valid candidate in Y
≠ automatic transfer to Z
```

A later context may support a different function, the same function under new evidence, analogy only, or no projection. Each is a new claim.

#### C29-LOSS-01 — foregrounding, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss are disclosed

Functional projection redistributes visibility. Even when Source Object Integrity is preserved, the target use foregrounds some source features, compresses others, excludes some as non-constitutive, carries uncertainty forward, and may rely on information already irrecoverable from prior transformations.

The canonical Loss declaration remains:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

For the anchor candidate, the bounded WP2 Loss view is:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_loss_view:
  foregrounded:
    - sedimented_expectations
    - accumulated_reopening_costs
    - prior_exclusions
    - residual_binding
    - historically_changed_alternatives
  loss:
    preserved:
      - source_reference_and_origin_type
      - ordered_trajectory_relation_relevant_to_Y
      - load_bearing_candidate_relations
      - inherited_source_limits_and_prior_dispositions
    compressed:
      - local_events_not_required_for_the_bounded_target_question
      - internal_variation_that_does_not_change_the_candidate_function
    excluded:
      - unrelated_source_frames
      - unsupported_person_level_explanations
      - target_structures_not_part_of_the_source_object
    uncertain:
      - unresolved_weight_of_each_candidate_feature
      - incomplete_relation_between_some_source_features_and_current_roles
    irrecoverable:
      - source_detail_already_lost_in_prior_valid_compression
      - unavailable_alternative_structure_not_supported_by_records
```

`foregrounded` explains visibility selection; it is not a sixth canonical Loss field. The canonical five-part Loss structure remains unchanged.

Loss must be claim-specific. A detail may be compressed safely for one bounded function and become constitutive for another. The analyst must therefore not copy one Loss statement across all target functions or contexts.

```text
same source object
+ new target function or context
→ new Loss assessment
```

Irrecoverable Loss sets a hard ceiling. If the unavailable source detail is necessary to distinguish the proposed function from a rival or no-projection account, the function cannot be strengthened through formal completeness or confident wording.

#### Boundedness does not compensate for discontinuity

A narrowly stated claim can still lack Functional Continuity. Conversely, a well-supported source-to-context relation can still overreach its target scope.

```text
narrow claim
≠ source-carried function automatically

strong source trace
≠ cross-context validity automatically
```

Functional Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, Type Integrity, Source Object Integrity, Loss, and Claim Ceiling remain non-compensatory. Each must be recorded separately in the later operation procedure.

#### Contextual function is not application authority

The bounded frame-function may improve the reconstruction of a review scene. It cannot decide which continuation should be chosen, assign responsibility to persons, establish legitimacy, impose a sanction, or inherit authority from the source trajectory.

```text
analytical orientation
≠ decision rule
≠ intervention mandate
```

The governance invariant remains:

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### Minimal WP2 integrity-and-continuity view

Sections 29.5–29.8 can now be condensed without executing `PROJECT_AS`:

```yaml
chapter_29_wp2_continuity_candidate:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_X
    origin_type: Trajectory
    reference_and_history_traceable: true
    prior_limits_and_loss_retained: true
  target:
    context: later_configuration_Y
    function: bounded_frame_function
    function_is_operator_type: false
    function_is_new_primitive: false
  source_load:
    load_bearing_candidates:
      - sedimented_expectations
      - accumulated_reopening_costs
      - prior_exclusions
      - residual_binding
      - historically_changed_alternatives
    associated_and_foregrounded_features_separated: true
  continuity:
    source_to_context_relation_declared: true
    bounded_source_variation_defined: true
    universal_causality_claimed: false
    opposite_source_invariant_label_permitted: false
  boundedness:
    scene_roles_level_duration_validity_and_claim_ceiling_declared: true
    automatic_context_transfer: false
    authority_inheritance: prohibited
  loss:
    canonical_five_part_declaration_required: true
    foregrounding_disclosed_separately: true
  contrast:
    same_source_context_Z: no_additional_target_function_candidate
  status:
    project_as_executed: false
    final_function_result_selected: false
    canonical_output_class_selected: false
```

This view is prose-bound and subordinate to the Shared Transformation Record. It does not add a schema, Rule, operation, Output Class, audit stage, target-function enum, or automated semantic test.

#### WP2 completion boundary

WP2 establishes:

- target function as distinct from origin type, operator type, and operator occurrence;
- no dependency, semantic identity, primitive, or authority inheritance from operator-like function language;
- Source Object Integrity as traceable identity, history, constitutive relation, prior limit, and inherited-Loss preservation;
- load-bearing source features as distinct from associated and merely foregrounded features;
- Functional Continuity as a source-to-context relation producing a bounded target-side difference;
- bounded source-variation testing without universal causal proof;
- same-source target-context pressure as distinct from source-variation pressure;
- functional-elasticity pressure where opposite source structures retain the same label;
- Contextual Boundedness through target scene, roles, relative level, duration, validity scope, and Claim Ceiling;
- complete canonical Loss disclosure plus separate foregrounding disclosure;
- non-compensation among integrity, continuity, boundedness, Loss, and claim limits.

WP2 does not yet establish:

- projection-without-replacement as the integrated Chapter-29 result;
- the final boundaries to Φ recontextualization, `COMPOSE`, and `DECOMPOSE`;
- separation of source integrity, target-function effect, prior disposition, and canonical Output Class as final result axes;
- complete Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, analogy-only, no-projection, and Non-Capture routing;
- re-entry and non-erasure rules for new target contexts or revised functions;
- the Chapter-30 handoff as completed;
- the full `PROJECT_AS` signature, operation procedure, alternatives, result, or Record;
- Chapter-29 or RETYPE lock.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 29 complete
≠ PROJECT_AS executed
≠ final target-function result selected
≠ canonical Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 29 provisionally locked
≠ RETYPE provisionally locked
```

The local WP2 result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

<a id="29-9-projection-without-replacement"></a>

### 29.9 Projection without Replacement

RETYPE adds a bounded functional relation to an already identified source object. It does not replace the source reconstruction, erase the route by which the source object was formed, or convert target-side usefulness into a new account of what the source object is. Projection is therefore additive in claim structure but non-substitutive in source authority.

```text
source record: X has origin type T under source reconstruction S
+
target claim: X performs bounded function F within C
→ two linked but separately accountable claims
```

The source claim remains answerable to its own evidence, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, operation history, disposition, and Loss. The target-function claim remains answerable to target context, target-side difference, Functional Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Contextual Boundedness, alternatives, and Claim Ceiling. A successful relation on one side cannot repair a defect on the other.

#### C29-NOREPLACE-01 — projection does not replace PATH, SUB, origin type, source heterogeneity, or prior Loss

Projection without replacement requires five preservations.

1. **PATH preservation.** If the source object was formed as a path or trajectory, its temporal formation, branch structure, Non-Events, asymmetries, bindings, and sedimentation remain part of the source account. The target function cannot replace that formation with a single higher-level label.
2. **SUB preservation.** If a separate `DECOMPOSE` occurrence opened the source object, its components, relations, unresolved structure, revised function effects, and finer-grained limits remain independently recorded. RETYPE may use that trace but may not absorb the decomposition into the function label.
3. **Origin-Type Preservation.** The origin type remains stated independently of the target function. The function does not become a retrospective type correction.
4. **Heterogeneity preservation.** Internal variation, competing subpaths, uneven source contributions, and unresolved relations remain visible where they constrain the target claim. A bounded function does not imply an internally homogeneous source.
5. **Loss preservation.** Earlier preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable material remains attached to the source record. The target claim adds its own claim-specific Loss assessment; it does not reset earlier Loss.

```text
PROJECT_AS candidate
≠ PATH substitute
≠ SUB substitute
≠ origin-type rewrite
≠ heterogeneity erasure
≠ Loss reset
```

The positive anchor therefore retains three distinguishable records or record roles:

```yaml
chapter_29_projection_without_replacement:
  source_formation:
    operation: COMPOSE_where_applicable
    object: documented_trajectory_X
    result_retained: true
  source_opening:
    operation: DECOMPOSE_where_separately_executed
    internal_trace_retained: true
    result_not_inherited_by_projection: true
  target_function_candidate:
    operation: PROJECT_AS_not_yet_executed
    target_context: later_configuration_Y
    proposed_function: bounded_frame_function
  preservation:
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_heterogeneity_visible: true
    prior_loss_retained: true
    prior_dispositions_retained: true
```

A function may foreground only the historically load-bearing relations needed for the target question. That bounded foregrounding is not replacement if the source object remains traceable and the excluded or compressed source structure remains declared. Replacement occurs when the target label becomes the only surviving account of the source or when target usefulness is used to overwrite failed, uncertain, or heterogeneous source findings.

#### Projection success cannot repair source failure

Suppose a target scene is more intelligible when a historical object is treated as a frame-function. That analytical convenience cannot establish that the source object was correctly typed as a trajectory if the trajectory burden failed. The target claim must re-enter from the strongest surviving source claim.

```text
failed trajectory claim
+ useful historical framing
≠ trajectory restored by projection
```

The same rule applies in the other direction. A well-supported trajectory can remain intact while a proposed frame-function fails in a target context.

```text
valid source object
+ unsupported target function
→ source claim retained
→ projection claim reduced, stopped, failed, or left uncaptured
```

Projection without replacement is therefore a non-erasure rule across operations and claims. It protects both successful and failed prior findings from being silently rewritten by later analytical movement.

#### No replacement by target-level simplification

A target context often treats the source object as one bounded unit. That relative-level use can be legitimate without implying that the source object is simple. The analyst must distinguish:

```text
source treated as one unit for target question
≠ source internally unitary
```

Where internal contradiction or heterogeneous contribution changes the target function, the target claim must preserve that difference, reduce its scope, or stop. Where internal detail does not alter the bounded target question, compression may be admissible but must remain disclosed. Fine detail has no automatic truth privilege, yet target-level convenience has no automatic compression privilege either.

#### Projection does not complete the source object

A source object may be provisional, contested, or partially admissible. RETYPE may test a bounded function from that source only under an appropriately reduced claim. It cannot treat projection as the missing completion step.

```text
provisional source
→ at most provisionally bounded projection claim
≠ stronger source certainty generated
```

The source and target claims retain separate ceilings. The lower ceiling governs the combined assertion wherever the target function depends on unresolved source structure.

<a id="29-10-functional-projection-as-a-typed-claim"></a>

### 29.10 Functional Projection as a Typed Claim

The complete conceptual form established by Chapter 29 is:

```text
Within target context C,
source object X,
while retaining origin type T,
performs bounded function F.
```

This sentence is not a stylistic preference. It is the minimum typed grammar required to keep source identity, source typing, target location, and function claim separately inspectable. Chapter 30 will operationalize the declaration and testing procedure. Chapter 29 consolidates what the claim must mean and what it must not collapse.

The typed claim can be represented conceptually as:

```yaml
functional_projection_claim:
  source_object: X
  origin_type: T
  source_reference: S
  target_context: C
  target_function: F
  relative_target_level: g_prime
  validity_scope: V
  claim_ceiling: bounded
  source_integrity_required: true
  functional_continuity_required: true
  origin_type_replaced: false
```

The representation is not a complete `PROJECT_AS` Record. It omits the operation signature, full preconditions, complete source and target declarations, alternatives, operation result, canonical mapping, and record-level validation owned by Chapter 30.

#### Typed claim, not claim about essence

The claim does not state what X is “in itself,” across all contexts, or at every analytical level. It states that X, under one retained source typing, performs one bounded function in one declared target context for one analytical purpose.

```text
X functions as F in C
≠ X is essentially F
≠ X always functions as F
≠ every similar X functions as F
```

The target function is relational. Remove or materially alter the target context and the function may disappear, change, become analogy-only, or require a new claim. Alter the source features carrying the function and the claim may weaken or fail. This two-sided dependence is what prevents target-function language from becoming an essence label.

#### C29-RESULTAXES-01 — source integrity, target-function effect, prior disposition, and Output Class remain separate

A RETYPE assessment must preserve at least four result axes:

| Axis | Governing question | Example values or states |
|---|---|---|
| Source integrity | Is the projected source object still identifiable and traceable under its origin type and inherited limits? | retained, revised, contested, insufficient |
| Target-function effect | Does the source object make the specific warranted difference claimed in C? | supported, bounded, absent, elastic, underdetermined |
| Prior disposition | What source-side or earlier-operation result enters the claim? | admissible, provisional, reduced, failed, stopped, non-captured |
| Canonical Output Class | How is the whole transformation mapped after the complete audit? | one of the ten canonical classes |

These axes are related but not interchangeable.

```text
source integrity retained
≠ target function supported automatically

target-function effect supported
≠ prior failed source claim erased

operation-specific finding
≠ canonical Output Class automatically
```

A source object can remain fully traceable while no additional target function is warranted. A bounded function can be provisionally supported while the source claim remains contested. A projection can fail because of target-context discontinuity without invalidating the source object. A Mandatory Stop can arise from source limits before the function is decidable. A Non-Capture result can remain after all fields are complete if rival functions or the no-projection account cannot be resolved without inflation.

Chapter 29 therefore prohibits one-dimensional result language such as “the projection works” unless the statement identifies which axis is being reported. Chapter 30 will require separate operation-specific and canonical result fields.

#### The anchor as a typed but still provisional claim

The Chapter-29 positive anchor is now conceptually complete:

```yaml
chapter_29_anchor_typed_claim:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  target_function: bounded_frame_function
  target_difference:
    - issue_selection_changes
    - expectations_remain_historically_structured
    - reopening_costs_are_not_fully_current
    - some_continuations_remain_historically_constrained
  source_load_candidates:
    - sedimented_expectations
    - accumulated_reopening_costs
    - prior_exclusions
    - residual_binding
    - historically_changed_alternatives
  contrasting_context:
    context: independent_configuration_Z
    result_candidate: no_additional_target_function
  status:
    conceptual_claim_complete: true
    project_as_executed: false
    final_target_function_result_selected: false
    canonical_output_class_selected: false
```

The negative contrast is not a weaker copy of the positive claim. It is a distinct target-context finding using the same source object. The trajectory may remain historically valid in both contexts while functioning as a bounded frame only in one.

#### C29-FAILSTOPNC-01 — Failure, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, analogy-only, no-projection, and Non-Capture remain possible and non-equivalent

RETYPE must remain capable of ending without a successful projection. The principal non-success routes are distinct:

| Route | Core condition | What remains |
|---|---|---|
| Failed transformation | The claimed function, type integrity, continuity, or contextual relation does not hold | source claim may remain intact |
| Mandatory Stop | Continuing would exceed source, claim, type, traceability, or authority limits | no stronger result may be inferred |
| Claim Reduction | A narrower function, scope, duration, or target difference is supportable | reduced claim remains testable |
| Analogy-only | Structural resemblance is useful but no source-dependent target function is established | analogy remains explicitly non-projective |
| No-projection | The source object is valid or relevant but adds no distinct warranted function in C | source object remains valid history |
