| Non-Capture | Available source and target structures do not support a warranted choice among rival functions, no-projection, or another model | unresolved remainder remains open |

These routes cannot be collapsed into a generic “not yet proven” state.

```text
analogy_only
≠ no_projection
≠ failed_transformation
≠ mandatory_stop
≠ non_capture
```

A no-projection finding is often a positive discriminative result: the source object does not perform an additional function in this target context. Analogy-only preserves resemblance without converting it into function. Claim Reduction retains only the strongest bounded claim that survives. Mandatory Stop prohibits continuation under current conditions. Non-Capture preserves unresolved structure rather than protecting a preferred weak label.

The canonical Output Class is selected only after the complete Chapter-30 operation and later RETYPE audit. Chapter 29 establishes route availability and non-equivalence, not the final mapping for the anchor.

#### C29-REENTRY-01 — a new context or revised function creates a new testable claim without erasing earlier results

RETYPE is context-sensitive but not result-amnesic. Any material change in target context, target function, relative level, source typing, source trace, validity period, or analytical purpose creates a new claim occurrence.

```text
new context C2
or revised function F2
or revised source claim X/T
→ new testable claim
→ separate Record and Loss
```

The new claim may cite prior records, but it may not inherit their result automatically. Nor may it erase them.

Examples:

- A frame-function supported in `later_configuration_Y` does not transfer automatically to a later institutional review.
- A no-projection result in `independent_configuration_Z` does not prohibit a different bounded function in another scene.
- A failed broad frame-function may be followed by a narrower expectation-ordering function, but the broad failure remains recorded.
- A revised source type requires re-entry rather than silent continuation under the old origin type.
- New evidence may reopen a Mandatory Stop only by creating a new claim with a new Source Ceiling assessment.

```text
new transformation = new testable claim
```

This rule is anti-immunizing. Re-entry is not a way to keep moving a function label until it survives. Each new claim must state what changed and why the earlier result does not already decide it.

<a id="29-11-retype-versus-recontextualization"></a>

### 29.11 RETYPE versus Recontextualization

Φ Recontextualization changes the Frame in which an existing structure is read, interpreted, or related. It can alter salience, meaning, relevance, comparison, or available interpretation. That change may be praxeologically important without constituting RETYPE.

`PROJECT_AS` goes further. It claims that an identified source object performs a specific bounded function within a declared target context and that the function is carried by source-traceable features whose bounded alteration matters to the target reconstruction.

#### C29-RECONTEXT-01 — changed legibility without a target-function claim remains recontextualization

The boundary is:

```text
X becomes differently legible under Frame C
→ Φ / recontextualization candidate

X, retaining origin type T, performs specific bounded function F in C
+ Source Trace
+ Functional Continuity
+ bounded counterpressure
→ PROJECT_AS candidate
```

Recontextualization may be sufficient when:

- the source object is placed beside new material and receives a different interpretation;
- a later Frame changes which source features are salient;
- a graph, Reader, table, or comparison makes an old relation easier to see;
- the same object is narrated under a new institutional, temporal, or conceptual context;
- no specific target-side work beyond changed legibility is claimed.

A target-function claim is required only when the analyst asserts more than changed reading—such as that the source object structures relevance, stabilizes recurrence, distributes costs, modulates action corridors, or binds a later scene in a specific way.

```text
new interpretation
≠ new target function automatically

increased salience
≠ Functional Continuity
```

The boundary also protects Φ. RETYPE does not subsume all recontextualization. A legitimate Φ finding can remain complete without being “upgraded” into `PROJECT_AS`. More structure does not mean a better or higher claim.

#### Confusion pressure: display function versus analytical target function

A Reader may use a trajectory as a navigation frame by placing it at the center of a view. That is a display function assigned by the tool or document. It does not show that the trajectory performs a frame-function in the reconstructed praxis scene.

```text
used as a frame by the analyst or interface
≠ functions as a frame in the target object
```

The former may be presentation or recontextualization. The latter requires source-dependent target-side work. Visual centrality, frequency, citation density, user interaction, or explanatory convenience cannot establish that work.

#### Recontextualization can precede but does not entail projection

A common chain is:

```text
source object X
→ Φ exposes a new target relation
→ PROJECT_AS candidate is formulated
```

The Φ occurrence and the later `PROJECT_AS` occurrence remain separate claims. Recontextualization can reveal a plausible function hypothesis; it cannot supply the function result. If the candidate fails, the recontextualized reading may still remain useful.

<a id="29-12-retype-versus-compose"></a>

### 29.12 RETYPE versus COMPOSE

`COMPOSE` forms a new composite analytical object from multiple or sequential source structures. `PROJECT_AS` assesses what bounded function an already formed source object performs within a declared target context. The difference concerns the transformation target.

```text
COMPOSE:
many or sequential source structures
→ new composite analytical object

PROJECT_AS:
origin-typed source object
→ bounded contextual target function
```

#### Compact PMS-grounded operation chain

The source-to-target relation can be made visible without repeating the source theory:

```text
warranted operator-typed occurrences and relations
→ COMPOSE
→ origin-typed source Trajectory X

Trajectory X with retained source reference
→ PROJECT_AS
→ bounded target function in C
```

Where finer carrier inspection is necessary, `DECOMPOSE` remains a separate occurrence with its own Record, Loss, and result. No universal Δ–Ψ formula for Trajectory formation is implied.

#### C29-COMPOSE-01 — formation of a new object remains a separate COMPOSE occurrence

If the analytical move selects several configurations, orders transitions, preserves constitutive relations, and forms a trajectory, the move is `COMPOSE`. Calling the resulting trajectory a frame-function does not retroactively make the formation process RETYPE.

The canonical chain is:

```text
configurations and transitions
COMPOSE
→ documented_trajectory_X

 documented_trajectory_X
PROJECT_AS candidate
→ bounded_frame_function in later_configuration_Y
```

Each arrow requires a separate claim, Record, Loss assessment, and result. The `COMPOSE` result supplies the source object. It does not supply the target function.

The inverse confusion also matters. If several historical structures are assembled directly into “the organizational frame,” the analyst may be hiding a composition inside a function label. The correct question is first whether a new composite object has been formed. Only after that object is identifiable can a separate target-function claim be tested.

```text
multiple sources summarized under one function label
≠ PROJECT_AS alone
```

Where the target claim itself depends on aggregating several source objects into a new target object, the operations must be segmented. A possible chain is:

```text
X1 + X2 + X3
COMPOSE
→ composite Q

Q
PROJECT_AS
→ function F in C
```

It is not permissible to use one mixed record in which composition selection, source formation, target function, Loss, and result are collapsed. A failure in Q’s composition cannot be erased by the apparent usefulness of F.

#### Same object, different question

The distinction does not depend on “upward” versus “cross-level” language. It depends on the claim:

- `COMPOSE` asks: **What new analytical object is formed from these sources and relations?**
- `PROJECT_AS` asks: **What bounded function does this already identified object perform in C?**

A trajectory can be the target object of `COMPOSE` and later the source object of `PROJECT_AS`. Its role changes across separate occurrences without its origin type being rewritten.

#### No automatic macro-function from composition

A well-formed path or trajectory may carry no distinct function in a later scene. Conversely, a weakly composed macro-label cannot be rescued by projecting it as a frame, attractor, or asymmetry function.

```text
admissible composite
≠ target function automatically

failed composition
≠ function label may bypass source failure
```

This keeps PATH’s formation burden and RETYPE’s function burden non-compensatory.

<a id="29-13-retype-versus-decompose"></a>

### 29.13 RETYPE versus DECOMPOSE

`DECOMPOSE` opens a compressed occurrence or composite under finer resolution while preserving reference to the same source object. `PROJECT_AS` keeps the source object as the unit entering the target claim and assesses a bounded function in another declared context.

```text
DECOMPOSE:
compressed occurrence or composite
→ finer relational reconstruction of the same reference object

PROJECT_AS:
origin-typed source object retained as source unit
→ bounded contextual function
```

#### C29-DECOMPOSE-01 — opening internal constitution remains a separate DECOMPOSE occurrence

A source object may need finer reconstruction before a proposed target function can be tested. For example, a trajectory’s frame-function may depend on sedimented exclusions and residual binding. SUB can open the trajectory to identify whether those structures are actually present and how they relate. That operation supplies source trace; it does not establish the target function.

```text
documented_trajectory_X
DECOMPOSE
→ finer source reconstruction

finer source reconstruction informs
PROJECT_AS candidate
→ bounded_frame_function in Y
```

The records remain separate because they answer different questions:

- `DECOMPOSE`: Which components and relations constitute or revise X under finer resolution?
- `PROJECT_AS`: Does X, retaining its source identity and type, perform F in C?

A list of internal components is not a target function. A target function is not an internal component. Nor does the fact that internal structures resemble the proposed function establish the source-to-context relation.

```text
internal constitution
≠ target-context performance
```

#### PROJECT_AS followed by DECOMPOSE

The reverse chain is also possible. A bounded function may be provisionally supported and later opened to determine how different source components carry, modulate, contradict, or fail to carry it.

```text
X PROJECT_AS F in C
→ separate bounded projection result

projected source occurrence or composite
DECOMPOSE
→ finer reconstruction of function-bearing relations
```

The later `DECOMPOSE` occurrence may confirm, revise, differentiate, or undermine the projection. It does not simply reveal the projection’s “true” finer form, and it cannot inherit the earlier Output Class. Fine resolution has no automatic truth privilege.

#### Separate records and Loss across the chain

Every chain segment requires:

```yaml
operation_chain_discipline:
  each_occurrence_has:
    - declared_source_and_target
    - operation_identity
    - independent_justification
    - independent_loss
    - independent_result
    - canonical_output_mapping
  inheritance:
    source_material: allowed_with_trace
    operation_success: prohibited
    output_class: prohibited
    authority: prohibited
```

A decomposition may reveal that only part of X carries the proposed function. The response may be Claim Reduction, a revised target function, a new source object formed through a later `COMPOSE`, or Failure. The analyst must not silently substitute the discovered component for X inside the existing projection claim. That substitution creates a new claim.

#### C29-HANDOFF-01 — Chapter 30 receives the conceptual constraints and alone owns the complete PROJECT_AS signature and record operation

Chapter 29 hands forward the following constraints:

- the source object and origin type precede projection;
- target context, scene or object, relative level, validity scope, purpose, and Claim Ceiling are mandatory;
- target function is specific, relational, source-traceable, and distinct from origin type and operator type;
- Source Object Integrity, Functional Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Contextual Boundedness, and complete Loss are non-compensatory;
- same-source context contrast and bounded source-variation pressure remain required;
- projection does not replace PATH, SUB, source heterogeneity, prior dispositions, or prior Loss;
- Recontextualization, `COMPOSE`, and `DECOMPOSE` remain separate operations or claims;
- source integrity, target-function effect, prior disposition, operation-specific result, and canonical Output Class remain separate;
- Failure, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, analogy-only, no-projection, and Non-Capture remain available and non-equivalent;
- every revised context, function, source typing, or scope creates a new testable claim;
- no projection creates a PMS primitive, person type, causal proof, legitimacy judgment, recommendation, sanction, or authority inheritance.

Chapter 30 alone will provide:

- the complete `PROJECT_AS` signature;
- formal preconditions;
- source, operation, target, admissibility, Loss, alternatives, governance, and result declarations;
- complete procedure and test ordering;
- operation-specific result vocabulary and canonical mapping;
- record completion and model synchronization rules.

Chapter 29 therefore closes its conceptual work without executing the operation.

<a id="chapter-29-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 29 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-29 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Gate controls, all twenty-four Chapter-29 Pressure Duties, the complete §§29.1–29.13 conceptual architecture, the stable same-source function-bearing/no-additional-function contrast, Origin-Type Preservation, Source Object Integrity, load-bearing-source discrimination, Functional Continuity, bounded source- and target-context variation, functional-elasticity pressure, Contextual Boundedness, canonical five-part Loss, projection without replacement, four-axis result separation, non-equivalent non-success routes, materially bounded re-entry, the boundaries to Φ recontextualization, `COMPOSE`, and `DECOMPOSE`, protected Chapter-30 ownership, Reference and Formal-Model synchronization, relative links, fingerprints, package integrity, ZIP CRC, and byte-identical roundtrip.

```text
Chapter 29 conceptual RETYPE architecture complete
+ source object X and origin type T remain separately visible
+ target context C and bounded function F remain relational
+ projection does not replace PATH, SUB, source heterogeneity, prior disposition, or Loss
+ operation and result boundaries remain separated
→ Chapter 29 provisionally locked
```

The Chapter-29 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The class applies to the chapter-level method under the still-open RETYPE Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not classify the `documented_trajectory_X` anchor as an executed projection, establish its candidate frame-function, select a canonical Output Class for that candidate, authorize another projection, or increase claim, application, governance, diagnostic, person-level, political, legal, or normative authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-29 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose: eight in WP1, eight in WP2, and eight in WP3. The integrated boundary records their closure without repeating their identifiers or creating additional duties.

No standalone Case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, schema field, target-function enum, origin-type enum, Loss field, score, semantic or causal classifier, Graph inference, Reader execution, person type, recommendation, sanction, legitimacy finding, or authority mechanism has been produced.

The integrated result preserves four independent axes:

```text
1. Source Object Integrity and origin-type status
2. target-function effect, no-function, or unresolved function status
3. prior source or operation disposition
4. canonical Output Class for an executed delimited claim
```

Chapter 29 locks the conceptual grammar, not an operation result. A valid source object may support no target function; a target-function candidate may fail without invalidating the source; source failure cannot be repaired by target usefulness; and a later context, function, source typing, relative level, scope, or purpose requires a new claim, separate Record, separate Loss, and independent result.

```text
Chapter 29 provisionally locked
≠ PROJECT_AS executed
≠ candidate target function established
≠ source relevance converted into function
≠ origin type replaced
≠ Φ recontextualization converted into PROJECT_AS
≠ COMPOSE or DECOMPOSE result inherited
≠ RETYPE provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, the Structure or Chapter Contract, source-object identity, origin type, target context, target function, Source Object Integrity, Functional Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Contextual Boundedness, canonical Loss, operation separation, result axes, non-success routing, re-entry, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, package integrity, or a later Chapter-30–40/LIMITS/integrated-audit dependency. Preference for a stronger function label, wider context, simpler narrative, denser Graph, more automation, automatic semantic classification, source-claim rescue, origin-type replacement, or authority transfer is insufficient.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 29 provisional lock
→ Chapter 30 Preparation Gate complete
→ Chapter 30 WP1
→ §§30.1–30.4
→ PROJECT_AS definition, minimal signature, preconditions, and source declaration
```

Chapter 30 receives the conceptual constraints of Chapter 29 but no executed projection, no established target function, no inherited operation result, no canonical Output Class, and no authority. It alone owns the complete `PROJECT_AS` operation procedure and Record architecture.

<a id="chapter-30-project-as-signature-context-and-validity-scope"></a>

## 30. PROJECT_AS: Signature, Context, and Validity Scope

Chapter 29 established the conceptual grammar of RETYPE. An independently reconstructed source object may be tested for a bounded function in a declared target context while its origin type, historical reference, prior disposition, and accumulated Loss remain visible. That conceptual grammar does not yet constitute an operation occurrence. Chapter 30 specifies the complete `PROJECT_AS` procedure through which a candidate relation becomes auditable, reducible, stoppable, rejectable, or provisionally supportable.

The governing question is:

> What declarations and tests make a proposed target function a valid `PROJECT_AS` occurrence rather than relabeling, analogy, source rewrite, type jump, recontextualization alone, hidden `COMPOSE`, hidden `DECOMPOSE`, or claim rescue?

A `PROJECT_AS` occurrence begins neither with a persuasive target label nor with the intuition that a source object “matters” at another level. It begins with an independently identifiable source object, a visible origin type, source-side coordinates and evidential basis, a declared target context, and a bounded function claim whose additional praxeological difference can later be tested. The procedure must retain the possibility that the source object remains valid while the projection fails, that the source typing is itself revised through source-side evidence, that no distinct target function is warranted, or that available materials cannot support capture.

The complete operation architecture is distributed across Sections 30.1–30.13. Sections 30.1–30.4 establish the exact operation identity, minimal signature, conjunctive entry conditions, and source declaration. Sections 30.5–30.8 specify target declaration, projection justification, Constitutive Source Trace, and Counterfactual Sensitivity. Sections 30.9–30.13 will complete validity scope, visibility and canonical Loss, alternatives, local result and canonical mapping, and the operation-specific Record view.

WP1 therefore creates an **operation-entry packet**, not an executed projection:

```text
operation identity
+ signature roles
+ conjunctive preconditions
+ complete source declaration
→ PROJECT_AS entry candidate

PROJECT_AS entry candidate
≠ target function warranted
≠ operation executed
≠ local result selected
≠ canonical Output Class selected
```

The chapter continues to operate under exactly three STRATA operations, exactly ten canonical Output Classes, the sixteen canonical admissibility checks, the twelve-stage audit, and the exact five-part Loss structure. No additional operation, primitive, Rule, class, audit stage, schema field, target-function enum, sensitivity score, causal classifier, person type, recommendation, sanction, or authority mechanism is introduced here.

<a id="30-1-definition"></a>

### 30.1 Definition

`PROJECT_AS` is the controlled projection of an origin-typed source object as a bounded function within a declared target context, while preserving the source reference, keeping the origin type visible, and treating the proposed target function as a separately testable contextual relation.

The operation has the directional form:

```text
origin-typed source object
→ bounded contextual target function
```

Each part of the definition is restrictive.

**Controlled** means that the projection is governed by explicit source and target declarations, a stated analytical purpose, an expected praxeological difference, Constitutive Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, validity scope, alternatives, canonical Loss, Stop, Non-Capture, and a Claim Ceiling. A relation does not become `PROJECT_AS` because it is rhetorically illuminating, graphically convenient, machine-readable, formally elegant, or familiar from another domain.

**Projection** means that the operation adds a bounded relation between a retained source object and a function claimed in a target context. It does not remove the source object from its source-side reconstruction, replace its origin type, or rewrite its prior history. The result, if later supported, is an additional claim about what the retained source does in `C_t`.

**Origin-typed source object** means that the source has already been identified independently of the proposed target function and has a declared source-side object category or occurrence typing. The origin type may be provisional, contested, layered, or later revised through source-side evidence. It may not be silently replaced merely because another label appears useful in the target context.

**Bounded function** means a specific, relational, context-limited contribution to legibility, expectation, access, cost, exposure, action corridors, binding, integration, temporal ordering, or another warranted praxis dimension. A target function is not an intrinsic property that follows the source object into every context.

**Declared target context** means a specified target scene, object, Frame relation, relative level, granularity, temporal interval, role relation, and analytical purpose sufficient to distinguish a target-side effect from current target structures, historical background relevance, analogy, or mere change of viewpoint. The phrase “at the macro level,” “for the system,” or “in a later context” does not by itself declare `C_t`.

**Preserving the source reference** means that the same historical, documentary, analytical, or case-bounded object remains traceable before and after projection. A function-shaped substitute assembled from convenient source fragments is not the same source merely because it receives the same name.

**Keeping the origin type visible** means that the target function is recorded alongside, not over, the source typing. Origin-Type Preservation does not immunize an erroneous typing. It prevents the projection occurrence from becoming the unmarked site of source-type revision.

**Separately testable contextual relation** means that the target-function claim can fail, narrow, remain unresolved, reduce to analogy, require Stop, or end in Non-Capture without automatically destroying the source object or repairing a prior failed source claim.

```text
source object X retains origin type T_o
+ X is tested for function F_t in context C_t
→ candidate contextual relation

candidate contextual relation
≠ X becomes F_t as origin type
≠ F_t becomes a new PMS primitive
≠ source relevance proves target function
```

#### C30-DEFINITION-01 — controlled bounded projection is neither relabeling nor source rewrite

Consider the Chapter-29 pressure object `documented_trajectory_X`. It has already been reconstructed as a Trajectory under a bounded source Frame, temporal scope, source basis, and prior disposition. A later analysis proposes that this trajectory may perform a bounded frame-function in `later_configuration_Y` because sedimented expectations, costs, exclusions, and bindings may organize what becomes legible and actionable there.

At operation entry, the admissible formulation is:

```text
source object: documented_trajectory_X
origin type: Trajectory
candidate relation: frame-function in later_configuration_Y
operation status: not executed
```

The inadmissible formulation is:

```text
documented_trajectory_X
→ is a Frame
```

The second formulation removes the target-context relation, collapses origin type into target function, and turns a proposed projection into relabeling. The first preserves the source object and establishes only the claim that Chapter 30 must later test.

#### PROJECT_AS is not renaming

Renaming substitutes one term for another while leaving the alleged analytical work unspecified. A valid projection must state what difference the target function makes in the declared context and which source features are proposed to carry that difference. A more evocative label is not an operation.

```text
new vocabulary
≠ new target function

operator-like word applied to X
≠ operator function established
```

#### PROJECT_AS is not source repair

A target context cannot rescue an invalid source object, an unsupported origin type, or an earlier failed transformation. If `X` lacks a traceable source reference, the analyst cannot move it into `C_t` and treat usefulness there as retroactive evidence that `X` was valid. A changed Frame, level, granularity, or function creates a new testable claim; it does not erase the disposition of the prior one.

#### PROJECT_AS is not automatic upward movement

The operation may often relate a source object to a function at a relatively higher level, but its direction is functional, not ontological. `g'` names a local target position relative to `g`. It does not imply a more real, more authoritative, more systemic, or more truthful layer. A locally lower-level target context is also possible where the relation is explicitly declared and functionally warranted.

#### PROJECT_AS remains distinct from Φ, COMPOSE, and DECOMPOSE

A changed reading or Frame may involve PMS `Φ` without assigning a target function. `COMPOSE` forms a new composite analytical object from multiple or sequential sources. `DECOMPOSE` opens a compressed occurrence or composite under finer resolution. `PROJECT_AS` retains one independently identified source object and tests what bounded function it performs in a target context.

```text
changed legibility alone
→ possible recontextualization

many or sequential source structures
→ possible COMPOSE

internal opening of the same reference object
→ possible DECOMPOSE

retained origin-typed source object tested for bounded contextual function
→ PROJECT_AS candidate
```

The operations may occur in sequence, but every occurrence requires a separate Record, separate Loss, independent result, and no inheritance of operation success, Output Class, or authority.

<a id="30-2-minimal-signature"></a>

### 30.2 Minimal Signature

The minimal `PROJECT_AS` signature is:

```text
PROJECT_AS:
(X_g, T_o, C_t)
→
(F_t, g', J, L, V)
```

The signature is an accountability map. It names the positions that a complete occurrence must declare and later test. It is not a generative law that produces valid outputs whenever three inputs are supplied.

#### Input positions

**`X_g` — source object at relative source level `g`.**  
`X` is the independently identifiable source occurrence or composite. The subscript `g` records its relative source position within the declared transformation relation. It does not make `X` ontologically micro, low, basic, or subordinate. `X_g` may be a configuration, Event-like object, Non-Event structure, Path, Trajectory, recurrent trajectory form, operator-typed occurrence, phase, composite, or another source-supported analytical object. The family inventory remains open because source identity is determined by the source reconstruction, not by a closed enum created for RETYPE.

**`T_o` — origin type.**  
`T_o` records the source-side typing that remains visible through the projection. It may be a Trajectory, Frame-typed occurrence, composite, Event-like object, or another warranted source type. It is not the target function and is not made permanently true by entering the signature. A later source-side revision must be recorded as such and triggers a new or revised projection claim.

**`C_t` — target context.**  
`C_t` identifies where the function is claimed. It must later be expanded through the target declaration into the relevant scene or object, target Frame, granularity, relative level, time span, roles, analytical purpose, and Claim Ceiling. It cannot be inferred from `F_t`: the same function label may be warranted in one context, unwarranted in another, or too elastic to discriminate anything.

#### Output and control positions

**`F_t` — bounded target function.**  
`F_t` names the specific relational work proposed for `X` in `C_t`. It is not produced automatically and is not an origin-type replacement. The target function remains a candidate until target declaration, justification, Constitutive Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, validity scope, alternatives, Loss, and the complete audit have been supplied.

**`g'` — relative target level.**  
`g'` records the target position relative to `g`. It is locally declared and may not be treated as a universal micro/meso/macro tier. A change from `g` to `g'` can accompany `PROJECT_AS`, but level difference alone does not establish the operation or its validity.

**`J` — projection justification.**  
`J` will state why a distinct target function is analytically needed, which source features are proposed to carry it, what target-side praxis difference is expected, and which rival or no-projection account remains plausible. A label, citation, analogy, convenience, or graph fit is not `J`.

**`L` — canonical Loss with separate visibility disclosure.**  
`L` retains exactly the five canonical fields `preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`. Projection-specific foregrounding and backgrounding will be disclosed separately as visibility changes; they do not create additional Loss fields. At WP1, the source declaration must already carry inherited Loss from prior operations so that the projection cannot begin from an artificially restored source.

**`V` — validity scope.**  
`V` will bound context, time, relative level, granularity, roles, praxis dimensions, transfer, termination or re-entry conditions, Claim Scope, and Claim Ceiling. It prevents a local target function from becoming an all-context property of `X`.

#### C30-SIGNATURE-01 — every signature symbol has a distinct role

The signature separates three kinds of position:

```text
source identity and source typing:
X_g, T_o

target location and candidate function:
C_t, F_t, g'

operation accountability:
J, L, V
```

No symbol may silently absorb another. In particular:

```text
T_o ≠ F_t
g ≠ g'
C_t ≠ F_t
J ≠ source citation
L ≠ visibility annotation
V ≠ global transfer permission
```

A complete-looking tuple that leaves any of these distinctions implicit is not a complete occurrence.

#### C30-NOCAUSELAW-01 — the signature is neither causal law nor automatic output generator

The arrow in the signature indicates the direction of the proposed analytical transformation. It does not state:

```text
if X_g, T_o, and C_t are present,
then F_t necessarily exists
```

Nor does it state that `X` causally produces `F_t`, that the target function is empirically true, or that a validator can compute the function from the input fields. The same source object and origin type may enter two target contexts with different results:

```text
(X_g, T_o, C_Y)
→ candidate F_Y

(X_g, T_o, C_Z)
→ no distinct function warranted
```

The signature therefore encodes a testable claim architecture, not a deterministic mapping. Formal completion can verify that each position has been declared. It cannot establish that the declared target function is semantically adequate, empirically warranted, causally produced, normatively valid, or authoritative.

#### Minimal signature expansion at operation entry

WP1 permits the following bounded expansion:

```yaml
project_as_entry:
  source_object: X_g
  origin_type: T_o
  target_context: C_t
  candidate_target_function: F_t
  target_level: g_prime
  justification_status: pending_WP2
  loss_status:
    inherited_loss_retained: true
    current_projection_loss_pending: true
  validity_scope_status: pending_WP3
  operation_status: entry_candidate_not_executed
```

This is not a second schema and does not require a new YAML artifact. It is a prose view of positions already carried by the Shared Transformation Record and existing `PROJECT_AS` payload. The unfilled positions are declared as pending rather than fabricated.

<a id="30-3-preconditions"></a>

### 30.3 Preconditions

`PROJECT_AS` may enter substantive testing only when all required entry conditions are present. The preconditions are conjunctive. Strength in one dimension cannot compensate for absence in another.

A complete operation-entry packet requires:

1. **an identifiable source object and source record;**
2. **a declared origin type and source reference;**
3. **declared source coordinates and preliminary target coordinates;**
4. **a specific target context and named candidate target function;**
5. **an expected praxeological difference that could distinguish function from relevance or resemblance;**
6. **a candidate route to Constitutive Source Trace;**
7. **a bounded Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;**
8. **sources sufficient to test the proposed relation rather than merely name it;**
9. **visible prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss;**
10. **continued availability of alternatives, Stop, and Non-Capture.**

The list is conjunctive but not a truth checklist. Satisfying entry conditions authorizes testing; it does not establish the result.

#### C30-PRECONDITIONS-01 — operation-entry burdens are conjunctive and explicit

The following combinations remain insufficient:

```text
clear target label
+ missing source reference
≠ PROJECT_AS entry

strong source object
+ unspecified target context
≠ PROJECT_AS entry

complete coordinates
+ no expected praxis difference
≠ PROJECT_AS entry

plausible historical relevance
+ no route to constitutive source trace
≠ PROJECT_AS entry

schema-complete record
+ exceeded Source Ceiling
≠ substantively admissible projection
```

No score, average, or majority rule is permitted. A missing source object cannot be offset by a precise target Frame. A rich archive cannot compensate for a function that makes no discriminating target-side difference. A bounded context cannot repair origin-type replacement. A well-formed record cannot replace material source sufficiency.

#### Identifiable source object and source record

The source object must be reconstructible before the target function is accepted. The Record must point to the prior source-side claim or transformation occurrence from which `X` enters. Where `X` was produced through `COMPOSE`, opened through `DECOMPOSE`, or revised through a later source-side claim, that lineage must remain visible.

A source object may enter without a prior STRATA operation where it has an independently supported source reconstruction. It may not enter as “whatever carries the target function.” That definition is circular.

#### Declared origin type and source reference

The origin type determines what is being preserved, not what can never be revised. The source reference identifies the historical, documentary, analytical, or case-bounded object to which the claim applies. Both are necessary because the same source label may refer to different occurrences, and the same occurrence may support rival or layered source typings.

#### Source and target coordinates

At entry, source Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, and basis must be explicit. The target context and relative target position must be sufficiently specified to prevent an unbounded function claim. Full target declaration belongs to Section 30.5, but `PROJECT_AS` cannot begin with an empty `C_t` placeholder.

#### Named target function and expected praxis difference

A candidate target function must be specific enough to state what would change in the target reconstruction if it were warranted. The expected difference may concern action corridors, costs, exposure, roles, access, expectations, bindings, alternatives, temporal ordering, interpretation, integration, or Stop conditions. “The source is relevant,” “history matters,” or “the pattern looks similar” is not enough.

The full justification and target-side comparison belong to Section 30.6. WP1 requires the burden to be named before result selection so the operation cannot reverse-engineer a difference after choosing its preferred label.

#### Candidate route to Constitutive Source Trace

At entry, the analyst must identify which source materials and source features could carry, modulate, weaken, or defeat the function claim. The complete trace categories are established in Section 30.7. A citation list alone does not satisfy this precondition. The entry burden is the existence of a credible route from source structure to target-function test.

#### Bounded claim and sufficient sources

Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling must be set before execution. The available source basis must be sufficient for the relation being proposed. A narrow local projection may remain testable where a broad historical or cross-domain projection exceeds the Source Ceiling. Narrowing the claim must be recorded as narrowing, not presented as confirmation of the original stronger claim.

#### Alternatives, Stop, and Non-Capture remain open

At least one rival target-function account and the no-projection option must remain representable. Stop must be available when continuation becomes unnecessary or inadmissible. Non-Capture must remain available where no adequate bounded STRATA relation can be supported without manufacturing source load or semantic continuity.

At WP1 these routes are protected, not selected.

#### Precondition failure and route discipline

Failure of an entry condition does not automatically select one canonical Output Class. The correct response depends on the tested claim and why entry cannot proceed:

- a missing but obtainable declaration may require completion before testing;
- a broader claim may require `claim_reduction_required` if a narrower one remains supportable;
- continuation beyond source or type limits may require `mandatory_stop`;
- a retained resemblance without functional warrant may later map to `analogy_only` as a separate claim;
- unavailable or unstable capture may require `non_capture`;
- an unsupported relation may become `failed_transformation` after the delimited claim is tested.

WP1 does not route these outcomes. It prevents the operation from treating entry failure as a successful projection.

#### Stop before execution

A Stop may occur before full operation execution when:

- the target context cannot be bounded;
- the source object cannot be independently identified;
- the proposed function requires origin-type replacement;
- the source route is exhausted or inaccessible;
- the expected difference is only rhetorical;
- the task is actually recontextualization, `COMPOSE`, or `DECOMPOSE`;
- continuation would exceed Source, Claim, Calibration, or Authority Ceiling;
- the no-projection account already explains the target context without loss of warranted discrimination.

An Optional Stop records that further projection is unnecessary. A Mandatory Stop records that continuation would be inadmissible. Neither is a claim that the source object is invalid or that the target context lacks structure.

<a id="30-4-source-declaration"></a>

### 30.4 Source Declaration

Every `PROJECT_AS` occurrence begins with a source declaration that makes the retained object, its source-side typing, coordinates, evidential basis, prior status, uncertainty, and inherited Loss independently inspectable.

The compact Structure view is:

```yaml
source:
  object:
  origin_type:
  source_frame:
  source_granularity:
  source_level:
  temporal_scope:
  source_basis:
```

The complete operation-entry view additionally carries fields already required by the Shared Record and Chapter-29 preservation duties:

```yaml
source:
  object:
  origin_type:
  source_reference:
  source_frame:
  source_granularity:
  source_level:
  temporal_scope:
  source_basis:
  prior_disposition:
  uncertainty:
  inherited_loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:
```

This expansion does not create a second schema or new canonical Loss fields. It brings existing record obligations into the `PROJECT_AS` source view.

#### Conditional PMS occurrence-route requirement

Where the source is a PMS operator occurrence, the source declaration identifies the concrete occurrence and its source Frame. Where the source is a Composite or derived object, `source_reference` and `source_basis` point to the prior Record that preserves the claim-relevant operator-typed constituents or relations. Where that detail is unavailable or immaterial, the limit is stated rather than inferred from the proposed target function.

```text
generic source label
without inspectable source route
≠ complete PMS-grounded source declaration
```

No new Record field is introduced. The burden is carried by the existing source reference, source basis, origin type, uncertainty, prior disposition, and inherited Loss positions.

#### C30-SOURCEDECL-01 — complete source declaration and source-reference trace

A valid source declaration must answer:

- What is the source object?
- Which source-side type or object category is currently claimed?
- Which exact historical, documentary, analytical, or record identity anchors it?
- Under which Frame, granularity, relative level, and temporal scope was it reconstructed?
- Which materials and prior records constitute its source basis?
- What prior disposition applies to the source claim or preceding operation?
- Which uncertainties remain open?
- Which Loss has already occurred and cannot be reset by projection?

For the continuing Chapter-29 pressure object, the operation-entry declaration may take the following non-executing form:

```yaml
source:
  object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked PATH record and declared source corpus for trajectory X
  source_frame: bounded historical-development frame used in the source reconstruction
  source_granularity: trajectory resolution preserving constitutive transitions, Non-Events, and sedimented relations
  source_level: source trajectory relative to later_configuration_Y
  temporal_scope: declared beginning, relevant transitions, and bounded endpoint of trajectory X
  source_basis: identified records and source pointers retained from the PATH and any later source-side audit
  prior_disposition: retained exactly from the source record; not upgraded by entry into RETYPE
  uncertainty:
    - unresolved source intervals or rival source constructions remain visible
    - target-function relevance is not yet established
  inherited_loss:
    preserved:
      - source reference and origin-type visibility
      - constitutive transitions retained by the source record
    compressed:
      - local variation already compressed by trajectory formation
    excluded:
      - source materials outside the bounded trajectory claim
    uncertain:
      - unresolved relations declared in the source record
    irrecoverable:
      - information already lost through prior selection or unavailable source history
```

The declaration identifies what enters `PROJECT_AS`. It does not establish that the trajectory performs a frame-function in `later_configuration_Y`, does not decide which features are load-bearing, and does not select a local or canonical result.

#### Source object and source reference

`object` names the analytical object. `source_reference` anchors the object to a specific source-side record, historical process, documentary bundle, case identity, or independently reconstructible occurrence. The two are related but not identical.

```text
object label
≠ source reference

repeated label
≠ preserved identity
```

Where the source object was produced by `COMPOSE`, the source declaration points to the composite record and its formation lineage. Where it was previously opened by `DECOMPOSE`, the declaration identifies whether the projected source is the original retained object or a separately reconstructed component/composite that requires its own source claim. Silent substitution is prohibited.

#### C30-ORIGIN-01 — origin type is preserved without source-type immunization

The source declaration records `origin_type` before the target function is tested. During one `PROJECT_AS` occurrence, the target function may not overwrite that typing.

```text
origin type preserved within the projection claim
≠ origin type immune to revision
```

If later source-side evidence undermines `T_o`, the analyst must revise the source claim or execute the appropriate source-side transformation. The existing projection cannot be preserved merely by saying that `F_t` remains useful. A changed origin type produces a materially revised claim and requires re-entry with a separate Record, Loss, and result.

Layered or rival source typings may be declared where warranted. The source field must not fabricate one exclusive type merely to satisfy the form. What is prohibited is unmarked replacement of the declared origin type by the candidate target function.

#### C30-COORDINATES-01 — source Frame, granularity, level, temporal scope, and basis are mandatory

The source coordinates determine what has actually been reconstructed and what the projection may legitimately carry forward.

**Source Frame** states the relevance boundary under which `X` was identified.  
**Source granularity** states the distinction density at which the source claim is warranted.  
**Source level** states the relative source position in the declared source-target relation.  
**Temporal scope** states the relevant interval, sequence, duration, or open endpoint where time is material.  
**Source basis** states the materials, records, and prior reconstructions that support the source object.

These coordinates may differ from target coordinates, but differences must later be declared rather than smuggled into the function label. Source-level and target-level language remains relational. No coordinate increase transfers truth or authority.

An incomplete declaration such as:

```yaml
source:
  object: organizational history
  origin_type: trajectory
  source_level: micro
```

is insufficient. It does not identify which history, which Frame, which granularity, which temporal boundary, which source corpus, or relative to what target relation “micro” is being used.

#### Source basis and Source Ceiling

The source basis must distinguish direct records, prior transformation records, derived reconstructions, unresolved gaps, and inaccessible materials where those differences matter. A source pointer enables inspection; it does not substitute for Constitutive Source Trace. The Source Ceiling follows the actual materials and reconstruction precision, not the ambition of the target function.

```text
many sources
≠ sufficient source trace

precise source coordinates
≠ target function warranted
```

#### C30-PRIORLOSS-01 — prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss remain visible before projection

A source object does not enter RETYPE as a restored, purified, or certainty-upgraded object. The source declaration must retain:

- the prior disposition of the source claim or operation;
- unresolved uncertainty and rival source constructions;
- all inherited canonical Loss;
- prior Stop, reduction, Failure, or Non-Capture boundaries where relevant;
- any limits on transfer into the target claim.

The five canonical Loss fields remain:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Projection may later generate additional Loss and visibility effects, but it cannot erase inherited Loss. An item previously marked `irrecoverable` cannot reappear as a supported load-bearing feature without new source evidence and a revised source-side record. A prior `claim_reduction_required` cannot become `admissible` merely because a narrower target function is attractive. The target claim must state whether it uses the reduced source object, tests a new source claim, or exceeds the permitted inheritance boundary.

```text
new target context
≠ source reset

new function label
≠ uncertainty removal

new Record
≠ prior disposition erased
```

#### Minimal source-entry integrity test

Before proceeding to Section 30.5, the analyst must be able to answer yes, no, or underdetermined—without selecting a final Output Class—to the following entry questions:

1. Is `X` independently identifiable before `F_t` is accepted?
2. Is the source reference precise enough to distinguish this object from similarly named objects?
3. Is `T_o` visible and separable from the candidate function?
4. Are source Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, and basis declared?
5. Is the prior disposition retained without upgrade?
6. Are uncertainty and inherited Loss explicit?
7. Can the source object remain valid if the later projection fails?
8. Would a source-type revision trigger a new or revised projection claim rather than silent replacement?

A “yes” pattern permits progression to target declaration. It does not establish substantive admissibility. A material “no” may require completion, Claim Reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture depending on the bounded claim and reason. An “underdetermined” result remains visible; it may not be converted into certainty by formal completion.

#### WP1 completion boundary

WP1 establishes:

- the exact `PROJECT_AS` operation identity;
- the distinct roles of all minimal-signature positions;
- the non-causal and non-automatic status of the signature;
- conjunctive operation-entry preconditions;
- the complete source declaration and source-reference trace;
- Origin-Type Preservation without source-type immunization;
- mandatory source coordinates and source basis;
- retention of prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited five-part Loss.

WP1 does not yet establish:

- the complete target declaration;
- a justified target function;
- the expected-difference assessment;
- Constitutive Source Trace categories or their adequacy;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity result;
- current projection Loss or visibility effects;
- alternatives assessment;
- validity scope;
- local operation result;
- canonical Output Class;
- a complete `PROJECT_AS` Record;
- operation success or Chapter-30 lock.

The local WP1 status is:

```text
PROJECT_AS definition and source-entry architecture complete
+ target-side and audit burdens remain pending
→ chapter_30_wp1_complete_chapter_open
```

No `PROJECT_AS` occurrence has been executed. The `documented_trajectory_X` pressure object remains an unexecuted candidate whose target function, source trace, sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, scope, and result are unresolved.

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 30 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 30 WP2
→ §§30.5–30.8
→ Target Declaration, Projection Justification,
   Constitutive Source Trace, and Counterfactual Sensitivity
```
<a id="30-5-target-declaration"></a>

### 30.5 Target Declaration

The source-entry packet established in Sections 30.1–30.4 is insufficient until the proposed function is located in a target-side reconstruction. A target declaration specifies **where**, **with respect to what**, **at which relational position**, **for which analytical purpose**, and **within which initial claim boundary** the retained source object is proposed to function. It prevents the target label from floating free of a scene and prevents cross-level language from supplying an undeclared object, Frame, or scope.

The operation-specific target view is:

```yaml
target:
  context:
  object_or_scene:
  target_function:
  target_frame:
  target_granularity:
  target_level:
  validity_scope:
  analytical_purpose:
  claim_ceiling:
```

The fields are conjunctive. A target context without a target object remains too diffuse; a target object without a function states only what is being inspected; a function without target coordinates cannot be bounded; and an analytical purpose without a Claim Ceiling can silently expand from reconstruction into explanation, evaluation, recommendation, or authority.

#### C30-TARGETDECL-01 — target context, object or scene, function, Frame, granularity, level, and scope are mandatory

A valid target declaration must answer:

- Which later, wider, narrower, parallel, or otherwise related context is under reconstruction?
- Which object, scene, configuration, process, or composite inside that context receives the proposed source-dependent function?
- What exact function is claimed?
- Which target Frame determines relevance and inside/outside boundaries?
- At what target granularity is the claimed difference visible?
- What is the source object’s relative target-level position in this declared relation?
- What initial validity boundary prevents transfer beyond the present occurrence?
- What analytical question makes the function worth testing?
- What is the highest claim type permitted by current sources and method?

For the continuing pressure object, a **candidate declaration**, not a projection result, may be written as:

```yaml
target:
  context: later_configuration_Y
  object_or_scene: bounded later scene in which expectations, roles, costs, exclusions, and bindings are reconstructed
  target_function: candidate bounded_frame_function
  target_frame: declared frame of later_configuration_Y rather than the historical source frame
  target_granularity: configuration-level distinctions sufficient to compare present structures with historical load
  target_level: trajectory X considered as one possible conditioning source relative to later_configuration_Y
  validity_scope: candidate scope limited to this scene, time window, role relation, and named praxis dimensions; full scope test pending Section 30.9
  analytical_purpose: test whether retained historical load changes later legibility and action corridors beyond current target structures alone
  claim_ceiling: bounded reconstructive function claim; no causal, deterministic, normative, person-level, recommendation, sanction, or authority claim
```

This declaration does not establish that the function exists. It creates a target-side object against which necessity, source load, alternatives, and sensitivity can be tested.

A declaration such as:

```yaml
target:
  context: society
  target_function: frame
  target_level: macro
```

is insufficient. “Society” does not identify a bounded target object or scene; “frame” does not specify what the source does; and “macro” supplies neither a relational comparison nor a Claim Ceiling.

#### Target context and target Frame

`context` names the bounded target situation in which the function is proposed. `target_frame` specifies the relevance structure used to reconstruct that situation. They may overlap, but they are not interchangeable.

```text
target context
≠ target Frame
≠ transformation context
```

A target context may contain several operative Frames. The transformation context additionally includes the analytical purpose, source-target relation, source basis, permitted claim, and conditions under which the operation is being conducted. A change in target Frame may be relevant to `PROJECT_AS`; it does not by itself establish a source-dependent target function.

#### Target granularity and relative target level

Target granularity states which distinctions must be visible for the claimed function to make a difference. Target level states the source object’s relative position in the declared source-target relation. Neither is a truth rank.

```text
finer target granularity
≠ stronger projection

higher relative target level
≠ higher authority
```

Where a function appears only after changing granularity, the Record must show that the finer or coarser distinction is supported rather than produced by resolution drift. Where the source is called “micro,” “meso,” or “macro,” the comparison object must be named locally.

#### Initial validity boundary and Claim Ceiling

Section 30.9 will operationalize the complete validity scope. Section 30.5 requires an initial boundary at declaration time so the function cannot be justified first and bounded later. At minimum, the target declaration marks the intended scene, time window, relative level, relevant roles or relations, affected praxis dimensions, transfer presumption, and Claim Ceiling.

The default transfer presumption is:

```text
validity outside the declared target context
→ not established
```

A later expansion is a new testable claim with a separate Record and Loss, not a clerical extension of the current occurrence.

#### C30-TARGETOBJFUNC-01 — target object and target function remain distinct

The **target object or scene** is what is being reconstructed in `C_t`. The **target function** is the bounded contribution the retained source object is proposed to make within that reconstruction.

```text
target object: later_configuration_Y
target function: candidate bounded_frame_function
```

The later configuration does not become the function, and the function is not a second target object. The source object remains `documented_trajectory_X`; the target object remains `later_configuration_Y`; the proposed relation states only what the source may do there.

This distinction prevents several collapses:

```text
target object named
≠ target function established

target function named
≠ target object adequately declared

source object resembles target object
≠ source performs target function
```

A target function must be phrased as a relational contribution, not as an intrinsic label. “Functions as one bounded source of later framing by altering credible interpretations and action corridors” is testable. “Is the macro Frame” collapses source, function, target object, and operator type.

#### Minimal target-entry integrity test

Before proceeding to justification, the analyst must be able to answer yes, no, or underdetermined to these questions without selecting a final operation result:

1. Is the target context bounded enough to distinguish this occurrence from other contexts?
2. Is the target object or scene independently identifiable?
3. Is the target function stated as a specific relational contribution?
4. Are target Frame, granularity, and relative level declared?
5. Is the initial validity boundary stated before support is assessed?
6. Is the analytical purpose reconstructive and claim-typed?
7. Is the Claim Ceiling explicit?
8. Can the target object remain valid if the candidate function fails?

A material “no” blocks progression until repaired, reduced, stopped, failed, or left non-captured as appropriate. Formal completion of the fields does not establish semantic adequacy.

<a id="30-6-projection-justification"></a>

### 30.6 Projection Justification

Projection justification states why a distinct target-function claim is analytically needed rather than merely attractive. It links the declared source object to a named target-side difference while retaining rival pressure and the possibility that present target structures already carry the full reconstructive load.

A complete justification answers four questions:

1. Why is a distinct source-dependent function needed in this target context?
2. Which source features are candidates to carry that function?
3. What additional praxeological difference should become visible if the function is warranted?
4. Which rival function or no-projection account could explain the target scene as well or better?

The justification is not a narrative bridge between two labels. It is a bounded claim about discriminative gain.

#### C30-JUSTIFICATION-01 — analytical need, source carrier, rival pressure, and target-side difference are required

A projection justification must contain four separable burdens.

**Analytical need.** The target reconstruction must be materially incomplete, distorted, or less discriminating without the candidate relation. “The source is historically important” does not show that a distinct target function is needed.

**Source carrier.** The justification must identify which source-side structures are proposed to bear the function. A source title, citation cluster, thematic resemblance, or general background relevance is not a carrier.

**Rival pressure.** At least one plausible rival function and the no-projection account must remain live. Full alternatives assessment belongs to Section 30.11, but justification cannot be written as though the preferred function were the only available description.

**Target-side difference.** The claim must state what changes in the target reconstruction if the source-dependent function is retained rather than omitted.

For `documented_trajectory_X` in `later_configuration_Y`, the provisional justification may be stated as:

```yaml
justification:
  analytical_need: current target structures may not alone explain why some interpretations, exits, and commitments are credible or costly in later_configuration_Y
  candidate_source_carriers:
    - sedimented expectations retained across the trajectory
    - accumulated asymmetric costs and exposure
    - historically closed or narrowed alternatives
    - persistent bindings and unresolved Non-Events
  rival_pressure:
    - present_configuration_frame_function
    - bounded_attractor_function
    - background_historical_relevance_without_distinct_projection
    - no_projection
  proposed_target_side_difference: the target reconstruction changes in its account of credible interpretations, action corridors, role burdens, exit costs, and expected continuations
  operation_status: justification_candidate_only
```

Every line remains testable and defeasible. The declaration does not determine which carrier is constitutive, whether the rival account is weaker, or whether the proposed difference is supported.

#### Analytical necessity is claim-relative

A target function can be unnecessary for one claim and necessary for another. A historical trajectory may add no difference to a narrow description of current formal roles while materially changing a claim about credible exit, expected repair, or binding continuity. The analyst must therefore name the target claim before declaring necessity.

```text
useful for one analytical purpose
≠ generally necessary

background relevance
≠ distinct target-function necessity
```

If removal of the function label leaves all warranted target claims unchanged, the projection falls below the relevance burden even when the source remains historically meaningful.

#### Rival pressure and parsimony

A rival account is not included merely to be dismissed. It must be stated at comparable granularity and Claim Scope. A no-projection account may be superior where current target Frames, present asymmetries, or immediate constraints explain the relevant praxis difference without historical functional load.

For the same `documented_trajectory_X` in `later_configuration_Z`, the pressure formulation is:

```yaml
contrast_justification:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  target_context: later_configuration_Z
  current_target_structures: sufficient candidate account of relevant roles, costs, and action corridors
  additional_source_dependent_difference: not yet identified
  no_projection_status: live and potentially more parsimonious
  operation_status: not executed
```

The contrast prevents the target function from becoming an invariant label attached to the source object across contexts.

#### C30-EXPECTEDDIFF-01 — the expected praxeological difference is named before result selection

Before the source trace or sensitivity result is evaluated, the Record must name the expected difference in at least one warranted praxis dimension. Relevant dimensions may include:

- legibility or interpretation;
- action corridors and blocked alternatives;
- roles and responsibility distribution;
- access, exposure, cost, or risk;
- expectations and Non-Events;
- temporal ordering, delay, persistence, or irreversibility;
- bindings, commitments, and exit conditions;
- integration, conflict, or Stop conditions.

The declaration should have the form:

```yaml
expected_praxeological_difference:
  affected_dimensions:
    - credible_interpretations
    - action_corridors
    - asymmetric_exit_costs
    - binding_expectations
  comparison:
    with_candidate_function: historical load is represented as an active bounded condition of the later scene
    without_candidate_function: the later scene is reconstructed from present structures and general background history only
  disconfirmation_condition: no warranted target claim changes after the candidate source load is removed or materially varied
```

Naming the difference before result selection blocks retrospective tailoring. The expected difference is a test target, not a finding. It may later be supported, narrowed, contradicted, underdetermined, or rendered untestable.

```text
expected difference declared
≠ expected difference observed
≠ target function warranted
```

A claim such as “the trajectory matters at the macro level” fails this burden because it specifies neither the target-side dimension nor the comparison under which a difference would appear.

<a id="30-7-constitutive-source-trace"></a>

### 30.7 Constitutive Source Trace

Constitutive Source Trace identifies which source-side structures are proposed to carry, shape, weaken, or fail to support the target function. It is the operation’s traceability core. A citation identifies where material may be inspected; a trace identifies how particular source structures bear upon the function claim.

The operation-specific trace view is:

```yaml
constitutive_source_trace:
  load_bearing_features:
  modulating_features:
  compressed_features:
  excluded_features:
  uncertain_relations:
  source_pointers:
```

The categories are analytical roles inside one projection occurrence. They do not change the source object’s origin type, create a closed feature ontology, or add new canonical Loss fields.

#### Operator-occurrence anchoring inside Constitutive Source Trace

If a `PROJECT_AS` claim rests materially on PMS operator-typed occurrences or relations, at least one load-bearing trace must identify the concrete occurrence-level relation that carries the proposed target difference. The trace preserves occurrence identity, source Frame, temporal or relational placement, source support, and the distinction among operator type, occurrence, Composite, and derived object.

Possible source-supported traces include:

```text
repeated supported non-repair
→ Λ-occurrence trace within a declared expectation Frame

accumulated unequal exit cost
→ Ω-occurrence relation retained across the source Trajectory

persistent commitment continuity
→ Ψ-related occurrence trace retained in its source dependency context
```

These are examples, not automatic typings from ordinary-language words. An operator label without occurrence identity, relation, placement, and support remains insufficient Source Trace.

#### C30-SOURCETRACE-01 — constitutive trace cannot be replaced by citation, salience, or resemblance

A Constitutive Source Trace must do more than list documents, quote a source description, name the source object, or show structural similarity. It must specify a bounded route from source structure to the expected target-side difference.

```text
source pointer
→ inspectability route

constitutive source trace
→ claimed load relation

source pointer
≠ claimed load relation
```

A source feature enters the trace only when its proposed relevance to `F_t` is stated. “Repeated delays are documented” is a source observation. “Repeated delays stabilized an expectation of non-repair that narrows credible action corridors in `later_configuration_Y`” is a candidate trace relation. The latter remains subject to source support, target comparison, and sensitivity testing.

Trace adequacy requires:

- source identity and location;
- the feature or relation being carried forward;
- its status as load-bearing, modulating, compressed, excluded, or uncertain;
- the target-side dimension it is proposed to affect;
- relevant temporal, relational, or sequence structure;
- known counterevidence or rival interpretation;
- a route by which material change could weaken or defeat the claim.

A dense list of source features is not automatically a stronger trace. Features that cannot be related to the declared target difference increase detail without increasing traceable load.

#### C30-LOADMOD-01 — load-bearing, modulating, compressed, excluded, and uncertain features remain separate

**Load-bearing features** are proposed to be constitutive for the target-function claim. Their material removal, reversal, or replacement must be capable of weakening, changing, or defeating the claim.

**Modulating features** alter degree, reach, timing, role distribution, or form without alone constituting the function. Their change may narrow or strengthen the claim rather than eliminate it.

**Compressed features** are relevant source structures retained only at reduced resolution in the target-function account. Compression must be disclosed because the function cannot preserve all source heterogeneity.

**Excluded features** are source structures deliberately outside the present target-function claim. Exclusion may reflect irrelevance, bounded scope, competing Frame, insufficient support, or deliberate claim reduction. Exclusion must not be described as nonexistence.

**Uncertain relations** are candidate source-function connections whose direction, strength, timing, or role cannot currently be warranted. Uncertainty remains visible and cannot be redistributed into confident load-bearing language.

A provisional trace for the pressure object may be organized as follows:

```yaml
constitutive_source_trace:
  load_bearing_features:
    - candidate: repeated documented non-repair altered expectations across the trajectory
      proposed_target_difference: repair is less credible and delay carries higher coordination cost in later_configuration_Y
    - candidate: accumulated asymmetric exit costs narrowed historically available alternatives
      proposed_target_difference: formal exit and practical exit remain distinct in the later scene
    - candidate: persistent bindings survived recontextualization
      proposed_target_difference: commitments remain action-structuring rather than merely remembered
  modulating_features:
    - later role changes may reduce or redistribute the historical framing load
    - present institutional safeguards may weaken some inherited expectations
    - current resources may reopen alternatives without erasing prior cost structure
  compressed_features:
    - local variation among intermediate transitions
    - heterogeneous role-specific experiences summarized at trajectory resolution
    - short-lived reversals that do not receive separate target-function claims here
  excluded_features:
    - source episodes outside the declared trajectory Frame
    - biographical or institutional material unsupported by the source record
    - present target structures assessed independently of historical load
  uncertain_relations:
    - whether one later recontextualization materially interrupts the claimed continuity
    - whether current safeguards carry more explanatory load than the trajectory
    - whether role-specific effects can be generalized across the target scene
  source_pointers:
    - locked PATH trajectory record for documented_trajectory_X
    - declared source corpus and later source-side audit references
```

This trace remains a candidate assessment. It does not establish that the listed features are truly constitutive, that their target-side effects are causal, or that one function label is superior to every rival.

#### Trace roles and inherited Loss

Trace categories must not overwrite inherited Loss. A feature compressed during prior `COMPOSE` cannot be treated as fully recovered because it is now called load-bearing. A feature marked uncertain remains uncertain unless new source evidence and a revised source-side claim warrant change. A feature previously irrecoverable cannot be reconstructed through target-side usefulness.

```text
load-bearing candidate
≠ recovered source detail

foregrounded in projection
≠ preserved without Loss

excluded from function claim
≠ absent from source object
```

The current projection may later add new compression or exclusion. Section 30.10 will record those effects under the exact five canonical Loss fields and separate visibility declarations.

#### Load-bearing test

A candidate feature may be treated as load-bearing only if the analyst can state:

1. which source relation is materially relevant;
2. which target-side difference it is proposed to support;
3. why present target structures or a rival function do not already carry the full load;
4. how alteration of the feature could change or defeat the function claim;
5. which source evidence and uncertainty bound the assertion.

Failure to answer these questions creates traceability, reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture pressure. It does not invite the addition of more citations until the same label appears plausible.

<a id="30-8-counterfactual-sensitivity"></a>

### 30.8 Counterfactual Sensitivity

Counterfactual Sensitivity tests whether the proposed target function responds to relevant change in its claimed source load. It is mandatory because an invariant function label that survives materially opposite source structures lacks discriminative dependence. The test is bounded by the declared source object, target context, expected praxis difference, source support, and Claim Ceiling.

The governing question is:

> Would a relevant change in the source structure alter the target-function claim?

The question does not ask whether the source is the sole cause of the target scene. It asks whether the projection is sensitive to the very source features it claims are constitutive.

#### Occurrence-level variation rule

Where an operator occurrence or occurrence relation is declared load-bearing, the sensitivity test varies that concrete occurrence or relation rather than merely changing the target label or narrative description. A test may replace supported non-repair with timely repair, equalize a supported cost or exposure gradient, interrupt a retained commitment relation, or alter a claim-relevant order or persistence relation. The abstract operator definition and the canonical Δ–Ψ grammar remain fixed.

```text
occurrence-level variation
≠ abstract operator revision
```

The existing Removal, Reversal, Order, Threshold, Substitution, and Context tests remain sufficient; this clause specifies their PMS-grounded object where applicable.

#### C30-COUNTERFACT-01 — bounded sensitivity is mandatory and does not prove causality

A valid sensitivity test specifies:

- the source feature or relation being varied;
- why the variation is materially relevant to the candidate function;
- what remains fixed in the target comparison;
- what change in the function claim would count as sensitivity;
- which source and target uncertainties limit the comparison;
- whether the counterfactual is reconstructible, boundedly hypothetical, underdetermined, or not testable.

Relevant variations may include:

**Removal.** Omit one proposed load-bearing feature while retaining the rest of the bounded reconstruction.

**Reversal.** Replace a source relation with a materially opposite relation, such as repeated repair instead of repeated non-repair.

**Order change.** Alter the sequence where temporal order is claimed to carry load.

**Threshold change.** Reduce duration, recurrence, asymmetry, or binding below the level at which the function is expected to persist.

**Source substitution.** Compare a rival source object that resembles the target label but lacks the alleged constitutive relation.

**Same-source context contrast.** Retain the source object while changing target context to test whether the function is genuinely bounded rather than intrinsic.

For the pressure object, a bounded test matrix may be declared without selecting a result:

```yaml
counterfactual_sensitivity:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  variations:
    - change: replace repeated non-repair with documented timely repair across the relevant interval
      expected_claim_effect: weaken or remove the expectation-based frame-function component
    - change: equalize accumulated exit costs while retaining other source history
      expected_claim_effect: narrow the asymmetry and action-corridor component of the function claim
    - change: remove persistent binding continuity after the final source transition
      expected_claim_effect: weaken the claim that historical commitments structure the later scene
    - change: retain trajectory X but move to later_configuration_Z where current structures carry the full target-side difference
      expected_claim_effect: no distinct projection may be warranted in Z
  controls:
    - target claim and affected praxis dimensions remain explicit
    - present target structures are not assumed constant where sources show change
    - source uncertainty and inherited Loss remain visible
  result_status: not_selected_in_wp2
```
