The expected claim effects are test commitments. They are not observations and do not establish a causal mechanism.

```text
counterfactual responsiveness
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ causal necessity
≠ empirical intervention result
```

A projection may be sensitive while multiple present and historical structures jointly carry the target difference. Conversely, a causal relation alleged elsewhere does not automatically satisfy `PROJECT_AS` if the contextual function remains unspecified or unbounded.

#### Counterfactual discipline under incomplete sources

Counterfactual testing must remain source-bounded. It may use documented alternatives, observed variation, rival trajectories, sequence contrasts, or carefully delimited hypothetical substitutions. It may not invent a fully known alternative history or treat missing information as evidence that a preferred function would persist.

Where material variation cannot be reconstructed:

```text
insufficient test basis
→ underdetermined or not testable
→ possible reduction, Stop, or Non-Capture pressure
```

The analyst must distinguish source unavailability from source insensitivity. “No contrary example is documented” does not mean the function is strongly sensitive.

#### Target-context contrast

The same-source contrast between `later_configuration_Y` and `later_configuration_Z` tests Contextual Boundedness as well as source dependence. In `Y`, historical load is proposed to alter credible interpretations, action corridors, costs, and bindings. In `Z`, current target structures may already explain the relevant differences, leaving the trajectory as background history without a distinct function.

```text
same source object
+ function candidate in Y
+ no-projection candidate in Z
→ contextual discrimination pressure
```

If the same target label remains unchanged across both contexts without a demonstrated target-side difference, the claim is functionally elastic. That pressure may later support Claim Reduction, Failure, analogy-only, no-projection, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture depending on the complete Record. WP2 does not select among those routes.

#### C30-SENSITIVITYRESULT-01 — sensitivity descriptions remain open, non-scored, and non-canonical

The following local descriptions may be used when the bounded test has been assessed:

```text
strongly sensitive
partially sensitive
weakly sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
not testable
```

They are deliberately open descriptors. They are not:

- canonical Output Classes;
- confidence scores;
- causal-strength values;
- universal thresholds;
- automatic decision-tree routes;
- person, institution, or practice ratings;
- substitutes for the complete Admissibility Band.

A possible recording form is:

```yaml
counterfactual_sensitivity:
  description:
  tested_variations:
  observed_or_warranted_claim_changes:
  unresolved_variations:
  source_limits:
  causal_inference: prohibited
  canonical_output_mapping: pending_complete_record
```

“Strongly sensitive” does not by itself make a projection admissible if target context, source support, Type Integrity, Functional Continuity, validity scope, Loss, alternatives, or Claim Ceiling fail. “Insensitive” creates severe traceability pressure but still requires the complete bounded claim to distinguish failed projection from label substitution, no-projection, claim reduction, or another non-equivalent route. “Not testable” does not automatically mean Non-Capture; the reason for non-testability matters.

No sensitivity descriptor is selected for `documented_trajectory_X` in WP2. The candidate trace and variation matrix are now explicit, but current projection Loss, complete validity scope, alternatives assessment, local operation result, and canonical mapping remain pending.

#### WP2 completion boundary

WP2 establishes:

- the complete target declaration and target-coordinate burden;
- target object/scene and target function as distinct positions;
- projection justification through analytical need, candidate source carrier, rival pressure, and target-side difference;
- declaration of the expected praxeological difference before result selection;
- Constitutive Source Trace beyond citation, salience, or resemblance;
- separation of load-bearing, modulating, compressed, excluded, and uncertain source features;
- mandatory bounded Counterfactual Sensitivity without causal proof;
- an open, non-scored, non-canonical sensitivity-description set.

WP2 does not yet establish:

- complete validity scope and re-entry conditions;
- current projection visibility and exact five-part current Loss;
- full rival-function, no-projection, and other-operation alternatives assessment;
- local operation result;
- canonical Output Class;
- one complete operation-specific Shared Record view;
- `PROJECT_AS` success, Chapter-30 lock, or RETYPE lock.

The local WP2 status is:

```text
source-entry packet complete
+ target declaration complete
+ justification and expected difference declared
+ candidate Constitutive Source Trace explicit
+ bounded sensitivity architecture explicit
+ no sensitivity or operation result selected
→ chapter_30_wp2_complete_chapter_open
```

The `documented_trajectory_X` pressure object remains an unexecuted `PROJECT_AS` candidate. Its target function has not been established. Its candidate trace and counterfactual matrix are now sufficiently explicit for WP3 to test scope, visibility, Loss, alternatives, result axes, output mapping, and the complete Record view.

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 30 WP2 complete
→ Chapter 30 WP3
→ §§30.9–30.13
→ Validity Scope
→ Projection Visibility and Loss
→ Alternative Projections and No-Projection
→ Projection Results
→ PROJECT_AS Record
```

<a id="30-9-validity-scope"></a>

### 30.9 Validity Scope

A `PROJECT_AS` claim is valid only within the target relation it has actually tested. Validity Scope records where, when, for whom, at which relative level and granularity, across which praxis dimensions, and under which Claim Ceiling the target-function claim may be used. It also records where the claim ends, what would require re-entry, and whether any transfer beyond the tested target context is warranted.

Validity Scope is not an after-the-fact disclaimer. It is part of the operation itself. Without it, a local function can silently become a trans-contextual property, a historical essence, a generalized institutional diagnosis, or an inherited authority claim.

```text
bounded target-function claim
+ explicit validity scope
→ locally testable projection

bounded target-function claim
+ implicit unlimited transfer
→ scope failure
```

#### C30-VALIDITY-01 — context, time, level, roles, praxis dimensions, transfer, and Claim Ceiling are bounded together

A complete validity declaration must specify at least:

- the target context and target object or scene;
- the target Frame and its relevant inside/outside boundary;
- the target granularity and relative target level;
- the temporal interval, review point, or termination condition;
- the roles, relations, and source-target positions covered;
- the praxis dimensions the proposed function is claimed to affect;
- the claim type and Claim Ceiling;
- any permitted transfer and its additional evidence burden;
- explicit exclusions;
- the re-entry trigger for a materially changed context, source object, function, or evidence basis.

A reusable validity form is:

```yaml
validity_scope:
  target_context:
  target_object_or_scene:
  target_frame:
  target_granularity:
  target_level:
  temporal_scope:
    begins:
    ends_or_review_point:
    termination_condition:
  covered_roles_and_relations:
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
  claim_scope:
  claim_ceiling:
  transferability:
    permitted:
    additional_burden:
  explicit_exclusions:
  reentry_triggers:
```

The fields are conjunctive controls. A precise time interval does not compensate for an undefined target Frame. A well-defined role relation does not authorize transfer to another institution, period, or level. A narrow Claim Ceiling does not rescue missing Functional Continuity.

#### Validity scope for the pressure object

For `documented_trajectory_X`, WP3 can delimit the candidate relation without deciding it:

```yaml
validity_scope:
  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  target_object_or_scene: bounded_later_coordination_scene_Y
  target_frame: current_coordination_and_interpretation_frame_Y
  target_granularity: configuration_with_role_and_action_corridor_resolution
  target_level: trajectory_relative_to_later_configuration
  temporal_scope:
    begins: entry_into_later_configuration_Y
    ends_or_review_point: first_material_repair_rebinding_or_role_reorganization
    termination_condition: source_load_no_longer_changes_target_reconstruction
  covered_roles_and_relations:
    - roles carrying inherited expectations from trajectory_X
    - relations in which exit cost, credibility, or binding remains source-sensitive
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
    - expectation
    - action_corridor
    - cost
    - asymmetry
    - binding
    - interpretation
  claim_scope: whether trajectory_X performs a bounded frame_function in Y
  claim_ceiling: reconstructive_contextual_function_claim_only
  transferability:
    permitted: false_without_new_operation
    additional_burden: new_target_declaration_trace_sensitivity_loss_alternatives_and_result
  explicit_exclusions:
    - every later scene outside Y
    - person-level traits or motives
    - universal historical necessity
    - causal sufficiency
    - normative legitimacy or recommendation
  reentry_triggers:
    - material change in target Frame or roles
    - new repair, binding, exit, or institutional structure
    - revised source trajectory or origin typing
    - different target function
    - transfer to later_configuration_Z or another target context
  status: declared_not_adjudicated
```

The same source object in `later_configuration_Z` requires a separate validity declaration. The fact that `X` is historically relevant in both contexts does not permit the scope from `Y` to be copied into `Z`.

#### Temporal expiry and review

A target function may weaken, disappear, or change as the target scene develops. Validity Scope must therefore include a review or termination condition wherever the source-target relation can be interrupted by new repair, new binding, institutional redesign, role replacement, resource redistribution, or another material target-side change.

```text
projection valid at t1
≠ projection automatically valid at t2
```

Expiry is not retroactive invalidation. It means that the earlier bounded claim and the later claim are distinct records. A later re-entry may confirm continuity, narrow the function, select a rival function, or support no projection. It may not silently overwrite the earlier result.

#### Transfer is a new testable claim

Transfer from one target context to another is never inherited from the source object or from an earlier successful projection. It requires a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with a new target declaration, target-side difference, trace relation, sensitivity test, scope, Loss, alternatives, and result.

```text
valid in C_t1
+ similar label in C_t2
≠ valid in C_t2
```

Similarity may motivate inspection. It does not carry validity. Where transfer cannot be discriminated responsibly, the available routes include reduction, Stop, Failure, analogy-only, or Non-Capture rather than automatic generalization.

#### Validity Scope and authority

Validity Scope limits analytical use; it does not create permission, legitimacy, diagnosis, recommendation, or sanction. A narrow projection can still be misused if converted into a person judgment or automatic action rule. Governance remains explicit:

```yaml
governance:
  claim_ceiling: reconstructive_only
  person_typing: prohibited
  diagnosis: prohibited
  automatic_recommendation: prohibited
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

<a id="30-10-projection-visibility-and-loss"></a>

### 30.10 Projection Visibility and Loss

`PROJECT_AS` changes what the target account places in analytical focus. It may foreground one source-supported function while leaving other source structures in the background. That visibility change must be recorded, but it is not itself the canonical Loss record.

The canonical Loss contract remains exactly:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

No sixth or seventh canonical field is introduced. The Structure wording `foregrounded` and `backgrounded` is retained as a separate visibility declaration.

#### Operator-relation visibility and canonical Loss

If a claim-relevant operator occurrence or relation is foregrounded, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable under projection, that condition is recorded through the existing visibility declaration and the five canonical Loss fields. Projection may compress occurrence-level heterogeneity, but it may not imply that the target function preserves the complete operator semantics or source dependency structure.

```text
operator-occurrence relation compressed in projection
≠ operator type simplified or changed
```

No sixth Loss field and no operator-specific Loss taxonomy are created.

#### C30-LOSS-01 — every projection retains exactly the five canonical Loss fields

The current `PROJECT_AS` occurrence must record a new operation-specific Loss layer while preserving prior Loss from the source record.

**Preserved** identifies source reference, origin type, constitutive relations, temporal order, load-bearing features, prior disposition, or other structures retained at the resolution necessary for the target-function claim.

**Compressed** identifies source heterogeneity or temporal detail retained only in reduced form. Compression may be justified, but it must remain visible because the target function cannot be treated as a lossless copy of the source object.

**Excluded** identifies structures deliberately outside the current projection claim. Exclusion may be warranted by scope, irrelevance, source limitation, or claim reduction. It must not be redescribed as nonexistence.

**Uncertain** records unresolved source relations, target effects, attribution, timing, or competing interpretations. Uncertainty cannot be converted into preserved load by rhetorical confidence.

**Irrecoverable** records information or distinction that the current operation cannot restore, including inherited irrecoverable Loss from prior transformations and any additional loss created by the projection.

The current operation must distinguish:

```text
inherited source-side Loss
≠ new PROJECT_AS Loss
≠ target visibility choice
```

A projection cannot recover detail lost during earlier `COMPOSE` merely because the target function makes that detail analytically desirable. Nor can it remove a prior uncertainty by placing one interpretation in the foreground.

#### C30-VISIBILITY-01 — foregrounding and backgrounding remain separate from canonical Loss

Projection visibility is recorded independently:

```yaml
projection_visibility:
  foregrounded:
  backgrounded:
```

**Foregrounded** means that a source-supported relation receives primary analytical attention in the target-function account. Foregrounding does not prove that the relation is constitutive, preserved without loss, normatively superior, or causally dominant.

**Backgrounded** means that a source structure remains part of the retained source object but is not central to the present target-function claim. Backgrounding does not mean compression, exclusion, absence, irrelevance in all contexts, or irrecoverable loss.

```text
foregrounded
≠ load-bearing by definition
≠ preserved without loss
≠ causally primary

backgrounded
≠ excluded
≠ compressed
≠ absent
≠ invalid
```

A source feature may be foregrounded and still uncertain. A feature may be backgrounded while fully preserved in the source record. A compressed feature may be foregrounded as a summary, provided the compression is disclosed. These categories answer different questions and must not be collapsed.

#### Candidate visibility and Loss for the pressure object

The following view completes the declaration burden without adjudicating the projection:

```yaml
projection_visibility:
  foregrounded:
    - sedimented expectation effects relevant to credibility and repair
    - accumulated exit-cost asymmetry relevant to practical alternatives
    - persistent binding relevant to later coordination
  backgrounded:
    - source episodes not required for the proposed frame_function
    - local reversals retained in the trajectory record
    - target-side structures assessed independently of historical load

loss:
  preserved:
    - identity of documented_trajectory_X
    - origin type Trajectory
    - source Frame, temporal scope, and prior disposition
    - candidate load-bearing relations and their uncertainty
    - same-source contrast between contexts Y and Z
  compressed:
    - intermediate transition heterogeneity summarized at trajectory resolution
    - role-specific variation not separately projected
    - short-lived source reversals represented without full local reconstruction
  excluded:
    - unsupported biographical or institutional material
    - source episodes outside the declared trajectory Frame
    - target claims beyond expectation, cost, action corridor, asymmetry, binding, and interpretation
  uncertain:
    - relative load of present safeguards versus historical trajectory
    - whether later recontextualization interrupts Functional Continuity
    - whether effects differ materially across covered roles
  irrecoverable:
    - inherited irrecoverable source loss from prior composition
    - unavailable intermediate detail not restored by the projection
```

This declaration remains a candidate inventory. It does not establish that the foregrounded relations are in fact constitutive or that the Loss profile is substantively adequate. Those judgments remain source- and context-grounded.

#### Loss can defeat or narrow a projection

A projection may be too lossy even when its target label is useful. Severe compression can remove the very heterogeneity needed to distinguish one target function from another. Exclusion can erase competing source features. Uncertainty can exceed the Claim Ceiling. Irrecoverable Loss can prevent Counterfactual Sensitivity from being tested.

No amount of target-side convenience compensates for those failures. Depending on the complete record, the proper route may be a narrower projection, a provisional claim, no projection, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, failed transformation, or Non-Capture.

#### Visibility is not authority allocation

Foregrounding one relation does not authorize privileging one person, institution, narrative, or intervention. Backgrounding does not diminish dignity or evidentiary standing. Projection visibility is an analytical arrangement inside a bounded claim, not a hierarchy of persons or interests.

<a id="30-11-alternative-projections-and-no-projection"></a>

### 30.11 Alternative Projections and No-Projection

A target-function claim is not adequately tested if its only alternative is the same claim stated less confidently. `PROJECT_AS` requires rival functions, the no-projection option, and relevant non-operation or other-operation accounts wherever the operation boundary is pressured.

Alternatives perform three roles:

1. they test whether the selected target function is discriminating rather than merely available;
2. they test whether a simpler account explains the target-side difference without projection;
3. they prevent a failed `PROJECT_AS` claim from being rescued by silently changing operation, context, level, or function.

#### C30-ALTERNATIVES-01 — rival function, no-projection, and relevant non-operation or other-operation accounts are mandatory

At minimum, an alternatives declaration must include:

- one rival target function grounded in the same source object;
- the no-projection option;
- recontextualization-only where changed legibility may explain the result;
- a `COMPOSE` alternative where a new object is actually being formed;
- a `DECOMPOSE` alternative where the analytical need is finer reconstruction of the same object;
- a different target granularity or target Frame where that distinction matters;
- analogy-only where structural resemblance remains but functional continuity is not warranted;
- Non-Capture where available materials do not support responsible selection.

A reusable form is:

```yaml
alternatives:
  rival_target_functions:
  no_projection:
  recontextualization_only:
  compose_alternative:
  decompose_alternative:
  different_target_coordinates:
  analogy_only:
  non_capture:
  comparative_basis:
  unresolved:
```

The declaration does not require every field to be positively applicable. It requires each relevant route to be considered and either developed or explicitly ruled out with reasons.

#### Rival target functions

The same source object may support more than one plausible function in the same target context. For a trajectory, candidate functions might include expectation frame, constraint frame, warning frame, legitimacy frame, background condition, or no distinct function. The existence of multiple candidates does not mean that all are equally warranted.

Rival comparison asks:

- Which source features carry each function?
- Which target-side difference does each function explain?
- Do the functions compete, overlap, or operate at different granularities?
- Which candidate is more sensitive to relevant source change?
- Which candidate requires less unsupported transfer or compression?
- Can more than one bounded function be retained without collapsing them into one label?

Where rival projections remain equally viable, the result may be provisional, partial, reduced, stopped, or non-captured. The analyst may not select the most rhetorically powerful label by default.

#### No-projection is a positive analytical option

No-projection means that the source object remains valid and possibly relevant, but no distinct target function is warranted in the declared context.

```text
source object remains valid
+ historical relevance remains possible
+ current target structures carry the full warranted difference
→ no distinct PROJECT_AS claim required
```

No-projection is not necessarily failed transformation. It can be the correct bounded conclusion when the target scene is already explained by current structures, when the proposed function adds no praxeological discrimination, or when projection would duplicate recontextualization without an additional functional claim.

For `later_configuration_Z`, the no-projection candidate is therefore not a weak fallback. It is the required contrast against functional elasticity.

#### Other-operation alternatives

A proposed projection may actually be:

- `COMPOSE`, if the analyst forms a new composite object from source structures;
- `DECOMPOSE`, if the analyst opens internal constitution under finer granularity;
- recontextualization, if only legibility changes in another Frame;
- analogy, if only structural resemblance is warranted.

These are not interchangeable result labels. If another operation is required, it receives a separate occurrence, record, Loss profile, alternatives set, and result. A failed projection cannot be converted into a successful composition by editing the operation name inside the same record.

#### Alternative matrix for the pressure object

```yaml
alternatives:
  rival_target_functions:
    - candidate: bounded_expectation_frame
      distinguishing_question: does trajectory_X primarily structure credibility and anticipated repair?
    - candidate: bounded_constraint_frame
      distinguishing_question: does trajectory_X primarily narrow practical action corridors through accumulated costs and bindings?
    - candidate: background_condition
      distinguishing_question: is the trajectory relevant but non-distinct from current target structures?
  no_projection:
    context_Y: remains_available_if_current_structures_carry_full_difference
    context_Z: primary_candidate_if_trajectory_adds_no_material_target_difference
  recontextualization_only:
    candidate: later_scene_becomes_legible_through_history_without_distinct_function
  compose_alternative:
    candidate: form_new_composite_from_trajectory_and_current_target_structures
    requires_separate_operation: true
  decompose_alternative:
    candidate: reopen_trajectory_X_to_test_role_specific_load
    requires_separate_operation: true
  different_target_coordinates:
    candidate: narrower_role_specific_target_granularity
  analogy_only:
    candidate: frame_like_resemblance_without_supported_functional_continuity
  non_capture:
    candidate: source_and_target_materials_do_not_support_responsible_selection
  comparative_basis:
    - source trace
    - expected praxis difference
    - counterfactual sensitivity
    - scope
    - loss
    - parsimony
  result_status: not_selected_in_wp3
```

The matrix prepares decision pressure. It does not execute the operation or establish that one alternative is superior.

#### Parsimony does not mean label minimization

A no-projection account is more parsimonious only if it preserves the warranted target reconstruction with fewer unsupported relations. Conversely, a projection may be necessary where current target structures cannot explain source-sensitive differences in expectation, cost, binding, access, or action corridors.

Parsimony is therefore claim-relative and source-sensitive. It cannot be automated by counting fields, labels, citations, or operations.

<a id="30-12-projection-results"></a>

### 30.12 Projection Results

Chapter 30 distinguishes the local operation finding from the source-object status, prior claim disposition, and canonical Output Class. These axes answer different questions and may change independently.

- **Source-object and origin-type status** asks whether the source remains identifiable and how its source typing stands.
- **Local `PROJECT_AS` result** describes what happened to the bounded projection claim.
- **Prior disposition** preserves the status inherited from earlier source-side work.
- **Canonical Output Class** maps the complete executed claim into the system-wide output vocabulary.

#### C30-RESULTAXES-01 — source status, local result, prior disposition, and canonical class remain separate

A valid result packet therefore has at least four positions:

```yaml
result_axes:
  source_object_status:
  origin_type_status:
  prior_disposition:
  local_project_as_result:
  canonical_output_class:
```

Examples of non-equivalence include:

```text
valid source object + failed projection
≠ invalid source object

revised origin type + no-projection
≠ projection repaired through retyping

provisional local projection
≠ canonical class automatically admissible_but_provisional

no-projection
≠ non_capture
≠ failed_transformation by definition
```

A source object may remain valid while the projection fails. A source typing may be revised by source-side evidence while the target claim remains unresolved. A prior `mandatory_stop` or `non_capture` cannot be erased by a new target function. Each axis remains inspectable.

#### Open local operation descriptions

Local result language may include:

```text
supported bounded projection
supported but narrow projection
provisional projection
competing projection candidates
partially supported projection
no distinct projection warranted
recontextualization-only
analogy pressure
label substitution detected
invalid type jump detected
insufficient constitutive source trace
counterfactually insensitive projection
underdetermined projection
operation entry failure
mandatory stop condition reached
non-capture condition reached
```

This vocabulary is open and operation-specific. It may be refined by Chapters 31–40 for family-specific use. It is not a second Output Class system.

`label substitution detected` means that a target label has replaced rather than represented a source-dependent function. `invalid type jump detected` means that the target function has been treated as a new origin type, operator type, or primitive. These are failure findings or route pressures, not canonical classes.

#### C30-OUTPUTMAP-01 — only the ten canonical Output Classes may be selected

The canonical mapping remains restricted to:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

No Structure phrase such as `admissible projection`, `narrow projection`, `competing projections`, `label substitution`, or `invalid type jump` becomes an eleventh class.

Mapping is contextual rather than mechanical. Illustrative routing pressure includes:

| Local operation finding | Possible canonical mapping pressure | Required caution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| supported bounded projection with complete trace, scope, Loss, and alternatives | `admissible` or `admissible_with_bounded_claim` | scope and Claim Ceiling determine whether broader wording must be reduced |
| supported relation with material unresolved evidence | `admissible_but_provisional` | provisional status must name the unresolved burden |
| some source-dependent function remains but original function is too broad | `partially_admissible` or `claim_reduction_required` | original claim remains recorded |
| no additional target difference at the tested resolution | `resolution_neutral` may be appropriate | no-projection is not automatically failure |
| only structural resemblance remains | `analogy_only` | analogy must not retain projection language |
| label substitution or invalid type jump defeats the claim | `failed_transformation`, `claim_reduction_required`, or `mandatory_stop` depending the complete route | the finding itself is not the class |
| operation cannot continue without violating type, source, scope, or governance limits | `mandatory_stop` | Stop is a positive result, not proof of the source claim |
| no responsible retained STRATA claim remains | `non_capture` | Non-Capture may not shield a weaker unsupported projection |

Exactly one canonical class is selected only for an executed and delimited claim after the complete admissibility and Decision-Tree route has been assessed. Formal completeness cannot select it automatically.

#### Result discipline for the pressure object

WP3 does not select a local operation result or canonical Output Class for `documented_trajectory_X`. The candidate target function, trace, sensitivity matrix, scope, visibility, Loss, and alternatives are now structurally representable. Their substantive adequacy is not adjudicated here.

```yaml
result_axes:
  source_object_status: retained_from_locked_source_record
  origin_type_status: Trajectory_preserved_not_immunized
  prior_disposition: retained
  local_project_as_result: not_selected_in_chapter_30_method_specification
  canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

This non-selection is deliberate. Chapter 30 owns the reusable operation procedure, not the family-specific truth of the frame-function claim that Chapter 31 will later test.

<a id="30-13-project-as-record"></a>

### 30.13 PROJECT_AS Record

The operation-specific `PROJECT_AS` view is embedded in the Shared Transformation Record. It is not a replacement schema, a second record type, or a permission to bypass the shared source, operation, target, admissibility, Loss, alternatives, and governance groups.

#### PMS occurrence-route completion question

Where PMS occurrence structure is material, the complete Record must permit the following question to be answered from existing fields:

> Do `source_reference`, `source_basis`, `constitutive_source_trace`, `counterfactual_sensitivity`, and `loss` together preserve an inspectable route from the warranted operator occurrence relation to the bounded target-function claim?

A negative or unavailable answer does not create a new local result family. It places pressure on the existing Source Trace, Source Ceiling, Claim Reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture routes.

#### C30-RECORD-01 — one complete operation-specific view is mapped into the Shared Record without schema duplication

The following thirty-three-position view shows the complete procedure using the Chapter-29/30 pressure object. Values marked `candidate`, `pending`, or `not_selected` preserve the fact that no family-specific projection is executed in Chapter 30.

```yaml
project_as:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_X
  source_frame: declared_trajectory_frame_X
  source_granularity: trajectory_with_transition_branch_non_event_and_load_resolution
  source_level: trajectory_relative_to_constitutive_configurations_and_later_scene
  temporal_scope: declared_source_interval_X
  source_basis: locked_source_record_and_declared_corpus
  prior_disposition: retained_from_source_record

  target_context: later_configuration_Y
  target_object_or_scene: bounded_later_coordination_scene_Y
  target_function: bounded_frame_function_candidate
  target_frame: current_coordination_and_interpretation_frame_Y
  target_granularity: configuration_with_role_and_action_corridor_resolution
  target_level: trajectory_relative_to_later_configuration

  analytical_purpose: test_whether_historical_load_changes_later_legibility_expectation_cost_binding_and_action_corridors
  expected_praxeological_difference:
    - altered_credibility_of_repair
    - asymmetric_practical_exit
    - source_sensitive_binding_and_coordination_cost
  justification:
    analytical_need: current_structures_may_not_fully_explain_declared_target_differences
    candidate_source_carrier: sedimented_expectations_exit_costs_and_persistent_bindings
    rival_pressure: current_target_structures_background_condition_or_no_projection
    status: declared_not_adjudicated

  constitutive_source_trace:
    load_bearing_features:
      - repeated_documented_non_repair
      - accumulated_asymmetric_exit_cost
      - persistent_binding_continuity
    modulating_features:
      - later_role_changes
      - current_safeguards
      - reopened_resources_and_alternatives
    compressed_features:
      - intermediate_transition_heterogeneity
      - role_specific_variation
      - short_lived_reversals
    excluded_features:
      - unsupported_material
      - episodes_outside_source_frame
      - current_target_structures_assessed_independently
    uncertain_relations:
      - interruption_by_later_recontextualization
      - relative_load_of_current_safeguards
      - cross_role_generalizability
    source_pointers:
      - locked_PATH_trajectory_record_X

  counterfactual_sensitivity:
    tested_variation_plan:
      - timely_repair_replaces_repeated_non_repair
      - exit_cost_is_equalized
      - binding_continuity_is_removed
      - same_source_moves_from_context_Y_to_context_Z
    description: not_selected
    causal_inference: prohibited

  origin_type_preserved: true_at_declaration_level_not_immunized
  reference_preserved: true_at_declaration_level
  functional_continuity: candidate_not_adjudicated
  contextual_boundedness: declared_not_adjudicated

  validity_scope:
    context: later_configuration_Y_only
    time: until_material_repair_rebinding_role_or_frame_change
    roles: declared_source_sensitive_roles_only
    praxis_dimensions:
      - expectation
      - action_corridor
      - cost
      - asymmetry
      - binding
      - interpretation
    transfer: prohibited_without_new_operation
    claim_ceiling: reconstructive_contextual_function_claim_only
    reentry: required_on_material_source_target_function_or_evidence_change

  projection_visibility:
    foregrounded:
      - expectation_effects
      - exit_cost_asymmetry
      - persistent_binding
    backgrounded:
      - non_constitutive_source_detail
      - local_reversals
      - independent_current_target_structures

  loss:
    preserved:
      - source_identity_origin_type_and_prior_disposition
      - candidate_source_load_and_uncertainty
      - context_Y_context_Z_contrast
    compressed:
      - intermediate_and_role_specific_heterogeneity
    excluded:
      - unsupported_and_out_of_frame_material
      - claims_beyond_declared_praxis_dimensions
    uncertain:
      - relative_historical_and_current_load
      - continuity_across_later_recontextualization
    irrecoverable:
      - inherited_irrecoverable_source_loss
      - unavailable_intermediate_detail

  alternatives:
    rival_functions:
      - bounded_expectation_frame
      - bounded_constraint_frame
      - background_condition
    no_projection: retained
    recontextualization_only: retained
    compose_alternative: separate_occurrence_if_new_object_is_formed
    decompose_alternative: separate_occurrence_if_internal_load_is_reopened
    analogy_only: retained
    non_capture: retained

  stop_condition: stop_if_source_trace_type_integrity_scope_or_responsible_discrimination_cannot_be_preserved
  non_capture_status: available_not_selected
  local_operation_result: not_selected
  canonical_output_class: not_selected
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

This view maps into the Shared Transformation Record as follows:

| Operation-specific positions | Shared Record carrier |
| --- | --- |
| source object, type, reference, Frame, granularity, level, temporal scope, basis, prior disposition | `source` |
| kind, purpose, expected difference, justification, trace, sensitivity, preservation checks | `operation` and `admissibility` |
| target context, object/scene, function, Frame, granularity, level, validity scope | `target` and `admissibility` |
| projection visibility | controlled operation-specific extension under `operation` or `admissibility` |
| exact five-part current Loss | `loss` |
| rival functions, no-projection, non-operation, other-operation, analogy, Non-Capture | `alternatives` |
| Stop, Non-Capture status, Claim Ceiling, local result, canonical class, authority prohibition | `governance` and result mapping |

The prose view does not require thirty-three new schema fields. Existing schema carriers and controlled extensions remain authoritative. Appendix F will later provide the full reusable template; the Transformation Record schema remains the syntax owner.

#### Formal-model boundary

The formal model may verify:

- presence and shape of declarations;
- permitted operation identity;
- exact five-part Loss;
- separation of visibility from Loss;
- availability of alternatives and no-projection;
- use of one of the ten canonical Output Classes where a class is selected;
- preservation flags, Claim Ceiling, Stop, Non-Capture, and authority prohibition;
- structural consistency across the Shared Record.

It may not determine:

- that the source features are truly load-bearing;
- that the proposed target function is semantically adequate;
- that the target difference is empirically real or causal;
- that one rival is more parsimonious in substance;
- that a counterfactual is true;
- that transfer is warranted;
- which local result or canonical class should be selected;
- any person-level, normative, legal, political, diagnostic, recommendation, sanction, or authority conclusion.

Machine-readable completeness remains a consistency condition, not a truth proof.

#### C30-HANDOFF-01 — Chapter 31 receives the reusable procedure, not a family-specific result or RETYPE lock

Chapter 31 receives:

- the exact `PROJECT_AS` identity and signature;
- conjunctive preconditions;
- complete source and target declarations;
- projection justification and expected praxeological difference;
- Constitutive Source Trace and Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- complete Validity Scope;
- projection visibility separated from exact five-part Loss;
- rival functions, no-projection, and operation-boundary alternatives;
- result-axis separation and canonical mapping discipline;
- the complete operation-specific Shared Record view;
- Stop, Non-Capture, Claim Ceiling, and authority prohibition.

Chapter 31 does not inherit:

- an established frame-function;
- a selected sensitivity descriptor;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- permission to generalize from `Y` to `Z`;
- authority to rewrite the source trajectory;
- a Chapter-30 or RETYPE lock before WP4.

Chapter 31 alone owns the family-specific question of when a Trajectory may function as a bounded frame-function. Chapters 32–35 retain their own projection-family burdens. Chapter 30 supplies the common procedure and does not pre-adjudicate those families.

<a id="chapter-30-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 30 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-30 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Gate controls, all twenty-four Chapter-30 Pressure Duties, the complete §§30.1–30.13 operation architecture, the exact `PROJECT_AS` identity and signature, conjunctive preconditions, complete source and target declarations, projection justification, expected praxeological difference, Constitutive Source Trace, bounded Counterfactual Sensitivity, complete Validity Scope, projection visibility separated from canonical five-part Loss, inherited and current Loss separation, alternatives and no-projection, result-axis and canonical-class separation, the thirty-three-position operation-specific Shared-Record view, Stop, Non-Capture, Claim Ceiling, authority prohibition, protected family-chapter ownership, Reference and Formal-Model synchronization, schema validity, relative links, fingerprints, package integrity, ZIP CRC, and byte-identical roundtrip.

```text
complete generic PROJECT_AS procedure
+ source, target, justification, trace, sensitivity, scope, Loss, alternatives, results, and Record remain separated
+ substantive family adjudication remains deferred
+ anchor remains unexecuted
→ Chapter 30 provisionally locked
```

The Chapter-30 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The class applies to the chapter-level operation specification under the still-open RETYPE Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not classify the `documented_trajectory_X` pressure object, establish a frame-function in `later_configuration_Y`, establish no-projection in `later_configuration_Z`, select a sensitivity descriptor, local `PROJECT_AS` result, or canonical Output Class, validate a family-specific projection, or increase claim, application, governance, diagnostic, person-level, political, legal, normative, recommendation, sanction, or authority status.

All twenty-four Chapter-30 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose: eight in WP1, eight in WP2, and eight in WP3. The integrated boundary records their closure without repeating their identifiers or creating additional duties.

No standalone Case, test Record, Smoke Record, test YAML, case YAML, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, schema field, target-function enum, origin-type enum, result enum, canonical Loss field, score, causal classifier, semantic engine, Graph inference, Reader execution, person type, recommendation, sanction, legitimacy finding, or authority mechanism has been produced.

The integrated result preserves five independent positions:

```text
1. source-object and origin-type status
2. prior source or operation disposition
3. proposed target-function and sensitivity status
4. local PROJECT_AS result
5. canonical Output Class for an executed delimited claim
```

Chapter 30 locks the reusable procedure, not a concrete occurrence. A structurally complete Record may remain substantively unresolved; a source citation may fail to provide constitutive trace; sensitivity may remain underdetermined or not testable; no-projection may be the most disciplined result; and an attractive function label may still require Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, analogy-only treatment, or Non-Capture.

```text
Chapter 30 provisionally locked
≠ PROJECT_AS executed
≠ target function established
≠ Counterfactual Sensitivity selected
≠ local operation result selected
≠ canonical Output Class selected for the anchor
≠ family-specific warrant inherited
≠ RETYPE provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, the Structure or Chapter Contract, operation identity, source or target declaration, origin type, target context, target function, expected difference, Constitutive Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Validity Scope, projection visibility, canonical Loss, alternatives, result axes, Shared-Record mapping, Stop, Non-Capture, Claim Ceiling, authority prohibition, a Pressure Duty, Reference or Formal-Model binding, schema or package integrity, or a later Chapter-31–40/LIMITS/integrated-audit dependency. Preference for a stronger function label, denser formalization, automatic semantic selection, score, closed target-function inventory, source-claim rescue, wider transfer, or authority increase is insufficient.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 30 provisional lock
→ Chapter 31 Preparation Gate complete
→ Chapter 31 WP1
→ §§31.1–31.4
→ Basic Claim, Source Object, Target Context, and Frame-Function
```

Chapter 31 receives the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure, the unexecuted same-source `Y/Z` pressure contrast, and all operation-level boundaries. It receives no established frame-function, no selected sensitivity descriptor, no local operation result, no canonical Output Class, no permission to treat Trajectory as `□`, no historical determinism, and no authority. Chapter 31 alone owns the family-specific test of when a reconstructed Trajectory performs a bounded frame-function in a declared later configuration.

---

<a id="chapter-31-trajectory-as-frame-function"></a>

## 31. Trajectory as Frame-Function

Chapter 31 applies the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure to the first principal RETYPE family: the bounded projection of an already reconstructed Trajectory as a frame-function in a later configuration. Chapters 11–12 own the definitions of Trajectory, sedimentation, and Path Dependence. Chapter 2 owns Frame and analytical coordinates. Chapter 29 owns functional projection without Origin-Type Replacement. Chapter 30 owns the complete operation procedure. This chapter therefore does not redefine any of those objects or operations. It asks a narrower family-specific question:

> When does a PATH-established Trajectory carry source-traceable historical load that materially structures what becomes legible, credible, expected, costly, available, or actionable in a declared later configuration?

The family claim is deliberately stronger than the statement that history is relevant and deliberately weaker than the statement that the past determines the present. A prior Trajectory can remain historically important without performing a distinct frame-function in the target scene. Conversely, a bounded frame-function can be analytically warranted without converting the Trajectory into the PMS Frame operator `□`, without treating it as the only frame source, and without assigning it permanent or universal reach.

The chapter uses one stable source object and two target contexts:

```text
source object: documented_trajectory_X
origin type: Trajectory

positive target candidate: later_configuration_Y
proposed function: bounded frame-function

negative contrast: later_configuration_Z
proposed result possibility: no distinct frame-function
```

The same source object must remain fixed across `Y` and `Z`. The contrast tests whether the proposed function depends on the target context and on a material source-to-target relation rather than on historical salience, narrative force, or the analyst's preference for a retrospective explanation.

```text
same historical source
+ different target contexts
→ function may be warranted in Y and absent in Z
```

WP1 establishes the family claim, the eligible source entry, the target-context declaration, and the meaning of a bounded frame-function. It does not yet adjudicate Historical Load, relative load among multiple frame sources, Counterfactual Sensitivity, competing projections, a local `PROJECT_AS` result, or a canonical Output Class. Those burdens remain open for §§31.5–31.11.

<a id="31-1-basic-claim"></a>

### 31.1 Basic Claim

A Trajectory may perform a frame-function when its independently reconstructed and source-traceable historical structure materially conditions a declared later configuration. The relevant target work may concern which differences become salient, which interpretations remain credible, which expectations organize roles, which alternatives appear available, which actions incur elevated cost, or which bindings and residues shape continuation. The function is therefore not a general statement that the past matters. It is a bounded claim that specified features of one source Trajectory perform identifiable framing work within one target context.

#### Bounded PMS Frame work

The target-side correspondence must name a limited aspect of canonical PMS Frame work—for example relevance structuring, boundary conditioning, contextual constraint, or role-space organization—rather than treating “frame” as a free synonym for historical influence. The claim remains contextual and partial:

```text
bounded performance of declared Frame work
≠ complete □ semantics
≠ □ operator occurrence created by projection
```

The linked source Trajectory remains the origin-typed source object. The target function does not inherit the full dependencies, portability, or identity of `□`.

#### C31-BASIC-01 — the family claim is bounded trajectory-to-frame-function projection

The minimal family form is:

```text
Within later target context C_t,
Trajectory X,
while retaining origin type Trajectory,
performs bounded frame-function F_frame
in relation to declared praxis dimensions D_t.
```

Every position remains explicit:

| Position | Required declaration | Family-specific burden |
|---|---|---|
| `X` | PATH-established source Trajectory | independently warranted before target-function adjudication |
| `Trajectory` | visible origin type | preserved throughout the projection |
| `C_t` | declared later configuration | bounded by Frame, granularity, level, roles, and time window |
| `F_frame` | specific frame-function candidate | must name a concrete difference in target reconstruction |
| `D_t` | framed praxis dimensions | legibility, expectation, role reading, action corridor, cost, binding, or alternative visibility |

The claim is relational. `F_frame` is neither stored inside the source object as an intrinsic property nor activated in every later scene. Its validity depends on the relation between `X`, the target configuration, the selected praxis dimensions, and the sources available for both periods.

```text
Trajectory X exists
≠ X frames every later configuration

X is historically salient
≠ X performs a distinct frame-function in C_t
```

A family claim therefore requires more than temporal succession. The target configuration must be later than, or analytically downstream from, the relevant source Trajectory, but chronological order alone does not establish framing. Nor does continuity of actors, institutions, vocabulary, or location. Such continuities can support source reference and target linkage, yet the projected function still requires a material difference in the target reconstruction.

#### Frame-function versus historical background

Historical background identifies conditions that help situate a later scene. A frame-function is narrower and more demanding. It claims that the source Trajectory actively organizes the target reconstruction along named dimensions. The distinction is not one of rhetorical emphasis but of analytical work:

```text
background relevance
→ the history helps locate or contextualize the scene

bounded frame-function
→ removing or materially changing the source-dependent framing relation
   would alter what is credibly legible, expected, costly, available, or actionable
   in the declared target context
```

The second statement remains a candidate until later sections supply Historical Load, multiple-source comparison, and counterfactual pressure. WP1 fixes the required form but does not treat the requirement as already satisfied.

#### C31-CONTRAST-01 — the same source must remain available for a function/no-function contrast

The family architecture preserves two equally admissible possibilities for the same `documented_trajectory_X`:

```yaml
same_source_contrast:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_X

  target_Y:
    candidate: bounded_frame_function
    required_target_difference:
      - credible_interpretations_change
      - expectations_or_role_reading_change
      - action_corridors_or_costs_change

  target_Z:
    candidate: no_distinct_frame_function
    pressure:
      - present_conditions_may_carry_the_target_reconstruction
      - removal_of_historical_load_may_leave_the_reconstruction_unchanged
```

`Y` is not the default positive case and `Z` is not a weak residual case. Both are test contexts. The positive family claim must survive source trace, present-condition comparison, multiple-frame-source pressure, and later counterfactual assessment. The negative contrast remains available even where the source Trajectory is well documented and historically important.

This contrast prevents three common inflations:

- treating a valid PATH object as if it automatically possessed a later function;
- treating historical salience as context-independent framing power;
- treating the availability of a plausible narrative as sufficient discrimination.

The family can therefore yield a bounded frame-function in one context, background relevance in another, or no warranted projection at all without changing the source object's PATH status.

<a id="31-2-source-object"></a>

### 31.2 Source Object

The source of a trajectory-to-frame-function projection must already exist as a warranted PATH object. Chapter 31 cannot construct the Trajectory retrospectively from the desired target result. It may reference the source record, preserve its uncertainty and Loss, and specify which parts of that record are candidate carriers for later framing. It may not revise the source selection merely because another periodization, endpoint, branch selection, or narrative arc would fit the proposed function better.

#### Operator-carrier access through the PATH source Record

Where material to the candidate frame-function, the source entry retains access to the PATH reconstruction of concrete operator occurrence relations that carry the historical load. Depending on the warranted source, these may include supported `Λ` relations within declared expectation Frames, recurrent `Α` stabilization relations, `Ω` cost or exposure gradients, `Ψ` continuity or breach-cost relations, and relevant `Φ`, `Χ`, or `Σ` occurrences that interrupt, redirect, distance, or integrate the load.

This is not a mandatory bundle and does not establish the frame-function. The PATH Record owns the occurrence warrant; Chapter 31 only traces whether already warranted load performs bounded target work.

#### C31-SOURCE-01 — the source is a prior PATH Trajectory selected independently of target fit

The minimum source entry is:

```yaml
source:
  object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_X
  source_selection_basis: independently_warranted_before_target_function_adjudication
  source_frame: declared_PATH_frame
  source_granularity: declared_PATH_granularity
  source_level: declared_relative_source_level
  temporal_scope: declared_source_period
  prior_disposition: retained
  source_uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

Each field protects a distinct claim boundary:

- `object` identifies the analytical source object;
- `source_reference` anchors that object to the prior reconstruction;
- `origin_type` preserves the object as a Trajectory;
- source coordinates preserve the conditions under which the PATH claim was made;
- `prior_disposition` prevents RETYPE from silently upgrading a provisional or reduced source claim;
- uncertainty and inherited Loss prevent later functional usefulness from repairing missing history.

```text
source Trajectory warranted by PATH
→ eligible source object for PROJECT_AS testing

eligible source object
≠ target function warranted
```

The source record may be revisited if new source-side evidence genuinely changes its boundaries or typing. Such revision is a source-side claim and must remain separate from the frame-function adjudication. The target context cannot serve as evidence that the source Trajectory must have had whichever shape makes the later scene appear coherent.

#### Independent selection and anti-retrospective discipline

A common failure occurs when the analyst begins with a later configuration, identifies the desired explanatory frame, and then selects only those earlier events that make that frame appear historically inevitable. This reverses the required order:

```text
invalid route:
target fit
→ retrospective source selection
→ coherent historical story
→ claimed frame-function
```

The controlled route is:

```text
independently warranted PATH Trajectory
→ declared later target context
→ separate frame-function test
```

Independent selection does not require that the source reconstruction be temporally completed before the target period existed. Historical analysis is often retrospective. It requires instead that the source Trajectory be warranted by source-period materials, explicit PATH selection rules, and a bounded reference object rather than by target-function fit alone. Later evidence may corroborate, challenge, or recontextualize the source record, but it must be marked as later evidence and may not substitute automatically for missing source-period support.

#### Separate evidence burdens

Chapter 31 maintains at least three evidence positions:

| Evidence position | Supports | Does not automatically support |
|---|---|---|
| source-period evidence | existence, structure, sequence, and load candidates of `X` | frame-function in `Y` or `Z` |
| target-period evidence | organization and present conditions of the later configuration | correctness of the prior PATH reconstruction |
| later retrospective evidence | later reports, interpretations, records, or comparisons | source-period facts or target-period function without source criticism |

The evidence warranting the Trajectory and the evidence warranting its projected function can overlap, but they are not interchangeable. A document may both record earlier commitments and show that later actors treated those commitments as relevant. Even then, the two evidentiary uses must be stated separately.

```text
source evidence establishes X
≠ same evidence automatically establishes F_frame

later actors narrate X as decisive
≠ X actually carried the claimed target function
```

WP1 records the separation. It does not yet determine which source features are load-bearing, which are modulating, or how strongly the function depends on them.

#### Source identity under partial uncertainty

A source Trajectory need not be complete or uncontested to enter the family test. It must be sufficiently bounded for the proposed target claim. Source uncertainty can concern:

- the exact beginning or endpoint;
- one or more intermediate transitions;
- the status of a branch or blocked alternative;
- the weight of a Non-Event;
- the relative contribution of an Asymmetry or Binding;
- the irrecoverability of compressed local variation.

Such uncertainty narrows the available frame-function claim. It does not authorize either automatic Failure or automatic passage. The key requirement at WP1 is that the source object, its uncertainty, and the parts unavailable for recovery remain visible rather than being hidden by a persuasive later function label.

<a id="31-3-target-context"></a>

### 31.3 Target Context

A trajectory-to-frame-function claim requires an explicit later configuration rather than an abstract “present,” “institution,” “culture,” or “system.” The target context determines what the projected function could mean, which praxis dimensions are relevant, which present conditions must be compared, and where the claim ceases to apply.

#### C31-TARGET-01 — later configuration, target coordinates, and temporal evidence boundary are explicit

The minimum target declaration is:

```yaml
target:
  object_or_scene: later_configuration_Y
  target_context: bounded_target_context_Y
  target_frame: declared_target_frame
  target_granularity: declared_target_granularity
  target_level: declared_relative_target_level
  roles: declared_target_roles
  time_window: declared_target_period
  praxis_dimensions:
    - legibility
    - expectation
    - role_reading
    - action_corridors
    - costs
    - bindings
    - alternative_visibility
  present_conditions: explicitly_recorded
  claim_scope: bounded_to_declared_dimensions
  claim_ceiling: no_determinism_no_operator_identity_no_authority
  evidence_boundary:
    source_period_evidence: separately_marked
    target_period_evidence: separately_marked
    later_retrospective_evidence: separately_marked_and_non_substitutive
```

The target Frame identifies the contextual boundary within which the later configuration is being reconstructed. It is not supplied by the source Trajectory merely because the proposed target function uses frame-language. The target may already contain institutional Frames, role rules, current constraints, new events, changed resources, or later Recontextualizations. Those structures remain analytically present from entry onward.

The target granularity and relative target level must be appropriate to the proposed function. A Trajectory may plausibly frame one role's reading of a local decision without framing an entire institution; it may frame a short negotiation window without framing all later practice; or it may structure one target-level alternative set while remaining only background for another. The target declaration therefore prevents family vocabulary from expanding beyond the scale and dimensions actually inspected.

#### C31-PRESENTCOND-01 — present target conditions enter before historical function is assessed

Present conditions cannot be added only after a historical account encounters counterevidence. They belong to the initial target declaration. Relevant present conditions may include:

- current institutional rules and enforcement;
- present role allocations and capacities;
- current resource and information distributions;
- new events after the source Trajectory;
- altered asymmetries, costs, or exit possibilities;
- current Frames and public narratives;
- intervening `Φ` Recontextualizations;
- available forms of `Χ` Distance, `Σ` Integration, or `Ψ` Self-Binding;
- environmental or technical constraints not carried by the source history.

Their inclusion serves two purposes. First, it prevents historical monopoly: the source Trajectory may be one contributor among several. Second, it makes the `Y/Z` contrast possible. In `Z`, present conditions may carry the full warranted reconstruction even though the same source history remains available.

```text
present conditions considered
≠ historical function defeated

present conditions omitted
→ historical-function claim underdetermined or inflated
```

Chapter 31 does not require a quantitative partition of historical and present load. It requires their distinct visibility and a later qualitative assessment anchored in sources. A statement that “both matter” is insufficient if it leaves the claimed difference and relevant role or time window unspecified.

#### Temporal evidence boundary

The target context also fixes which evidence can speak to which period. Three boundaries are especially important:

1. **Source-period evidence** supports the prior Trajectory and its candidate historical-load carriers.
2. **Target-period evidence** supports how the later configuration was organized, experienced in practice, documented, or constrained at the relevant time.
3. **Later retrospective evidence** may reveal later interpretation, institutional memory, or subsequent consequences, but must not silently replace evidence about either earlier period.

Retrospective evidence can be indispensable, especially where target-period records are incomplete. Its use must therefore be calibrated rather than prohibited. The analyst must state whether it corroborates a period claim, supplies a later interpretation, or reconstructs an otherwise unavailable relation. The evidentiary limitation must then enter uncertainty and Claim Ceiling.

```text
later retrospective coherence
≠ contemporaneous target framing automatically
```

#### Target `Y` and contrast `Z`

The two target contexts share the same source object but differ in their current configuration and in the proposed source-to-target work:

| Position | `later_configuration_Y` | `later_configuration_Z` |
|---|---|---|
| source object | `documented_trajectory_X` | `documented_trajectory_X` |
| source type | Trajectory | Trajectory |
| target context | declared `Y` | declared `Z` |
| present conditions | recorded | recorded |
| candidate relation | bounded frame-function | no distinct frame-function remains possible |
| required discrimination | source-dependent change in named praxis dimensions | target reconstruction unchanged when historical framing is removed or altered |

The contrast is not a claim that `Y` and `Z` are empirically identical except for one variable. It is a controlled family comparison: the same source must not receive the same function merely because it is available to the analyst.

<a id="31-4-frame-function"></a>

### 31.4 Frame-Function

A frame-function is the bounded target-context work through which a source Trajectory conditions how a later configuration is delimited, interpreted, expected, or practically navigated. It is not the source object's origin type and not an occurrence of the PMS Frame operator generated by relabeling. It is a contextual function claim under `PROJECT_AS`.

The function must specify what is framed and for whom or what role-position, during which time window, at which relative level, and across which praxis dimensions. “The Trajectory frames the present” is too elastic. A valid candidate has a form such as:

```text
Within later_configuration_Y,
for declared role-set R during time window W,
Trajectory X conditions the credible interpretation of difference D,
the expectation of continuation E,
and the practical cost or availability of action corridor A.
```

This formulation still requires later evidence. Its value at WP1 is to make the candidate discriminating enough to fail.

#### C31-FRAMEFUNC-01 — the proposed function names a concrete framed praxis difference

A frame-function candidate must identify at least one target reconstruction difference and the dimension on which it appears. Possible dimensions include:

| Framed praxis dimension | Candidate target work |
|---|---|
| legibility | makes one difference salient while another remains backgrounded |
| credible interpretation | narrows or orders interpretations treated as practically plausible |
| expectation | conditions what actors or roles anticipate as likely, required, absent, or costly |
| role reading | changes how a role, obligation, exposure, or responsibility gradient is read |
| action corridor | opens, narrows, delays, or raises the threshold of a continuation |
| cost | redistributes or sediments expected costs of action, exit, repair, or non-action |
| binding | conditions which prior commitments remain practically active |
| alternative visibility | makes some continuations visible, remote, blocked, or no longer credible |

The list is open and non-taxonomic. It does not create a closed family inventory or new schema enum. Whatever dimension is selected must be stated with enough precision that the target reconstruction could remain unchanged, change differently, or be better explained by a rival present or historical source.

A target difference may be qualitative and still be testable. STRATA does not require a score. It requires source-bound discrimination:

```text
candidate frame-function supported only if
named target difference
+ declared role/time/level scope
+ later historical-load trace
+ present-condition comparison
+ alternative pressure
can be inspected together
```

WP1 establishes the first two positions and reserves the others for WP2–WP3.

#### C31-NOTBOX-01 — Trajectory remains the origin type and does not become `□`

The canonical type statement is:

```text
origin type: Trajectory
projected target function: bounded frame-function
```

It is not:

```text
Trajectory X = □
Trajectory X becomes a Frame operator occurrence by projection
frame-function = new PMS primitive
```

The PMS operator `□` retains its canonical Base meaning and dependencies. Chapter 31 neither decomposes it nor creates a second Frame type. The projection states only that the source Trajectory performs frame-like analytical work in a delimited target context. Operator-like vocabulary identifies the kind of target work being proposed; it does not confer operator identity.

Origin-Type Preservation also protects the source history from being flattened into its later use. A Trajectory contains temporal order, transitions, sedimentation, alternatives, Non-Events, Asymmetries, Bindings, uncertainty, and Loss. A bounded frame-function foregrounds only those aspects relevant to the target claim. The remaining source structure does not disappear and cannot be inferred to be irrelevant merely because it is not foregrounded in `Y`.

```text
X PROJECT_AS bounded frame-function in Y
≠ X exhaustively described by that function
≠ X performs the same function in Z
≠ Y explained only by X
```

#### Frame-function and present target Frame

The target context already has a Frame under which the reconstruction is conducted. The projected frame-function is not identical with that analytical target Frame. The target Frame specifies the boundary of inquiry; the frame-function specifies one proposed source-dependent contribution within that inquiry.

```text
target Frame
→ where the later configuration is reconstructed

projected frame-function
→ what source Trajectory X is proposed to do within that reconstruction
```

Confusing the two would make the projection circular: the analyst would define the target through the history and then cite the same definition as proof that the history frames the target. Chapter 31 therefore requires independent target declaration and separate source-to-target support.

#### WP1 candidate packet

At the end of WP1, the family packet is complete only at declaration level:

```yaml
chapter_31_wp1_candidate:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_X
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_X
    selection_independent_of_target_fit: required
    prior_disposition: retained
    uncertainty: retained
    inherited_loss: retained

  target_Y:
    object_or_scene: later_configuration_Y
    coordinates: declared
    present_conditions: declared
    proposed_function: bounded_frame_function
    framed_praxis_difference: specified_but_not_yet_adjudicated

  target_Z:
    object_or_scene: later_configuration_Z
    coordinates: declared
    present_conditions: declared
    proposed_function: no_distinct_frame_function_remains_available

  evidence_boundary:
    source_period: separate
    target_period: separate
    later_retrospective: marked_and_non_substitutive

  result_status:
    historical_load: not_yet_adjudicated
    relative_frame_load: not_yet_adjudicated
    counterfactual_frame_test: not_yet_performed
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

The packet is ready for the Historical Load and multiple-frame-source analysis of WP2. It is not an executed projection record and does not select a result for either `Y` or `Z`.

#### C31-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives a declared family claim without historical-load or result adjudication

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 31 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 31 WP2 §§31.5–31.7
→ Historical Load
→ Frame-Function without Historical Determinism
→ Multiple Frame Sources
```

WP2 receives:

- one independently warranted source Trajectory;
- one retained source record, origin type, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss profile;
- explicit target contexts `Y` and `Z`;
- marked source-period, target-period, and retrospective evidence layers;
- present target conditions from operation entry;
- a concrete but still unadjudicated frame-function candidate;
- the same-source function/no-function contrast.

WP2 does not inherit:

- proof that any historical feature carries target load;
- a relative-load conclusion;
- historical determinism or causal monopoly;
- a selected sensitivity descriptor;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- permission to treat Trajectory as `□`;
- a Chapter-31 or RETYPE lock;
- any person, normative, political, legal, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority claim.

<a id="chapter-31-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 31.1–31.4 establish the trajectory-to-frame-function claim form, prior and independently selected PATH source entry, target-context and temporal-evidence declaration, present-condition inclusion, concrete framed praxis difference, Origin-Type Preservation, denial of `□` identity, and the stable same-source `Y/Z` contrast.

```text
family claim declared
+ source and target evidence positions separated
+ concrete function candidate stated
+ present conditions retained
+ no-function contrast retained
→ WP1 complete
```

This result does not establish Historical Load, Functional Continuity for the family occurrence, relative contribution among multiple frame sources, Counterfactual Sensitivity, a successful projection in `Y`, no-projection in `Z`, a local operation result, a canonical Output Class, Chapter-31 lock, RETYPE lock, or increased authority.

<a id="31-5-historical-load"></a>

### 31.5 Historical Load

For the trajectory-to-frame-function family, **Historical Load** is the source-traceable work through which constitutive features of a prior Trajectory remain materially active in a declared later configuration. It is not the mere fact that the source object is old, extended, memorable, documented, institutionally named, or historically important. Nor is it established by the source Trajectory's prior PATH warrant alone.

```text
warranted Trajectory X
+ continued historical relevance
≠ Historical Load for frame-function in Y
```

The source claim and the target-function claim answer different questions:

| Claim position | Governing question | Required support |
|---|---|---|
| source-object warrant | Was `X` validly reconstructed as a Trajectory? | PATH evidence, transitions, sedimentation, alternatives, uncertainty, and inherited Loss |
| target-function warrant | Do features of `X` perform bounded framing work in `Y`? | source-to-target trace, named praxis difference, present-condition comparison, scope, and later counterfactual pressure |

A source Trajectory can remain fully warranted while the frame-function claim fails, remains provisional, becomes background relevance only, or cannot be responsibly captured. Conversely, a rhetorically compelling target fit cannot repair a weak source Trajectory. The two warrants remain separately auditable throughout the family analysis.

#### Conservative PMS carrier anchoring

Historical carriers remain ordinary source structures unless their operator typing is independently warranted. Where it is warranted, the following correspondences may sharpen the trace:

| Existing carrier language | Conservative occurrence-level anchoring |
|---|---|
| sedimented expectations or repeated Non-Repair | supported `Λ` occurrences within declared Frames and retained temporal relations |
| recurrent continuation pressure | supported `Α` occurrence relations whose persistence belongs to the PATH Record |
| accumulated asymmetric costs | supported `Ω` gradients carried through the Trajectory |
| persistent Commitments or Bindings | supported `Ψ` relations with temporal and dependency context retained |
| later reframing, distancing, interruption, or integration | supported `Φ`, `Χ`, or `Σ` occurrences that may weaken, redirect, or terminate the historical function |

No carrier is universally required. Resemblance does not establish typing, and target fit cannot retroactively create a source occurrence.

#### C31-HISTLOAD-01 — Historical Load is source-traceable target work, not duration, salience, or inherited source warrant

A proposed Historical Load must identify:

1. a concrete feature or relation already present in the source Trajectory;
2. the temporal and evidentiary route through which that feature remains available in the target period;
3. the specific target praxis dimension it conditions;
4. the roles, relative level, and time window for which that conditioning is claimed;
5. present conditions and rival sources that may reproduce, weaken, redirect, or supersede the same target pattern;
6. uncertainty, compression, exclusion, and irrecoverable Loss relevant to the relation.

The canonical family burden is therefore:

```text
HistoricalLoad(X, Y)
only if
source feature in X
→ traceable persistence, transmission, or reactivation
→ bounded difference in Y
under declared present and rival-source conditions
```

The arrow is a reconstructive trace, not an automatic causal law. It may be supported through documented continuity, retained role rules, recurring expectations, institutional memory, durable cost distributions, persistent Commitments, repeated Non-Repair, or other source-bound carriers. The analyst must state which route is being claimed and where the evidence changes from direct to indirect or retrospective.

Historical Load may be uneven. A source feature may remain active for one role but not another, during one target interval but not a later interval, or along one praxis dimension while being irrelevant to another. Such unevenness is not noise to be averaged away. It is part of the Validity Scope.

#### Historical-load carriers

Potential carriers include, but are not limited to:

| Candidate carrier | Source-side requirement | Possible target work | Typical defeat pressure |
|---|---|---|---|
| sedimented expectations | repeated supported expectation structure in `X` | conditions what is anticipated, feared, required, or treated as missing in `Y` | target actors no longer rely on or recognize the expectation |
| accumulated asymmetric costs | documented unequal cost or exposure trajectory | changes credible exit, repair, refusal, or participation corridors | present resources or role changes remove the historical gradient |
| persistent Bindings | retained Commitments, obligations, or breach consequences | conditions which continuations remain practically binding | binding was released, superseded, or never carried into the target period |
