| institutionalized roles or rules | source-supported stabilization beyond isolated events | conditions role reading, access, responsibility, or default action | new institutional arrangement supplies the target structure independently |
| repeated Non-Repair or Non-Decision | supported expectation and repeated structured non-occurrence | conditions trust, timing, credibility, or alternative availability | no relevant expectation survived into the target context |
| open residues and foreclosed alternatives | source record shows unresolved residue or altered reachability | narrows plausible interpretations or makes alternatives costly, remote, or unavailable | later event or Recontextualization materially reopens the alternatives |
| recurrent Attractor pressure | source Trajectory contains supported recurrence and friction differentials | conditions which continuation appears locally easier or normal | current attractor has a different and sufficient present source |

The table does not create a closed carrier taxonomy. A named carrier is admissible only where its source position, target work, and possible defeat are inspectable.

#### C31-CARRIERS-01 — load-bearing and modulating historical features remain distinct

A **load-bearing feature** is necessary to the particular frame-function claim as formulated: materially changing or removing it would require the function claim to be revised, narrowed, or withdrawn. A **modulating feature** alters strength, distribution, timing, or visibility without carrying the function claim by itself.

```text
load-bearing
→ constitutive for this bounded function claim

modulating
→ changes expression or scope without independently warranting the function
```

This distinction is claim-relative. A feature can be load-bearing for role-set `R1` during window `W1`, modulating for `R2`, and irrelevant for a later window `W2`. The analyst must not universalize one local role into a property of the entire target scene.

The distinction also protects against historical overloading. If every source detail is declared load-bearing, the function becomes insensitive to source variation and exceeds the Traceability Ceiling. If no source feature can be identified as carrying the target difference, “history” has become a label without traceable load.

#### Historical Load in the `Y/Z` contrast

The same source Trajectory `X` remains available in both target contexts:

- In `Y`, the candidate claim is that one or more source features remain load-bearing for a named framed praxis difference after present conditions and rival sources are considered.
- In `Z`, the candidate pressure is that the same historical material may remain relevant or narratively available while present conditions carry the target reconstruction and no distinct frame-function remains warranted.

This contrast prevents a stable source record from generating a stable target function by default.

```text
same source Trajectory
+ different present conditions or source-to-target relations
→ possibly different functional status
```

WP2 establishes the carrier architecture and evidentiary burden. It does not yet perform the full Counterfactual Frame Test or select the family result for `Y` or `Z`.

<a id="31-6-frame-function-without-historical-determinism"></a>

### 31.6 Frame-Function without Historical Determinism

A supported frame-function conditions later praxis; it does not determine it completely. Historical Load may alter which interpretations are credible, which expectations are active, which costs are anticipated, which roles appear available, or which alternatives remain visible. None of these relations entails inevitability, exhaustive explanation, prediction, or causal monopoly.

#### C31-NODETERM-01 — historical determinism, inevitability, prediction, and causal monopoly are prohibited

Chapter 31 must reject claims of the following form:

```text
because X occurred,
Y had to be interpreted or enacted in exactly this way
```

A frame-function claim becomes historically deterministic when it:

- treats one later outcome as necessary;
- eliminates meaningful alternative interpretation or action;
- explains every target feature through the source Trajectory;
- converts constrained possibility into prediction;
- makes contrary present evidence irrelevant by definition;
- or treats a successful projection as proof of causal exclusivity.

The denial of determinism is substantive, not ceremonial. A claim that says “not determined” while assigning all explanatory work to `X` remains overextended. The reconstruction must preserve actual present conditions, contrary events, Recontextualizations, new Bindings, Distance, Integration, and alternative continuations where the sources support them.

Historical framing can be strong without being total. It may make one continuation substantially more costly, one interpretation more credible, or one role expectation harder to escape while leaving revision and alternative action possible. Constraint is not destiny.

#### C31-CONDITIONING-01 — conditioning is stated across named praxis dimensions

The bounded claim must state **what kind of conditioning** is proposed. Examples include:

| Conditioning dimension | Bounded formulation |
|---|---|
| interpretive | `X` changes which readings of difference `D` are practically credible for role-set `R` |
| expectational | `X` changes what response, repair, absence, or continuation is anticipated during `W` |
| corridor | `X` raises, lowers, delays, or redistributes the practical threshold of action `A` |
| cost | `X` changes expected or realized costs of exit, repair, refusal, disclosure, or participation |
| role | `X` conditions how obligations, exposure, authority, or responsibility are read |
| binding | `X` keeps a prior Commitment, breach, or unresolved obligation active in the target scene |
| alternative visibility | `X` renders an option credible, remote, blocked, or no longer legible |

A single occurrence need not involve all dimensions. Claim breadth must follow the supported trace. A function supported only for expectation may not be inflated into a general claim about all target action.

Conditioning may also be negative or limiting: the Trajectory may prevent a target interpretation from becoming credible, keep an alternative outside the practical field, or raise the evidentiary burden for a new Frame. Such negative work still requires a supported expectation or corridor relation and must not be inferred from mere absence.

#### C31-ROLEWINDOW-01 — the function is bounded by role and time window where required

Historical Load rarely distributes uniformly. The Record must state whether the proposed function applies to:

- all declared target roles or only specified role-positions;
- direct participants, institutional successors, observers, or later entrants;
- the immediate target interval, a bounded continuation period, or a later reactivation window;
- one relative level or several explicitly related levels.

```text
frame-function for role R during W
≠ permanent frame-function for every actor and later context
```

A role/time limitation can be constitutive rather than merely cautious. A prior Binding may frame only those positions that inherited the Commitment. An asymmetric cost trajectory may remain active for the exposed role while being invisible to a new entrant. A later event may weaken the function after a specific threshold without erasing the source Trajectory.

Any material change in role-set, time window, target Frame, present conditions, or framed praxis dimension creates a new testable `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. It does not silently extend the old one.

<a id="31-7-multiple-frame-sources"></a>

### 31.7 Multiple Frame Sources

A later configuration may be structured by several concurrent sources. A prior Trajectory is therefore never entitled to explanatory priority merely because it is historically earlier, narratively coherent, or source-rich. Chapter 31 must compare the proposed Historical Load with present and alternative historical contributions without collapsing them into a single total Frame.

#### Concrete frame-source comparison

The projected historical frame-function may coexist or compete with target-side Frame occurrences, institutional Frames, present rules, and material `Φ` recontextualizations where those are source-supported. It does not compete with the abstract `□` operator type. Relative-load comparison concerns concrete contributions within the target Configuration, not rivalry among operator names.

#### C31-MULTIFRAME-01 — multiple present and historical frame sources remain possible

Relevant sources may include:

| Source family | Possible target contribution |
|---|---|
| present target Frame and immediate conditions | defines current relevance, resources, constraints, and interaction boundaries |
| current institutions, rules, and role assignments | supplies active expectations, access conditions, obligations, and sanctions |
| new events or Non-Events | opens, closes, delays, or redirects target possibilities |
| other historical Trajectories | carries distinct expectations, costs, Bindings, or residues into the same scene |
| current Attractors and repeated practices | stabilizes present continuations independently of the selected source history |
| Recontextualization and competing narratives | changes the meaning or practical use of inherited structures |
| Distance, Integration, and new Self-Binding | weakens, reorganizes, or deliberately carries historical pressure |
| material or technical conditions | alters feasibility and cost without being reducible to the source Trajectory |

The list is open. It does not create new PMS operators or a closed model enum. The question is whether the selected source Trajectory contributes a traceable and discriminating function after these other sources remain visible.

Multiple sources may reinforce, offset, mask, translate, or replace one another. Their effects are not assumed to be additive. A present institution may reproduce a historical role distinction through a new mechanism; a later event may recontextualize a prior residue; two different histories may lead to indistinguishable target scenes. The analysis must preserve these possibilities rather than assign all visible structure to `X`.

#### C31-RELLOAD-01 — relative load is qualitative, relational, and source-bound rather than scored

The chapter must state the proposed contribution of `X` relative to other declared sources. This can be expressed through open qualitative relations such as:

- constitutive for the bounded function claim;
- materially contributory but not sufficient;
- modulating the expression, timing, or role distribution;
- background relevance without a distinct target function;
- displaced or superseded in the declared target window;
- unresolved because available sources do not discriminate responsibly.

These phrases are not canonical Output Classes and do not form a closed scale. They must be tied to evidence and a named target difference. No numerical score, percentage allocation, ranking, or pseudo-precision is required or authorized.

```text
relative load statement
= relation among supported source contributions
≠ quantified causal share
≠ authority ranking
```

Where two sources are jointly constitutive and cannot be responsibly separated, the claim must say so. Where a present source fully carries the target reconstruction, the historical source may remain background relevant without a distinct frame-function. Where the evidence cannot distinguish historical carry-over from current reproduction, Non-Capture remains available rather than forcing a weak allocation.

#### Applying multiple-source pressure to `Y` and `Z`

For `Y`, the candidate frame-function survives WP2 pressure only as an open claim if:

- one or more source features of `X` remain traceable into the target period;
- their target work is not fully supplied by present conditions or rival sources;
- the function is bounded by named dimensions, roles, and time window;
- alternative interpretations and continuations remain possible.

For `Z`, the no-distinct-function candidate remains open if:

- the same `X` is historically relevant but adds no warranted target discrimination;
- present conditions or another source carry the complete bounded reconstruction;
- removing the proposed historical contribution would not require changing the target claim.

Neither side is adjudicated in WP2. The next work package must test rhetorical-history failure, bounded counterfactual sensitivity, competing projections, and failed projection routes.

#### WP2 carrier packet

At the end of WP2, the family packet adds architecture but no selected result:

```yaml
chapter_31_wp2_candidate:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory

  historical_load:
    definition: source_traceable_target_work
    source_object_warrant_separate: true
    target_function_warrant_separate: true
    candidate_carriers: declared
    load_bearing_features: candidate_not_yet_counterfactually_tested
    modulating_features: declared_where_supported

  conditioning:
    determinism: prohibited
    causal_monopoly: prohibited
    named_praxis_dimensions: required
    role_scope: declared
    time_window: declared

  frame_sources:
    present_conditions: retained
    rival_present_sources: retained
    rival_historical_sources: retained
    interaction: non_additive_and_open
    relative_load: qualitative_source_bound_not_scored

  result_status:
    counterfactual_frame_test: pending_wp3
    rhetorical_history_test: pending_wp3
    competing_projection_analysis: pending_wp3
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

#### C31-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives Historical-Load architecture without a final family result

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 31 WP2 complete
→ Chapter 31 WP3 §§31.8–31.11
→ Rhetorical History versus Frame-Function
→ Counterfactual Frame Test
→ Competing Frame Projections
→ Failed Frame Projection
```

WP3 receives:

- separate source-object and target-function warrants;
- declared candidate carriers with load-bearing/modulating distinction;
- bounded conditioning dimensions, roles, and time windows;
- explicit denial of determinism, inevitability, prediction, and causal monopoly;
- retained present and historical rival frame sources;
- qualitative, source-bound relative-load statements;
- the unchanged same-source `Y/Z` contrast.

WP3 does not inherit:

- proof that any candidate carrier is counterfactually constitutive;
- a successful frame-function in `Y`;
- a supported no-projection result in `Z`;
- a causal attribution or quantified share;
- a selected local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- a Chapter-31 or RETYPE lock;
- any person, normative, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority claim.

<a id="chapter-31-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 31.5–31.7 establish the family-specific Historical-Load burden, carrier distinction, bounded non-deterministic conditioning, role/time limitation, multiple-source requirement, and qualitative relative-load discipline.

```text
Historical-Load architecture specified
+ candidate carriers separated
+ conditioning bounded without determinism
+ multiple sources and relative load retained
+ no final family result selected
→ WP2 complete
```

This result does not perform the Counterfactual Frame Test, establish the final status of any carrier, defeat rhetorical history in the concrete pressure object, select a competing projection, establish frame-function in `Y`, establish no-projection in `Z`, choose a local operation result, map a canonical Output Class, lock Chapter 31, lock RETYPE, or increase authority.

<a id="31-8-rhetorical-history-versus-frame-function"></a>

### 31.8 Rhetorical History versus Frame-Function

Historical vocabulary can make a later configuration appear explained before a source-to-target relation has been reconstructed. Terms such as “history,” “legacy,” “tradition,” “culture,” “institutional memory,” or “the past” may identify a relevant background, but they do not by themselves establish a frame-function. A trajectory-to-frame-function claim requires an independently warranted source Trajectory, a declared target configuration, a constitutive trace across the temporal boundary, a specific target difference, and pressure from present conditions, rival frame sources, and counterfactual variation.

Rhetorical history begins where historical language supplies closure that the transformation Record has not earned. The problem is not that the historical description is necessarily false. The problem is that the description is being used as if it already proved a bounded `PROJECT_AS` relation.

```text
historical label
+ chronological continuity
+ retrospective narrative coherence
≠ bounded frame-function
```

#### C31-RHETORIC-01 — rhetorical history is separated from source-dependent framing

A historical invocation remains rhetorical for the present family claim when one or more of the following conditions hold:

- no PATH-established source Trajectory is identifiable;
- the source Trajectory is selected or periodized only after the desired target function is known;
- source-period, target-period, and later retrospective evidence are not separated;
- “history matters” replaces a named source feature, transmission relation, and target difference;
- the target function is stated through a broad label rather than through a bounded praxis dimension;
- present conditions and rival frame sources are omitted;
- contradictory target evidence is treated as further proof of hidden historical influence;
- the function claim survives every material change in the source structure;
- the past is assigned permanent, universal, or person-level explanatory reach;
- the historical narrative is used to close legitimacy, responsibility, diagnosis, recommendation, or sanction questions.

The minimum non-rhetorical route is:

```text
independently warranted Trajectory X
→ identified candidate carrier in X
→ supported persistence, transmission, institutionalization, or reactivation relation
→ declared target difference in Y
→ bounded role, time, level, and praxis dimension
→ present-condition and rival-source comparison
→ counterfactual pressure
```

Every arrow remains contestable. The route is not a causal chain automatically inferred from sequence, and the presence of all declarations does not prove the substantive relation. It merely states what must be supported before the projection can be admitted.

#### Historical labels as possible background relevance

Failure to establish a frame-function does not require deleting the historical material. A source Trajectory may remain:

- relevant to orientation or periodization;
- a documented predecessor of the target scene;
- a source of vocabulary or public memory;
- part of the contextual background;
- a rival explanatory candidate;
- or an unresolved source whose target function cannot be responsibly discriminated.

These statuses remain distinct from a bounded frame-function. Background relevance may be analytically useful while failing to change the target reconstruction along the declared dimensions.

```text
historical background retained
≠ frame-function warranted
≠ source Trajectory invalidated
```

#### Retrospective narration and target leakage

Later accounts may identify relations that were not legible during the source or target period. Such accounts can support reconstruction when their temporal status, evidentiary basis, and inferential role are declared. They may not silently replace missing source-period or target-period support.

A retrospective account is especially vulnerable to target leakage when it:

- selects only source features that resemble the later outcome;
- treats the realized target as the necessary endpoint of the Trajectory;
- suppresses branch points, reversals, and unrealized alternatives;
- converts later categories into earlier actors’ operative Frames without evidence;
- or interprets every ambiguity in the source as anticipation of the target.

Target leakage can produce a coherent narrative while destroying Counterfactual Sensitivity. If the same target label would have been applied regardless of source variation, the projection has become retrospectively elastic.

#### C31-TRACEFAIL-01 — citation, salience, target-fit, and retrospective narrative do not substitute for Constitutive Source Trace

A Constitutive Source Trace must identify more than references to the past. The family-specific trace must distinguish at least four evidence roles:

| Evidence role | Required question | Insufficient substitute |
|---|---|---|
| source-object support | What warrants `X` as a Trajectory independently of `Y`? | a later target narrative that reconstructs `X` to fit |
| carrier support | Which feature or relation in `X` is proposed to carry historical load? | source density, duration, salience, or label repetition |
| transmission support | How does that feature persist, transmit, institutionalize, or reactivate across the temporal boundary? | chronological proximity or continuity of names alone |
| target-function support | What changes in the reconstruction of `Y` because of that source-dependent relation? | target plausibility, resemblance, or retrospective coherence |

Trace failure occurs when any indispensable role is absent or when one evidence layer is used as a substitute for another. A large archive can establish the source object while leaving the target function unsupported. Strong target fit can motivate a test while leaving source selection circular. Repeated citation can show salience while failing to identify any material target work.

The required trace remains source- and claim-bound:

```yaml
frame_function_trace:
  source_object_warrant:
    independent_of_target_fit: required
    evidence_period: source_period_or_marked_reconstruction
  candidate_carrier:
    source_feature: required
    role: load_bearing_or_modulating_candidate
  temporal_relation:
    mode:
      - persistence
      - transmission
      - institutionalization
      - reactivation
    support: required
  target_function_warrant:
    target_difference: required
    evidence_period: target_period_or_marked_later_evidence
  rival_pressure:
    present_conditions: retained
    other_historical_sources: retained
    contrary_evidence: retained
```

This structure is not a new schema or closed trace taxonomy. It is a prose-level family discipline mapped onto the existing Constitutive Source Trace, evidence, alternatives, and Validity Scope carriers.

#### Rhetorical-history pressure on `Y` and `Z`

For `Y`, a plausible story about sedimented expectations and asymmetric costs remains only a candidate until the trace identifies which features of `X` continue to perform target work and why current conditions do not fully carry the same reconstruction.

For `Z`, the same historical vocabulary may remain available while the bounded target reconstruction is carried by present rules, new events, role changes, or other sources. In that configuration, invoking `X` may add background orientation without adding a distinct frame-function.

The contrast therefore asks:

```text
Does the historical claim discriminate between Y and Z,
or does the same narrative close both contexts regardless of their present structure?
```

A claim that cannot answer this question remains rhetorically historical rather than functionally projected.

<a id="31-9-counterfactual-frame-test"></a>

### 31.9 Counterfactual Frame Test

The Counterfactual Frame Test assesses whether the proposed frame-function is sensitive to relevant changes in the source carriers, target context, or competing frame-source configuration. It does not replay history, prove causality, establish a numerical causal share, or authorize prediction. Its role is discriminative: a source-specific function claim should change when the source structures claimed to carry that function materially change.

#### PMS-grounded carrier variation

Occurrence-level variants may pressure the specific carrier claim: timely repair may replace a claimed `Λ` non-repair relation; equalized exit cost may weaken an `Ω` gradient; interrupted commitment continuity may pressure a `Ψ` carrier; changed duration, order, or persistence may pressure a relevant `Θ` relation; and a material `Φ` recontextualization may alter whether earlier load still frames the target scene.

```text
concrete occurrence relation varied
≠ abstract PMS operator revised
```

Only operator typings already supported by the source Record enter these tests.

#### C31-CFTEST-01 — the family test varies carriers and contexts without converting sensitivity into causal proof

The bounded test architecture contains four distinct pressure routes:

| Test route | Controlled question | Claim pressure |
|---|---|---|
| carrier removal or replacement | Would the frame-function weaken, narrow, or disappear if a proposed load-bearing feature were absent or materially different? | tests whether the named carrier is constitutive rather than merely associated |
| source-trajectory variation | Would a relevant alternative Trajectory produce a different bounded target framing under otherwise comparable target conditions? | tests source specificity |
| same-source context variation | Does the same `X` perform a function in `Y` but not in `Z` when target conditions differ? | tests Contextual Boundedness |
| present/rival-source variation | Does the proposed historical function change when current rules, events, roles, or rival histories change? | tests whether the claim is over-attributing target structure to `X` |

The planned carrier variations for the stable pressure object include:

```yaml
counterfactual_frame_test_plan:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  carrier_variations:
    - timely_repair_replaces_repeated_non_repair
    - accumulated_exit_cost_is_equalized_or_removed
    - persistent_binding_continuity_is_interrupted
    - foreclosed_alternative_remains_available
  context_variations:
    - same_source_in_later_configuration_Y
    - same_source_in_later_configuration_Z
  rival_source_variations:
    - present_rules_supply_the_expectation_structure
    - later_event_recontextualizes_the_historical_residue
    - alternative_historical_trajectory_supplies_equivalent_target_framing
  permitted_descriptions:
    - sensitive
    - partially_sensitive
    - insensitive
    - underdetermined
    - not_testable_with_available_sources
  selected_description: not_selected
  causal_inference: prohibited
```

The descriptions are inherited analytical findings, not new Output Classes. They require justification and may differ by carrier, role, time window, and praxis dimension.

#### Test 1 — proposed load-bearing carrier removal

If repeated non-repair is claimed to condition later expectations, the analysis asks whether a counterfactual history containing timely and credible repair would require a different expectation-frame claim in `Y`. If the claim remains unchanged, repeated non-repair may be modulating, redundant with another source, or not constitutive for the bounded function.

If accumulated asymmetric exit cost is claimed to condition the later action corridor, the test asks whether equalized or substantially reduced exit cost would alter the corridor claim. A function label that persists unchanged after the relevant cost gradient is removed is insufficiently source-sensitive.

If persistent Binding is claimed to structure role reading, interruption or release of the Binding must pressure the scope and duration of the function. The Trajectory may remain historically important while the frame-function ends or changes after the interruption.

#### Test 2 — same source across `Y` and `Z`

The same source object remains fixed:

```text
X in Y
versus
X in Z
```

The test asks whether differences in present conditions, roles, target Frames, or time windows change the function claim. A bounded frame-function may remain plausible in `Y` where inherited expectations and costs materially structure credible continuation, while no distinct function may be warranted in `Z` where new rules, repair, role replacement, or resource changes carry the full target reconstruction.

The test is failed if `X` receives the same function merely because it is the same history. Contextual function must respond to context.

```text
same source identity
≠ invariant target function
```

#### Test 3 — source variation under comparable target pressure

A rival source Trajectory `X_alt` may be considered where sources support a materially different history leading toward a comparable later configuration. The purpose is not to invent a free counterfactual but to test whether the selected source structure adds discrimination.

Relevant questions include:

- Would a history without the proposed carrier support the same target expectations?
- Would a history with different roles or costs yield the same action corridor?
- Does the target function depend on `X`, or would any plausible past receive the same label?
- Is the claimed target function actually carried by a present institution that reproduces similar structure independently of `X`?

A source-specific projection becomes weak when materially different histories are treated as interchangeable without a trace-based explanation.

#### C31-SAMEEND-01 — different histories may yield indistinguishable later scenes

Different Trajectories can converge on later configurations that are indistinguishable at the declared target granularity. This possibility must remain open.

```text
X₁ ≠ X₂
and
Y(X₁) ≈ Y(X₂) at declared target dimensions
```

This does not prove that history is irrelevant. It creates three possible pressures:

1. **Source-specific claim reduction.** If the target reconstruction remains the same across materially different histories, the claim that `X₁` uniquely performs the frame-function must be narrowed or withdrawn.
2. **Functionally equivalent carriers.** Different historical structures may produce equivalent bounded framing work. The correct claim concerns a function family or shared relation, not exclusive dependence on one history.
3. **Resolution or source insufficiency.** The declared target granularity or available evidence may fail to reveal historically carried differences. The result may remain underdetermined or non-capturable rather than being forced into no-projection.

The same-end pressure must not erase PATH’s distinction between similar endpoints and different histories. It asks a different question: whether those historical differences remain materially discriminating for the specific target function and scope now claimed.

```text
same later scene at target granularity
≠ same historical path
but may imply
no responsibly discriminable source-specific frame-function at that granularity
```

#### Interpreting sensitivity without overclaim

A sensitive result supports only the bounded relation tested. It does not prove necessary causation, exclusive causation, historical inevitability, or predictive control. A partially sensitive result may require Claim Reduction, role/time limitation, or a modulating rather than load-bearing status. An insensitive result pressures the function claim toward background relevance, present-frame sufficiency, another projection, or Failure. An underdetermined or not-testable result may require provisional status, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture depending on source and claim ceilings.

No route is automatic. The complete audit remains required.

<a id="31-10-competing-frame-projections"></a>

### 31.10 Competing Frame Projections

The same source Trajectory may support more than one bounded function candidate in the same target configuration. These candidates can describe different target work, operate at different roles or levels, or conflict over what the source history materially contributes. RETYPE must preserve this plurality without turning a list of plausible labels into multiple successful projections.

Examples from the Structure include warning, legitimacy, expectation, constraint, identity, and background functions. These are open analytical descriptions, not a closed enum. “Legitimacy frame” describes how legitimacy claims become structured in a target scene; it does not decide political or legal legitimacy. “Identity frame” describes bounded role or collective self-description; it does not type persons or establish an essence.

#### C31-COMPETING-01 — rival frame projections and present-frame accounts remain independently testable

Each candidate function requires its own claim packet:

```yaml
competing_projection_claim:
  same_source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  same_or_declared_target_context: later_configuration_Y
  candidate_function: one_bounded_function
  target_difference: separately_declared
  constitutive_source_trace: separately_supported
  counterfactual_sensitivity: separately_tested
  validity_scope: separately_bounded
  projection_loss: separately_declared
  alternatives: retained
  result: independently_selected_if_executed
```

Possible candidates include:

| Candidate | Required target work | Typical overreach to avoid |
|---|---|---|
| warning frame | changes which risks or continuations are treated as credible | every reference to a past failure becomes warning |
| expectation frame | structures anticipated response, repair, omission, or recurrence | repetition is assumed without target evidence |
| constraint frame | changes practical thresholds, costs, or available action corridors | historical limits are treated as permanent impossibility |
| legitimacy-claim frame | structures which justificatory claims become credible or contestable | the analysis decides legitimacy itself |
| role or identity frame | conditions role reading or collective self-description | persons are typed or identities treated as fixed essence |
| background condition | situates the scene without a distinct target-function difference | background relevance is inflated into `PROJECT_AS` success |

Two projections may be compatible where they address different praxis dimensions and preserve separate traces. They may compete where they assign the same target difference to different functions, imply incompatible scopes, or rely on contradictory carrier accounts. Compatibility cannot be inferred from semantic similarity, and competition cannot be resolved by label preference.

#### Projection comparison discipline

Rival claims are compared through:

- independence and stability of the source object;
- specificity of the target difference;
- Constitutive Source Trace;
- sensitivity to carrier and context variation;
- present and rival-source pressure;
- role, time-window, and level scope;
- Type Integrity and Origin-Type Preservation;
- projection visibility and exact five-part Loss;
- parsimony without reducing substantive adequacy to formal simplicity;
- Stop and Non-Capture conditions.

A candidate with broader semantic coverage is not automatically superior. A narrower claim may be more admissible because it remains traceable and counterfactually sensitive.

#### Present-frame and recontextualization alternatives

A present-frame account may explain the target configuration through current rules, resources, roles, events, or institutions without requiring a distinct historical frame-function. A recontextualization-only account may show that `X` is newly interpreted in `Y` while failing to establish that `X` materially structures target legibility or action.

```text
X is discussed differently in Y
→ possible Φ recontextualization

X materially conditions bounded target praxis in Y
→ candidate PROJECT_AS frame-function
```

These accounts can coexist, but they remain separate claims. Recontextualization does not become a fallback label for a failed projection, and a present-frame account does not erase the source Trajectory.

#### C31-NOPROJ-01 — background relevance and no-projection remain positive analytical possibilities

No-projection is warranted as a live possibility when the source history is documented but no distinct, traceable, context-sensitive frame-function is required for the target reconstruction. It is not an analytical failure by default.

Three positions must remain distinct:

| Position | Meaning |
|---|---|
| bounded frame-function | `X` performs identifiable source-dependent framing work in the declared target context |
| background relevance only | `X` helps situate or interpret the scene but adds no distinct function at the claimed dimensions |
| no-projection | the proposed `PROJECT_AS` relation is not warranted for the delimited claim |

A present-frame account may be sufficient in `Z` while `X` remains historically relevant. The analyst must not preserve a weak projection merely to avoid the no-projection result.

No-projection also remains possible where:

- the target difference disappears after present conditions are modeled;
- rival histories produce the same bounded target reconstruction;
- the source carrier is not counterfactually discriminating;
- the function label is rhetorical, analogical, or retrospectively selected;
- the target scope would exceed the available evidence;
- or a non-operation account is more precise.

Where the evidence cannot responsibly distinguish no-projection from competing bounded functions, Non-Capture may be more disciplined than forced selection.

#### Same source, multiple claims, separate Records

A different function, target context, role-set, time window, granularity, or framed praxis dimension creates a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. Compatible functions may later be compared or composed analytically, but they do not share an operation result automatically.

```text
X PROJECT_AS expectation-frame in Y
≠
X PROJECT_AS constraint-frame in Y
```

Each claim retains separate justification, trace, sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, and canonical mapping. This prevents a supported narrow function from authorizing broader historical framing.

<a id="31-11-failed-frame-projection"></a>

### 31.11 Failed Frame Projection

A frame projection fails when the delimited source-to-target function claim cannot satisfy the family-specific burdens within the Admissibility Band. Failure concerns the transformation claim. It does not automatically invalidate the source Trajectory, erase historical relevance, or determine the result of another projection.

#### C31-FAILURE-01 — failed frame projection is represented without source-object invalidation

Failure pressures include:

| Failure site | Family-specific failure condition | What remains open |
|---|---|---|
| source entry | no independently warranted Trajectory or circular source selection from target fit | a different PATH reconstruction or no operation |
| temporal evidence | source, target, and later retrospective evidence are conflated | marked provisional reconstruction or Stop |
| Constitutive Source Trace | no carrier, transmission relation, or separate target-function support | background relevance or another claim |
| target difference | the later scene is reconstructed the same without the proposed historical function | present-frame account or no-projection |
| Counterfactual Sensitivity | the claim remains unchanged across relevant carrier and source variation | Claim Reduction, rival function, or Failure |
| multiple-source discrimination | present and historical sources cannot be responsibly separated | Non-Capture or bounded provisional claim |
| Type Integrity | the Trajectory is treated as `□`, a person property, or a new primitive | Mandatory Stop |
| Validity Scope | the function is made permanent, universal, or transferable without a new operation | Claim Reduction or Mandatory Stop |
| Claim/Authority Ceiling | projection is used for diagnosis, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority inheritance | Mandatory Stop |

The local phrase `failed frame projection` is not a new canonical Output Class. An executed claim that fails the complete audit may map to `failed_transformation`; a narrower supported remainder may require `claim_reduction_required` or `partially_admissible`; inability to discriminate may require `non_capture`; prohibited continuation may require `mandatory_stop`. Mapping remains claim-specific and non-automatic.

#### Failure does not rewrite PATH

The following inference is prohibited:

```text
frame-function claim fails
→ source Trajectory did not exist
```

The source object retains its prior PATH disposition unless independent source-side evidence requires revision. Failure may instead show that:

- the Trajectory carries no distinct function in this target context;
- the proposed function was mis-specified;
- present conditions carry the reconstruction;
- the relevant role or time window was too broad;
- source loss prevents a responsible function claim;
- or the available evidence cannot discriminate among rival projections.

A revised source object or periodization creates a new PATH claim before a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. RETYPE may not repair a weak source object by finding a useful target label.

#### Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture

Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture remain non-equivalent:

```text
Failure
→ the delimited transformation claim does not satisfy its burdens

Mandatory Stop
→ continuation would require prohibited overreach or exceed source/claim ceilings

Non-Capture
→ responsible discrimination among live claims cannot be achieved without destructive simplification or unsupported closure
```

Mandatory Stop applies where continuing would require:

- origin-type replacement;
- universal historical determination;
- untraceable carrier invention;
- retrospective source reconstruction solely from target fit;
- erasure of present or rival frame sources;
- person typing, diagnosis, legitimacy judgment, recommendation, or sanction;
- or authority inheritance.

Non-Capture remains available where source and present contributions are inseparable, different histories yield indistinguishable target scenes at the relevant granularity, or rival bounded functions remain equally supported and cannot be responsibly selected.

#### Family pressure-object status after WP3

WP3 completes the test architecture but does not fabricate evidence for `documented_trajectory_X`. The pressure object remains:

```yaml
chapter_31_wp3_family_packet:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_X
  origin_type: Trajectory

  rhetorical_history_controls:
    independent_source_trajectory_required: true
    source_target_retrospective_evidence_separated: true
    constitutive_trace_beyond_citation_or_salience: required
    target_leakage_prohibited: true

  counterfactual_frame_test:
    carrier_variation: specified
    source_variation: specified
    same_source_Y_Z_context_variation: specified
    present_and_rival_source_variation: specified
    same_end_different_history_pressure: retained
    selected_sensitivity_description: not_selected
    causal_inference: prohibited

  competing_projections:
    rival_bounded_functions: retained
    present_frame_account: retained
    recontextualization_only: retained
    background_relevance: retained
    no_projection: retained
    separate_claims_and_records_required: true

  failure_architecture:
    source_trace_target_difference_sensitivity_scope_type_and_discrimination_failures: representable
    source_trajectory_automatically_invalidated: false
    mandatory_stop: retained
    non_capture: retained

  result_status:
    frame_function_in_Y: not_selected
    no_distinct_function_in_Z: not_selected
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
    chapter_31_provisional_lock: false
    retype_part_locked: false
```

#### C31-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives a complete family architecture without RETYPE lock or case-YAML production

WP4 receives:

- the bounded trajectory-to-frame-function claim;
- independently warranted source entry and temporal evidence separation;
- explicit target coordinates and present-condition controls;
- Historical Load and carrier architecture;
- bounded non-deterministic conditioning;
- multiple frame sources and qualitative relative load;
- rhetorical-history and trace-failure criteria;
- the complete Counterfactual Frame Test architecture;
- same-end/different-history pressure;
- competing bounded projections, present-frame accounts, background relevance, recontextualization-only, and no-projection;
- failed projection, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture routes;
- all twenty-four Chapter-31 Pressure Duties;
- exact five-part Loss, Claim Ceiling, and authority prohibition inherited from Chapter 30.

WP4 must perform the integrated Chapter Contract, redundancy, twenty-four-duty, Reference, Formal-Model, schema, inventory, link, fingerprint, package, CRC, and byte-roundtrip audit. It may provisionally lock the chapter-level method if the completion gate passes. It must not fabricate a successful `PROJECT_AS` occurrence for `X/Y`, fabricate a no-projection result for `X/Z`, create test, case, or smoke YAMLs, select an anchor Output Class without evidence, lock RETYPE, or increase authority.

The Chapter-32 Preparation handoff must preserve:

- the distinction between source Trajectory and target function;
- the complete generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure;
- the family-specific non-automaticity demonstrated here;
- separate evidence, counterfactual, Loss, and result burdens for the macro-event family.

complete trajectory-to-frame-function family method
+ source and target warrants remain separate

```

The Chapter-31 result is:

admissible_but_provisional

The class applies to the chapter-level family specification under the still-open RETYPE Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not classify `documented_trajectory_X`, establish Historical Load in `later_configuration_Y`, establish a frame-function in `Y`, establish no-projection in `later_configuration_Z`, select a Counterfactual Sensitivity description, choose a local `PROJECT_AS` result, map a canonical Output Class for the pressure object, prove causal transmission, quantify relative historical load, or increase claim, application, governance, diagnostic, person-level, political, legal, normative, recommendation, sanction, or authority status.

A valid PATH Trajectory may remain background relevant without a distinct frame-function; present conditions may carry the target reconstruction; different histories may yield indistinguishable later scenes; historical and present contributions may remain inseparable; and a rhetorically compelling history may still require Claim Reduction, Failure, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture.

```text
Chapter 31 provisionally locked
≠ source Trajectory converted into □

≠ frame-function established for Y
≠ no-projection established for Z
≠ causal relation proven

It receives no permission to punctualize a Trajectory, let a period label determine source boundaries, merge `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS`, treat a Macro-Event as a new primitive, infer a causal turning point, fabricate a positive or negative family result, create case/test/smoke YAMLs during Block drafting, or increase authority. Chapter 32 alone owns the bounded trajectory-to-macro-event family.

<a id="chapter-32-trajectory-as-macro-event"></a>

## 32. Trajectory as Macro-Event

Chapter 32 applies the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure to the second principal RETYPE family: the bounded projection of an already reconstructed Trajectory as a Macro-Event function within a wider Path or temporal Frame. Chapters 1 and 3 own the distinctions among Event, extended Event, Transition, Path, and Trajectory. Chapters 9–17 own PATH and the `COMPOSE` formation of the source object. Chapter 29 owns functional projection without Origin-Type Replacement. Chapter 30 owns the complete generic operation procedure. Chapter 31 demonstrates that a projection family can be methodologically specified without fabricating an occurrence result. This chapter therefore does not redefine Event, Trajectory, temporal extension, or `PROJECT_AS`. It asks a narrower family-specific question:

> When may a PATH-established, temporally extended, and internally heterogeneous Trajectory function as one source-traceable transition-bearing unit in a wider declared Path without being converted into a punctual Event, retrospectively periodized from a later outcome, or detached from the internal structure that makes the projection possible?

The family claim is stronger than descriptive period compression and weaker than a causal turning-point claim. A historical development may be summarized as a period without performing a distinct Macro-Event function in the target reconstruction. Conversely, a bounded Macro-Event function may be warranted even though the source remains extended, contains reversals, does not act alone, and does not cause the later state by itself.

The chapter uses one stable source object and two target contexts:

```text
source object: documented_trajectory_M
origin type: Trajectory
source temporal scope: period_A

positive target candidate: wider_path_B
proposed function: bounded macro-event function

negative contrast: wider_path_C
proposed result possibility: no distinct macro-event function
```

The source object must remain fixed across `B` and `C`. A materially different start, end, phase selection, or branch structure creates a different PATH claim before it can become the source of another `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. This protects the family test against a common circularity: selecting whichever historical interval best fits a desired later headline and then treating that fit as evidence for the projection.

```text
same source Trajectory
+ different wider target Paths
→ transition-bearing function may be warranted in B and absent in C
```

WP1 establishes the family claim, the eligible source entry, the meaning of Macro-Event as a target function, the wider target Frame, and the boundary-selection obligations. It does not yet adjudicate preservation of internal duration, heterogeneous phase load, the concrete transition effect, causal limits, punctualization error, the `COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` chain result, Counterfactual Sensitivity, rival periodizations, alternative source objects, a local operation result, or a canonical Output Class. Those burdens remain open for §§32.5–32.11.

<a id="32-1-basic-claim"></a>

### 32.1 Basic Claim

A PATH-established Trajectory may perform a Macro-Event function when, within a wider declared Path or temporal Frame, it serves as a bounded and source-traceable transition-bearing unit whose inclusion materially changes the reconstruction of the wider development. The target work may concern which larger phases are distinguishable, which later possibilities become available or unavailable, which before/after relation becomes praxeologically discriminating, or how the wider Path is segmented into materially different continuation conditions.

#### PMS-structured source, non-operator target function

The linked PATH Record must preserve, where material, which operator-typed occurrences and relations constitute the Trajectory's phase structure and historical load. Chapter 32 references that source warrant; it does not reconstruct the Trajectory from the desired Macro-Event function.

```text
operator-structured source Trajectory
→ eligible source for PROJECT_AS testing

Macro-Event target function
≠ PMS operator identity
```

No operator equivalent for Macro-Event is introduced.

#### C32-BASIC-01 — the family claim is bounded trajectory-to-macro-event projection

The minimal family form is:

```text
Within wider target Path or temporal Frame C_t,
Trajectory M,
while retaining origin type Trajectory,
performs bounded Macro-Event function F_macro
in relation to declared target transition dimensions D_t.
```

Every position remains explicit:

| Position | Required declaration | Family-specific burden |
|---|---|---|
| `M` | PATH-established source Trajectory | independently warranted before Macro-Event adjudication |
| `Trajectory` | visible origin type | preserved throughout the projection |
| `C_t` | wider Path or temporal Frame | distinct from the source object and explicitly coordinated |
| `F_macro` | specific Macro-Event function candidate | must name transition-bearing target work |
| `D_t` | target transition dimensions | later possibilities, wider-path segmentation, before/after relation, or bounded path-structuring difference |

The claim is relational. `F_macro` is not an intrinsic property stored in the source Trajectory and not a permanent classification of the historical period. The same Trajectory may perform a Macro-Event function in one wider Path, provide only descriptive background in another, and support no distinct projection in a third. Its function depends on the declared target Frame, granularity, relative level, role-set, time scope, and praxeological transition dimensions.

```text
Trajectory M exists
≠ M is a Macro-Event in every larger history

M is historically prominent
≠ M performs a transition-bearing function in C_t
```

A Macro-Event candidate requires more than chronological inclusion. The source must occupy a bounded relation to developments before and after it, and the target reconstruction must gain a concrete distinction that cannot be reduced to naming a period. The function may mark, organize, or carry a transition without proving that `M` was the sole cause, the decisive turning point, or a necessary historical step.

#### Source object versus target transition claim

The source Trajectory and the Macro-Event function answer different questions:

```text
PATH source claim:
Which configurations, transitions, events, Non-Events, branches,
and sedimented relations constitute Trajectory M?

RETYPE family claim:
What bounded transition-bearing work does that already reconstructed M
perform in the wider target Path C_t?
```

A valid answer to the first question is necessary but not sufficient for the second. Chapter 32 cannot rescue an under-specified historical sequence by assigning it a useful Macro-Event label. Nor may it revise the source boundaries solely because a different interval would produce a cleaner before/after narrative.

#### C32-SOURCE-01 — the source is a prior PATH Trajectory with retained record, disposition, uncertainty, and Loss

The minimum source entry is:

```yaml
source:
  object: documented_trajectory_M
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_M
  source_selection_basis: independently_warranted_before_macro_event_adjudication
  source_frame: declared_PATH_frame
  source_granularity: declared_PATH_granularity
  source_level: declared_relative_source_level
  temporal_scope: period_A
  constitutive_phases:
    - phase_M1
    - phase_M2
    - phase_M3
  internal_turning_points: retained
  reversals_and_parallel_subpaths: retained
  prior_disposition: retained
  source_uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

The source entry protects several independent positions:

- `source_reference` anchors the object to its prior PATH reconstruction;
- `origin_type` prevents target-function language from rewriting the object as Event;
- source coordinates preserve the conditions under which the Trajectory claim was warranted;
- constitutive phases and internal turning points make later compression inspectable;
- `prior_disposition` prevents RETYPE from upgrading or erasing an earlier source result;
- uncertainty and inherited Loss remain active burdens rather than disappearing at the higher target level.

The declaration `source_selection_basis: independently_warranted_before_macro_event_adjudication` is necessary but not self-validating. Substantive independence remains a human and source-grounded judgment. If the target outcome, later period name, or desired transition story determines the source interval retrospectively, the family claim must be reduced, stopped, or returned to PATH for a new source reconstruction.

#### Stable source and target contrast

The family architecture retains two equally legitimate possibilities:

```yaml
same_source_contrast:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_M
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_M

  target_B:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_B
    candidate: bounded_macro_event_function
    required_transition_difference:
      - later_possibilities_change
      - wider_path_segmentation_changes
      - before_after_relation_becomes_praxeologically_discriminating

  target_C:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_C
    candidate: no_distinct_macro_event_function
    pressure:
      - wider_path_reconstruction_may_remain_unchanged
      - period_label_may_add_no_transition_gain
      - internal_reversals_may_defeat_unitary_event_function
```

`B` is not a presumed positive case and `C` is not an analytical failure by default. Both are test contexts. A well-supported source Trajectory may remain historically important while providing no additional Macro-Event function in `C`. The contrast prevents source validity, narrative prominence, or period familiarity from becoming automatic target-function warrant.

<a id="32-2-macro-event-as-target-function"></a>

### 32.2 Macro-Event as Target Function

A Macro-Event is the bounded target function through which an extended Trajectory is treated as one transition-bearing analytical unit in a wider Path. The phrase **one unit** refers to its role at the target level. It does not assert that the source occurred at one instant, consisted of one act, had one causal origin, or can be assigned to one responsible agent.

#### C32-MACROFUNC-01 — the target function must name concrete transition-bearing work

A valid candidate must specify what changes in the wider target reconstruction when the source Trajectory is projected as a Macro-Event. Permissible function descriptions include:

- marking a bounded transition between materially different wider-path configurations;
- changing which later continuations are available, costly, credible, or institutionally supported;
- structuring the segmentation of the wider Path into praxeologically discriminating phases;
- carrying a before/after distinction that remains traceable to the source Trajectory;
- concentrating an extended transition cluster into one inspectable target-level relation while preserving source-level access.

The candidate must be stated through a target difference rather than through a title:

```text
weak:
"period_A was a major historical episode"

stronger candidate form:
"within wider_path_B, Trajectory M functions as the bounded transition-bearing
unit separating target configuration B_pre from B_post because the available
continuations, role relations, costs, or institutional pathways differ in
source-traceable ways across that relation"
```

The stronger form remains a candidate until later sections test duration, heterogeneity, phase load, target transition gain, rival periodizations, and counterfactual pressure. WP1 fixes the required claim grammar; it does not yet establish that the difference is supported.

#### Origin type and target function remain separate

```text
source object: Trajectory M
origin type: Trajectory

target function: bounded Macro-Event function
```

The projection adds a relation. It does not replace the source object:

```text
Trajectory M PROJECT_AS Macro-Event in wider_path_B
≠ M becomes an Event occurrence as origin type
≠ Macro-Event becomes a PMS operator
≠ Macro-Event becomes a new PMS primitive
```

The term `Macro-Event` therefore belongs to the open family of contextual target functions. It is not a twelfth PMS operator, a fourth STRATA operation, a new object primitive, a closed enum, or a universal historical category. It remains bounded by one target context and one delimited claim.

#### C32-NOTPUNCTUAL-01 — target-level unity does not imply punctual occurrence, Event-type replacement, or primitive status

The following reductions are prohibited:

```text
one target element
→ one instant

one target element
→ one act

one target element
→ one responsible agent

Macro-Event function
→ Event origin type
```

A target-level representation may compress an extended source into one analytical position only if the internal temporal structure remains recoverable and relevant Loss is disclosed. The source retains duration, order, phase relations, reversals, parallel subpaths, differentiated roles, unequal costs, Non-Events, and heterogeneous consequences. WP2 owns the full preservation test. WP1 establishes that these features cannot be presumed absent merely because the target notation uses one unit.

#### Extended Event versus Macro-Event function

An extended Event occurrence and a Macro-Event function are not interchangeable. An extended Event is an event-like source object whose occurrence itself spans time. A Macro-Event function is a target-context role assigned through `PROJECT_AS` to an origin-typed source object—here a Trajectory. The family therefore requires both source identity and target function to remain visible:

```text
extended Event occurrence
≠ Trajectory projected as Macro-Event

shared temporal extension
≠ shared origin type
```

This distinction prevents similarity of scale or duration from becoming operator identity. A source object better reconstructed as an extended Event, Transition cluster, narrower Path, or another composite must first be warranted under the appropriate source operation. RETYPE may not choose Trajectory merely because the word “macro” appears suitable.

#### No causal turning-point claim at family entry

A transition-bearing function does not by itself establish causal priority:

```text
M marks or structures a transition in wider_path_B
≠ M alone caused B_post
≠ B_post was historically necessary
≠ no rival source contributed
```

The family can be analytically useful without causal monopoly. Causal claims require independent domain evidence and remain outside automatic STRATA determination. The target function is praxeological and reconstructive: it concerns the organization of the wider Path, not a complete causal theory of history.

<a id="32-3-target-frame"></a>

### 32.3 Target Frame

A Macro-Event function is unintelligible without a wider target Path or temporal Frame. The target must be broader than the source in the declared compositional relation and must contain a before/after or preceding/following structure within which the source can perform transition-bearing work. Merely renaming the source period at the same level does not create a target context.

#### C32-TARGETFRAME-01 — the wider target Path or temporal Frame must be explicit, distinct, and claim-bounded

The minimum target declaration is:

```yaml
target:
  object_or_scene: wider_path_B
  target_frame: wider_history_B
  target_granularity: declared_wider_granularity
  target_level: declared_relative_target_level
  temporal_scope: declared_wider_period
  relation_to_source:
    source_period_contained_or_positioned: true
    preceding_developments: declared
    following_developments: declared
    before_after_relation: declared
  relevant_roles: declared
  target_praxis_dimensions:
    - later_possibilities
    - wider_path_segmentation
    - transition_bearing_contrast
  present_and_adjacent_conditions: declared
  claim_ceiling: bounded_reconstructive_macro_event_function_only
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

Each coordinate performs a separate control function:

- `object_or_scene` identifies the wider Path being reconstructed;
- `target_frame` sets the relevance boundary for the Macro-Event claim;
- target granularity and relative level explain why one extended source can occupy one target-level analytical position;
- temporal scope identifies the wider interval within which transition work is claimed;
- preceding and following developments make the claimed relation inspectable;
- roles and praxis dimensions prevent a generic “historical importance” claim;
- adjacent conditions keep rival present and contemporaneous sources visible;
- Claim Ceiling blocks causal, normative, diagnostic, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority inflation.

The target Frame is not optional because `Macro-Event` has no context-free meaning. The same source Trajectory may segment one institutional Path, remain background context in another, and be irrelevant to a third. Without a wider declared object, the label merely repeats that the source was extended or important.

#### Wider target relation versus vague scale increase

“Macro” does not mean objectively large, socially important, or ontologically higher. It means that the source is used as one bounded transition-bearing unit relative to a wider declared target reconstruction. A short Trajectory may be macro relative to a narrower sequence; a multi-decade development may still lack a Macro-Event function if no wider Path gains a discriminating transition relation.

```text
larger duration
≠ higher relative level automatically

public prominence
≠ Macro-Event function

wider target relation
+ explicit transition work
→ admissible family candidate
```

Relative level must therefore be locally declared. The chapter does not create a universal micro–meso–macro hierarchy, a standard duration threshold, or a fixed historical scale.

#### Target before/after relation

A target before/after distinction must be praxeological rather than merely chronological. It may concern changes in:

- available or closed action corridors;
- institutional or relational roles;
- recurring costs and asymmetries;
- expectations and binding structures;
- reachable branches in the wider Path;
- visibility or feasibility of later integration, repair, or exit.

Chronological adjacency alone is insufficient. A source can lie between two dates without carrying the transition between their configurations. Conversely, an internally extended source may overlap surrounding developments and still perform a bounded target function when its relation to them is explicitly reconstructed.

#### Same source across wider target Paths

The `B/C` contrast remains active:

```text
M within wider_path_B
→ bounded Macro-Event function candidate

M within wider_path_C
→ no distinct Macro-Event function remains possible
```

The target change may alter function, but it must not silently alter the source Trajectory. If the relevant phases, start, end, or branches differ materially, the analysis must either declare a different source object or return to PATH. This prevents a moving historical interval from masquerading as context sensitivity.

<a id="32-4-boundary-selection"></a>

### 32.4 Boundary Selection

Macro-Event projection depends on defensible boundaries. The source Trajectory must already possess a PATH-supported temporal scope, but Chapter 32 must additionally explain why that scope is appropriate for the proposed target function. Source-object warrant, boundary warrant for this projection, and target transition-function warrant remain distinct.

#### Operator-relevant boundary evidence

Start, end, phase, and turning-point boundaries should be supported by changes in the reconstructed praxis structure rather than calendar convenience or later naming alone. Source-supported evidence may include changed Frame or role-space organization, emergence or interruption of material `Λ`, `Α`, `Ω`, or `Ψ` load, a material `Φ` recontextualization, or changed `Θ` order, persistence, delay, or trajectory relation.

```text
operator-relevant configuration change may support a phase boundary
≠ operator symbol automatically fixes a historical boundary
```

The relevant occurrence relations depend on the source Record; no fixed checklist is imposed.

#### C32-BOUNDARY-01 — start, end, phases, turning points, and adjacent developments require source-supported declaration

The boundary packet must include:

```yaml
boundary_selection:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_M
  start:
    candidate: source_supported_start
    supporting_transition_or_configuration: declared
    uncertainty: retained
  end:
    candidate: source_supported_end
    supporting_transition_or_configuration: declared
    uncertainty: retained
  constitutive_phases:
    - phase_M1
    - phase_M2
    - phase_M3
  internal_turning_points: declared
  reversals_and_parallel_subpaths: retained
  relation_to_preceding_developments: declared
  relation_to_following_developments: declared
  alternative_boundary_sets:
    - boundary_set_R1
    - boundary_set_R2
    - no_unitary_boundary
  selection_justification: required
```

The start and end need not be perfectly sharp. Bounded uncertainty is admissible when sources support an interval, transition zone, or contested periodization. What is prohibited is false precision that hides source uncertainty or a boundary chosen solely because it produces a convenient target story.

Constitutive phases and internal turning points protect the source against hollow compression. They identify which internal relations must remain available for later duration, heterogeneity, phase-load, and Counterfactual Macro-Event testing. Their declaration does not yet determine which phases are load-bearing. That adjudication belongs to WP2 and WP3.

#### Three boundary warrants

The analysis must distinguish:

| Warrant | Governing question | Failure risk |
|---|---|---|
| source-object boundary warrant | Why does PATH reconstruct this interval as Trajectory `M`? | retrospectively invented source object |
| projection boundary warrant | Why is this source scope the relevant unit for testing `F_macro`? | convenient but functionally arbitrary periodization |
| target-placement warrant | Why does `M` occupy this relation to preceding and following developments in the wider Path? | period label without transition gain |

One warrant cannot silently substitute for another. A canonical historical period may be a valid PATH source object yet fail as a Macro-Event in the selected target. A target transition may be real while the chosen source interval is too broad. A narrow source may carry the function even when the conventional period name does not.

#### C32-ANTILABEL-01 — period labels, headlines, and later outcomes cannot alone fix boundaries or function

The following are insufficient by themselves:

- a familiar era name;
- a retrospective headline;
- a later institutional self-description;
- the fact that a later state exists;
- citation frequency or public salience;
- visual separation in a timeline, Graph, or Reader;
- a model field declaring `start`, `end`, or `macro_event`;
- a later outcome that makes one interval look narratively decisive.

```text
period label
→ candidate for testing

period label
≠ source boundary warrant
≠ projection boundary warrant
≠ target transition function
```

Later retrospective evidence may help formulate rival periodizations or test whether participants and institutions subsequently treated the interval as a transition. It must be marked as later evidence and may not silently replace source-period or target-period support.

#### Outcome-driven boundary selection

A particularly serious error occurs when the analyst begins from `B_post`, identifies the history that best explains it, and then selects that history as both source object and Macro-Event. This collapses source selection, boundary selection, target-function support, and retrospective explanation into one circular claim.

```text
later outcome observed
→ interval selected because it fits outcome
→ interval called Macro-Event
```

This sequence is not sufficient. The source Trajectory must be independently warranted; the boundary relation must survive alternative periodizations; and the target transition difference must be supported separately. Where those positions cannot be separated, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture remains available.

#### WP1 family packet

WP1 leaves the following declared but unadjudicated packet:

```yaml
chapter_32_wp1_family_packet:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_M
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_M
    selection_independent_of_macro_event_result: required
    temporal_scope: period_A
    constitutive_phases: declared
    internal_turning_points: retained
    reversals_and_parallel_subpaths: retained
    prior_disposition: retained
    uncertainty: retained
    inherited_loss: retained

  target_B:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_B
    target_frame: wider_history_B
    coordinates: declared
    proposed_function: bounded_macro_event_function
    transition_difference: specified_but_not_yet_adjudicated

  target_C:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_C
    coordinates: declared
    proposed_result_possibility: no_distinct_macro_event_function

  boundary_controls:
    start_and_end: source_supported_candidates
    constitutive_phases: declared
    internal_turning_points: declared
    preceding_and_following_developments: declared
    rival_periodizations: retained
    label_only_selection: prohibited
    outcome_driven_selection: prohibited

  type_controls:
    origin_type_preserved: true
    punctual_event_identity: false
    new_primitive: false

  result_status:
    internal_duration_preservation: not_yet_adjudicated
    internal_heterogeneity_preservation: not_yet_adjudicated
    event_function: not_yet_adjudicated
    counterfactual_macro_event_test: not_yet_performed
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

The packet is ready for the duration, heterogeneity, and event-function analysis of WP2. It is not an executed projection record and does not select a result for either `B` or `C`.

#### C32-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives a bounded family packet without duration, heterogeneity, or event-function adjudication

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 32 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 32 WP2 §§32.5–32.7
→ Internal Duration
→ Internal Heterogeneity
→ Event Function
```

WP2 receives:

- one independently warranted PATH Trajectory with retained source reference;
- visible origin type, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited five-part Loss;
- one explicit wider target Path `B` and one same-source contrast Path `C`;
- declared target coordinates, before/after relation, roles, praxis dimensions, adjacent conditions, and Claim Ceiling;
- a concrete but still unadjudicated Macro-Event function candidate;
- source-supported boundary candidates, constitutive phases, internal turning points, and adjacent developments;
- rival periodizations and the prohibition on label-only or outcome-driven boundary selection;
- explicit denial of punctual Event identity and new-primitive status.

WP2 does not inherit:

- proof that internal duration survives target compression;
- proof that heterogeneity remains traceable;
- a determination of load-bearing or modulating phases;
- an established transition difference in `B`;
- a no-projection result in `C`;
- causal turning-point proof, necessity, or explanatory monopoly;
- a Counterfactual Sensitivity description;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- a Chapter-32 or RETYPE lock;
- any person, normative, political, legal, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority claim.

<a id="chapter-32-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 32.1–32.4 establish the bounded trajectory-to-macro-event family claim, prior PATH source entry, retained source disposition/uncertainty/Loss, concrete Macro-Event target-function grammar, Origin-Type Preservation, denial of punctual Event identity and primitive status, a wider explicit target Path or temporal Frame, praxeological before/after coordinates, source-supported boundary obligations, anti-label and anti-outcome periodization controls, and the stable same-source `B/C` function/no-function contrast.

```text
family claim declared
+ source and target objects separated
+ Macro-Event function stated as transition-bearing target work
+ target Frame and boundary warrants declared
+ punctualization and retrospective label substitution prohibited
→ WP1 complete
```

This result does not establish internal-duration preservation, internal heterogeneity preservation, load-bearing phase status, a supported transition effect, causal boundaries, punctualization failure, the `COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` chain result, Counterfactual Sensitivity, rival-periodization adjudication, a preferred alternative source object, a successful projection in `B`, no-projection in `C`, a local operation result, a canonical Output Class, Chapter-32 lock, RETYPE lock, or increased authority.

<a id="32-5-internal-duration"></a>

### 32.5 Internal Duration

A Trajectory does not cease to be temporally extended when it is used as one analytical element in a wider Path. The Macro-Event function compresses the source for a declared target reconstruction; it does not convert the source into a punctual Event. Internal duration therefore remains a constitutive property of the origin-typed object and a mandatory part of the projection trace.

```text
one target-level analytical position
≠ one instant
≠ simultaneous source process
≠ lossless temporal summary
```

The relevant question is not whether every source-side date remains foregrounded in the target account. It is whether the duration, sequence, and phase relations whose alteration would change the proposed transition function remain identifiable and recoverable from the source record.

#### Temporal anchoring without temporal retyping

Where warranted in the source reconstruction, internal duration, order, delay, persistence, and phase relation are carried through concrete `Θ`-typed temporal occurrence relations. Treating the Trajectory as one target-level unit must preserve access to those relations.

```text
Θ-related temporal load retained
≠ Trajectory becomes Θ
≠ Macro-Event becomes a temporal operator
```

This is a source-preservation requirement, not a new theory of Temporality.

#### C32-DURATION-01 — target compression must preserve internal duration, sequence, and phase relations

A Macro-Event candidate must retain at least the following temporal structure:

```yaml
internal_duration:
  source_temporal_scope: period_A
  duration_status: extended
  sequence:
    - phase_M1
    - phase_M2
    - phase_M3
  phase_relations:
    - transition_M1_to_M2
    - transition_M2_to_M3
  internal_turning_points: retained
  delays_and_non_events: retained_where_constitutive
  reversals: retained_where_constitutive
  parallel_subpaths: retained_where_constitutive
  temporal_uncertainty: declared
  target_level_representation: one_bounded_transition_bearing_unit_candidate
  punctual_event_identity: false
```

This declaration does not require all phases to receive equal narrative space. It requires their order and constitutive relations to remain available for inspection. A target account may foreground the source as one unit while its source record preserves the temporal architecture that makes the unit intelligible.

#### Duration as source property, not target decoration

Duration cannot be preserved merely by adding the adjective “extended” to a Macro-Event label. Preservation requires traceable temporal content. At minimum, the analysis must be able to answer:

- What persisted across the source interval?
- What changed between constitutive phases?
