- Which sequence relations are necessary to the candidate target function?
- Which delays, absences, or deferred transitions altered later possibilities?
- Which reversals or partial recoveries prevent a simple linear reading?
- Where are the source boundaries uncertain, gradual, or contested?

A label that answers none of these questions does not preserve duration. It only marks that duration once existed.

```text
extended label
≠ retained temporal architecture

temporal architecture retained
→ sequence, phase relations, and uncertainty remain inspectable
```

#### Temporal compression versus punctualization

Target-level compression is legitimate when it reduces representational density while retaining the source relations that carry the target claim. Punctualization occurs when the compressed representation changes the type of temporal relation being asserted—for example, when a distributed development is treated as one instantaneous cause, one decision, one act, or one responsibility-bearing occurrence.

The distinction is:

| Target treatment | Duration status | Provisional interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| one target unit with source phases and sequence trace retained | internally extended | possible Macro-Event candidate |
| one target unit with some local timing backgrounded but recoverable | compressed but traceable | possible bounded claim |
| one date used as a conventional marker while extended source remains explicit | symbolic placement only | not necessarily punctualization |
| one date treated as the actual occurrence of the whole Trajectory | duration erased | punctualization risk |
| one act or actor made to carry the distributed process | duration and role structure collapsed | inadmissible pressure |

A conventional date may orient a wider account, but it cannot replace the source interval. If the date becomes the sole event identity, the projection has crossed from compression into type-distorting punctualization.

#### Sequence dependence

Internal sequence matters when changing the order of phases would change the source's target-level function. For example, stabilization followed by breakdown may structure a wider Path differently from breakdown followed by stabilization, even if the same local elements are present. A Macro-Event claim must therefore not reduce source content to an unordered inventory.

```text
same phases
+ different order
→ potentially different target transition function
```

This is a temporal traceability requirement, not a claim that every order difference is praxeologically relevant. Where order variation produces no warranted target difference, the sequence may be backgrounded for the bounded claim. Where the sources do not support the ordering, uncertainty must remain visible rather than being repaired through retrospective narrative.

#### Phase relations and open boundaries

Some Trajectories have gradual starts, overlapping phases, or unresolved endings. Internal-duration preservation does not require false temporal precision. The record may retain intervals, transition zones, and competing boundaries:

```yaml
boundary_precision:
  start: bounded_interval_or_contested_transition
  end: bounded_interval_or_open_continuation
  overlapping_phases: allowed_if_declared
  retrospective_sharpening: prohibited_without_support
```

An open or contested boundary may weaken the Macro-Event claim, but it does not automatically invalidate the source Trajectory. The effect depends on whether the disputed boundary is constitutive for the proposed target transition.

#### Temporal evidence layers

Duration claims must keep evidence temporally typed:

```text
source-period evidence
→ supports phase sequence and internal transitions

target-period evidence
→ supports the wider before/after relation

later retrospective evidence
→ may support rival periodization or subsequent interpretation
→ may not silently replace source-period sequence support
```

The distinction protects against a common retrospective error: later actors or analysts narrate a long development as one decisive moment, and that later narration is then treated as evidence that the development was always one Macro-Event. Retrospective compression may itself be a relevant target-context fact, but it is not source-side proof of internal temporal structure.

#### Duration and the same-source `B/C` contrast

The same source duration must remain fixed across target Paths `B` and `C`. Context sensitivity permits the target function to differ; it does not permit the analyst to shorten, lengthen, or reorder the source until it fits the preferred target result.

```text
M in B
+ source interval period_A
→ Macro-Event function candidate

M in C
+ same source interval period_A
→ no distinct Macro-Event function remains possible
```

If `B` requires phases `M1–M3` while `C` uses only `M2`, the comparison no longer tests the same source object. It must either declare a new source object and return to PATH or treat `M2` as an alternative source candidate for later testing.

<a id="32-6-internal-heterogeneity"></a>

### 32.6 Internal Heterogeneity

A Trajectory may be used as one Macro-Event candidate without being internally homogeneous. The target function can compress plurality into one bounded analytical position only if the plurality relevant to the claim remains traceable. Heterogeneity is not noise to be removed after the target label has been selected. It is part of the source object and may support, limit, divide, or defeat the proposed unitary function.

#### Operator-load variation across phases

Internal heterogeneity may include different claim-relevant occurrence loads across phases, roles, and subpaths: one phase may carry structured `Λ` non-occurrence, another stronger `Ω` exposure or obligation gradients, a later phase `Ψ` continuity or release, or recurrent `Α` stabilization interrupted by supported `Φ`, `Χ`, or `Σ` changes. The combination remains source-specific and must not be inferred from stylistic prominence.

If target-level compression obscures a material occurrence relation or phase difference, that loss is placed under `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, or `irrecoverable` as appropriate. No phase profile becomes a new type.

#### C32-HETEROGENEITY-01 — relevant reversals, parallel subpaths, roles, costs, asymmetries, Non-Events, and consequences must remain traceable

The heterogeneity inventory must include, where supported and relevant:

```yaml
internal_heterogeneity:
  phases:
    - phase_M1
    - phase_M2
    - phase_M3
  reversals: declared
  parallel_subpaths: declared
  local_differences: declared
  roles_and_positions: declared
  cost_distributions: declared
  asymmetry_distributions: declared
  non_events_and_delays: declared
  binding_changes: declared
  heterogeneous_consequences: declared
  conflicting_interpretations: retained_where_source_supported
  unresolved_internal_relations: retained
```

The inventory is not an instruction to maximize detail. Each retained difference must possess praxeological purchase for the source or target claim. The requirement is that target compression cannot erase a difference whose removal changes the interpretation of who could act, which alternatives were available, where costs accumulated, how the sequence unfolded, or whether one transition-bearing unit remains warranted.

#### Heterogeneity versus fragmentation

Internal variation does not automatically defeat a Macro-Event function. A source can contain conflict, reversal, and uneven effects while still performing one bounded transition role in a wider Path. Conversely, formal unity of period, institution, or label does not establish a unitary function.

```text
internal heterogeneity
≠ automatic fragmentation

period unity
≠ functional unity
```

The question is relational: can the source's heterogeneous elements be reconstructed as jointly carrying, modulating, limiting, or contesting one target transition claim? Where the relations cannot be responsibly stated, the claim may require reduction, an alternative source object, or Non-Capture.

#### Load-bearing and modulating phases

#### C32-PHASELOAD-01 — distinguish load-bearing from modulating phases or relations without turning the distinction into a score

WP2 introduces a qualitative phase-load distinction:

```yaml
phase_load:
  load_bearing:
    - phase_or_relation_whose_material_change_would_alter_the_candidate_function
  modulating:
    - phase_or_relation_that_changes_scope_direction_intensity_or_distribution_without_alone_carrying_the_function
  backgrounded:
    - source_supported_structure_not_required_for_the_bounded_target_claim_but_retained_in_source_trace
  uncertain:
    - phase_or_relation_whose_role_cannot_yet_be_responsibly_discriminated
```

A load-bearing phase or relation is constitutive for the candidate target function: its material removal, reversal, or replacement would be expected to alter the Macro-Event claim. A modulating phase shapes how the transition is distributed, interpreted, delayed, accelerated, or bounded without independently establishing the function. Backgrounded structure remains part of the source but is not required for this claim. Uncertain structure must remain uncertain.

These positions are not numerical weights, causal shares, importance rankings, or fixed properties of the source. A phase may be load-bearing in target Path `B`, modulating in another target context, and irrelevant to a third bounded claim. The classification therefore remains target-relative and revisable.

```text
load-bearing
≠ most dramatic
≠ most cited
≠ morally central
≠ causally sufficient

modulating
≠ dispensable in every context
```

At WP2, the chapter establishes the architecture for this distinction. It does not select actual phase statuses for the stable pressure object.

#### Role and cost heterogeneity

A Macro-Event claim must resist the tendency to compress distributed processes into one generalized actor or one uniform cost. The source may have different meanings for different roles, institutions, or affected positions. The target function must therefore declare its role scope.

#### C32-ROLESCOPE-01 — bound the candidate function by target roles, time, level, and praxis dimensions

```yaml
macro_event_validity_scope:
  target_path: wider_path_B_or_C
  target_frame: declared
  target_roles: declared
  relative_level: declared
  target_granularity: declared
  time_window: declared
  praxis_dimensions:
    - action_corridors
    - roles
    - costs
    - asymmetries
    - expectations
    - bindings
    - alternatives
  excluded_roles_or_domains: declared
  generalization_prohibited: true
```

A source may mark a transition for one institutional role while remaining background for another. It may change exit costs without changing formal rules, or alter expectations without producing the same consequences across all positions. A valid claim may therefore be asymmetric and partial. It must not be generalized beyond the roles, time window, relative level, and dimensions for which the trace exists.

#### Non-Events and delayed effects

The internal heterogeneity of a Trajectory includes structured absences, delayed decisions, blocked transitions, and unrealized alternatives where they carry source or target load. A Macro-Event account that retains only positive occurrences may falsely present an extended process as a sequence of decisive acts.

```text
positive events only
→ possible erasure of Λ-load

retained Non-Events and delays
→ target transition may remain historically extended and structurally distributed
```

Not every information gap is a Non-Event. The expectation frame and praxeological effect must remain source-supported. Uncertain missing information belongs under `loss.uncertain`, not under an invented Λ-claim.

#### Conflicting local consequences

One source Trajectory may open possibilities for some roles while closing them for others; reduce one cost while increasing another; stabilize one branch while destabilizing another. A unitary Macro-Event function may still be possible if it states the transition at an adequately bounded target level and preserves these differences in the trace. It fails if “the transition” implies homogeneous consequences that the source does not support.

The target statement should therefore distinguish:

- the common target-level relation, if one exists;
- divergent role-specific effects;
- countervailing or delayed effects;
- consequences excluded from the bounded claim;
- unresolved differences that prevent stronger synthesis.

#### Internal reversal pressure

An internal reversal does not automatically cancel earlier phases. It may:

- undo a local effect;
- redirect the source while retaining accumulated costs;
- reopen a previously closed alternative;
- produce a later state unlike the one suggested by the initial direction;
- show that no unitary target function survives.

The relevant test is not “Did the source contain reversals?” but “Does the proposed target function remain discriminating when the reversals are retained?” WP2 preserves this pressure without performing the full counterfactual adjudication reserved for §32.10.

#### C32-COMPRESSION-01 — disclose target foregrounding and exact five-part Loss without equating compression with punctualization

The projection must state what becomes more visible at the target level and record the consequences of compression through the unchanged canonical Loss schema:

```yaml
projection_visibility:
  foregrounded:
    - proposed_transition_bearing_relation_in_wider_path
  backgrounded:
    - source_details_not_required_for_the_bounded_target_claim_but_remaining_traceable

loss:
  preserved:
    - origin_type
    - source_reference
    - internal_duration
    - constitutive_phase_relations
    - relevant_heterogeneity
    - role_cost_and_asymmetry_variation
  compressed:
    - local_timing_density
    - repeated_similar_occurrences
    - non_constitutive_detail
  excluded:
    - declared_out_of_scope_material
  uncertain:
    - unresolved_boundaries_phase_roles_and_relations
  irrecoverable:
    - source_information_not_reconstructable_after_target_compression_if_any
```

`foregrounded` and `backgrounded` describe visibility. They are not additional Loss fields. The five canonical fields remain exact and complete.

```text
foregrounded/backgrounded
≠ preserved/compressed/excluded/uncertain/irrecoverable
```

Compression is not punctualization merely because detail is reduced. It becomes punctualization when internal duration, sequence, roles, costs, or heterogeneous relations are represented as if they never existed, occurred simultaneously, or belonged to one act. Conversely, a long descriptive account may still punctualize if it assigns the whole process to one decisive instant or actor.

#### Irrecoverable loss threshold

Some source information may already be unavailable because the original PATH composition compressed it or sources never supported it. RETYPE must inherit that loss rather than implying that Macro-Event projection can reconstruct what was never preserved. New irrecoverable loss introduced by the projection requires special scrutiny. If it removes the relations needed to inspect duration, heterogeneity, or target transition work, the claim may exceed the Traceability Ceiling.

```text
inherited irrecoverable loss
→ retained and disclosed

projection-introduced irrecoverable loss
+ constitutive trace destroyed
→ Claim Reduction, Failure, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture pressure
```

WP2 does not yet select among those routes. It establishes the conditions under which WP3 must test them.

<a id="32-7-event-function"></a>

### 32.7 Event Function

A Macro-Event function is warranted only if the origin-typed Trajectory performs concrete transition work in the wider target Path. The function is not the assertion that the source was long, important, dramatic, canonical, or widely named. It is a bounded claim that the wider target reconstruction changes when the source is treated as one internally extended transition-bearing unit.

#### PMS-reconstructible target transition difference

The wider-Path difference should be stated through reconstructible praxis changes rather than generic historical importance. Depending on the target Record, `B_pre` and `B_post` may differ in Frame or role-space organization, expectation and Non-Event conditions, action-corridor availability, `Ω` exposure or cost gradients, `Ψ` commitment or exit conditions, or temporal reachability and persistence.

These target-side differences support a bounded transition-function claim; they do not prove that the source Trajectory caused every change.

#### C32-EVENTFUNC-01 — require a concrete praxeological transition difference in the wider Path

The event-function claim must specify at least one source-traceable difference concerning:

- available, closed, or newly costly possibilities;
- segmentation of the wider Path into praxeologically distinct before/after configurations;
- role, asymmetry, cost, expectation, or binding structure;
- branch reachability or alternative visibility;
- persistence, delay, or irreversibility in later continuation;
- the relation between preceding developments and subsequent transitions.

A minimal declaration is:

```yaml
event_function_candidate:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_M
  origin_type: Trajectory
  target_path: wider_path_B
  function: bounded_macro_event_function
  proposed_transition_difference:
    before_configuration: B_pre
    after_configuration: B_post
    changed_praxis_dimensions: declared
    source_trace:
      load_bearing_phases_or_relations: candidate_not_yet_adjudicated
      modulating_phases_or_relations: candidate_not_yet_adjudicated
    adjacent_conditions: retained
  target_C_contrast:
    target_path: wider_path_C
    proposed_result_possibility: no_distinct_macro_event_function
  result_status: unadjudicated
```

The transition difference must be more than a date boundary. `B_pre` and `B_post` must differ in a way relevant to praxis. If the wider Path is reconstructed identically without the Macro-Event function, the candidate may fall below the Relevance Floor.

#### Event function versus descriptive period compression

A useful period summary may be legitimate even when no Macro-Event function is warranted. Descriptive compression can organize material for navigation, teaching, or presentation without claiming a distinct transition-bearing role. RETYPE must preserve this alternative.

```text
useful historical summary
≠ Macro-Event function

descriptive period compression only
→ possible bounded non-projection result
```

The distinction is important because analytical convenience is not praxeological purchase. A label may improve readability while leaving the wider Path unchanged. Such a result need not be treated as failure of the source Trajectory or of the period summary. It means the stronger functional projection is not warranted.

#### Transition-bearing relation

The proposed function may take different forms, including:

- **segmentation function:** the source makes a before/after distinction in the target Path reconstructively necessary;
- **possibility-shift function:** the source changes which branches or action corridors remain reachable;
- **constraint function:** the source introduces persistent costs, bindings, or asymmetries that structure later continuation;
- **reorientation function:** the source changes the direction or interpretation of subsequent transitions;
- **threshold function:** cumulative source relations cross a bounded target threshold without implying one punctual crossing instant.

These are open descriptions, not a closed taxonomy or new Output Classes. More than one may apply, and a rival function may better fit the same source and target. The chapter must not convert family vocabulary into a new formal inventory.

#### Event function and target-level unity

The source can perform one target function even when no single source phase carries it alone. Target-level unity may emerge from relations among phases, repeated transitions, accumulated Non-Events, or sedimented asymmetries. Conversely, a highly salient phase may fail to carry the wider function if removing it leaves the target transition unchanged.

```text
one target function
≠ one source phase necessarily sufficient

one salient phase
≠ load-bearing phase automatically
```

This is why the event-function claim depends on the phase-load architecture and later Counterfactual Macro-Event Test. WP2 identifies the required relation but does not yet execute phase-removal or boundary-variation pressure.

#### Causal boundary

#### C32-CAUSALBOUND-01 — transition-bearing function must not be inflated into causal turning-point proof, necessity, or explanatory monopoly

A supported Macro-Event function would establish a bounded reconstructive relation, not a complete causal theory. The claim may state that the source structures, marks, or carries a transition in the wider Path. It may not infer without independent evidence that:

- the source alone caused `B_post`;
- the later configuration could not have occurred otherwise;
- every constituent phase was necessary;
- no present or parallel condition contributed;
- the source explains all roles and consequences equally;
- the target Path has one uniquely correct periodization;
- the source possesses normative, political, legal, or historical legitimacy.

```text
transition-bearing target function
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ causal necessity
≠ counterfactual uniqueness
≠ explanatory monopoly
≠ legitimacy verdict
```

Causal evidence may exist in a domain-specific inquiry, but STRATA does not generate it from formal projection. The `PROJECT_AS` Record can preserve causal uncertainty and adjacent conditions; it cannot decide empirical causation automatically.

#### Present and adjacent condition pressure

The wider target difference may also be carried by developments outside the source Trajectory: contemporaneous policies, external shocks, role changes, institutional restructuring, resource shifts, or later acts. The Macro-Event claim must retain these adjacent conditions rather than absorbing them into `M`.

A bounded projection may still be admissible when the source is one among several transition-bearing structures. The claim then states the source's contextual function without assigning explanatory monopoly. Where the target difference cannot be separated from adjacent conditions at the available evidence level, `partially_admissible`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, or `non_capture` may later become relevant.

#### Function sensitivity without result selection

WP2 requires that the candidate function be formulated so it can be defeated. A material change in load-bearing phase relations, source boundaries, wider target Path, target roles, or adjacent conditions must be capable of changing the result. A function that survives all such changes unchanged is likely too elastic.

At this stage, however, the chapter does not perform the full variation test. It records the pending pressure:

```yaml
pending_wp3_pressure:
  phase_removal: required
  phase_reversal: required_where_relevant
  boundary_variation: required
  alternative_source_object: required
  target_B_C_variation: required
  adjacent_condition_pressure: required
  causal_proof_inference: prohibited
```

#### Updated family packet after WP2

WP2 hands forward the following architecture:

```yaml
chapter_32_wp2_family_packet:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_M
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_M
    temporal_scope: period_A
    internal_duration:
      sequence_and_phase_relations: retained
      internal_turning_points: retained
      delays_non_events_and_reversals: retained_where_constitutive
      uncertainty: retained
    internal_heterogeneity:
      parallel_subpaths: retained
      role_cost_asymmetry_variation: retained
      heterogeneous_consequences: retained
      unresolved_relations: retained
    inherited_loss: retained_exactly_five_part

  projection_visibility:
    foregrounded: proposed_transition_bearing_relation
    backgrounded: non_constitutive_source_detail_retained_in_trace

  projection_loss:
    preserved: declared
    compressed: declared
    excluded: declared
    uncertain: declared
    irrecoverable: declared

  phase_load_architecture:
    load_bearing: category_declared_status_not_selected
    modulating: category_declared_status_not_selected
    backgrounded: category_declared_status_not_selected
    uncertain: category_declared_status_not_selected

  target_B:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_B
    proposed_function: bounded_macro_event_function
    proposed_transition_difference: specified_but_not_yet_adjudicated
    role_time_level_and_praxis_scope: declared
    adjacent_conditions: retained

  target_C:
    object_or_scene: wider_path_C
    proposed_result_possibility: no_distinct_macro_event_function

  causal_controls:
    causal_sufficiency: not_claimed
    causal_necessity: not_claimed
    explanatory_monopoly: not_claimed
    legitimacy_or_authority: not_claimed

  pending:
    punctualization_error_test: WP3
    compose_project_as_separation_audit: WP3
    counterfactual_macro_event_test: WP3
    rival_periodization_and_alternative_source_test: WP3
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

The packet defines what must be preserved and tested. It does not claim that the pressure object satisfies those conditions.

#### C32-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives duration, heterogeneity, compression, scope, and event-function architecture without final adjudication

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 32 WP2 complete
→ Chapter 32 WP3 §§32.8–32.11
→ Punctualization Error
→ Macro-Event versus COMPOSE
→ Counterfactual Macro-Event Test
→ Failed Macro-Event Projection
```

WP3 receives:

- the unchanged PATH-established source Trajectory `M` and same-source `B/C` contrast;
- explicit internal duration, sequence, phase relations, turning points, delays, Non-Events, reversals, and temporal uncertainty;
- explicit heterogeneity across subpaths, roles, costs, asymmetries, bindings, and consequences;
- a qualitative load-bearing/modulating/backgrounded/uncertain phase architecture without selected statuses;
- target-level foregrounding and backgrounding declarations;
- exact five-part Loss disclosure with no new Loss fields;
- a bounded role-, time-, level-, and praxis-dimension scope;
- a concrete but unadjudicated transition difference in wider Path `B`;
- retained descriptive-compression and no-projection alternatives;
- explicit causal, necessity, monopoly, legitimacy, and authority prohibitions;
- pending phase, boundary, source, target, and adjacent-condition variation pressure.

WP3 does not inherit:

- proof that target compression actually preserves every constitutive phase;
- selected load-bearing or modulating phases for `M`;
- proof that the wider Path gains a distinct transition relation;
- proof that `B_pre` and `B_post` differ because of `M`;
- a no-projection result for `C`;
- completed Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- an adjudicated rival periodization or preferred alternative source object;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-32 or RETYPE lock;
- any person, normative, political, legal, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority result.

<a id="chapter-32-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 32.5–32.7 establish the preservation requirements for internal duration and sequence, the retention of relevant heterogeneity, a qualitative phase-load architecture, role/time/level/praxis boundedness, visibility versus canonical Loss discipline, a concrete transition-bearing event-function grammar, descriptive-compression and no-projection alternatives, and strict causal/necessity/monopoly limits.

```text
internal duration architecture declared
+ heterogeneity architecture declared
+ phase-load categories separated
+ exact five-part Loss retained
+ target transition difference made testable
+ causal inflation prohibited
→ WP2 complete
```

This result does not establish that duration and heterogeneity are substantively preserved in the pressure object, select load-bearing phases, prove a transition gain in `B`, establish no-projection in `C`, identify a causal turning point, complete the `COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` chain audit, perform Counterfactual Sensitivity, choose a rival periodization or alternative source object, select a local operation result or canonical Output Class, lock Chapter 32 or RETYPE, or increase authority.

<a id="32-8-punctualization-error"></a>

### 32.8 Punctualization Error

A Macro-Event projection compresses a temporally extended source into one bounded target-level function. That compression becomes a punctualization error when the representation ceases to preserve the source Trajectory's constitutive duration, phase relations, reversals, Non-Events, or heterogeneous load and instead treats the source as though it were one instantaneous Event occurrence. The error is not produced merely by using one target element, one label, one timeline marker, or one Record entry. It arises when target unity is allowed to overwrite source temporality or to support claims that only a punctual origin object could carry.

```text
one target-level functional unit
≠ one source instant
≠ punctual Event occurrence
≠ single act
≠ single responsible agent
```

#### Operator-relation loss under punctualization

Punctualization also occurs when the target marker erases source `Θ` order and duration, converts repeated or distributed `Λ`, `Ω`, `Α`, or `Ψ` occurrence load into one undifferentiated event point, or hides phase variation that is material to the target claim. Event remains an analytical object class, not a newly introduced PMS operator.

#### C32-PUNCTERROR-01 — punctualization error is represented through Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, and exact five-part Loss consequences

A punctualization error is present when one or more of the following occur:

- the source origin type `Trajectory` is replaced by `Event`;
- a multi-phase source is narrated as one instantaneous intervention;
- internal sequence is omitted even though order changes the target function;
- relevant delays, Non-Events, reversals, or branch closures disappear;
- heterogeneous roles, costs, asymmetries, or bindings are assigned to one undifferentiated occurrence;
- a convenient target date is presented as the source's unique real boundary;
- a target-level before/after distinction is converted into a punctual causal turning point;
- responsibility is concentrated on one actor because the target representation uses one event-like unit;
- the resulting projection cannot be traced back to the source Trajectory without reconstructing information the projection declared irrelevant.

The error must be recorded through existing STRATA controls rather than through a new error class or Loss field:

```yaml
punctualization_pressure:
  origin_type_preserved: required
  source_duration_traceable: required
  constitutive_phase_relations_traceable: required
  internal_heterogeneity_traceable: required
  punctual_causal_inference: prohibited
  single_agent_inference: prohibited
  loss:
    preserved: declared
    compressed: declared
    excluded: declared
    uncertain: declared
    irrecoverable: declared
  possible_consequences:
    - claim_reduction_required
    - mandatory_stop
    - failed_transformation
    - non_capture
```

This is a routing discipline, not an automatic mapping. Minor target compression may be corrected by narrowing the claim or restoring trace. A projection requires Mandatory Stop when continued use depends on erased constitutive temporal or heterogeneous structure. Failure applies when the claimed Macro-Event function cannot be preserved without the punctualization. Non-Capture remains possible when neither a unitary Macro-Event function nor a responsible alternative compression can retain the source load.

#### Punctual representation versus punctual origin

A diagram, table, index, reader, or model may display the Macro-Event candidate as one node or record. Such display compression does not itself determine the origin type. The representation remains admissible only when it points to the extended source record and does not invite stronger punctual claims.

```text
one visual node
+ explicit source-Trajectory trace
+ retained internal temporal architecture
→ possible bounded representation

one visual node
+ erased duration and heterogeneity
+ punctual causal or responsibility inference
→ punctualization error
```

Machine-readable consistency cannot decide whether the retained trace is substantively sufficient. It may verify the presence of duration, phase, heterogeneity, and Loss declarations; the historical and praxeological adequacy of those declarations remains source-grounded and human-assessed.

#### Boundary concentration

A broad Trajectory may contain one especially salient phase, threshold, or decision. That phase may be load-bearing, modulating, or merely visible. Salience does not permit the analyst to collapse the entire source into the phase without a new PATH claim and a comparison of source alternatives.

If a narrower Transition cluster or extended Event carries the target function more precisely, the correct response is not to retain the broad Trajectory label while tacitly using the narrower source. The analyst must declare the alternative source object and test it separately.

```text
broad Trajectory named
+ narrow phase actually carries all target work
→ alternative-source pressure
≠ broad source automatically validated
```

<a id="32-9-macro-event-versus-compose"></a>

### 32.9 Macro-Event versus COMPOSE

The source Trajectory ordinarily enters Chapter 32 as the result of a prior PATH `COMPOSE` occurrence. The Macro-Event function, by contrast, can arise only through a distinct `PROJECT_AS` occurrence in a wider target Path or temporal Frame. The two operations may form a chain, but they do not merge into one transformation and do not inherit one another's result.

```text
COMPOSE
many or sequential source structures
→ Trajectory M

PROJECT_AS
Trajectory M with retained origin type
→ bounded Macro-Event function in B
```

#### Compact two-operation visibility

```text
operator-typed Configurations, Events, Non-Events, and relations
→ COMPOSE
→ origin-typed Trajectory M

Trajectory M
→ PROJECT_AS
→ bounded Macro-Event function in wider Path B
```

The first occurrence forms the source object. The second tests its contextual function. Each keeps its own Record, Loss, and possible result.

#### C32-COMPOSESEP-01 — `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` require separate occurrences, Records, Loss profiles, and results

The minimum chain declaration is:

```yaml
operation_chain:
  first_occurrence:
    operation: COMPOSE
    source_objects: declared_PATH_material
    target_object: documented_trajectory_M
    result: retained_from_PATH
    loss: retained_from_PATH
  second_occurrence:
    operation: PROJECT_AS
    source_object: documented_trajectory_M
    origin_type: Trajectory
    target_context: wider_path_B
    target_function: bounded_macro_event_function_candidate
    result: not_selected
    loss: separately_declared_projection_loss
```

The chain imposes six non-inheritance rules:

1. an admissible `COMPOSE` result does not establish a Macro-Event function;
2. a failed `COMPOSE` source claim cannot be repaired by projection;
3. a failed Macro-Event projection does not automatically invalidate the prior Trajectory;
4. source-composition Loss remains inherited and visible during projection;
5. projection introduces a separate visibility and Loss profile;
6. a later operation cannot retroactively change the earlier operation's source selection, result, or Claim Ceiling.

```text
admissible Trajectory formation
≠ admissible Macro-Event projection

new transformation
= new testable claim
```

#### Operation-chain contamination

Operation-chain contamination occurs when an analyst:

- treats selection and ordering choices from `COMPOSE` as evidence for the target function;
- selects the `COMPOSE` interval because it produces the desired Macro-Event narrative;
- imports the later target Path into the earlier source formation without declaring retrospective reconstruction;
- uses the `PROJECT_AS` result to upgrade an uncertain or provisional Trajectory;
- combines inherited and projection-introduced Loss into one undifferentiated list;
- reports one chain-level success while one occurrence remains failed or underdetermined.

A chain-level summary may be useful, but every occurrence must remain individually inspectable. Integrated audit comes later and does not erase local results.

#### Source revision and new occurrence

Counterfactual pressure may reveal that `M` is too broad, too narrow, or incorrectly bounded. When the source object materially changes, the analysis must return to PATH and create a new `COMPOSE` claim before a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence begins.

```text
change of target function only
→ new PROJECT_AS claim

material change of source boundaries or constitutive phases
→ new PATH/COMPOSE claim
→ then new PROJECT_AS claim
```

This rule blocks a common hidden repair: changing the source interval inside the projection Record while preserving the appearance of one stable source.

<a id="32-10-counterfactual-macro-event-test"></a>

### 32.10 Counterfactual Macro-Event Test

The Counterfactual Macro-Event Test asks whether the proposed transition-bearing function is sensitive to material changes in the source phases, source boundaries, source-object choice, and wider target Path. It does not reconstruct an unrealized history as fact, prove causality, calculate a causal share, or identify one uniquely correct periodization. Its purpose is discriminative: a source-specific Macro-Event claim must be capable of changing when the structures said to carry that function materially change.

#### Occurrence-level phase variation

Where the source Record warrants the typing, counterfactual pressure may remove or reverse a phase whose `Λ`, `Ω`, `Α`, or `Ψ` load is claimed to carry the transition; alter a constitutive `Θ` order, delay, persistence, or duration relation; or test whether a major `Φ` recontextualization changes the defensible source boundary or target transition function. Operator typing comes from the source Record, not from the counterfactual design.

#### C32-CFTEST-01 — bounded phase, boundary, source, and target variation pressures the family claim without causal proof

The test contains four required routes:

| Variation route | Controlled question | Primary pressure |
|---|---|---|
| phase variation | Would removal, reversal, delay, or replacement of a proposed load-bearing phase alter the target transition difference? | tests phase constitution and anti-salience discipline |
| boundary variation | Would a materially earlier or later start/end change the function, Loss, or result? | tests periodization dependence |
| source-object variation | Would a narrower Path, Transition cluster, extended Event, or other composite carry the target work more precisely? | tests source precision |
| target variation | Does the same `M` perform a function in `B` but not in `C`, or does the claim remain unchanged across materially different wider Paths? | tests Contextual Boundedness |

A bounded plan for the stable pressure object is:

```yaml
counterfactual_macro_event_test_plan:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_M
  phase_variations:
    - remove_candidate_phase_M1
    - reverse_or_delay_candidate_phase_M2_where_supported
    - retain_phase_M3_but_change_adjacent_conditions
  boundary_variations:
    - earlier_start_candidate
    - later_start_candidate
    - earlier_end_candidate
    - later_end_candidate
  alternative_source_objects:
    - narrower_path_or_subpath
    - transition_cluster
    - extended_event_like_object
    - no_single_source_object
  target_variations:
    - same_source_in_wider_path_B
    - same_source_in_wider_path_C
  prohibited_inferences:
    - causal_sufficiency
    - causal_necessity
    - numerical_causal_share
    - unique_periodization
    - prediction
```

The plan does not assert that every listed variation is historically realizable or equally supported. Each variation must remain bounded by actual source evidence and the available alternative space. Unsupported counterfactual invention is not a valid test.

#### C32-BOUNDARYSENS-01 — rival periodizations remain visible and may revise the function, Loss, or result

Boundary sensitivity is not a defect by itself. Every historical source object has boundaries. The problem arises when small or poorly justified boundary changes produce radically different Macro-Event claims while the analysis still presents one periodization as self-evident.

For each serious rival periodization, the Record must ask:

- Does the source remain the same Trajectory or become a new object?
- Which constitutive phases enter or leave?
- Which inherited Loss changes?
- Does the wider before/after distinction remain?
- Does the proposed event function narrow, broaden, disappear, or change family?
- Does the target role/time/level scope change?
- Is the rival periodization better supported, merely equally admissible, or unresolved?

```text
rival periodization changes result
→ boundary-sensitive claim revision required

rival periodization cannot be discriminated
→ provisionality, Claim Reduction, Stop, or Non-Capture may be required
```

The test must not select the periodization that maximizes narrative drama, target fit, or apparent explanatory power. Boundary warrant remains prior to preferred outcome.

#### C32-ALTSOURCE-01 — narrower or different source objects are tested when they may carry the target function more precisely

A Macro-Event claim may fail because the target work is real but the selected source object is imprecise. The relevant source may instead be:

- one bounded subpath;
- a Transition cluster;
- an extended Event-like object;
- a specific institutional arrangement;
- a distributed composite spanning only some phases;
- or no single source object at the available evidence level.

An alternative-source test compares source precision, traceability, retained duration and heterogeneity, Loss, and target discrimination. It does not allow silent substitution.

```text
alternative source fits better
≠ original Trajectory retroactively retyped

better source candidate
→ new source claim where required
→ separate PROJECT_AS occurrence
```

#### Same-source `B/C` pressure

The `B/C` contrast is the strongest contextual check available within the family architecture. The same fixed source `M` must be tested against both wider target Paths.

For `B`, the candidate must show that treating `M` as one internally extended transition-bearing unit changes the reconstruction along declared praxis dimensions.

For `C`, the same source may remain historically relevant while present conditions, other developments, or the target Path's segmentation make a distinct Macro-Event function unnecessary.

```text
same M
+ different target Paths
+ different transition-bearing result
→ contextual boundedness may be supported

same M
+ materially different target Paths
+ identical broad Macro-Event label regardless of target structure
→ functional elasticity pressure
```

No result is selected here. The test architecture makes both a bounded positive result and a bounded no-projection result representable without fabricating either.

<a id="32-11-failed-macro-event-projection"></a>

### 32.11 Failed Macro-Event Projection

A failed Macro-Event projection means that the stronger target-function claim is not warranted for the declared source and target occurrence. It does not mean that the source Trajectory never existed, that the period lacks historical importance, that descriptive compression is useless, or that no narrower source could carry a transition function. Failure is local to the tested transformation and Claim Scope.

#### C32-NOPROJ-01 — period background, descriptive compression, and no-projection remain positive bounded possibilities

Before declaring Failure, the analysis must preserve weaker but legitimate outcomes:

- the Trajectory remains relevant background for the wider Path;
- the period label remains useful for navigation or teaching;
- the source supports a descriptive compression but no distinct event function;
- the target transition is carried by present or adjacent conditions;
- a rival periodization or alternative source object is more precise;
- the source-target relation remains underdetermined;
- the available evidence does not support one unitary target function.

```text
no distinct Macro-Event function
≠ no historical relevance
≠ source Trajectory invalid
≠ analytical work useless
```

No-projection is not a separate canonical Output Class. It must be mapped through the existing ten-class system according to warrant, provisionality, reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture conditions.

#### C32-FAILURE-01 — failed projection is represented without automatic source-Trajectory invalidation

A Macro-Event projection fails when one or more indispensable conditions cannot be met, for example:

- the source boundaries are outcome-selected or unsupported;
- internal duration or heterogeneity must be erased to sustain target unity;
- no concrete target transition difference is identifiable;
- the claimed function survives all material phase, boundary, source, and target variations unchanged;
- a narrower or different source object carries the target work while `M` contributes only background;
- `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` cannot be separated without circularity;
- relevant adjacent conditions are absorbed into the source;
- inherited or projection-introduced Loss destroys Reference or Functional Continuity;
- causal turning-point, necessity, legitimacy, or authority language is required to make the claim appear stronger than the evidence permits.

The local failure relation is:

```text
failed PROJECT_AS occurrence for M in B or C
≠ prior PATH Trajectory M automatically failed
≠ every future projection of M failed
≠ rival source object excluded
```

A failed source Trajectory would require a separate PATH audit. Chapter 32 may expose pressure on the source claim, but it may not rewrite the earlier result from inside `PROJECT_AS`.

#### Claim reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture

The routes remain distinct:

| Route | Family-specific condition |
|---|---|
| `claim_reduction_required` | a weaker descriptive or narrower transition claim remains supportable |
| `mandatory_stop` | continued projection would depend on unsupported boundaries, erased source structure, circular source selection, source/target evidence substitution, or prohibited causal/authority inference |
| `failed_transformation` | the declared Macro-Event function is contradicted or lacks indispensable warrant for this occurrence |
| `non_capture` | no responsible unitary function, periodization, or source-object choice captures the target relation without unacceptable loss or false precision |

Formal completeness, clean period labels, or a compelling narrative cannot compensate for missing PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, Functional Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, Counterfactual Sensitivity, or source support.

#### Adversarial family questions

Before WP4 may lock the family method, the integrated audit must be able to answer:

1. Can the source remain a Trajectory while functioning as one target element?
2. Is internal duration inspectable rather than merely asserted?
3. Are heterogeneous phases, roles, costs, and Non-Events retained?
4. Are `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` separate occurrences?
5. Would phase or boundary variation change the claim?
6. Is a narrower source more precise?
7. Does the same source discriminate between `B` and `C`?
8. Can no-projection, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture be reached without source invalidation?
9. Is causal, legitimacy, person, recommendation, sanction, and authority inflation prohibited?
10. Are exact five-part Loss and canonical inventories unchanged?

These questions define family-method adequacy. They do not adjudicate the pressure object.

#### Updated family packet after WP3

```yaml
chapter_32_wp3_family_packet:
  source:
    object: documented_trajectory_M
    origin_type: Trajectory
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_M
    source_boundaries: fixed_for_B_C_test
    inherited_loss: retained_exactly_five_part

  preservation:
    internal_duration: required_and_traceable
    internal_heterogeneity: required_and_traceable
    punctual_origin_identity: prohibited
    single_agent_inference: prohibited

  operation_chain:
    compose_occurrence: retained_separately
    project_as_occurrence: declared_separately
    records_loss_and_results: separate
    authority_or_result_inheritance: prohibited

  counterfactual_pressure:
    phase_variation: required
    boundary_variation: required
    alternative_source_test: required
    same_source_B_C_variation: required
    adjacent_conditions: retained
    causal_proof: prohibited

  alternatives:
    rival_periodizations: retained
    narrower_path_or_subpath: retained
    transition_cluster: retained
    extended_event_like_object: retained
    descriptive_period_compression: retained
    background_relevance: retained
    no_projection: retained

  failure_routes:
    claim_reduction_required: representable
    mandatory_stop: representable
    failed_transformation: representable
    non_capture: representable
    source_trajectory_automatic_invalidation: prohibited

  anchor_status:
    macro_event_function_in_B: not_established
    no_projection_in_C: not_established
    phase_load_selection: not_performed
    preferred_periodization: not_selected
    preferred_alternative_source: not_selected
    local_project_as_result: not_selected
    canonical_output_class: not_selected
```

#### C32-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives a complete family architecture without RETYPE lock or case/test YAML production

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 32 WP3 complete
→ Chapter 32 WP4
→ integrated Contract and redundancy audit
→ twenty-four-duty audit
→ Reference and Formal-Model audit
→ provisional Chapter-32 lock decision
→ Chapter-33 Preparation handoff
```

WP4 receives:

- a fixed PATH-established source Trajectory and stable same-source `B/C` contrast;
- explicit source, target, boundary, duration, heterogeneity, phase-load, role/time/level, event-function, and exact five-part Loss architecture;
- punctualization-error criteria and consequences;
- separate `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` occurrences, Records, Loss, and results;
- phase, boundary, source-object, target, and adjacent-condition counterfactual pressure;
- rival periodizations and alternative source objects;
- descriptive compression, background relevance, no-projection, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture routes;
- source-Trajectory non-invalidation and causal/person/normative/legitimacy/recommendation/sanction/authority prohibitions.

WP4 does not inherit:

- an established Macro-Event function for `B`;
- an established no-projection result for `C`;
- selected load-bearing phases;
- a preferred periodization or source object;
- a completed empirical or causal test;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result or canonical Output Class for the anchor;
- permission to create test, case, or smoke YAML files during Block production;
- RETYPE lock or additional authority.

<a id="chapter-32-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 32.8–32.11 establish punctualization-error control, strict `COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` occurrence separation, bounded phase/boundary/source/target counterfactual pressure, rival periodization and alternative-source testing, no-projection and weaker-description routes, and local Failure/Stop/Non-Capture discipline without automatic source invalidation.

```text
punctualization error representable
+ operation chain separated
+ counterfactual pressure complete at method level
+ alternatives and failure routes retained
+ anchor result protected from fabrication
→ WP3 complete
```

This result does not establish a Macro-Event function in `B`, no-projection in `C`, phase load, preferred boundaries, preferred source object, causal turning point, local operation result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-32 lock, RETYPE lock, or additional authority.
<a id="chapter-32-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 32 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-32 Contract, all twenty-four family Pressure Duties, the complete §§32.1–32.11 architecture, prior PATH source entry, fixed source identity across the `B/C` contrast, independent source-, boundary-, target-placement-, and target-function warrants, wider target-Path declaration, anti-label and anti-outcome periodization, internal-duration preservation, internal-heterogeneity preservation, qualitative phase-load architecture, bounded role/time/level/praxis scope, target visibility separated from exact five-part Loss, concrete transition-bearing function, causal and authority boundaries, punctualization-error control, separate `COMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` occurrences, phase/boundary/source/target counterfactual pressure, rival periodizations and alternative source objects, descriptive compression, background relevance, no-projection, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, source-Trajectory non-invalidation, Claim Ceiling, authority prohibition, Reference and Formal-Model synchronization, schema validity, relative-link integrity, fingerprints, package integrity, ZIP CRC, and byte-identical roundtrip.

```text
complete trajectory-to-macro-event family method
+ source duration and heterogeneity remain traceable
+ periodization and source choice remain defeasible
+ COMPOSE and PROJECT_AS remain separate occurrences
+ no anchor result is fabricated
→ Chapter 32 provisionally locked
```

The Chapter-32 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The class applies to the chapter-level family specification under the still-open RETYPE Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not classify `documented_trajectory_M`, establish a Macro-Event function in `wider_path_B`, establish no-projection in `wider_path_C`, select load-bearing or modulating phases, select a preferred periodization or alternative source object, establish a Counterfactual Sensitivity description, choose a local `PROJECT_AS` result, map a canonical Output Class for the pressure object, prove a causal turning point, assign responsibility to one act or agent, or increase claim, application, governance, political, legal, normative, recommendation, sanction, or authority status.

All twenty-four Chapter-32 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose: eight in WP1, eight in WP2, and eight in WP3. The integrated boundary records their closure without repeating their identifiers or creating additional duties.

No standalone family Case, Transformation Record, test Record, Smoke Record, test YAML, case YAML, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, schema field, Macro-Event enum, periodization enum, phase-load score, alternative-source enum, canonical Loss field, causal classifier, semantic engine, Graph inference, Reader execution, person type, recommendation, sanction, legitimacy finding, or authority mechanism has been produced.

The integrated result preserves six independent positions:

```text
1. source-Trajectory identity and prior PATH/COMPOSE disposition
2. source-boundary and target-placement warrant
3. family-specific Macro-Event target-function warrant
4. Counterfactual Sensitivity, rival-periodization, and alternative-source status

6. canonical Output Class for that executed occurrence
```

Chapter 32 locks the family method, not a concrete occurrence. A valid Trajectory may remain historical background without a distinct Macro-Event function; a period label may remain descriptively useful without transition gain; a narrower source object may carry the target work more precisely; rival periodizations may remain unresolved; and internal duration or heterogeneity may make a unitary projection inadmissible.

```text
Chapter 32 provisionally locked
≠ source Trajectory converted into punctual Event
≠ Macro-Event function established for B
≠ no-projection established for C
≠ load-bearing phases selected
≠ preferred periodization selected
≠ causal turning point proven
≠ local PROJECT_AS result selected
≠ canonical Output Class selected for the anchor
≠ RETYPE provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, the Structure or Chapter Contract, source-Trajectory identity, source or projection boundaries, target placement, internal duration, internal heterogeneity, phase-load discrimination, target transition work, punctualization, `COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` separation, Counterfactual Sensitivity, rival periodization, alternative source objects, exact five-part Loss, source non-invalidation, Stop, Non-Capture, Claim Ceiling, authority prohibition, a Pressure Duty, Reference or Formal-Model binding, schema or package integrity, or a later Chapter-33–40/LIMITS/integrated-audit dependency. Preference for a cleaner epoch label, a single turning point, causal closure, one responsible agent, universal periodization, automatic source choice, person-level interpretation, or authority increase is insufficient.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 32 provisional lock
→ Chapter 33 Preparation Gate complete
→ Chapter 33 WP1
→ §§33.1–33.4
→ Basic Claim, Single Trajectory versus Recurrent Form,

```

Chapter 33 receives the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure, the locked non-punctual Macro-Event family, and the requirement to keep source formation, target function, comparison warrant, recurrence mechanism, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, result, and canonical mapping separate. It receives no permission to infer an attractor from one trajectory, use recurrence count as a universal threshold, convert a recurrent form into Α, treat retrospective similarity as mechanism, absorb opposite sequences under one elastic label, infer determinism or probabilities without evidence, type persons or groups, fabricate a positive or negative family result, create case/test/smoke YAMLs during Block drafting, or increase authority. Chapter 33 alone owns the bounded recurrent-trajectory-form-to-attractor-function family.
