<a id="chapter-33-recurrent-trajectory-form-as-attractor-function"></a>

## 33. Recurrent Trajectory Form as Attractor-Function

Chapter 33 applies the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure to a third principal RETYPE family: the bounded projection of a recurrent form reconstructed across multiple trajectories as a dynamic attractor-function in a declared later path field. Chapter 11 owns Trajectory. Chapters 12–17 own recurrence, sedimentation, path dependence, alternatives, Non-Events, and `COMPOSE`. Chapter 21 owns decomposition of Attractor-typed occurrences. Chapters 29–30 own functional projection and the generic operation procedure. This chapter therefore does not redefine Trajectory, recurrence, Attractor, Α, source formation, or `PROJECT_AS`. It asks a narrower family question:

> When may a recurrent form reconstructed across independently warranted and sufficiently comparable trajectories perform bounded path-shaping work in a later context, rather than remaining a retrospective motif, a shared-frame effect, a selection artifact, or an overelastic pattern label?

The source of the projection is not one Trajectory and not the PMS Attractor operator. It is a derived recurrent-form composite whose source basis must remain reconstructible across multiple trajectories. The target is not a new Attractor type. It is a bounded contextual function that concerns the stabilization of a transition form or sequence in a declared later path field.

```text
source trajectories T1, T2, T3 ...
+ declared comparability coordinates
+ discriminating recurrent-form source claim R
→ R may enter PROJECT_AS as a dynamic attractor-function candidate in D
```

The source trajectories may be numerous and still fail to form a responsible recurrent source object. They may share vocabulary, endpoints, public labels, visual sequence, or broad Frame while differing in constitutive transitions, roles, costs, Non-Events, temporal direction, evidence quality, or later path effect. Conversely, a small but unusually well-supported source set may justify only a bounded provisional recurrent-form claim. Count is therefore neither a proof nor a universal threshold.

The chapter uses a stable pressure architecture:

```text
source trajectories:
  documented_trajectory_T1
  documented_trajectory_T2
  documented_trajectory_T3

candidate source object:
  recurrent_trajectory_form_R
  origin type: derived recurrent-form composite

positive target candidate:
  later_path_field_D
  proposed function: bounded dynamic attractor-function

negative and rival pressure:
  later_path_field_E
  proposed possibility: no distinct attractor-function
```

The source trajectories, comparison coordinates, and candidate recurrent form must remain fixed across `D` and `E`. A material change in trajectory selection, boundaries, comparison dimensions, or constitutive form requires a new source claim before a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence.

WP1 establishes the bounded family claim, the recurrent-form source object, the separation between one Trajectory and a recurrent form, the dynamic target-function grammar, explicit comparability coordinates, and the context-sensitive Pattern Threshold. It does not yet adjudicate constitutive phases, reproduction mechanism, Attractor Load, later-path influence, dynamic-versus-static result, retrospective-similarity failure, sampling bias, Counterfactual Attractor testing, a local operation result, or a canonical Output Class. Those burdens remain open for §§33.5–33.10.

<a id="33-1-basic-claim"></a>

### 33.1 Basic Claim

A recurrent trajectory form may perform a bounded dynamic attractor-function when multiple independently warranted and sufficiently comparable trajectories support one discriminating source form and when that form is eligible to be tested for later path-shaping work in a declared target context. The family claim is stronger than repeated resemblance and weaker than a causal or probabilistic law. It does not state that later paths must repeat. It states only that a source-traceable recurrent form may alter the friction, expectation, role configuration, repair or exit pattern, or accessibility of later continuations within a bounded context.

#### Bounded dynamic Attractor work

The target function concerns a limited subset of Attractor-like work: stabilization of transition accessibility, friction, expectation, role recurrence, repair or exit structure, or continuation cost in the declared target context. It does not create an `Α` occurrence in the target or transfer the complete semantics and dependencies of `Α` to the recurrent-form Composite.

```text
bounded dynamic attractor-function
→ contextual transition-stabilizing work

bounded dynamic attractor-function
≠ source object becomes Α
≠ full Α semantics inherited
```

#### C33-BASIC-01 — the family claim is recurrent-form-to-dynamic-attractor-function projection

The minimal family form is:

```text
Within later path field D,
recurrent trajectory form R,
while retaining origin type derived recurrent-form composite,
performs bounded dynamic attractor-function F_dyn
with respect to declared continuation dimensions P_D.
```

Every position remains explicit:

| Position | Required declaration | Family-specific burden |
|---|---|---|
| source trajectories | independently warranted PATH objects | cannot be selected only because they fit the desired pattern |
| `R` | derived recurrent-form source object | must remain traceable to comparable trajectories and discriminating relations |
| origin type | derived recurrent-form composite | preserved through projection; not converted into Α |
| `D` | declared later path field | bounded by Frame, granularity, roles, time, level, evidence, and Claim Scope |
| `F_dyn` | dynamic attractor-function candidate | concerns stabilization of a transition form, not merely recurrence of an endpoint |
| `P_D` | continuation dimensions | friction, expectation, roles, Non-Events, repair/exit, alternative cost, or path accessibility |

The claim is relational and contextual. The candidate source does not possess an intrinsic attractor-function in all target fields. A recurrent source form may influence one later path field, remain only background in another, or fail to support any distinct function.

```text
recurrent source form exists
≠ attractor-function established

repetition observed
≠ later paths structured by the repetition

later similarity
≠ earlier recurrent form attracted the later path
```

The family claim also preserves three warrant layers:

1. **Trajectory warrant:** each source Trajectory is independently established through PATH.
2. **Recurrent-form warrant:** the source trajectories are comparable and support one discriminating recurrent source object.
3. **Target-function warrant:** the source form performs bounded later-path work in the declared target context.

No layer inherits the result of another. A valid set of Trajectories may fail to form one recurrent source. A valid recurrent source may fail to perform an attractor-function in `D`. A supported function in `D` would not imply the same function in `E`.

#### Family relevance versus family function

A recurrent form can be analytically relevant without functioning as an attractor. It may support comparison, classification, historical description, or a bounded motif. The stronger function claim requires later path difference:

```text
descriptive recurrent form
→ source trajectories share a warranted discriminating structure

bounded dynamic attractor-function
→ the recurrent form additionally alters later path accessibility,
   friction, expectation, role recurrence, repair/exit structure,
   or alternative cost in the declared target context
```

WP1 establishes the logical difference but does not treat the second condition as satisfied. It preserves both `D` and `E` as live targets and reserves mechanism and target work for WP2.

<a id="33-2-single-trajectory-versus-recurrent-form"></a>

### 33.2 Single Trajectory versus Recurrent Form

A Trajectory is one historically developed path object. A recurrent trajectory form is a derived source object reconstructed across multiple independently warranted trajectories under declared comparison coordinates. The recurrent form may preserve a sequence of constitutive transitions while permitting bounded variation in local phases, roles, duration, or surface expression. It is not the average of its source trajectories and not an abstract label detached from them.

#### Occurrence-level comparison coordinates

Where the source Trajectories are PMS-derived, comparison coordinates identify which claim-relevant operator occurrences or relations recur, vary, oppose, or remain unavailable across the linked PATH Records. Depending on the source material, these may include `□`-conditioned entry boundaries, `Λ` Non-Event relations, `Α` stabilization relations, `Ω` gradients, `Θ` order or persistence, `Ψ` continuity, and countervailing `Φ`, `Χ`, or `Σ` occurrences.

```text
same operator label across Trajectories
≠ comparable occurrence relation
```

Only source-supported relations enter; Chapter 33 does not re-prove the individual Trajectories.

#### C33-SOURCEFORM-01 — the source is a derived recurrent form reconstructed across multiple independently warranted trajectories

The minimum source packet is:

```yaml
source_trajectories:
  - object: documented_trajectory_T1
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_T1
    prior_disposition: retained
  - object: documented_trajectory_T2
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_T2
    prior_disposition: retained
  - object: documented_trajectory_T3
    source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_T3
    prior_disposition: retained

candidate_source:
  object: recurrent_trajectory_form_R
  origin_type: derived_recurrent_form_composite
  source_selection_basis: independently_warranted_before_target_function_adjudication
  source_formation_status: candidate_not_yet_adjudicated
  inherited_uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

The three trajectories are a pressure-object design, not a universal minimum. The source claim must disclose why these trajectories enter, why relevant divergent or truncated trajectories do or do not enter, and which comparison dimensions are available. A large source set cannot compensate for weak source records, incompatible Frames, missing negative cases, or an elastic pattern boundary.

Source formation is analytically prior to target-function adjudication. Later recurrence may test or revise the candidate source; it may not retroactively decide which earlier trajectories or phases count as the recurrent form.

```text
target fit selects earlier cases
→ retrospective source construction
→ source claim reduction or Stop required
```

The recurrent source object remains derived. Its identity depends on the declared source trajectories, their boundaries, their comparison relation, and the discriminating form reconstructed across them. Changing any of those materially creates a new source claim.

#### C33-SINGLESEP-01 — one Trajectory cannot be universalized as a recurrent form

One Trajectory may contain repeated phases or local cycles. That internal recurrence can be a legitimate PATH or SUB object, but it does not by itself establish a recurrent form across trajectories. The family distinction is:

```text
single Trajectory
→ one historically developed path object

recurrent trajectory form
→ derived source object across multiple independently warranted
  and sufficiently comparable Trajectories
```

A single path cannot supply the cross-trajectory discrimination needed to determine whether its form recurs, whether variation is allowable, whether opposite sequences are excluded, or whether later similarities arise independently. Treating one path as universal pattern would collapse source object and pattern claim.

The prohibition does not imply that every recurrent-form claim requires a fixed numerical minimum. It means that the cross-trajectory family cannot be established from one source object alone. Where source availability is extremely limited, the warranted result may be a provisional motif, a hypothesis for later testing, or Non-Capture—not a disguised universal recurrent form.

#### Independent source trajectories and non-inheritance

Each source Trajectory retains its own PATH status, uncertainty, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Ceiling. Inclusion in `R` does not overwrite the source records or transfer one Trajectory's support to another.

```text
T1 supported
≠ T2 supported by inheritance

R provisionally formed
≠ every local phase in T1–T3 made equivalent

R later fails projection
≠ T1–T3 automatically invalidated
```

The recurrent-form source must therefore be inspectable downward without pretending that `DECOMPOSE(R)` can recover all source information losslessly. Cross-trajectory composition introduces selection, compression, excluded divergence, uncertain correspondence, and potentially irrecoverable heterogeneity. Those positions remain in the canonical five-part Loss architecture and are not replaced by pattern vocabulary.

<a id="33-3-dynamic-attractor"></a>

### 33.3 Dynamic Attractor

An attractor-function may concern a relatively stable configuration or a recurrent transition form. Chapter 33 is concerned with the latter. A dynamic attractor-function is a bounded target-context function through which a recurrent source form makes a sequence or transition architecture easier to re-enter, reproduce, anticipate, or continue relative to declared alternatives.

A schematic candidate may be:

```text
configuration entry
→ intensification
→ binding or role pressure
→ withdrawal or failed repair
→ reframing
→ renewed entry
```

The sequence is illustrative, not canonical. A dynamic attractor-function may involve institutional routines, relational cycles, organizational escalation, recurring coordination failure, repeated repair attempts, or other temporal forms. The family requires source trace and later-path work, not adherence to one sequence template.

#### C33-DYNAMIC-01 — transition-form stabilization is distinct from static-state stabilization

The claim must identify which target function is being tested:

```text
static attractor-function
→ stabilizes a configuration, state range, or location in a path field

dynamic attractor-function
→ stabilizes a transition form, sequence, or recurrent movement pattern
```

A recurring endpoint does not establish a dynamic attractor. Similar movement does not establish a static attractor. Some cases may contain both, but each function requires a separate claim, source trace, target difference, Validity Scope, and result.

Dynamic stabilization does not mean exact repetition. It may preserve a relation among phases while local timing, actors, intensity, or surface form varies. Yet allowable variation must remain bounded. If reversal, omission, or substitution of every phase leaves the label unchanged, the candidate is not discriminating.

The target work remains provisional at WP1. Later sections must identify whether the recurrent form is associated with:

- lower friction for re-entering the known sequence;
- stabilized expectation of a particular transition;
- repeated assignment of roles or burdens;
- recurring Non-Events, delays, failed repairs, or exits;
- greater cost or reduced accessibility of alternative sequences;
- path continuation shaped by prior recurrence.

WP1 names these as eligible target dimensions. It does not establish their presence, mechanism, relative load, causal status, or probability.

#### C33-NOPRIMITIVE-01 — Α remains the PMS operator type and no deterministic or person/group type follows

The canonical type statement is:

```text
origin type: derived recurrent-form composite
projected target function: bounded dynamic attractor-function
```

It is not:

```text
R = Α
R becomes a new Attractor operator occurrence by label substitution
recurrent form = person type
recurrent form = group, culture, institution, or identity essence
attractor-function = deterministic law
```

Α retains its PMS Base meaning and dependencies. Chapter 33 neither decomposes Α nor creates a recurrence operator. Operator-like vocabulary specifies the kind of contextual work being tested. It does not confer primitive identity or additional authority.

A bounded dynamic attractor-function may alter friction, expectation, accessibility, or cost. It does not imply that later paths are determined, that recurrence probabilities are known, or that an actor, group, or institution possesses a fixed essence. Probability claims would require independent data, calibration, and claim authority not supplied by STRATA.

```text
path influence
≠ determinism
≠ prediction
≠ probability estimate
≠ diagnosis or person/group classification
```

<a id="33-4-pattern-threshold"></a>

### 33.4 Pattern Threshold

The Pattern Threshold is the bounded warrant required before a candidate recurrent form may enter attractor-function testing. It is not a universal recurrence count, a confidence score, a visual similarity threshold, or a formal schema gate. It is a relational threshold across source quality, comparability, constitutive recurrence, discrimination, temporal direction, mechanism eligibility, later-path effect eligibility, alternatives, and Claim Scope.

#### C33-COMPARABILITY-01 — comparability coordinates must be declared before recurrence is inferred

At minimum, the source trajectories must be compared across:

```yaml
comparison_coordinates:
  frame_relation: declared
  granularity_relation: declared
  role_relation: declared
  temporal_scope_relation: declared
  evidence_quality_relation: declared
  transition_structure_relation: declared
  operator_weighting_relation: declared_but_not_scored
  comparability_result: open
```

These coordinates do not require identity. They require a warranted relation. Different Frames may be comparable under a declared translation, or they may make recurrence claims inadmissible. Different granularities may be aligned, or they may hide decisive internal differences. Role positions may recur, invert, or become incomparable. Evidence quality may vary enough that apparent recurrence reflects documentation density rather than praxis structure.

Incomparability is a positive analytical result. It cannot be repaired by averaging, enlarging the label, removing deviant cases, or treating missing information as a Non-Event.

```text
shared vocabulary
≠ comparable Trajectories

similar diagram
≠ comparable transition structure

many cases
≠ comparability established
```

Relevant operator weighting may be declared qualitatively where it affects the transition form, but it is not scored and does not alter PMS Base. Chapter 35 owns the later systematic treatment of operator weighting. Chapter 33 uses only the minimum comparison required to prevent unlike trajectories from being collapsed into one form.

#### C33-PATTERNTHRESH-01 — the Pattern Threshold is context-sensitive and cannot be replaced by a universal count

A candidate recurrent source may cross the threshold only when the following remain jointly inspectable:

- multiple independently warranted source Trajectories;
- sufficient comparability across declared coordinates;
- a candidate set of constitutive transitions or relations;
- bounded allowable variation;
- explicit exclusion of incompatible or opposite sequences;
- temporal ordering that makes prior recurrence available to later paths;
- an eligible source-traceable reproduction or path-influence relation;
- a concrete later-path difference capable of later testing;
- relevant divergent, failed, truncated, or non-recurring trajectories;
- exact Claim Scope, uncertainty, Loss, Stop, and Non-Capture conditions.

WP1 establishes the threshold architecture but leaves several positions open for WP2–WP3. Constitutive phases have not been selected. A reproduction or path-influence mechanism has not been established. Later-path effect has not been demonstrated. Sampling and survivorship pressure have not been adjudicated. Counterfactual variation has not been performed.

Count remains evidentially relevant but never sufficient:

```text
three comparable trajectories
≠ universal threshold reached

one hundred similar records
≠ recurrent source established

small source set
→ at most bounded provisional claim where other warrants are unusually strong

large source set with selection or mechanism failure
→ claim reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture remains possible
```

The threshold must be capable of rejecting the candidate. If every deviation is declared allowable, every contrary case is excluded after the fact, and every target context receives the same function, the pattern has crossed neither the Relevance Floor nor the Traceability Ceiling responsibly. It has become a label without discriminating load.

#### Threshold and formal model boundary

The formal model may require declarations for source references, comparison coordinates, candidate recurrent form, target-function kind, threshold burdens, exact five-part Loss, alternatives, Stop, Non-Capture, and canonical-class membership. It may not determine substantive comparability, the correct pattern boundary, which phases are constitutive, whether recurrence reflects attraction or common conditions, whether later-path accessibility changed, or which result is correct.

```text
formally complete threshold packet
≠ Pattern Threshold substantively crossed
```

#### C33-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives a bounded source and threshold packet without constitutive-load or target-effect adjudication

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 33 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 33 WP2 §§33.5–33.7
→ Constitutive Repetition
→ Attractor Load
→ Dynamic versus Static Attractor-Function
```

WP2 receives:

- multiple independently warranted source Trajectories;
- one candidate recurrent-form source object with visible origin type;
- fixed source selection and inherited uncertainty/Loss;
- declared comparison coordinates and incomparability route;
- one dynamic attractor-function candidate and explicit static/dynamic distinction;
- a context-sensitive Pattern Threshold without universal count;
- preserved `D/E` positive/negative target pressure;
- prohibition of Α replacement, determinism, unsupported probability, person/group typing, and authority inheritance.

WP2 does not inherit:

- selected constitutive or variable phases;
- an established reproduction or path-influence mechanism;
- established Attractor Load;
- a demonstrated later-path difference in `D`;
- no-projection in `E`;
- a dynamic-versus-static result;
- a Counterfactual Attractor Test;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result or canonical Output Class;
- permission to create test, case, or smoke YAML files during Block drafting;
- Chapter-33 or RETYPE lock;
- additional application or governance authority.

<a id="chapter-33-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 33.1–33.4 establish the recurrent-form-to-dynamic-attractor-function family grammar, derived source formation across independently warranted trajectories, one-Trajectory/recurrent-form separation, explicit comparison coordinates, dynamic/static target distinction, Origin-Type Preservation, anti-determinism and anti-person/group-typing boundaries, and a context-sensitive non-numerical Pattern Threshold.

```text
family claim declared
+ recurrent-form source packet bounded
+ comparability architecture explicit
+ dynamic target-function grammar separated
+ Pattern Threshold remains defeasible
+ no result fabricated
→ WP1 complete
```

This result does not establish constitutive repetition, reproduction mechanism, Attractor Load, later-path influence, dynamic or static function in `D`, no-projection in `E`, retrospective-similarity failure, sampling-bias result, Counterfactual Sensitivity, local operation result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-33 lock, RETYPE lock, or additional authority.

<a id="33-5-constitutive-repetition"></a>

### 33.5 Constitutive Repetition

Constitutive repetition is the recurrence of a bounded relation among phases, transitions, roles, Non-Events, costs, and continuation effects across independently warranted and sufficiently comparable Trajectories. It does not require literal identity of episodes. It requires enough structural continuity that the candidate recurrent form remains discriminating under variation and can be broken by material countervariation.

```text
surface similarity
≠ constitutive repetition

identical vocabulary
≠ recurrent transition relation

bounded variation
≠ unlimited pattern elasticity
```

The source claim therefore asks not only whether similar phases appear, but whether their relation performs recurrent source work. A phase may be present in every source record yet remain background. A phase may vary in surface form while preserving a constitutive relation. Conversely, two sequences may contain the same named phases but differ in order, role allocation, cost distribution, or continuation effect strongly enough to be incompatible.

#### Occurrence relations within recurrence classes

The constitutive/variable/background/uncertain/incompatible classification also applies to concrete operator occurrence relations. A `Λ`, `Ω`, `Θ`, `Ψ`, or other relation may be constitutive for one recurrent-form claim, variable or background for another, and incompatible with a third. The classification concerns the occurrence relation, never the abstract operator type, and it preserves canonical dependencies even when practical load differs across phases or Trajectories.

#### C33-CONSTITUTIVE-01 — constitutive, variable, background, uncertain, and incompatible positions remain distinct

For every candidate recurrent form, the source trace must distinguish at least five qualitative positions:

| Position | Function in the recurrent-form claim | Required discipline |
|---|---|---|
| constitutive | removal, reversal, or material alteration would change or dissolve the candidate form | must be source-supported and counterfactually pressureable |
| variable | may change within declared bounds without dissolving the form | allowable variation must be stated rather than inferred after the fact |
| background | may recur but does not carry the form | cannot be promoted merely because it is salient or frequent |
| uncertain | correspondence, order, role, or evidence remains unresolved | uncertainty must remain visible and cannot be averaged away |
| incompatible | contradicts the candidate relation or belongs to a materially different form | cannot be absorbed by expanding the label |

These positions are target-independent source distinctions at the point of source formation. Their later functional relevance may differ by target context, but the target result may not retroactively decide which source phases were constitutive.

A candidate form can be represented schematically as a relation rather than a fixed string:

```text
entry condition
→ intensification relation
→ role or binding pressure
→ withdrawal, failed repair, or blocked transition
→ reframing relation
→ renewed entry or path continuation
```

The schema remains illustrative. In a given source, a delayed repair may occupy the place held by withdrawal in another, provided the declared relation and continuation effect remain comparable. If the order can be freely reversed, every phase omitted, opposite roles substituted, and incompatible outcomes included without changing the label, no constitutive form has been established.

#### Allowable variation and break conditions

Allowable variation must be specified before target-function adjudication. Relevant dimensions include:

- phase duration and spacing;
- surface event form;
- role occupant while retaining a comparable role relation;
- intensity and local cost magnitude;
- presence of optional intermediate phases;
- partial repair or partial exit;
- local reversals that do not dissolve the larger transition relation;
- source uncertainty that remains within the declared Claim Ceiling.

Break conditions include, where material to the candidate:

- reversal of the constitutive order;
- removal of the transition that links one phase to the next;
- replacement of a recurring role relation by a non-comparable one;
- disappearance of the Non-Event or cost structure claimed to carry continuation;
- a target-independent divergence in which the source follows an opposite continuation;
- evidence that the apparent recurrence is generated separately without preserved source relation.

A break condition does not invalidate the individual Trajectory. It pressures whether that Trajectory belongs to `R`, whether `R` must be narrowed, whether multiple recurrent forms are present, or whether no stable recurrent source object can be responsibly formed.

#### C33-REPMECH-01 — a source-traceable reproduction or later-path influence mechanism requires temporal direction

Repeated form is not yet attraction. The analysis must identify how earlier recurrence becomes available to structure later continuation. Eligible mechanisms include:

- stabilized expectations that make a known transition easier to anticipate or enact;
- learned or institutionalized role allocation;
- reduced coordination cost for reproducing the familiar sequence;
- repeated Non-Events that normalize delay, non-repair, or non-exit;
- accumulated Binding or Asymmetry that raises the cost of alternative sequences;
- preserved records, routines, procedures, or material arrangements that transmit the form;
- reactivation of residual structures when a comparable entry condition appears;
- path-dependent narrowing of accessible continuations.

The mechanism claim must specify:

```text
prior recurrent source feature
→ persistence, transmission, institutionalization, or reactivation relation
→ later path field in which the feature can perform work
```

Temporal availability is necessary. Simultaneous resemblance, independent responses to the same present condition, or later narrative alignment cannot by themselves establish reproduction. The mechanism may be distributed across roles, records, rules, material arrangements, and Non-Events; it need not be attributable to a single actor or conscious intention.

The mechanism remains a bounded reconstructive claim. STRATA does not infer physical causality, psychological learning, statistical transition probability, or necessity merely because temporal direction and source trace are present.

```text
source-traceable temporal pathway
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ exclusive mechanism
≠ quantified recurrence probability
```

#### C33-SOURCELOAD-01 — recurring source carriers remain visible without scoring or operator replacement

The recurrent-form source trace may include:

- recurring Frames that delimit comparable entry and continuation conditions;
- recurring role relations and responsibility distributions;
- recurring costs, exposure, and exit asymmetries;
- recurring Non-Events, delays, omissions, failed repairs, or blocked exits;
- recurring Bindings and continuity expectations;
- recurring transition timing or sequencing;
- relevant qualitative operator weighting where it affects the form.

These carriers must remain linked to source records and the exact five-part Loss architecture. They are not combined into an Attractor score, recurrence index, causal share, maturity measure, or person/group profile. Qualitative operator weighting does not reorder Δ–Ψ or create a new dependency. Chapter 35 owns the systematic treatment of weighting and modulation; Chapter 33 uses only the minimum source description required for the recurrent transition-form claim.

```text
many recurring carriers
≠ high Attractor Load automatically

operator weighting description
≠ operator score
≠ new operator type
```

A carrier may be constitutive in one recurrent-form claim, variable in another, and irrelevant in a third. Material reclassification requires a new or revised source claim; it cannot be silently changed to preserve target fit.

<a id="33-6-attractor-load"></a>

### 33.6 Attractor Load

Attractor Load is the bounded, target-relative work through which a recurrent source form changes the accessibility, friction, expectation, role distribution, cost, or continuation structure of a later declared path field. The term names functional load, not quantity. It does not imply a scalar magnitude, probability, force, essence, or causal monopoly.

```text
Attractor Load
→ source-traceable later-path work

Attractor Load
≠ recurrence count
≠ frequency score
≠ causal percentage
≠ person or group property
```

The load claim begins only after the recurrent-form source has been formed independently. It then asks what changes in `D` because `R` is available as a recurrent structure, relative to relevant present conditions, alternatives, and the contrast context `E`.

#### Source occurrence relation, derived form, and target function remain separate

```text
claim-relevant occurrence relations across T1 ... Tn
→ discriminating recurrent-form Composite R
→ documented persistence, transmission, institutionalization, or reactivation pathway
→ bounded later-path difference in D
```

The source carrier, the derived recurrent form `R`, and the target dynamic-attractor function are three distinct claim positions. They cannot collapse into one `Α` claim.

#### C33-ATTRLOAD-01 — bounded Attractor Load must be specified through concrete target work

Eligible load dimensions include:

1. **Friction load.** Re-entering the familiar sequence requires less coordination, interpretation, or negotiation than entering relevant alternatives.
2. **Expectation load.** Roles anticipate a known transition, making some responses more legible and others less available.
3. **Role-recurrence load.** Recurring role assignments, burdens, or initiative patterns shape who can act, wait, repair, withdraw, or bear cost.
4. **Non-Event load.** Repeated delay, non-decision, missing repair, or missing exit becomes part of the expected continuation structure.
5. **Repair/exit load.** Earlier recurrence alters the accessibility, timing, or cost of repair and exit.
6. **Alternative-cost load.** Competing sequences become more difficult, expensive, risky, or institutionally unavailable.
7. **Continuation load.** A known transition form becomes easier to resume after interruption or recontextualization.
8. **Visibility load.** The recurrent form structures which alternatives, warnings, or deviations become visible within the target Frame.

A responsible claim identifies the relevant dimension, target roles, time window, relative level, and source carriers. It does not merely state that the pattern is “strong,” “deep,” “dominant,” or “systemic.”

A minimal target trace is:

```text
recurrent source relation in R
→ named reproduction or path-influence pathway
→ concrete target difference in D
→ bounded roles, level, time, and praxis dimensions
→ rival and no-function pressure retained
```

The load may be partial, distributed, modulating, interrupted, or underdetermined. It need not be uniform across all roles. A sequence may be low-friction for one role and high-cost for another. A familiar transition may become easier to reproduce while also becoming more visible and therefore easier to interrupt. These mixed effects must remain available rather than being collapsed into one net score.

#### C33-LATERPATH-01 — the function requires a concrete difference in later path accessibility or continuation

The later-path claim must state what continuation becomes differently reachable, costly, expected, delayed, or visible. Examples include:

- a familiar escalation or repair sequence is entered with less coordination;
- a role assignment is repeated before alternatives are considered;
- delay or non-decision becomes the default continuation;
- an alternative exit remains formally available but becomes practically more costly;
- a recurrent reframing route redirects repair into renewed entry;
- interruption becomes more difficult because the sequence is institutionally embedded;
- a prior recurrent form is reactivated after a triggering configuration;
- the target path remains unchanged, supporting no distinct function.

The claim is insufficient if the source form merely describes earlier trajectories without changing the bounded reconstruction of later continuation. Historical recurrence may remain relevant, illustrative, or backgrounded without carrying a distinct attractor-function.

```text
recurrent source established
+ no discriminable later-path difference
→ no distinct Attractor Load warranted
```

For the stable pressure object, `D` and `E` remain equally important:

```text
R in D
→ dynamic attractor-function candidate where later accessibility changes

R in E
→ no distinct attractor-function remains possible where similar sequences
  arise independently or present conditions fully explain continuation
```

WP2 specifies the testable target differences. It does not establish them for either context.

#### Attractor Load and countervailing structure

Attractor Load may coexist with structures that interrupt, attenuate, or redirect recurrence. Relevant countervailing conditions include:

- new Frames or rules;
- changed role or resource distributions;
- credible repair;
- accessible exit;
- Χ-supported interruption;
- competing recurrent forms;
- external constraints;
- loss of the transmission medium;
- deliberate redesign or institutional change.

The presence of countervailing structure does not automatically eliminate the function. It may narrow its scope, reduce it to a modulating role, or make the result underdetermined. Conversely, the fact that a later sequence recurs despite countervailing structure does not prove that `R` caused it.

#### C33-SCOPE-01 — Attractor Load remains bounded by context, roles, level, time, and praxis dimensions

Every load claim must declare:

- target path field and Frame;
- source and target relative levels;
- target granularity;
- affected roles or role relations;
- temporal onset, persistence, interruption, and expiry conditions;
- affected praxis dimensions;
- relevant present and rival source conditions;
- Claim Ceiling and source uncertainty;
- Stop and Non-Capture conditions.

No function transfers automatically to another target field, role configuration, period, institution, or domain. A recurrence observed in one path field cannot be projected into a person, group, culture, or institution as a stable type.

The following inferences remain prohibited:

```text
bounded Attractor Load
→ universal path law
→ known recurrence probability
→ causal monopoly
→ diagnosis or person/group classification
→ recommendation, sanction, legitimacy, or authority
```

Probability claims require independently appropriate data, sampling, calibration, and claim authority. STRATA may state that alternatives become more or less accessible within a bounded reconstruction; it may not convert that qualitative relation into a statistical estimate without an external evidential basis.

<a id="33-7-dynamic-versus-static-attractor-function"></a>

### 33.7 Dynamic versus Static Attractor-Function

Dynamic and static attractor-functions answer different target questions. A static function concerns stabilization around a configuration or range. A dynamic function concerns stabilization of a transition relation, sequence, or movement architecture. The same source material may support one, both, neither, or an underdetermined relation, but the claims must remain separate.

#### C33-DYNSTATIC-01 — dynamic and static functions require separate claims, traces, and results

The distinction is:

| Function candidate | Target work | Required source trace | Typical counterpressure |
|---|---|---|---|
| static attractor-function | makes a configuration, state range, role arrangement, or position easier to occupy or return to | source structures carrying state stabilization | similar state produced by different transitions; no recurrent sequence |
| dynamic attractor-function | makes a transition form, sequence, or recurrence pathway easier to enter, reproduce, anticipate, or continue | recurrent relation among phases plus temporal reproduction/path-influence pathway | same sequence generated independently; opposite sequences absorbed by an elastic label |

A recurrent sequence ending in the same state may support a dynamic claim without establishing static stabilization at the endpoint. A stable state reached by many non-comparable routes may support a static claim without supporting one recurrent transition form. Where both are claimed, each requires its own target context, justification, Constitutive Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Validity Scope, Loss, alternatives, local result, and canonical mapping.

```text
dynamic attractor-function
≠ static attractor-function

co-occurrence
≠ shared operation result
```

A dynamic function must preserve temporal direction. A static function may abstract from some route differences, but it may not erase them when those differences matter to the claim. Neither function converts `R` into Α or changes the origin type of the source object.

#### Function-combination and claim discipline

A bounded later path field may exhibit:

- dynamic recurrence without stable endpoint;
- stable endpoint without recurrent route;
- dynamic recurrence that repeatedly restores a stable configuration;
- competing dynamic forms around the same configuration;
- one dynamic form leading to multiple later configurations;
- no discriminable attractor-function despite descriptive recurrence.

These possibilities prevent Chapter 33 from using “attractor” as a single elastic label. If a claim moves from transition-form stabilization to state stabilization, that shift must be declared as a new target-function claim. A change in target context, role set, time window, or affected praxis dimension likewise creates a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence.

#### Family packet after WP2

WP2 completes the recurrence/load architecture at declaration and test-design level:

```text
source object:
  recurrent trajectory form R
  origin type: derived recurrent-form composite

source architecture:
  constitutive positions: method specified, not selected for pressure object
  variable/background/uncertain/incompatible positions: method specified
  reproduction or path-influence pathway: required, not established
  recurring Frames/roles/costs/Asymmetries/Bindings/Non-Events: retained without score

target architecture:
  positive context: D
  contrast context: E
  target function: bounded dynamic attractor-function candidate
  Attractor Load dimensions: specified
  later-path accessibility difference: required, not established
  dynamic/static separation: explicit
  validity scope: bounded

result status:
  Pattern Threshold crossed: not selected
  constitutive form established: not selected
  mechanism established: not selected
  Attractor Load in D: not selected
  no-projection in E: not selected
  dynamic/static result: not selected
  local PROJECT_AS result: not selected
  canonical Output Class: not selected
```

The packet is complete enough for adversarial testing. It is not an empirical result.

<a id="33-8-recurrent-form-versus-retrospective-similarity"></a>

### 33.8 Recurrent Form versus Retrospective Similarity

A recurrent trajectory form is not established merely because several completed histories can be narrated under the same motif. Retrospective similarity is produced whenever analysts select comparable-looking episodes after outcomes are known, align them under a shared vocabulary, suppress divergent cases, or interpret later recurrence as evidence that an earlier common form must already have existed. Such alignment may be descriptively useful. It is not yet a recurrent-form warrant, reproduction pathway, or dynamic attractor-function.

```text
retrospective motif
≠ recurrent-form source object

narrative coherence after the fact
≠ temporally available reproduction mechanism

repeated label
≠ Attractor Load
```

#### C33-RETROSIM-01 — retrospective similarity and narrative motif are insufficient as recurrence or mechanism evidence

A defensible recurrent-form claim must preserve the temporal and evidential order of its own formation. The relevant source Trajectories must be independently warranted before the target-function result is known. Their comparison coordinates, candidate constitutive relations, allowable variation, and break conditions must not be invented only after the later path field has been inspected.

The evidence route therefore separates:

| Evidence layer | Question | Prohibited substitution |
|---|---|---|
| individual-Trajectory evidence | is each source Trajectory independently warranted? | later similarity cannot repair a weak PATH record |
| recurrent-form evidence | do sufficiently comparable Trajectories support one bounded relation under declared variation? | shared title, motif, or outcome cannot create recurrence |
| reproduction/path-influence evidence | was a source feature temporally available through persistence, transmission, institutionalization, or reactivation? | later narrative alignment cannot create an earlier mechanism |
| target-function evidence | does the recurrent source perform bounded work in the declared later path field? | source recurrence cannot substitute for target-period difference |

Retrospective evidence may reveal a candidate, expose an omitted source, or motivate re-examination. It must be marked as retrospective and cannot silently replace source-period or target-period support. Where a source record is revised materially after target inspection, the recurrent-form claim and any dependent `PROJECT_AS` occurrence must be reopened as new testable claims.

A motif may remain a legitimate descriptive compression when it improves navigation or comparison without supporting reproduction, Attractor Load, or later-path stabilization. Chapter 33 does not force every recurring description into a functional projection.

#### C33-SELECTIONBIAS-01 — sampling, survivorship, missing-trajectory, and source-selection pressure remain explicit

The apparent recurrence may depend on which Trajectories entered the comparison set. At minimum, the analysis must ask:

- whether only successful, visible, completed, documented, or memorable Trajectories were retained;
- whether interrupted, weak, opposite, non-recurrent, or failed Trajectories are missing;
- whether the target result guided source inclusion or exclusion;
- whether source availability differs systematically across roles, institutions, periods, or outcomes;
- whether repeated records are independent or duplicate the same underlying process;
- whether the comparison set contains enough negative variation to pressure the candidate form;
- whether the source boundary was narrowed after counterexamples appeared;
- whether survivorship creates the appearance that the recurrent form reliably continues when only continuations are observable.

The obligation is not to reconstruct an impossible complete population. It is to disclose the accessible source field, plausible missingness, inclusion rule, exclusion rule, dependence among records, and effect of known omissions on the Claim Ceiling.

```text
available sample
≠ complete trajectory population

many documented recurrences
≠ unbiased recurrence warrant

absence of counterexample in the record
≠ counterexample shown impossible
```

Where missingness is material and directionally unknown, the result may remain `admissible_but_provisional`, require claim reduction, or end in Non-Capture. Where the sample was selected to fit the proposed form and cannot be repaired without circularity, continued projection may require Mandatory Stop.

#### C33-ELASTICITY-01 — the recurrent form must exclude incompatible or opposite sequences and remain capable of failure

A candidate recurrent form is discriminating only if material countervariation can break it. The analysis must state which changes would:

- remove a constitutive relation;
- reverse the relevant temporal order;
- replace the claimed role or cost relation with a non-comparable one;
- eliminate the Non-Event, Binding, Asymmetry, or continuation effect that carries the form;
- produce an opposite later-path influence;
- divide the source set into multiple recurrent forms;
- show that the apparent recurrence is generated independently in each case;
- leave no stable source object after legitimate exclusions.

A label is overelastic when entry and exit can be reversed, roles exchanged, costs inverted, contradictory outcomes included, and constitutive phases omitted without changing the claim. Expanding the label to absorb each counterexample does not strengthen the pattern. It removes its discriminative content.

```text
pattern survives every material alteration
→ pattern lacks counterfactual load

opposite sequences included without revision
→ recurrent-form claim overextended
```

The form may be narrowed rather than discarded where a smaller source set remains independently justified and the narrowing was not target-driven. Such narrowing must disclose excluded Trajectories and inherited Loss; it creates a revised source claim rather than an invisible repair.

<a id="33-9-counterfactual-attractor-test"></a>

### 33.9 Counterfactual Attractor Test

The Counterfactual Attractor Test asks whether relevant changes in the source architecture, comparison relation, reproduction pathway, or target field would alter the recurrent-form or attractor-function claim. It is a sensitivity discipline, not a simulation of inaccessible histories and not a proof of causal necessity.

#### PMS-grounded recurrent-form variants

Where warranted by the source Records, the test may replace recurrent non-repair with timely repair, remove or redistribute an `Ω` exposure or exit-cost gradient, alter constitutive `Θ` order or persistence, interrupt a `Ψ` continuity relation, introduce a supported `Χ` interruption or `Σ` repair relation, or change a `□` entry condition or material `Φ` recontextualization. Only the concrete occurrence relation is varied; the abstract operator and Δ–Ψ grammar remain fixed.

#### C33-CFTEST-01 — bounded phase, frame, role, cost, source, comparison, and target variation must pressure the claim

The test uses multiple variation routes because no single counterfactual can carry the entire burden:

| Variation route | Pressure question | Claim pressure if unchanged |
|---|---|---|
| constitutive-phase variation | would removal, reversal, delay, or replacement dissolve or materially narrow `R`? | phase may not be constitutive, or form may be elastic |
| Frame variation | would altered entry, relevance, or continuation conditions change the recurrent relation? | common-frame account may replace attractor claim |
| role variation | would redistributed roles, capacities, exposure, or responsibility alter the sequence? | claimed role mechanism may lack traceable load |
| cost/Asymmetry variation | would changed exit, repair, delay, or alternative costs alter recurrence or later accessibility? | Attractor Load may be unsupported |
| source-set variation | do omitted, interrupted, opposite, or newly available Trajectories narrow, split, or dissolve `R`? | selection or survivorship pressure may dominate |
| comparison-coordinate variation | does a legitimate change in granularity, time window, or evidence standard change comparability? | recurrent source may be underdetermined or non-comparable |
| mechanism variation | if persistence, transmission, institutionalization, or reactivation is absent, does later recurrence remain? | independent regeneration or common-condition account gains force |
| same-source target variation | does the same `R` perform discriminable work in `D` but not in `E`? | target function may be overgeneralized |
| target-condition variation | do current rules, resources, shocks, actors, or Frames explain the later path without `R`? | present-condition account may be sufficient |

The relevant counterfactuals must be source-constrained. They vary documented or responsibly inferable relations, not arbitrary imagined worlds. Where the sources cannot support the variation, the test result is underdetermined rather than favorable by default.

The test may yield sensitive, partially sensitive, insensitive, underdetermined, or not-testable-with-available-sources findings under the already established Counterfactual Sensitivity vocabulary. These are local findings, not new Output Classes. They do not quantify causal strength or recurrence probability.

```text
counterfactual sensitivity
≠ causal necessity
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ numerical transition probability
≠ prediction
```

The same-source `D/E` contrast is especially important. If `R` is said to carry the same dynamic attractor-function in materially different path fields regardless of present Frames, roles, costs, and available alternatives, the function is likely overelastic. Conversely, a difference between `D` and `E` does not by itself establish that `R` caused the difference; it only supports context-bounded discrimination when the Source Trace remains intact.

#### C33-ALTSOURCE-01 — common-frame, common-condition, independent-regeneration, multiple-form, and no-stable-source alternatives remain live

A strong attractor claim must compare at least the following rival accounts where relevant:

| Rival account | Core possibility | Required pressure |
|---|---|---|
| common Frame | similar entry and relevance conditions generate similar sequences | test whether recurrence disappears under Frame change |
| common present condition | later paths resemble one another because current rules, resources, shocks, or constraints dominate | compare target-period explanation with inherited recurrent source |
| independent regeneration | each sequence is produced anew without preserved cross-case relation | identify or fail to identify transmission, persistence, institutionalization, or reactivation |
| multiple recurrent forms | the source set contains two or more discriminating transition forms | test whether one label hides incompatible subforms |
| static-function-only | a state or role configuration stabilizes without one recurrent transition form | separate state stabilization from sequence stabilization |
| descriptive motif or background recurrence | similarity aids description but performs no independent target work | retain non-projective description |
| no stable recurrent source | legitimate variation, missingness, or incompatibility prevents source formation | permit Mandatory Stop or Non-Capture |

The existence of a rival does not automatically defeat the attractor projection. The analysis must compare PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, TypeIntegrity, ContextualBoundedness, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, source economy, and discriminatory performance. Where the accounts cannot be separated with available sources, the correct result is underdetermination or Non-Capture—not forced synthesis.

#### C33-NOPROJ-01 — descriptive motif, background recurrence, static-only function, and no-projection remain valid outcomes

Chapter 33 preserves a full no-projection space. A recurrent description may remain:

- a useful descriptive motif without a stable recurrent-form source;
- a source-supported recurrent form with no independent later-path work;
- background recurrence dominated by present conditions or another source;
- a static attractor-function candidate without a dynamic function;
- a dynamic form whose target context is outside the declared Validity Scope;
- an analogy-only relation;
- a non-projectable or non-capturable structure.

```text
recurrent source established
≠ dynamic attractor-function required

historically recurrent
≠ later path structured by recurrence

no-projection
≠ source history irrelevant
```

No-projection is not a residual bin and does not invalidate the individual source Trajectories. It states that the bounded target-function claim is not warranted in the declared target context under the available source and counterpressure.

<a id="33-10-failed-attractor-projection"></a>

### 33.10 Failed Attractor Projection

An attractor projection fails when the recurrent-form source, the reproduction/path-influence relation, the target work, or the type and scope boundary cannot carry the claimed function. Failure must be located at the correct claim position rather than transferred backward to every source record or upward into a new label.

#### C33-FAILURE-01 — failed attractor projection does not automatically invalidate each source Trajectory

Failure conditions include:

- only one independently warranted Trajectory is available;
- source Trajectories are materially incomparable;
- the Pattern Threshold is crossed only by count, salience, or shared vocabulary;
- constitutive positions cannot be separated from variable or incompatible phases;
- the candidate survives opposite sequences only through label expansion;
- sampling, survivorship, duplication, or target-driven source selection remains unbounded;
- no temporally available reproduction or path-influence pathway is source-supported;
- common Frame, present condition, or independent regeneration explains the recurrence more economically;
- no concrete later-path accessibility, friction, expectation, role, cost, repair/exit, continuation, or visibility difference is established;
- dynamic and static functions are conflated;
- Attractor Load is replaced by frequency, score, causal percentage, or person/group property;
- the same function is asserted across materially different target contexts without sensitivity;
- Source Trace, exact five-part Loss, Validity Scope, Claim Ceiling, Stop, or Non-Capture cannot be preserved.

The resulting disposition depends on where and how the burden fails:

| Condition | Appropriate route |
|---|---|
| a narrower recurrent form or function remains supportable | `claim_reduction_required` |
| continued analysis would require circular selection, unsupported mechanism, or type violation | `mandatory_stop` |
| the declared `PROJECT_AS` occurrence lacks a warranted source/function relation | `failed_transformation` |
| available sources cannot responsibly decide among recurrent form, rivals, or no-projection | `non_capture` |
| method and source are adequate but support remains incomplete | `admissible_but_provisional` |

These are canonical Output Classes already defined by STRATA. Chapter 33 creates no additional class for motif, static-only, independent regeneration, or source instability. Local findings must be mapped to exactly one canonical class only after the integrated audit.

```text
failed dynamic attractor projection
≠ each source Trajectory false

failed recurrent-form source claim
≠ all observed similarity erased

mandatory stop
≠ negative person or group verdict
```

A failed source-form claim may require removing or revising `R`; a failed target-function claim may leave `R` intact as a descriptive recurrent source. A failed dynamic claim may leave a separate static claim open. Each materially revised claim requires its own record, Loss, alternatives, and result.

<a id="chapter-34-composite-structures-as-higher-level-functions"></a>

## 34. Composite Structures as Higher-Level Functions

Chapter 34 applies `PROJECT_AS` to relational composites whose components and internal relations have already been reconstructed under Foundations, PATH, SUB, or a prior `COMPOSE` occurrence. Chapter 1 owns Composite Structure and derived analytical objects. Chapters 20–22 own decomposition, component roles, relation maps, distributed function, redundancy, substitution, and internal conflict. Chapter 29 owns projection without Origin-Type Replacement, and Chapter 30 owns the complete generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure. This chapter therefore does not infer a composite from a target-level label, redefine operator types, or treat the visibility of a macro-pattern as evidence of its formation. It asks a narrower family-specific question:

> When may an independently warranted relational composite perform a bounded higher-level function in a declared target field because relations among its components generate a source-traceable praxis difference that no mere list or accidental aggregate supplies?

The family claim is stronger than the statement that many local structures coexist and weaker than the statement that the composite is a new operator, an autonomous entity, or a source-free emergent level. A composite may be analytically useful while functionally inert in the target context. It may also perform one bounded higher-level function while failing another. The source composite, target context, target-function candidate, component relations, inherited uncertainty, and five-part Loss therefore remain separate claim positions.

The stable pressure architecture is:

```text
source occurrences:
  local_asymmetry_occurrence_A1
  local_asymmetry_occurrence_A2
  local_asymmetry_occurrence_A3
  local_asymmetry_occurrence_A4

candidate source:
  distributed_local_asymmetry_composite_Q
  origin type: derived relational composite

positive target candidate:
  institutional_access_field_H
  proposed function: bounded higher-level asymmetry/access function

negative and rival target:
  parallel_local_occurrence_field_I
  proposed possibility: no distinct higher-level function
```

The same source occurrences, composite boundary, relation map, uncertainty, prior disposition, and inherited Loss must remain fixed across `H` and `I`. A material change in source components, component boundaries, relation topology, temporal scope, or target function constitutes a new source and/or projection claim. `H` is not presumed positive, and `I` is not a residual failure scene. Both are equally legitimate target contexts for testing whether the source composite performs additional target-level work.

WP1 establishes the generic family claim, eligible source composite, target-context packet, and three initial higher-level function candidates. It does not yet adjudicate component load, substitution, smaller-subset sufficiency, emergence, aggregation versus functional formation, threshold crossing, target effect, local `PROJECT_AS` result, or canonical Output Class. Those burdens remain open for §§34.5–34.11.

<a id="34-1-basic-claim"></a>

### 34.1 Basic Claim

A relational composite may perform a bounded higher-level function when its independently warranted components and relations jointly alter a declared target-context reconstruction in a way that remains traceable to distributed source load. The higher-level function is not inferred from the number of components, the breadth of the source Frame, the visual density of a graph, the stability of a label, or the analyst's ability to summarize many local occurrences with one phrase. It requires a specific relation between one source composite and one target field.

#### PMS occurrence topology in the source Composite

Where components are PMS operator-typed occurrences, each remains linked to its occurrence Record, source Frame, role, temporal position, uncertainty, and relevant dependency context. The relation map specifies which occurrence relations coordinate, reinforce, oppose, substitute, delay, repair, or transfer load. The proposed target function may not supply missing source typing.

```text
operator-typed component inventory
≠ operator-relation topology

operator-relation topology
≠ higher-level function automatically
```

Across the chapter, a boundary-function names bounded `□`-like separation work; an attractor-function bounded `Α`-like stabilization work; an asymmetry/access function bounded `Ω`-like gradient work; a binding-function bounded `Ψ`-like continuity and breach/exit work; and an integration-function bounded `Σ`-like coordination or repair work. In every family:

```text
bounded function correspondence
≠ operator occurrence identity
≠ complete operator semantics
≠ dependency inheritance by the Composite
```

#### C34-BASIC-01 — the family claim binds source composite, origin type, target context, target function, and authority ceiling

The minimum family form is:

```text
Within declared target context C_t,
relational composite Q,
while retaining origin type derived relational composite,
performs bounded higher-level function F_h
through source-traceable component-and-relation load L_q,
without operator replacement or authority increase.
```

Each position carries a distinct burden:

| Position | Required declaration | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| `Q` | independently warranted relational composite | target function |
| origin type | derived relational composite | PMS operator identity |
| `C_t` | bounded higher-level target field | universal applicability |
| `F_h` | specific operator-like target-function candidate | truth, rank, legitimacy, or causal monopoly |
| `L_q` | component-and-relation Source Trace | equal load of every component |
| authority ceiling | no inheritance or increase | recommendation, sanction, or governance right |

The word `higher-level` is relational. It means that the source composite is tested as one functional unit within a declared larger or differently organized target field. It does not name an ontological layer, a superior explanatory scale, or a stronger warrant. The same object may occupy a higher relative position in one transformation and a lower or lateral position in another.

```text
higher relative level
≠ higher truth
≠ higher authority
≠ more fundamental being
```

A family candidate is admissible for testing only if the proposed function names a concrete target-context difference. Eligible differences may concern access, exclusion, expectation, recurrence, cost distribution, role stabilization, action corridors, binding continuity, repair, integration, or alternative visibility. A generic claim that the composite “matters at the macro level” does not specify a function.

The family claim remains open to at least four non-success positions:

- the source is a valid composite but performs no distinct target function;
- a smaller subset performs the target work and the larger composite is overinclusive;
- the apparent unity is a Frame-selection artifact;
- no responsible source-to-target function can be captured under available evidence.

The chapter therefore treats functional formation as a claim to be tested, not as a semantic consequence of the word `composite`.

#### C34-SOURCECOMPOSITE-01 — the source composite requires independently warranted components and a relation trace before target adjudication

The minimum source packet is:

```yaml
source:
  object: distributed_local_asymmetry_composite_Q
  origin_type: derived_relational_composite
  source_selection_basis: independent_of_target_function_result
  components:
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A1
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A2
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A3
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A4
  component_boundaries: declared
  relation_map:
    coordination: declared
    reinforcement: declared
    dependency: declared
    temporal_order: declared
    countervailing_relations: retained
  source_frame: declared
  source_granularity: declared
  source_relative_level: declared
  temporal_scope: declared
  prior_disposition: retained
  source_uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

A component list is insufficient because the proposed family concerns relational work. The source packet must show which local occurrences are included, why they belong to the same composite, how their boundaries are drawn, and which relations make their joint treatment analytically defensible. Relations may involve coordination, reinforcement, dependency, sequence, mutual constraint, shared resource pathways, repeated load transfer, or a common effect on later accessibility. Their mere co-presence in one institution, archive, diagram, dataset, or Reader panel does not establish a relational composite.

```text
component enumeration
≠ relation trace

many warranted occurrences
≠ warranted composite

warranted composite
≠ higher-level function warranted
```

The source composite must be formed independently of the desired target result. If `Q` is enlarged, narrowed, or re-bounded only after `H` is inspected, the source claim risks retrospective fit. A justified revision remains possible, but it requires a new source declaration, revised relation map, revised Loss, alternatives, and a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. Target success may not silently repair an under-warranted source composite.

The component roles remain open. WP1 does not decide which components are load-bearing, modulating, redundant, replaceable, background, uncertain, incompatible, or countervailing. It only requires that those distinctions remain possible and that the source packet preserve enough structure for later substitution, removal, smaller-subset, relation-disruption, and contradiction tests.

#### C34-TARGETCTX-01 — the higher-level target context and same-source `H/I` contrast must be fully declared

A target-context declaration must identify:

```yaml
target_context:
  object_or_field: institutional_access_field_H
  target_frame: declared
  target_granularity: declared
  target_relative_level: declared
  temporal_scope: declared
  roles_and_positions: declared
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
    - access
    - exclusion
    - cost_distribution
    - role_readability
    - action_corridors
    - alternative_visibility
  present_target_conditions: retained
  rival_frames_and_sources: retained
  claim_scope: bounded
  claim_ceiling: reconstructive_only

same_source_contrast:
  positive_candidate: institutional_access_field_H
  negative_or_rival_context: parallel_local_occurrence_field_I
  source_composite_Q_unchanged: required
```

The target field must be distinct from the source composite. A broader label attached to the same source description is not a target context. The target declaration states where, for whom, over what period, at what granularity, and along which praxis dimensions the proposed function would make a difference. It also preserves present conditions and rival Frames that may explain the target field more economically than the historical or distributed source composite.

The `H/I` contrast disciplines contextual boundedness:

```text
same source composite Q
+ target context H
→ higher-level function candidate

same source composite Q
+ target context I
→ no distinct higher-level function remains possible
```

The contrast does not require a positive result in `H` or a negative result in `I`. It requires that the family claim remain sensitive to target context. If the same function label is assigned to every target field irrespective of relevant differences, the projection has become context-insensitive and risks exceeding the Traceability Ceiling.

<a id="34-2-local-differences-as-higher-level-boundary-function"></a>

### 34.2 Local Differences as Higher-Level Boundary Function

Local differences may jointly perform a higher-level boundary-function when their relations generate a stable and target-relevant inside/outside, eligible/ineligible, reachable/unreachable, visible/invisible, or admitted/excluded distinction that no isolated occurrence or accidental aggregate responsibly captures. The source occurrences remain difference-, Frame-, role-, access-, or rule-typed structures at their origin. Their target function does not convert them or their composite into `□`.

#### C34-BOUNDARYFUNC-01 — coordinated local differences may carry a bounded higher-level boundary-function only through relationally produced target separation

The generic candidate form is:

```text
local differences d1 … dn
+ coordination, dependency, sequence, or mutual reinforcement
+ declared target field H
+ stable target-relevant separation effect
→ bounded higher-level boundary-function candidate
```

The target separation must be praxeologically specified. Examples include:

- several access rules jointly making one route practically reachable and another unavailable;
- distributed role distinctions jointly determining who can initiate, review, delay, or close a process;
- multiple local eligibility conditions jointly producing a target-level admission boundary;
- recurrent local exclusions jointly making an alternative invisible or prohibitively costly.

The function is not established merely because local differences can be grouped under one conceptual category. At least three burdens remain distinct:

1. **source burden:** the local differences and their relations are warranted;
2. **formation burden:** the relations jointly produce more than coexistence;
3. **target burden:** the formed composite alters a declared target-level separation.

```text
shared category
≠ coordinated boundary production

multiple exclusions
≠ one higher-level boundary automatically

stable target separation
≠ legitimacy of the boundary
```

A boundary-function may be distributed and internally heterogeneous. Some components may open access while others close it; some may be formally symmetric while their interaction produces asymmetric reachability; some may be redundant or compensatory. Homogeneity is not required. What is required is a traceable relation showing how the distributed differences jointly organize the target field.

The candidate must remain vulnerable to later tests:

- removing or replacing a component may leave the function intact;
- disrupting one relation may dissolve the target separation despite all components remaining present;
- a smaller subset may explain all relevant boundary work;
- a rival Frame may show that the apparent inside/outside distinction was analyst-produced;
- current target rules may account for the separation without historical or distributed source load.

WP1 names these pressures but does not adjudicate them. A useful target description that cannot survive them may remain descriptive aggregation, background structure, or no-projection rather than a higher-level boundary-function.

The function carries no normative endorsement. A well-supported boundary-function may describe inclusion, exclusion, or reachability without deciding whether the boundary is justified, fair, legal, desirable, or actionable.

<a id="34-3-repeated-non-events-as-higher-level-attractor-function"></a>

### 34.3 Repeated Non-Events as Higher-Level Attractor-Function

Repeated Non-Events may jointly perform a higher-level attractor-function when their expectation Frames, temporal recurrence, and relation to later possibilities stabilize a target-level field of non-decision, delay, non-repair, non-entry, non-exit, or recurrent return. The source must preserve each Non-Event as a structured non-occurrence relative to a warranted expectation. Missing information, undocumented activity, or retrospective disappointment cannot be converted into Λ merely because repetition would strengthen the macro-claim.

#### C34-NONEVENTFUNC-01 — repeated Non-Events require shared or coordinated expectation structure and a traceable later-possibility effect

The generic candidate form is:

```text
source-supported Non-Events n1 … nn
+ declared expectation Frames and temporal windows
+ recurrent or coordinated non-occurrence relation
+ altered later possibility field in H
→ bounded higher-level attractor-function candidate
```

The relevant relation may involve:

- repeated decisions not taken within known windows;
- repairs repeatedly expected but not performed;
- exits formally available but recurrently unrealized under stable cost conditions;
- responsibility repeatedly deferred across roles;
- recurring non-entry into an otherwise accessible process;
- repeated interruption before one transition can complete.

The proposed target work must be concrete. Repetition may stabilize expectations that no decision will occur, lower the visibility of alternatives, increase the cost of initiating repair, normalize delay, preserve a role distribution, or make renewed non-decision locally easier than resolution. These are target-function candidates, not automatic findings.

```text
repeated absence
≠ repeated Non-Event

repeated Non-Events
≠ higher-level attractor-function automatically

expectation stability
≠ causal monopoly
≠ prediction
```

The expectation Frame for each Non-Event must remain inspectable. A broad common Frame may connect the occurrences, but it may also create a false unity. If the expectations differ materially, the time windows are incomparable, or the non-occurrences produce opposite effects, the source may require separation into several composites or may end in Non-Capture.

The function is operator-like only in the target context. It is not `Α`, does not redefine `Λ`, and does not assert that absence becomes an autonomous causal force. The source trace must remain capable of distinguishing:

- constitutive repeated Non-Events;
- merely parallel absences;
- delayed but later completed events;
- unknown or undocumented events;
- countervailing positive events;
- present target conditions that independently stabilize the same field.

The same-source `H/I` pressure remains active. Repeated Non-Events may carry an attractor-function in one target field yet remain historical background, descriptive recurrence, or functionally inert in another. WP1 does not select either result.

<a id="34-4-distributed-asymmetries-as-higher-level-omega-function"></a>

### 34.4 Distributed Asymmetries as Higher-Level Ω-Function

Distributed local asymmetries may jointly perform a higher-level asymmetry/access function when their coordination, reinforcement, temporal persistence, and repeated load distribution alter target-level access, exposure, burden, exit, timing, role capacity, or alternative cost. The family claim does not require one dominant local asymmetry, equal direction across every component, or internal homogeneity. It requires a relation map that shows how local gradients combine, counteract, or transfer load within the target field.

#### C34-ASYMFUNC-01 — distributed asymmetries require coordinated or reinforcing load and a concrete action-corridor effect

The stable pressure candidate is:

```yaml
source:
  object: distributed_local_asymmetry_composite_Q
  components:
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A1
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A2
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A3
    - local_asymmetry_occurrence_A4
  required_relation_candidates:
    - coordination
    - mutual_reinforcement
    - repeated_load_transfer
    - temporal_persistence
    - shared_access_or_exit_effect

target_H:
  candidate_function: bounded_higher_level_asymmetry_access_function
  required_difference:
    - access_changes
    - exposure_or_burden_changes
    - exit_or_timing_cost_changes
    - role_capacity_changes
    - action_corridor_or_alternative_visibility_changes

target_I:
  retained_possibility: no_distinct_higher_level_function
```

The source relation may be serial, parallel, recursive, or distributed. One local rule may limit information, another may delay access, a third may increase exit costs, and a fourth may transfer review burden. None alone need carry the target function. Their relation may jointly produce a stable access gradient.
