Conversely, many local gradients may cancel, remain isolated, or affect different roles and periods without forming one target-level function.

The projection therefore requires more than cumulative burden language. It must show:

- which asymmetries are independently warranted;
- how their directions and affected roles relate;
- which loads persist, transfer, reinforce, compensate, or conflict;
- what target-level access or action-corridor difference follows;
- why a smaller subset, current condition, or rival Frame is insufficient;
- where uncertainty, missingness, and inherited Loss constrain the claim.

```text
sum of local burdens
≠ higher-level asymmetry automatically

graph centrality or density
≠ distributed load relation

higher-level access gradient
≠ moral rank
≠ legal or political legitimacy
```

Internal contradiction remains evidentially valuable. If one component reduces the burden generated by another, the target function may be modulated, split by role, bounded to a period, supported only provisionally, or not responsibly capturable. The family method does not force all local differences into one directional macrogradient.

#### C34-NOPRIMITIVE-01 — every higher-level family candidate preserves operator types and prohibits essence and authority inheritance

Across boundary-, attractor-, and asymmetry-function candidates, the canonical form is:

```text
origin type: derived relational composite
projected target function: bounded operator-like higher-level function
```

It is not:

```text
composite becomes □
composite becomes Α
composite becomes Ω
macrofunction becomes a new PMS primitive
macrofunction becomes a person, group, institution, culture, or identity essence
higher-level legibility creates authority
```

The operator-like term indicates the kind of bounded target work under examination. It does not change the Δ–Ψ grammar, create a new dependency, or assign operator identity to the source composite. The same rule will govern the later Ψ-like and Σ-like families in WP2.

A supported higher-level function would remain reconstructive and context-bound. It could not by itself establish empirical causality, universal generalization, moral judgment, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance power, or application authority. Those claims require independent warrant and authority outside STRATA.

#### C34-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives the family packet without component-load, emergence, target-effect, result, or class adjudication

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 34 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 34 WP2 §§34.5–34.7
→ Repeated Commitments as Higher-Level Ψ-Function
→ Multiple Integrations as Higher-Level Σ-Function
→ Emergent Function
```

WP2 receives:

- the bounded composite-to-higher-level-function family grammar;
- one independently warranted relational-composite source candidate `Q`;
- visible component boundaries, relation-map requirements, uncertainty, prior disposition, and inherited five-part Loss;
- a fully declared higher-level target context and stable same-source `H/I` contrast;
- initial boundary-, Non-Event/attractor-, and asymmetry/access-function candidate grammars;
- the requirement for concrete target-level praxis difference;
- operator-type, new-primitive, essence, causality, legitimacy, and authority ceilings;
- open component-substitution, relation-disruption, smaller-subset, rival-Frame, present-condition, no-projection, Stop, and Non-Capture pressure.

WP2 does not inherit:

- selected load-bearing, modulating, redundant, replaceable, background, incompatible, or countervailing components;
- a supported boundary-, attractor-, or asymmetry/access function;
- an established target effect in `H` or no-projection in `I`;
- a source-traceable emergence result;
- aggregation-versus-functional-formation adjudication;
- a threshold result;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result or canonical Output Class;
- permission to create test, case, or smoke YAML files during Block drafting;
- Chapter-34 or RETYPE lock;
- additional truth, rank, legitimacy, application, governance, or authority.

<a id="chapter-34-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 34.1–34.4 establish the bounded composite-to-higher-level-function family claim, independently warranted relational-composite source packet, component-and-relation trace requirement, explicit higher-level target context, same-source `H/I` contrast, and the initial boundary-, repeated-Non-Event/attractor-, and distributed-asymmetry/access-function grammars. They preserve Origin-Type visibility, deny operator replacement and source-free macrofunction inference, and retain all later substitution, subset, emergence, threshold, rival, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture burdens.

```text
family claim declared
+ source composite and relation packet bounded
+ target context and H/I contrast explicit
+ three initial function families discriminated
+ operator and authority ceilings preserved
+ no result fabricated
→ WP1 complete
```

This result does not establish component load, functional formation, source-traceable emergence, a boundary-function, attractor-function, or asymmetry/access function in `H`, no-projection in `I`, a threshold result, Counterfactual Sensitivity, local `PROJECT_AS` result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-34 lock, RETYPE lock, or additional authority.
<a id="34-5-repeated-commitments-as-higher-level-psi-function"></a>

### 34.5 Repeated Commitments as Higher-Level Ψ-Function

Repeated commitments may perform a bounded higher-level binding-function when their continuity, relation, and accumulated consequences alter how a declared target field can be entered, maintained, revised, exited, repaired, or broken. The family claim is not that several promises, contracts, routines, or recorded obligations add up to `Ψ`. Nor is every repeated commitment evidence of one shared binding structure. The projection requires a relational composite in which locally warranted commitments become mutually conditioning across time and roles.

A commitment occurrence may be explicit or institutionally embedded, but its source status must remain inspectable. Formal wording, repeated performance, expected continuity, role assignment, shared records, practical dependency, repair practice, and breach response are different evidential positions. One may support another; none may silently replace the others. A document that uses commitment language does not by itself establish practical binding, and repeated conduct does not by itself establish a self-binding claim.

#### C34-BINDINGFUNC-01 — repeated commitments require continuity, accumulated breach or exit cost, and role stabilization without becoming Ψ

The generic candidate form is:

```text
source-supported commitment occurrences c1 … cn
+ declared temporal and role relations
+ continuity across occurrences
+ accumulated breach, revision, or exit consequences
+ stabilized expectations or role capacities in target field H
→ bounded higher-level binding-function candidate
```

The candidate may be carried by several source relations:

- later commitments presuppose or incorporate earlier commitments;
- records, routines, or role assignments preserve continuity across personnel or episodes;
- breach in one location changes access, trust, timing, repair burden, or participation elsewhere;
- repeated commitments increase the practical cost of unilateral revision or exit;
- obligations are redistributed or inherited through declared institutional or relational pathways;
- recurrent repair maintains continuity despite local failure;
- several local commitments jointly make a future course more available and alternatives more costly.

These relations must be demonstrated rather than inferred from repetition alone. A set of independent commitments to unrelated objects may be numerous and durable while carrying no single higher-level binding-function. Conversely, a small number of commitments may be sufficient when their dependency and continuity structure is strong. WP2 therefore does not introduce a count threshold.

The target-level praxis difference must be concrete. A binding-function candidate may alter:

- who can rely on continued participation;
- which roles remain recognizable and actionable;
- which revisions require coordination rather than unilateral choice;
- how breach, repair, withdrawal, or succession is processed;
- which alternatives remain practically available;
- how costs and exposure are distributed when continuity fails;
- whether a target field persists across local interruption.

```text
repeated commitment
≠ higher-level binding-function

formal obligation
≠ practical continuity automatically

accumulated breach cost
≠ normative legitimacy
≠ coercive authorization
```

Breach cost is descriptive and praxeological. It may include lost access, increased coordination burden, disrupted expectations, repair requirements, exposure, timing loss, or practical exit friction. It does not establish that the commitment is justified, morally binding, legally valid, or enforceable. A higher-level binding-function may be supported in a target context while the underlying commitments remain contested, asymmetric, revisable, partially fulfilled, or normatively unresolved.

The function remains operator-like, not operator-identical:

```text
relational commitment composite PROJECT_AS bounded binding-function
≠ source composite becomes Ψ
≠ institution, group, or person becomes a Ψ-type
```

`Ψ` remains the PMS Self-Binding operator type under the unchanged Δ–Ψ grammar. Chapter 34 tests whether a derived relational composite performs bounded continuity- and binding-related target work. It does not create collective selfhood, infer interior commitment, assign person or group essence, or increase application authority.

Internal variation must remain visible. Some commitments may be fulfilled, deferred, revised, contested, delegated, or breached. A stable target-level binding-function need not imply identical local commitments or uninterrupted performance. It does, however, require a traceable account of how continuity survives, changes, splits, or fails through those differences. If the target function is preserved only by ignoring material breach, exit, or contradiction, the projection exceeds the Traceability Ceiling.

The same-source `H/I` contrast remains active. Repeated commitments may stabilize institutional access and succession in `H` while remaining parallel local undertakings without a distinct higher-level function in `I`. WP2 does not select either result.

<a id="34-6-multiple-integrations-as-higher-level-sigma-function"></a>

### 34.6 Multiple Integrations as Higher-Level Σ-Function

Multiple local integrations may perform a bounded higher-level integration-function when their relations coordinate otherwise separate practices, translations, repairs, roles, records, or transition pathways in a larger target field. The family claim does not require a contradiction-free totality. It requires that locally warranted integration occurrences jointly create a target-level capacity that is not supplied by their isolated presence.

Local integration may take different forms: reconciliation of incompatible records, coordination of roles, repair between subsystems, translation across Frames, consolidation of partial decisions, synchronization of temporal sequences, or accommodation of residual conflict. These occurrences need not use identical procedures or produce one uniform outcome. Their higher-level relevance depends on how they relate and what target work their relation performs.

#### C34-INTEGRATIONFUNC-01 — multiple local integrations require relational coordination while unresolved contradiction and partiality remain visible without becoming Σ

The generic candidate form is:

```text
independently warranted local integration occurrences i1 … in
+ declared coordination, dependency, sequence, or translation relations
+ preserved unresolved residues and incompatibilities
+ concrete target-level coordination or continuation difference in H
→ bounded higher-level integration-function candidate
```

Possible target work includes:

- enabling records or decisions from one local field to remain usable in another;
- coordinating role transitions that no single local integration can carry;
- preserving system continuation despite local incompatibility;
- routing residual conflict into bounded repair rather than erasing it;
- synchronizing otherwise divergent temporal sequences;
- maintaining translation across non-equivalent Frames;
- making distributed revision possible without requiring total reset;
- preventing one unresolved local contradiction from automatically dissolving the wider field.

A higher-level integration-function is not established by a positive count of local resolutions. Several successful local integrations may remain disconnected, compete for resources, rely on incompatible Frames, or produce opposite target effects. Likewise, one unresolved local conflict does not automatically defeat a wider coordinating function. The analysis must show whether contradiction is carried, redirected, compartmentalized, compensated, amplified, or left outside the function's declared Validity Scope.

```text
multiple local integrations
≠ integrated whole automatically

higher-level integration-function
≠ global coherence
≠ contradiction eliminated
≠ Σ origin type
```

The source trace must preserve partiality. At minimum, it must identify:

- which local tensions were integrated and which were not;
- which relations connect the local integrations;
- which roles or subfields remain outside the coordination;
- which translation losses, residual conflicts, and asymmetries persist;
- which repairs are recurrent rather than completed;
- which target effects depend on current conditions rather than source integration;
- where the function splits by role, period, or praxis dimension.

An integration-function may therefore be bounded to one target task while failing another. For example, a composite may coordinate access records without integrating burden distribution, or preserve procedural continuity while leaving substantive disagreement unresolved. Separate target functions require separate claims, traces, Loss profiles, and results.

The operator boundary remains strict. `Σ` is not a label for any coordinated aggregate. The source composite retains its origin type, and the target function remains contextual:

```text
relational integration composite PROJECT_AS bounded integration-function
≠ composite becomes Σ
≠ local repair proves total integration
≠ higher-level coherence creates legitimacy or authority
```

The same-source `H/I` contrast again remains open. In `H`, local integrations may jointly support cross-field access, repair, or continuity. In `I`, the same integrations may remain isolated local accommodations with no distinct higher-level coordination. WP2 does not establish either result.

<a id="34-7-emergent-function"></a>

### 34.7 Emergent Function

Within STRATA, an emergent function is a bounded target function that becomes visible only when a warranted relational composite is considered as a composite, while remaining reconstructible through its components, relations, source conditions, uncertainty, and Loss. Emergence names a level-relative visibility condition. It does not name source-free production, spontaneous ontology, causal independence, explanatory monopoly, or a new PMS primitive.

#### Occurrence-level emergence trace

Composite-level visibility is admissible only where the target difference remains reconstructible through the concrete component occurrences, their relation topology, countervailing relations, and the exact Loss introduced by source formation and projection. Where PMS-typed components are material, the emergence claim identifies which relation among their occurrences is absent at isolated-component level and becomes visible only at Composite level.

```text
newly visible relation at Composite level
≠ source-free novelty
≠ new operator
```

#### C34-EMERGENCE-01 — emergent function means composite-level visibility with complete source trace, not source-free novelty

The minimum form is:

```text
function not attributable to any isolated component under the declared claim
+ function visible in the warranted component-relation topology
+ material source or relation variation changes the candidate function
+ constitutive source trace remains reconstructible
→ source-traceable emergent-function candidate
```

“Complete source trace” does not mean lossless recovery of every local detail. Composite formation and projection may compress information. It means that the claim retains enough warranted source structure to identify the components and relations that carry, modulate, replace, oppose, or limit the function; distinguish uncertainty from exclusion; disclose irrecoverable loss; and show why the function is not merely supplied by the target Frame or present target conditions.

```text
visible only at composite level
≠ detached from source

not reducible to one component
≠ untraceable to component relations

emergent function
≠ new entity
≠ new operator
≠ higher authority
```

Source trace is relational. The function may depend on timing, ordering, coordination, reinforcement, dependency, repeated load transfer, translation, shared repair, or mutual constraint. A component inventory without this topology cannot carry an emergence claim.

#### C34-COMPONENTROLE-01 — component roles remain qualitative, claim-bound, revisable, and non-scored

For a declared function candidate, components may occupy at least the following analytical roles:

| Role | Local meaning | Required caution |
|---|---|---|
| constitutive | removal or material alteration defeats or fundamentally changes the candidate function | not an intrinsic property across all targets |
| modulating | changes scope, direction, timing, intensity, accessibility, or role distribution without solely carrying the function | not a numerical weight |
| redundant | duplicates target work already carried elsewhere under current conditions | redundancy may disappear after other removal |
| substitutable | can be replaced by a source-supported alternative while preserving bounded function | replacement may change Loss or Validity Scope |
| countervailing | weakens, redirects, compensates, or limits the candidate function | not automatically disqualifying |
| background | belongs to the source field but does not carry the tested target difference | may become relevant under a different claim |
| uncertain | role cannot be responsibly assigned under available evidence | uncertainty must not be forced into absence |
| incompatible | cannot be integrated into the declared single-function formation without narrowing, splitting, or rejecting the claim | incompatibility is not noise |

These roles are relational to one source composite, target context, function candidate, and time window. They are not permanent component types, ordinal levels, centrality scores, causal percentages, or person classifications. One component may be constitutive for a boundary-function, modulating for an access function, and background for an integration-function.

No role is assigned to `A1–A4` in WP2. The role architecture is established; the pressure object remains unadjudicated.

#### C34-RELATIONTRACE-01 — relation topology must carry the proposed function in addition to component presence

A sufficient relation trace must declare, where relevant:

```yaml
relation_trace:
  coordination:
  reinforcement:
  dependency:
  temporal_sequence:
  shared_target_effect:
  load_transfer:
  translation_or_repair:
  countervailing_relations:
  relation_uncertainty:
```

Not every field must be positive, but the trace must identify which relations are claimed and which are absent, uncertain, excluded, or countervailing. The target function must react to material changes in this topology. If all relevant relations can be removed while the label remains unchanged, the proposed function lacks Traceable Load.

Relation trace also prevents source laundering. Components may be individually well evidenced, yet their coordination may be speculative. In that case, the appropriate result may be descriptive aggregation, a provisional narrower claim, Mandatory Stop, failed transformation, or Non-Capture. Formal completeness cannot upgrade relation evidence.

#### C34-SUBSTITUTION-01 — removal, replacement, redundancy, smaller-subset sufficiency, and overinclusive-source pressure remain mandatory

The family method must ask:

1. What happens if one component is removed?
2. Can another warranted component replace it?
3. Does the function persist because of redundancy?
4. Does a smaller subset perform all target work?
5. Does relation disruption matter more than component removal?
6. Does the declared larger composite add discriminating praxis difference?
7. Does substitution change Loss, scope, affected roles, or time?

```text
function survives component removal
≠ removed component irrelevant in every claim

function survives substitution
≠ source object unchanged

smaller subset carries all target work
→ larger source may be overinclusive
→ Claim Reduction remains possible
```

Substitution tests do not seek atomistic reduction. Their purpose is to locate distributed load and protect against overinclusive macroclaims. A function may be genuinely distributed and still tolerate individual replacement. Conversely, a source composite may be invalidated by relation loss even when every component remains present.

WP2 does not decide whether `Q` is overinclusive or whether a smaller subset is sufficient. It preserves those tests for WP3.

#### C34-INTERNALCONFLICT-01 — internal contradiction may weaken, redirect, split, defeat, stop, or render the function non-capturable

Internal conflict is not automatically evidence against composite identity or higher-level function. A function may depend on compensation, repair, negotiated incompatibility, role differentiation, or bounded containment of contradiction. But conflict must not be erased to stabilize the macro-label.

Material contradiction may lead to different consequences:

- **weakening:** the function persists with reduced scope or continuity;
- **redirection:** the composite performs a different target function than proposed;
- **split:** distinct roles, periods, or subfields require separate function claims;
- **no distinct function:** local structures remain without higher-level formation;
- **Claim Reduction:** only a narrower subset, time window, or praxis dimension is supported;
- **Mandatory Stop:** continuation would require invented coordination or erased counterevidence;
- **Failed Transformation:** the declared projection does not meet its requirements;
- **Non-Capture:** available sources cannot responsibly adjudicate among non-equivalent formations.

```text
internal conflict
≠ automatic failure
≠ automatic integration
≠ disposable noise
```

The function candidate must therefore preserve countervailing and incompatible components in the source trace. A higher-level claim that survives only by excluding every contrary relation after target inspection is not source-independent and risks retrospective functional formation.

#### Family-load packet after WP2

WP2 adds the following burdens to the Chapter-34 family packet:

```yaml
family_load_packet:
  binding_function:
    continuity_required: true
    accumulated_breach_revision_or_exit_consequence_required: true
    role_or_expectation_stabilization_required: true
    psi_identity_prohibited: true
  integration_function:
    relational_coordination_required: true
    unresolved_contradiction_retained: true
    partiality_and_scope_required: true
    sigma_identity_prohibited: true
  emergence:
    composite_level_visibility_required: true
    source_free_novelty_prohibited: true
    component_and_relation_trace_required: true
  component_roles:
    qualitative_and_claim_bound: true
    scores_prohibited: true
  substitution_and_conflict:
    removal_replacement_redundancy_subset_and_relation_disruption_open: true
    contradiction_may_weaken_redirect_split_or_defeat: true
```

The packet is a testing architecture. It does not establish that any component is constitutive, that `Q` forms a higher-level function, that `H` is positive, or that `I` is no-projection.

#### C34-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives the complete family-load architecture without emergent-function adjudication or target result

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 34 WP2 complete
→ Chapter 34 WP3 §§34.8–34.11
→ Aggregation versus Functional Formation
→ Thresholds
→ Higher-Level Function without Authority Increase
→ Failed Higher-Level Projection
```

WP3 receives:

- the independently warranted relational-composite and target-context packet from WP1;
- five bounded operator-like function families: boundary, attractor, asymmetry/access, binding, and integration;
- source-traceable emergence as composite-level visibility without source-free novelty;
- qualitative component roles without scores;
- relation-topology requirements beyond enumeration;
- mandatory removal, replacement, redundancy, smaller-subset, overinclusive-source, and relation-disruption pressure;
- internal contradiction as weakening, redirection, split, no-function, reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture pressure;
- unchanged `H/I` target contrast, exact Loss, Claim Ceiling, and authority boundary.

WP3 does not inherit:

- selected component roles for `Q`;
- a crossed functional-formation threshold;
- a supported boundary-, attractor-, asymmetry/access-, binding-, or integration-function;
- an emergent-function finding;
- a preferred source subset, composite boundary, or rival Frame;
- a target effect in `H` or no-projection in `I`;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result or canonical Output Class;
- permission to create new test, case, or smoke YAML files during Block drafting;
- Chapter-34 or RETYPE lock;
- truth, causal monopoly, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority.

<a id="chapter-34-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 34.5–34.7 establish bounded binding- and integration-function grammars, source-traceable emergence, qualitative component-role architecture, relation-topology requirements, substitution and smaller-subset pressure, and explicit internal-conflict routing. They preserve partiality, unresolved contradiction, operator-type integrity, exact Loss, target-context boundedness, and the right of the family claim to weaken, split, fail, stop, or remain non-capturable.

```text
binding and integration families specified
+ emergence remains source-traceable
+ component roles and relation load declared
+ substitution, subset, and contradiction pressure retained
+ no target result fabricated
→ WP2 complete
```

This result does not establish functional formation, a threshold crossing, any component role for `Q`, a higher-level function in `H`, no-projection in `I`, Counterfactual Sensitivity result, rival-Frame preference, local `PROJECT_AS` result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-34 lock, RETYPE lock, or additional authority.
<a id="34-8-aggregation-versus-functional-formation"></a>

### 34.8 Aggregation versus Functional Formation

A relational composite may be descriptively important without performing a distinct higher-level function. Chapter 34 therefore separates **aggregation** from **functional formation** before any threshold, target result, or Output Class is selected. Aggregation identifies that several warranted occurrences belong within one declared source field or analytical inventory. Functional formation claims more: the components and their relations jointly carry a bounded target function that changes a declared praxis dimension in a way not supplied by isolated component presence, retrospective grouping, or current target conditions alone.

The distinction does not devalue aggregation. A careful aggregate may preserve components, local variation, uncertainty, and relation gaps more responsibly than a stronger macrofunction claim. It may be the correct endpoint where the source supports co-presence, repetition, or distribution but not coordination, reinforcement, dependency, sequence, load transfer, repair, or a shared target effect.

#### Operator-sensitive relation-disruption test

If the same component occurrence types remain present while coordination, dependency, sequence, repair, or load-transfer relations are disrupted, the candidate function must respond. If it remains unchanged, the relation map may not be constitutive. If it changes, the result may support relational load but does not prove causal monopoly.

#### C34-AGGREGATION-01 — component accumulation remains distinct from relational functional formation, and descriptive aggregation remains a legitimate weaker outcome

The minimal contrast is:

```text
warranted components collected within one declared source field
+ no sufficiently warranted relation topology or additional target work
→ descriptive aggregation only

warranted components
+ source-supported relation topology
+ concrete target-level praxis difference
+ counterfactual response to material source changes
→ functional-formation candidate
```

Functional formation requires all of the following positions to remain separately inspectable:

1. **component warrant:** each included occurrence or component is independently supported;
2. **relation warrant:** coordination, reinforcement, dependency, sequence, translation, repair, load transfer, or countervailing relations are supported rather than inferred from co-presence;
3. **composite-boundary warrant:** the declared source boundary is neither target-driven nor merely inherited from an administrative, narrative, graph, or Reader grouping;
4. **target-placement warrant:** the declared target context and relative level are independently specified;
5. **target-function warrant:** the composite performs concrete work in a declared praxis dimension;
6. **difference warrant:** the higher-level claim discriminates cases that descriptive aggregation alone does not;
7. **counterfactual warrant:** material changes in components, relations, boundary, target context, or target conditions may alter, narrow, split, or defeat the candidate function;
8. **Loss warrant:** compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable heterogeneity remain visible.

```text
component accumulation
≠ relational formation

relational formation
≠ operator formation

functional formation
≠ causal totality
≠ ontological emergence
```

A component set can be large, persistent, densely connected, institutionally named, or historically salient while remaining only an aggregate for the tested claim. Conversely, a small composite may perform a higher-level function if its component relations and target work are discriminating. Neither size nor visibility determines the result.

The source composite must also survive an anti-retrospective check. If the target result in `H` was used to select only those local occurrences that make the function appear coherent, then the source object has been formed by the projection it is supposed to test. The appropriate response is not to call the pattern emergent, but to reconstruct a target-blind source composite or reduce, stop, fail, or leave the claim non-captured.

The same-source `H/I` contrast remains decisive:

```text
same Q + target H
→ higher-level function candidate may remain testable

same Q + target I
→ descriptive aggregation or no distinct target function may remain testable
```

WP3 does not establish either result. It specifies what would distinguish them.

Functional formation is also function-specific. A composite might support a bounded access function without supporting a binding function; it might coordinate repair without stabilizing a boundary; it might redistribute exposure without integrating contradiction. Each target function requires its own source trace, relation map, Validity Scope, Loss profile, Counterfactual Sensitivity, and result.

```text
one source composite
≠ one universal higher-level function

one supported target function
≠ all related function families supported
```

Descriptive aggregation, background composite relevance, a narrower source claim, and no-projection therefore remain positive methodological outcomes. They are not failures to recognize a “real” macrolevel. They protect the Admissibility Band from both under-description and abstraction without traceable load.

<a id="34-9-thresholds"></a>

### 34.9 Thresholds

A higher-level function requires a threshold because not every relationally organized composite performs target work strong enough to justify projection. The threshold is not a fixed quantity. It is the point at which the declared source composite, under the declared target context and Claim Scope, produces a source-traceable praxis difference that survives relevant alternatives and remains within the Traceability Ceiling.

#### C34-THRESHOLD-01 — the functional-formation threshold is context-sensitive and praxis-difference based, never established by count, duration, repetition, density, or salience alone

The threshold must be stated through qualitative burdens such as:

```yaml
functional_formation_threshold:
  source_components_independently_warranted: required
  relation_topology_source_supported: required
  composite_boundary_non_retrospective: required
  target_context_fully_declared: required
  concrete_target_praxis_difference: required
  difference_not_exhausted_by_aggregation: required
  material_source_change_can_alter_function: required
  smaller_subset_and_rival_composite_tested: required
  rival_frame_and_present_condition_pressure_retained: required
  source_trace_and_five_part_loss_preserved: required
```

This packet is not a new schema or automated decision object. It expresses the substantive questions that prose, evidence, and the Shared Transformation Record must answer.

The following do not cross the threshold by themselves:

- number of components;
- duration of the composite;
- frequency of repeated occurrences;
- graph density or centrality;
- institutional reach;
- public visibility or historical salience;
- administrative classification;
- convergence of labels;
- formal completeness of YAML fields;
- Reader placement or cross-reference density;
- model-generated similarity or clustering.

```text
many + long + repeated + visible
≠ function formed

formally complete record
≠ threshold substantively crossed
```

The threshold remains relative to the tested function. The same source may cross a boundary-function threshold in one target context while failing an integration-function threshold in the same context, or may support a function in `H` but not in `I`. A threshold decision must therefore name the function, target context, time window, roles, praxis dimensions, source boundary, competing account, and Claim Ceiling.

A threshold is crossed only when the stronger projection yields more discriminating praxeological purchase than the weaker alternatives while preserving source trace. That difference may concern access, exclusion, continuation, repair, breach, burden transfer, role capacity, exit friction, alternative visibility, or temporal reach. It may not be supplied merely by a more impressive label.

#### C34-SUBSETSUFF-01 — narrower subsets and alternative composites remain live, and an overinclusive source requires Claim Reduction

Smaller-subset sufficiency tests whether the declared composite is larger than the target function requires. The procedure asks:

1. Does a subset of components carry all identified target work?
2. Does removing peripheral components leave the relation topology and target difference unchanged?
3. Do excluded components contribute countervailing or incompatible load that the larger claim suppresses?
4. Does a differently bounded composite explain the function with less compression and fewer unsupported relations?
5. Does the larger source add a distinct praxeological difference, or merely narrative breadth?
6. Does the smaller subset alter scope, roles, time, Loss, or competing explanations?

```text
smaller subset carries the full warranted target difference
+ larger composite adds no discriminating work
→ larger source is overinclusive
→ claim_reduction_required remains available
```

Claim Reduction does not imply that excluded components are irrelevant in every analysis. It states only that they do not carry the tested function under the current source, target, and Claim Scope. A removed component may remain historically important, background-relevant, or constitutive for another function.

The test also protects against false minimality. A smaller subset is not automatically preferable. If its apparent sufficiency depends on hiding redundancy, repair, distributed substitution, countervailing relations, or temporal continuity supplied by the wider composite, the reduced source may fall below the actual Traceable Load requirement. The comparison must therefore disclose what each boundary preserves, compresses, excludes, leaves uncertain, or renders irrecoverable.

#### C34-FRAMEARTIFACT-01 — justified rival Frames must be tested so that analytical boundary selection cannot manufacture a macrofunction

A source composite may appear coherent because one analytical Frame selects compatible components, suppresses countervailing relations, synchronizes non-equivalent periods, or treats administrative proximity as functional relation. A justified rival Frame must therefore test whether the proposed formation persists when the source field is reconstructed under another defensible boundary.

Relevant rival-Frame pressures include:

- narrower role or institutional Frames;
- different temporal windows;
- access-centered versus burden-centered Frames;
- local-practice versus cross-field coordination Frames;
- realized-path versus unavailable-alternative Frames;
- formal-rule versus practical-operation Frames;
- repair-centered versus continuity-centered Frames;
- source-population Frames that retain missing, interrupted, or contrary occurrences.

```text
function appears only under one untested broad Frame
→ frame-selection artifact remains possible

function survives justified rival-Frame pressure
≠ unique or final Frame proven
```

Frame sensitivity may narrow or split the function rather than simply defeat it. A boundary-function may remain supported for one role but not another; an access function may reverse under an exposure-centered Frame; an integration-function may be visible procedurally while substantive conflict remains outside its scope. Such results require bounded claims, not forced unification.

No universal threshold follows from these tests. The method requires explicit vulnerability to reduction, rival boundaries, and no-projection. It does not promise a context-free formula for deciding emergence or macrofunction truth.

<a id="34-10-higher-level-function-without-authority-increase"></a>

### 34.10 Higher-Level Function without Authority Increase

A higher-level function can improve reconstruction without increasing the authority of the claim. It may make distributed relations, delayed consequences, cross-field coordination, or cumulative access effects more legible. That analytical gain remains subordinate to source quality, target scope, Claim Ceiling, and the same governance prohibitions that apply at every other relative level.

Before authority boundaries are stated, the complete family claim must undergo an integrated counterfactual test.

#### Concrete occurrence variation

When a component is PMS operator-typed, variation alters or removes the concrete occurrence or its relation while preserving the abstract operator definition and canonical grammar. The chapter does not require a full Δ–Ψ variation matrix; only family- and claim-relevant occurrences are tested.

#### C34-CFTEST-01 — components, relations, subsets, target contexts, target conditions, source Frames, and function candidates must be varied without converting sensitivity into causal proof

The **Counterfactual Higher-Level Function Test** asks at least:

1. **Component variation:** what changes when a component is removed, added, materially altered, or shown uncertain?
2. **Substitution variation:** can a warranted replacement preserve the function, and with what change in scope or Loss?
3. **Relation variation:** what changes when coordination, reinforcement, dependency, sequence, load transfer, translation, or repair is disrupted while components remain present?
4. **Boundary variation:** does a narrower, wider, or differently bounded source composite carry the target work more responsibly?
5. **Target-context variation:** does the same source perform the candidate function in `H` but not in `I`?
6. **Target-condition variation:** do current rules, resources, roles, infrastructure, or expectations explain the target difference without the source composite?
7. **Frame variation:** does a justified rival Frame preserve, narrow, reverse, split, or defeat the formation claim?
8. **Function-candidate variation:** is another bounded function better supported than the proposed boundary-, attractor-, asymmetry/access-, binding-, or integration-function?
9. **Internal-conflict variation:** do countervailing or incompatible components weaken, redirect, or divide the function?
10. **Missingness variation:** would inaccessible, excluded, interrupted, or contrary occurrences materially change the source or target claim?

Possible qualitative results include:

```text
sensitive
partially sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
not testable with available sources
```

These are Counterfactual Sensitivity descriptions, not new Output Classes.

```text
counterfactual sensitivity
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ causal necessity
≠ causal percentage
≠ prediction
≠ unique source boundary
```

A function that survives some component replacement may reflect distributed redundancy rather than causal independence. A function that disappears after relation disruption may support relation load without proving causal monopoly. A function that varies across `H/I` may demonstrate contextual boundedness without proving why the difference occurred. The test disciplines the claim; it does not automate empirical causality.

The pressure object remains open. No component of `Q` has been removed, substituted, or assigned a substantive role; no rival Frame, subset, present-condition account, or function candidate has been selected; and no `H/I` result has been established during WP3.

#### C34-AUTHORITY-01 — higher-level legibility, comparison, or reconstruction never increases truth, rank, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority

The governing reduction is:

```text
higher-level function
→ potentially wider relational legibility

higher-level function
≠ higher truth
≠ stronger empirical warrant
≠ causal privilege
≠ ontological rank
≠ normative legitimacy
≠ recommendation
≠ sanction
≠ governance authority
≠ authority inheritance
```

A higher-level function does not authorize action against a person, group, institution, culture, or practice. It does not assign collective intention, shared interiority, maturity, guilt, responsibility essence, or identity. It does not establish legal validity, political legitimacy, moral rank, diagnostic status, or intervention priority.

The relative level is analytical. “Higher” means only that the object occupies a composite or contextual position relative to declared source elements. It does not mean better, deeper, more real, more complete, more important, or more authoritative.

Formalization does not alter this boundary:

```text
schema-valid record
+ complete relation map
+ reproducible model check
+ passed smoke test
≠ substantive macrofunction truth
≠ application authority
```

The model may verify that fields, operations, Output Classes, Loss, Claim Ceiling, Stop conditions, and inventory constraints are present and consistent. It may not decide whether the relation topology is empirically true, whether emergence occurred, which Frame is correct, whether a threshold was crossed, or what should be done.

The same authority ceiling applies when the function improves comparison across cases. Cross-case recurrence or formal similarity cannot turn a bounded projection into a universal type. Chapter 34 remains structural and claim-bound.

<a id="34-11-failed-higher-level-projection"></a>

### 34.11 Failed Higher-Level Projection

A higher-level projection fails when the declared `PROJECT_AS` claim cannot sustain its source, relation, target, threshold, type, continuity, Loss, counterfactual, or authority requirements. Failure must be localized to the precise claim position. It must not spread automatically downward to each source component or upward into a judgment about persons, institutions, or domains.

#### C34-FAILURE-01 — failed higher-level projection is localized without automatically invalidating components or relations, while Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain distinct

Different failure locations must remain separable:

| Failure location | What fails | What does not automatically fail |
|---|---|---|
| component warrant | one or more source occurrences are unsupported or misbounded | every other component; the entire domain |
| relation warrant | claimed coordination, reinforcement, dependency, or sequence lacks support | individual component occurrences |
| composite boundary | source is overinclusive, retrospective, or Frame-manufactured | all narrower or rival composites |
| target placement | target context, relative level, roles, or time are misdeclared | source composite as source object |
| target function | no distinct bounded function is supported | descriptive aggregation or background relevance |
| threshold | target difference is insufficient or exhausted by weaker alternatives | all source history or local effects |
| type integrity | function is treated as □, Α, Ω, Σ, Ψ, or a new primitive | bounded function vocabulary after correction |
| Counterfactual Sensitivity | claim is insensitive, underdetermined, or not testable | possibility of a narrower future claim |
| authority boundary | truth, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or person/group type is inferred | structural reconstruction within a reduced Claim Ceiling |

The canonical routing distinctions are:

- **`claim_reduction_required`** where a smaller subset, narrower Frame, shorter time window, fewer roles, or weaker descriptive claim is supported;
- **`mandatory_stop`** where continuation would require invented relations, target-driven source selection, erased contradiction, source-free emergence, operator replacement, person/group typing, causal monopoly, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority inheritance;
- **`failed_transformation`** where the declared projection has been tested and does not meet its required source-to-target conditions;
- **`non_capture`** where available sources cannot responsibly choose among non-equivalent composites, Frames, relation maps, function candidates, or target accounts.

```text
failed higher-level projection
≠ all source components false
≠ all source relations irrelevant
≠ lower-level reconstruction erased
≠ no future narrower claim possible
```

A failed target function may leave a valid relational composite, descriptive aggregate, local operator-typed occurrences, or source-side `COMPOSE`/`DECOMPOSE` result intact. Conversely, a valid source composite does not protect a failed projection. Each transformation remains a new testable claim.

The exact five-part Loss declaration remains mandatory:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Failure analysis must disclose whether local variation was compressed, contrary components excluded, relations uncertain, or heterogeneity irrecoverably lost. A role label such as `background`, `countervailing`, or `incompatible` does not replace Loss accounting.

The `Q/H/I` pressure object remains unexecuted and unclassified. WP3 does not conclude that `Q` is a functional formation, that a smaller subset is sufficient, that one rival Frame is superior, that a target function exists in `H`, that no-projection holds in `I`, or that any canonical Output Class applies to the anchor.

#### C34-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete family architecture and Chapter-35 preparation boundary without RETYPE lock or new case/test YAML production

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Chapter 34 WP3 complete
→ Chapter 34 WP4 integrated lock audit
→ verify all 24 duties and §§34.1–34.11
→ decide only the family-method provisional lock
→ prepare Chapter 35 without drafting it
```

WP4 receives:

- the independently warranted relational-composite source requirements;
- stable source/target coordinates and same-source `H/I` contrast;
- five bounded operator-like function families;
- source-traceable emergence;
- qualitative, claim-bound component roles;
- relation-topology, substitution, redundancy, smaller-subset, overinclusive-source, relation-disruption, contradiction, rival-Frame, present-condition, and missingness pressures;
- aggregation versus functional formation;
- a qualitative, context-sensitive threshold;
- the integrated Counterfactual Higher-Level Function Test;
- exact Loss, Claim Ceiling, authority prohibition, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture routes.

WP4 does not inherit:

- selected component roles for `A1–A4`;
- an established functional formation or threshold crossing;
- a preferred subset, composite boundary, rival Frame, present-condition account, or function candidate;
- a function in `H` or no-projection in `I`;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result or canonical Output Class for the pressure object;
- permission to create new test, case, or smoke YAML files during Block drafting;
- RETYPE lock;
- empirical truth, causal monopoly, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority.

The Chapter-35 preparation boundary is also fixed. Chapter 35 may examine operator weighting, modulation, and emergent functional profiles only after Chapter 34 confirms that higher-level functions remain relation-traceable, non-primitive, threshold-bound, non-authoritative, and vulnerable to reduction, rival Frames, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture. Chapter 34 does not predefine Chapter-35 profiles, weighting criteria, or projection results.

<a id="chapter-34-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 34.8–34.11 complete the Chapter-34 family architecture by separating aggregation from functional formation, requiring a qualitative and context-sensitive threshold, retaining smaller subsets and rival Frames, supplying the integrated counterfactual test, prohibiting every authority increase, and localizing failed projection without erasing valid source structure.

```text
aggregation/formation boundary explicit
+ threshold remains praxis-difference based and non-numerical
+ subset, Frame, target-condition, and function rivals retained
+ counterfactual test complete
+ no authority increase permitted
+ Failure, Stop, Reduction, and Non-Capture separated
+ no pressure-object result fabricated
→ WP3 complete
```

This result does not establish component roles, functional formation, a threshold crossing, source-traceable emergence in the pressure object, a function in `H`, no-projection in `I`, a preferred source boundary or Frame, local `PROJECT_AS` result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-34 provisional lock, Chapter-35 content, RETYPE lock, or additional authority.

<a id="chapter-34-completion-boundary"></a>

### Chapter 34 Completion Boundary

Chapter 34 provisionally locks the **composite-to-higher-level-function family method**. The lock concerns the conditions under which a relational composite may be tested as a bounded target function. It does not establish that the stable `Q/H/I` pressure object actually forms such a function.

The integrated family relation is:

```text
independently warranted components
+ source-supported relation topology
+ target-blind composite boundary
+ declared target context and function
+ concrete target-level praxis difference
+ qualitative threshold and live weaker alternatives
+ Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, Stop, and Non-Capture
→ admissible higher-level-function test architecture
```

The chapter-level method result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This is not the result of an executed `PROJECT_AS(Q, H)` or `PROJECT_AS(Q, I)` occurrence. It is the result of the chapter-method audit only.

#### Integrated family-method finding

The completed architecture requires both **components and relations**. Component presence, number, duration, repetition, density, centrality, salience, institutional naming, Reader grouping, or schema completeness cannot substitute for a relation map or target difference. A relational composite must be independently warranted before target-function adjudication, and any material change in its components, boundaries, relations, temporal scope, Frame, role set, evidence, or inherited Loss creates a new source and/or projection claim.

Five bounded target-function families remain distinguishable:

```text
coordinated local differences
→ possible higher-level boundary-function

repeated source-supported Non-Events
→ possible higher-level attractor-function

distributed coordinated asymmetries
→ possible higher-level asymmetry/access function

repeated commitments with continuity and breach/exit consequences
→ possible higher-level binding-function

multiple related local integrations
→ possible higher-level integration-function
```

None becomes a PMS operator type:

```text
higher-level boundary-function ≠ □
higher-level attractor-function ≠ Α
higher-level asymmetry/access function ≠ Ω
higher-level binding-function ≠ Ψ
higher-level integration-function ≠ Σ
```

The source object retains its derived relational-composite origin type. The target function remains contextual, bounded, source-traceable, defeasible, and non-primitive.

#### Source-traceable emergence and relational load

The chapter fixes a strict meaning of emergence:

```text
visible only at composite level
+ reconstructible through warranted components and relations
+ sensitive to material source changes
+ exact uncertainty and Loss retained
→ source-traceable emergent-function candidate
```

Emergence therefore does not permit source-free novelty, hidden causal essence, ontological elevation, or macro-level immunity from component and relation tests. A function that cannot be reconstructed through its source load has crossed the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling.

Component roles remain qualitative and claim-bound:

```text
constitutive
modulating
redundant
substitutable
countervailing
background
uncertain
incompatible
```

They are not scores, causal percentages, permanent component types, or properties of persons and groups. Relation topology must remain visible alongside component roles, including coordination, reinforcement, dependency, sequence, shared effect, compensation, contradiction, and disruption.

#### Aggregation, threshold, and weaker alternatives

Descriptive aggregation remains a legitimate weaker result. It may preserve co-presence, distribution, repetition, or grouping without claiming a distinct target function.

```text
descriptive aggregation
≠ failed source description
≠ functional formation
```

Functional formation requires a concrete target-context praxis difference that is not exhausted by component accumulation, analytical grouping, current target conditions, or a more responsible narrower source. The threshold is qualitative, context-sensitive, function-specific, and source-bound. It cannot be reduced to a universal count, duration, recurrence rate, density measure, profile score, or model-completeness test.

The method remains vulnerable to:

- component removal and replacement;
- redundancy and substitutability;
- smaller-subset sufficiency;
- overinclusive or misbounded source composites;
- relation disruption while components remain;
- internal contradiction and countervailing load;
- justified rival Frames;
- changed target contexts and current target conditions;
- source missingness, uncertainty, and excluded counterstructure;
- alternative function candidates and no-projection.

A narrower subset is not automatically superior, because the wider composite may carry redundancy, repair, substitution capacity, temporal continuity, or distributed load. Conversely, the wider source cannot be protected merely because it is more comprehensive. Source breadth and functional sufficiency remain separately testable.

#### Counterfactual and authority boundary

The Counterfactual Higher-Level Function Test varies components, substitutions, relations, composite boundaries, Frames, target contexts, target conditions, function candidates, internal conflict, and missingness. The results remain qualitative:

```text
sensitive
partially sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
not testable with available sources
```

These descriptions do not prove causal necessity, causal sufficiency, causal monopoly, causal percentage, prediction, or a uniquely correct source boundary.

The authority ceiling remains absolute:

```text
higher-level legibility
≠ higher truth
≠ stronger empirical warrant
≠ causal privilege
≠ ontological rank
≠ legitimacy
≠ recommendation
≠ sanction
≠ governance authority
```

A schema-valid record, complete relation map, reproducible model check, successful smoke test, or polished visualization cannot supply substantive emergence or macrofunction truth. No target function licenses person, group, institution, culture, identity, maturity, guilt, intention, or responsibility essence.

#### Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and source preservation

Failure must be localized to the exact claim position: component warrant, relation warrant, composite boundary, target placement, target function, threshold, Type Integrity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, or authority boundary.

The canonical routes remain distinct:

- `claim_reduction_required` where a narrower source, Frame, time window, role set, or weaker descriptive claim is warranted;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation would require invented relations, erased contradiction, target-driven source selection, source-free emergence, operator replacement, person/group typing, causal monopoly, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority inheritance;
- `failed_transformation` where the declared projection has been tested and fails its source-to-target conditions;
- `non_capture` where available sources cannot responsibly select among non-equivalent source composites, Frames, relation maps, function candidates, or target accounts.

```text
failed higher-level projection
≠ each source component false
≠ each source relation irrelevant
≠ valid source-side COMPOSE or DECOMPOSE result erased
```

A new Frame, relative level, granularity, source composite, or target function is a new testable claim. It cannot repair a failed earlier claim by relabeling it.

#### Protected non-adjudication of the pressure object

The stable pressure object remains deliberately unexecuted:

```yaml
source:
  object: distributed_local_asymmetry_composite_Q
  origin_type: derived_relational_composite

target_H:
  context: institutional_access_field_H
  proposed_function: bounded_higher_level_asymmetry_access_function

target_I:
  context: parallel_local_occurrence_field_I
  proposed_possibility: no_distinct_higher_level_function
```

The chapter lock does not establish:

- substantive component roles for `A1–A4`;
- a preferred component subset or composite boundary;
- a preferred rival Frame or present-condition account;
- functional formation or a crossed threshold for `Q`;
- source-traceable emergence in the pressure object;
- a higher-level function in `H`;
- no-projection in `I`;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class for the pressure object.

```text
Chapter 34 provisionally locked
≠ Q established as functional formation
≠ threshold crossed
≠ function established in H
≠ no-projection established in I
≠ RETYPE locked
```

#### Integrated completion decision

All §§34.1–34.11 are complete. All twenty-four assigned Chapter-34 duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. The method preserves source/target independence, components and relations, operator-type integrity, exact Loss, rival Frames, smaller subsets, contradiction, no-projection, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and the prohibition of authority inheritance.

```text
complete composite-to-higher-level-function family specification
+ twenty-four-duty closure
+ integrated scientific and formal audit pass
+ protected non-adjudication of Q/H/I
→ Chapter 34 provisionally locked
```

The chapter-level methodological class is `admissible_but_provisional`. This class does not flow to the pressure object and does not preselect any later case result.

#### Chapter-35 handoff boundary

Chapter 35 receives the following locked constraints:

- weighting concerns existing operator occurrences and their configuration-bound relative load, accessibility, visibility, threshold, or temporal effect;
- weighting never changes Δ–Ψ order or dependencies;
- a modulator is a contextual condition, not an operator or fourth STRATA operation;
- a profile is a bounded relation among existing occurrences, not a formal type or person essence;
- emergent profile visibility must remain source-traceable;
- profile description and profile projection are separate claims;
- a projected profile requires a concrete later-praxis difference, qualitative threshold, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, and live no-projection;
- attractive labels, add-on vocabulary, schema validity, and repeated examples add no warrant or authority.

Chapter 35 receives no preselected weighting, modulator, profile, trajectory effect, target function, local operation result, Output Class, person/group type, recommendation, sanction, governance authority, or RETYPE lock.

<a id="chapter-35-operator-weighting-modulation-and-emergent-functional-profiles"></a>

## 35. Operator Weighting, Modulation, and Emergent Functional Profiles

Chapter 35 applies the RETYPE discipline to configuration-bound differences in the relative load, accessibility, visibility, threshold effect, and temporal effect of already warranted PMS operator occurrences. It does not reopen the Δ–Ψ grammar, assign numerical weights to operator types, infer hidden traits from symbolic distributions, or treat a compact profile label as a functional result. PMS Base owns the meanings, sequence, and dependencies of Δ–Ψ. Chapters 20–22 own source-side decomposition of occurrences, relations, redundancy, substitution, and internal conflict. Chapters 29–30 own origin-type preservation and the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure. Chapter 34 owns source-traceable composite-level functional formation. This chapter therefore asks a narrower family-specific question:

> When may differences among already warranted operator occurrences and contextual conditions be described as a bounded weighting-and-modulation packet, and when may that packet later support a source-traceable profile claim—without reordering PMS, inventing a new operator, converting configuration description into person typing, or smuggling prediction and authority into RETYPE?

The entry claim is deliberately modest. A Configuration or Composite can make some operator occurrences more load-bearing, accessible, visible, temporally persistent, or threshold-relevant than others. Such relative prominence may matter for later reconstruction. It does not follow that the prominent operators are independent, exclusive, causal, numerically dominant, or predictive. Nor does it follow that a stable profile or projected target function exists. Weighting, modulation, profile formation, and profile projection are distinct claim positions.

```text
operator weighting
≠ operator replacement
≠ dependency reordering

modulator ≠ operator
modulator ≠ operation
modulator ≠ person or group type

modulator
≠ PMS operator
≠ STRATA operation
≠ person or group type
```

The stable pressure architecture is:

```text
source configuration:
  operator_weighting_configuration_P
  origin type: configuration-bound relational composite
  candidate description: omega_theta_prominent
  status: candidate only

contextual conditions:
  access_condition_m1
  timing_condition_m2
  resource_constraint_m3

positive target candidate:
  later_continuation_field_K
  proposed function: bounded continuation-cost modulation function

negative and rival target:
  parallel_continuation_field_L
  proposed possibility: no distinct profile function
```

The source operator occurrences, relation map, Frame, roles, temporal window, modulators, uncertainty, prior disposition, and inherited five-part Loss must remain fixed across `K` and `L`. A material change in any of these positions creates a new source and/or projection claim. `K` is not presumed positive. `L` is not a residual failure scene. Both are legitimate target contexts for testing whether a source-side weighting and modulation packet performs additional later-praxis work.

WP1 establishes the family purpose, source-occurrence packet, qualitative weighting grammar, dependency-preservation boundary, modulator grammar, stable target coordinates, and anti-typing/anti-authority ceiling. It does not establish a stable profile, an emergent profile, a trajectory effect, a target function in `K`, no-projection in `L`, a local `PROJECT_AS` result, or a canonical Output Class. Those burdens remain open for §§35.5–35.12.

<a id="35-1-purpose"></a>

### 35.1 Purpose

The purpose of this chapter is to make operator-relative load and contextual modulation analytically legible without converting either into an alteration of PMS Base. The relevant source object is not an operator type and not a person. It is a declared Configuration or Composite in which concrete operator occurrences and their relations have already been warranted under the current Frame, granularity, relative level, role set, temporal scope, source basis, uncertainty, and Claim Ceiling.

#### Invoked-occurrence scope

The source packet lists only operator occurrences invoked by the weighting claim, together with material countervailing or uncertain occurrences. It is not a complete Δ–Ψ census. An occurrence that is not invoked or not source-supported is not assigned an implicit zero weight and is not declared absent from every possible reconstruction.

```text
occurrence not invoked or not source-supported
≠ zero weight
≠ operator function absent in every sense
```

#### C35-BASIC-01 — the family claim binds source configuration, origin type, optional target function, and non-authority boundary

The minimum family form is:

```text
Within declared source configuration P,
already warranted operator occurrences O1 ... On
carry a qualitative weighting relation W
under contextual modulators M,
while P retains origin type configuration-bound relational composite.

Within separately declared target context K or L,
P may later be tested through PROJECT_AS
for bounded target function F_p,
without changing Δ–Ψ, creating a profile type,
or inheriting authority.
```

Each claim position remains separate:

| Claim position | Required declaration | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| source object `P` | identifiable Configuration or Composite | profile or target function |
| operator occurrences | independently warranted source occurrences | numerical weight or dominance |
| relation map | how occurrences condition, support, oppose, delay, or stabilize one another | causal closure |
| weighting relation `W` | qualitative relative load, access, visibility, threshold, or temporal effect | dependency reordering |
| modulators `M` | contextual conditions affecting the occurrence field | operator or operation identity |
| target context `K/L` | bounded later continuation field | profile-function success |
| proposed target function `F_p` | concrete later-praxis difference | prediction, legitimacy, or authority |

A weighting-and-modulation claim is analytically useful only if it changes a warranted reconstruction. It may clarify why one occurrence becomes more accessible, why a threshold is reached earlier or later, why a Non-Event remains central, why an asymmetry carries more practical load, why distance becomes difficult to sustain, or why one continuation becomes more costly. A statement that “Ω and Θ are important here” is not yet a weighting claim because it has not declared relative to what, for whom, during which period, through which relations, and with what source support.

The chapter therefore preserves three nested but non-equivalent claims:

```text
operator-occurrence warrant
≠ qualitative weighting warrant
≠ profile-formation warrant
≠ profile-projection warrant
```

A source Configuration may support a weighting claim while failing to support a stable profile. A stable descriptive profile may remain functionally inert in `K` and `L`. A projected function may be admissible in one target context and unavailable in another. None of these differences changes the source operator types.

The authority ceiling applies from the first sentence of the family claim:

```text
more legible weighting
≠ more truth
≠ psychological access
≠ prediction
≠ legitimacy
≠ recommendation
≠ sanction
≠ governance authority
```

The family is therefore a bounded method for reconstructing configuration-relative load and possible later function. It is not a method for scoring people, ranking institutions, diagnosing cultures, or generating action directives.

#### C35-SOURCEOCC-01 — weighting requires independently warranted occurrences, relations, coordinates, uncertainty, and inherited Loss

Before any weighting language is admissible, the source packet must identify the occurrence-level basis of the claim. The minimum packet is:

```yaml
source:
  object: operator_weighting_configuration_P
  origin_type: configuration_bound_relational_composite
  source_reference: independently_warranted_occurrence_and_relation_record_P
  frame: declared_practice_frame_F
  granularity: declared_source_granularity
  relative_level: declared_source_level
  temporal_scope: period_P
  roles:
    - role_r1
    - role_r2
  operator_occurrence_traces:
    frame_occurrence: retained
    non_event_occurrence: retained
    attractor_occurrence: retained
    asymmetry_occurrence: retained
    temporality_occurrence: retained
    distance_occurrence: retained
    integration_occurrence: retained
    binding_occurrence: retained
  relation_trace:
    dependencies: retained
    temporal_order: retained
    reinforcement: retained_where_supported
    countervailing_relations: retained_where_supported
    uncertainty: explicit
  canonical_dependencies_preserved: true
  prior_disposition: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

The list does not require every PMS operator to have equal presence or equal evidential support. It requires that every occurrence invoked by the weighting claim be independently warranted and that relevant omitted, uncertain, countervailing, or inaccessible occurrences remain visible. An absent occurrence must not be fabricated to complete a profile. A poorly documented occurrence must not receive weight because a profile label expects it.

The source-selection rule is target-independent:

```text
select occurrences and relations because source evidence warrants them
→ then test weighting
→ then, if warranted, test profile formation
→ then, if warranted, test target function
```

The prohibited sequence is:

```text
desired profile or target outcome
→ select matching operator symbols
→ infer weighting
→ retrofit source evidence
```

Symbol frequency, word count, YAML field count, diagram prominence, graph centrality, heading density, Reader grouping, or recurrence of a label may indicate where to inspect. None independently warrants an operator occurrence or relative load. The source packet must remain reconstructible in ordinary source terms, including relevant Non-Events, role differences, time windows, access conditions, costs, uncertainty, and competing interpretations.

Weighting also inherits source-side loss. If role variation has already been compressed, local contexts excluded, temporal ordering rendered uncertain, or modulator conditions irrecoverably lost, the weighting claim cannot silently restore them. A profile label cannot repair missing source structure.

```text
formal occurrence inventory
≠ warranted occurrence field

warranted occurrence field
≠ weighting established

weighting candidate
≠ profile established
```

This source discipline protects Chapter 35 from becoming a vocabulary-driven classification layer. The source object remains a Configuration or Composite whose contents are claim-bound and revisable.

<a id="35-2-operator-weighting"></a>

### 35.2 Operator Weighting

Operator weighting designates a qualitative relation among already warranted operator occurrences within a declared source object. It does not assign weight to the abstract operator types themselves. The same operator type can carry different practical load across roles, phases, Frames, or target questions because its concrete occurrences and relations differ.

#### C35-WEIGHTING-01 — weighting is qualitative relative load, accessibility, visibility, threshold effect, or temporal effect

A weighting claim must state at least one declared dimension:

| Weighting dimension | Family-specific question | Prohibited shortcut |
|---|---|---|
| relative load | Which occurrence carries more of the current reconstruction and why? | symbol count |
| accessibility | Which operator function becomes easier or harder to enact, maintain, or revise? | presumed capacity |
| visibility | Which occurrence becomes more structurally legible or obscured within the Frame? | textual prominence |
| threshold effect | Which occurrence materially changes when a transition, repair, exit, or stabilization threshold is reached? | universal score |
| temporal effect | Which occurrence persists, accumulates, delays, reverses, or becomes phase-bound? | duration alone |

The dimensions may co-occur, but they must not be collapsed. An occurrence can be highly visible but carry little constitutive load. A background occurrence can be weakly visible while remaining necessary. An asymmetry occurrence can be highly load-bearing for `role_r1` and only modulating for `role_r2`. A distance occurrence can become briefly decisive during one phase without defining the entire Configuration.

Illustrative labels such as

```text
Λ / Θ prominent
Ω / Θ prominent
Χ / Σ stabilizing
Α prominent
Ψ sensitive
```

are admissible only as compressed candidate descriptions. Each must expand into a source-traceable statement. For example:

```text
“Ω / Θ prominent”
→ specified asymmetry occurrences
+ specified temporal accumulation or sequencing
+ declared roles and time window
+ relations to Frame, Non-Event, Attractor, Distance, Integration, and Binding occurrences
+ uncertainty and Loss
```

The label does not establish that Ω and Θ are the only relevant occurrences, that they cause the Configuration, that they dominate every role and phase, or that the profile predicts continuation. Prominence remains relational and question-bound.

Weighting must also distinguish constitution from modulation. A source occurrence may be necessary for the Configuration to remain the same object, may alter the intensity or timing of another occurrence, may counteract a dominant tendency, or may remain evidentially uncertain. WP1 does not assign these roles to `P`; it only requires that later profile formation be able to distinguish them.

The weighting vocabulary is qualitative because the chapter supplies no universal metric. A later domain method may independently warrant measurement, but that measurement would be an additional claim with its own scale, calibration, evidence, uncertainty, and authority limits. STRATA does not infer weights from the formal model.

```text
qualitative weighting
≠ arbitrary impression

non-numerical
≠ untestable

measurable in a later domain
≠ measured by STRATA automatically
```

A weighting claim remains testable through role variation, phase variation, source substitution, occurrence removal, changed access conditions, and rival descriptions. If the claimed prominence survives every material change, the weighting language may be too elastic to carry discriminating load.

<a id="35-3-weighting-is-not-reordering"></a>

### 35.3 Weighting Is Not Reordering

PMS operator order and dependencies are not empirical weights. They define the canonical grammar. A Chapter-35 weighting claim operates only over concrete occurrences whose dependencies remain visible. Relative salience within one Configuration cannot rewrite the conditions under which an operator is defined.

#### C35-NOREORDER-01 — high relevance preserves Δ–Ψ order, dependencies, origin typing, and non-primitive status

The governing constraints are:

```text
high Ω relevance
≠ Ω independent of Α and its canonical conditions

high Θ relevance
≠ Θ precedes or replaces the structures on which its occurrence depends

high Χ relevance
≠ Χ becomes a primitive

high Σ or Ψ relevance
≠ lower-order conditions disappear
```

A later operator can be more practically decisive in a particular reconstruction without becoming formally prior. A source Configuration may be most discriminatingly explained through an Ω/Θ relation while still retaining the Frames, Non-Events, attractor patterns, differences, impulses, and other relations required by the source occurrences. Likewise, a Χ/Σ-stabilizing candidate does not remove Φ, Θ, □, or the concrete conditions under which distance and integration become possible.

Four errors must remain distinct:

1. **operator replacement:** a prominent occurrence is treated as if it substitutes for other warranted occurrences;
2. **dependency deletion:** the source conditions of a prominent occurrence disappear from the reconstruction;
3. **dependency reordering:** practical prominence is converted into a new canonical sequence;
4. **primitive creation:** a recurring profile relation is named as a new PMS operator or derived primitive.

The admissible statement is configuration-relative:

```text
Within P and period_P,
for declared roles and claim scope,
Ω- and Θ-typed occurrences may carry greater relative load
while all canonical operator identities and dependencies remain unchanged.
```

The inadmissible statement is grammar-relative:

```text
Because Ω and Θ appear prominent in P,
Ω or Θ now organize PMS independently,
precede their dependencies,
or constitute a new operator family.
```

Operator weighting also cannot become a hidden fourth STRATA operation. `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` remain the complete operation inventory. Weighting is a source-side analytical relation that may be described within a record and may later become part of a projection source. It does not transform an object by itself.

```text
weighting declaration
≠ COMPOSE
≠ DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS
≠ fourth operation
```

Where a weighting claim requires source-side opening of a compressed object, the operation is `DECOMPOSE`. Where multiple occurrences are formed into a new relational composite, the operation is `COMPOSE`.
