Where an origin-typed profile source is tested for a bounded target function, the operation is `PROJECT_AS`. Chapter 35 does not blur those occurrences into “modulation” as an operation.

<a id="35-4-modulator"></a>

### 35.4 Modulator

A modulator is a contextual condition that changes how already warranted operator occurrences become accessible, reach a threshold, persist, stabilize, reverse, or interact. It does not belong to the Δ–Ψ inventory and does not name an additional STRATA operation. Its status is relational: the same condition may modulate one source relation, remain background in another, or have no discriminating effect in a third.

#### C35-MODULATOR-01 — a modulator affects access, threshold, timing, persistence, or stabilization without becoming an operator, operation, cause by label, or person type

Eligible modulator dimensions include:

| Modulator dimension | Possible bounded effect | What must remain open |
|---|---|---|
| access condition | changes whether a role can enact, contest, or exit a structure | alternative access explanation |
| timing condition | accelerates, delays, sequences, or phase-bounds an occurrence | Θ occurrence and chronology remain distinct |
| resource constraint | changes the cost or feasibility of continuation, repair, or distance | Ω and target-condition rivals |
| procedural condition | changes thresholds for recognition, decision, integration, or binding | formal rule versus practical effect |
| visibility condition | changes which occurrence is legible to which role | occurrence truth versus observer access |
| persistence condition | changes how long an attractor, Non-Event, asymmetry, or binding remains load-bearing | duration alone is insufficient |

The pressure object declares:

```text
m1 = access_condition_m1
m2 = timing_condition_m2
m3 = resource_constraint_m3
```

These names are placeholders for independently described contextual conditions. They do not establish that `m1–m3` have effects, are constitutive, are non-redundant, or jointly form a stable profile. WP2 must later test removal, substitution, reversal, redundancy, and changed access conditions.

A modulator must have a specified relation path:

```text
contextual condition m
→ changes access, threshold, timing, persistence, or stabilization
→ changes one or more declared source-occurrence relations
→ may alter a later praxis difference
```

The following is insufficient:

```text
condition is named a modulator
→ therefore modulation occurred
```

Modulator language therefore does not by itself establish causality. It identifies a candidate contextual relation to be tested. The condition may correlate with the observed Configuration, may be carried by another source relation, may be redundant with another condition, or may reflect a present target condition rather than historical source load.

The modulator boundary also protects persons and groups:

```text
person, group, institution, culture, or identity
≠ modulator as essence
```

A person may occupy a role within a contextual condition. A group may be part of an institutional arrangement. Neither becomes a modulator type. Chapter 35 reconstructs conditions and relations, not inherent characteristics.

#### C35-TARGETCTX-01 — the same source weighting/modulation packet is fixed across the declared `K/L` target contrast

The target packet is:

```yaml
target_K:
  context: later_continuation_field_K
  proposed_function: bounded_continuation_cost_modulation_function
  required_difference:
    - alternative_continuations_change_in_cost_or_accessibility
    - role_specific_exit_or_revision_conditions_change
    - later_path_selection_becomes_praxeologically_discriminating

target_L:
  context: parallel_continuation_field_L
  proposed_possibility: no_distinct_profile_function
  live_rivals:
    - present_target_conditions_only
    - single_operator_occurrence_suffices
    - alternative_profile_description
    - descriptive_profile_but_functionally_inert
    - no_stable_profile
```

Across `K/L`, the source object `P`, occurrence traces, canonical dependencies, Frame, roles, time window, modulators, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss remain fixed. Only the declared target context and target-function question differ. If the analyst changes the source occurrences, weighting criterion, modulator set, relation map, or temporal scope to make `K` succeed, the result is a new claim rather than confirmation of the old one.

At WP1, the phrase `bounded_continuation_cost_modulation_function` names a target-function candidate only. No cost difference, access difference, exit difference, revision difference, path-selection difference, profile stability, or no-projection has been established. The target contrast merely preserves the possibility that the same source packet performs additional work in `K` but not in `L`, or that neither context supports a distinct function.

```text
same source packet
+ different target context
→ different testable function claim

changed source packet
→ new source and/or projection claim
```

This separation prevents profile formation from being inferred retrospectively from target fit.

#### C35-NOTYPE-01 — weighting and modulation remain configuration-bound and cannot become person/group essence, diagnosis, prediction, add-on authority, or inherited authority

All WP1 descriptions belong to `P` under declared coordinates. They do not classify the bearers of the occurrences.

```text
configuration-bound Ω/Θ-prominent candidate
≠ Ω/Θ person type
≠ institutional essence
≠ cultural profile
≠ maturity level
≠ diagnosis
≠ moral category
```

The same person or group can participate in several configurations with different weighting relations. Different roles inside one Configuration can carry different load. A profile that ignores this variation is overinclusive even before projection.

Weighting language also has no automatic predictive force. The statement that certain continuations are more accessible or costly under a source configuration is not a probability estimate and not a claim that later action must follow. Path dependence, constraints, and attractors may narrow or reshape possibilities without determining an outcome.

```text
relative continuation accessibility
≠ predicted action
≠ quantified probability
≠ inevitability
```

Add-on lenses may later provide stress cases or domain-specific rival descriptions. They do not define STRATA weighting criteria, authorize profile labels, create evidence, select a target result, or elevate a claim ceiling. Formal-model completeness and successful smoke tests likewise establish consistency of the artifacts, not substantive weighting truth.

The governance boundary remains:

```yaml
governance:
  person_typing: prohibited
  diagnosis: prohibited
  prediction_without_independent_evidence: prohibited
  legitimacy_inference: prohibited
  recommendation: prohibited
  sanction: prohibited
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### C35-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives the source, weighting, modulation, and target packet without profile or function adjudication

WP1 hands forward:

- the independently warranted source Configuration `P`;
- explicit operator-occurrence and relation traces;
- preserved Δ–Ψ order and dependencies;
- qualitative weighting dimensions;
- declared role and phase sensitivity;
- the non-operator modulator packet `m1–m3`;
- stable same-source `K/L` target coordinates;
- exact five-part Loss, uncertainty, Claim Ceiling, Stop, Non-Capture, and authority boundaries.

WP1 does not hand forward:

- a selected weighting for `P`;
- an established effect for any modulator;
- a stable or emergent profile;
- a trajectory or continuation effect;
- dominance or exclusivity;
- a preferred rival profile;
- a projected function in `K`;
- no-projection in `L`;
- a local operation result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-35 or RETYPE lock.

```text
source-occurrence packet complete
+ weighting grammar explicit
+ dependency preservation explicit
+ modulator and K/L packets explicit
≠ stable profile established
≠ trajectory effect established
≠ profile projection established
≠ Output Class selected
```

<a id="chapter-35-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 35.1–35.4 establish the bounded entry architecture for weighting and modulation. They preserve source independence, occurrence-level evidence, canonical dependencies, Configuration/Composite scope, qualitative and role-sensitive weighting, non-operator modulation, target-context separation, exact Loss, anti-typing, anti-prediction, and non-authority.

The stable `P/K/L` pressure object remains unexecuted. `omega_theta_prominent` remains a candidate description only. `m1–m3` remain candidate contextual conditions only. No profile, target function, no-projection result, local `PROJECT_AS` result, or canonical Output Class has been selected.

The next controlled step is WP2, §§35.5–35.7: Modulating Profile, From Weighting to Trajectory Form, and Emergent Functional Profile.

<a id="35-5-modulating-profile"></a>

### 35.5 Modulating Profile

A modulating profile is a bounded and source-traceable relation among already warranted operator occurrences, their qualitative weighting, and declared contextual modulators within one Configuration or Composite. It does not consist in collecting prominent symbols, assigning a memorable label, or converting a recurrent description into a formal type.

#### Label-removal completion question

A profile remains reconstructible from its declared occurrence and relation packet when the profile label is removed. If the underlying relation remains intelligible, the label may be acceptable compression. If the relation disappears with the label, the label is carrying work that belongs to Source Trace and creates possible profile-label substitution pressure.

```text
profile label removed
+ source relation still reconstructible
→ label may be acceptable compression

profile label removed
+ claim relation disappears
→ possible profile label substitution
```

#### C35-PROFILEFORM-01 — a modulating profile is a relation topology among warranted occurrences and modulators, not a list, label, score, or type

A minimally adequate profile packet contains:

```yaml
modulating_profile_candidate:
  source_object: operator_weighting_configuration_P
  source_scope:
    frame: declared_practice_frame_F
    temporal_scope: period_P
    roles:
      - role_r1
      - role_r2
  warranted_occurrences: retained
  canonical_dependencies: preserved
  occurrence_relations:
    support: declared
    opposition: declared
    sequence: declared
    accessibility: declared
    threshold_relation: declared
    persistence_relation: declared
  qualitative_weighting_dimensions:
    - relative_load
    - accessibility
    - visibility
    - threshold_effect
    - temporal_effect
  contextual_modulators:
    - access_condition_m1
    - timing_condition_m2
    - resource_constraint_m3
  stability_window: declared_but_not_adjudicated
  role_and_phase_variation: retained
  uncertainty: retained
  loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

The packet must identify how occurrences relate. An inventory such as `Ω present`, `Θ present`, `Λ present`, and `m1 present` does not yet form a profile. The profile claim begins only where the relation topology explains why some occurrences become more accessible, persistent, threshold-relevant, or temporally load-bearing under the declared conditions.

```text
operator list
≠ weighting relation

weighting relation
≠ stable profile automatically

compact profile label
≠ formal type
≠ projected target function
```

The illustrative phrase `omega_theta_prominent` therefore remains a candidate compression of a relation packet. It is admissible only if every material part of that compression remains recoverable: which Ω- and Θ-occurrences are meant, for which roles and phases, through which dependencies and modulators, against which rival descriptions, and with which uncertainty and Loss.

A profile may be descriptively useful even when it supports no additional target function. Conversely, a rhetorically weak label may describe a source-traceable relation well. Profile quality therefore depends on discriminating relation trace, not linguistic attractiveness.

#### C35-STABILITY-01 — profile stability is bounded to a declared window and must preserve role, phase, context, and instability pressure

A profile is stable only relative to declared coordinates. Stability means that the material occurrence-and-relation pattern remains sufficiently reconstructible across the selected window to support one bounded description. It does not mean permanence, invariance, identity, or immunity from role and phase differences.

The stability audit must ask:

- Does the same relation topology recur across the declared phases, or does the proposed profile reverse?
- Do `role_r1` and `role_r2` carry materially different weighting relations?
- Does the profile persist when the Frame remains stable but access conditions change?
- Does it survive only because contradictory or low-visibility phases were excluded?
- Is the selected window narrow enough to manufacture apparent stability?
- Would a wider or differently justified temporal window produce a rival profile or no stable profile?

Possible local descriptions include:

```text
stable within declared window
phase-bounded
role-differentiated
intermittent
reversing
contested
insufficiently supported
no stable profile
```

These are local analytical descriptions, not new Output Classes. A profile may be stable for one role and unstable for another; it may be stable as a source description but functionally inert in `K`; or it may fail because the observed prominence disappears under a justified change of periodization.

```text
bounded stability
≠ timeless property
≠ person or group trait
≠ predictive power
```

Stability claims must therefore preserve the exact uncertainty and five-part Loss of the source packet. Compression of phase variation belongs under `compressed`; deliberate omission of out-of-scope material under `excluded`; unresolved weighting or modulator relations under `uncertain`; and local distinctions that cannot be reconstructed after profile compression under `irrecoverable`.

#### C35-DOMINANCE-01 — prominence or dominance never implies exclusivity and cannot erase non-prominent occurrences or dependencies

A source-supported profile may identify one occurrence or relation cluster as comparatively prominent. This means only that it carries more of the declared qualitative dimension under the current coordinates. It does not mean that other operators disappear, become causally irrelevant, or lose their PMS dependencies.

For the candidate phrase `omega_theta_prominent`, the following must remain visible:

- Ω depends on the warranted relational structures through which asymmetry becomes legible;
- Θ preserves sequence, duration, accumulation, and temporal position rather than replacing the occurrences it temporalizes;
- □, Λ, Α, Χ, Σ, Ψ, and other relevant occurrences may remain constitutive, modulating, countervailing, or necessary for the profile's intelligibility;
- prominence may differ by role, phase, or qualitative dimension;
- another profile may describe the same source more discriminatingly.

```text
prominent in relative load
≠ exclusive source of the Configuration

prominent in visibility
≠ constitutive in trajectory formation

prominent in one phase
≠ dominant across period_P

operator dominance claim
≠ dependency removal
≠ causal monopoly
```

The profile must fail or narrow if its name remains unchanged after material removal of allegedly constitutive occurrences, relations, or dependencies. Label invariance under source variation is evidence of profile inflation, not robustness.

<a id="35-6-from-weighting-to-trajectory-form"></a>

### 35.6 From Weighting to Trajectory Form

Operator weighting can contribute to a Path or Trajectory form only through source-supported temporal relations. Chapter 35 does not infer a trajectory from a static profile and does not convert a weighting description into prediction. The question is narrower: does the declared weighting-and-modulation packet alter how transitions, Non-Events, attractors, costs, or continuations are structured across time?

#### C35-TRAJECTORYFORM-01 — weighting may shape transition accessibility, Λ-centrality, attractor persistence, cost sedimentation, or continuation without determining outcomes or probabilities

A trajectory-form claim requires a previously warranted temporal object or sufficient PATH reconstruction. The profile must then make a discriminating difference in at least one declared dimension:

| Temporal dimension | Required source-traceable difference | Prohibited inference |
|---|---|---|
| transition accessibility | some transitions become easier, harder, delayed, or role-specific | inevitable transition |
| Non-Event centrality | a warranted expected non-occurrence becomes more structurally load-bearing | every absence is Λ |
| attractor persistence | recurrence becomes easier to resume or harder to interrupt | Α causes the whole path |
| cost sedimentation | continuation, exit, repair, revision, or distance costs accumulate asymmetrically | quantified causal share |
| continuation field | later alternatives differ in accessibility, visibility, or feasibility | probability or predicted choice |
| temporal reversal | a later phase changes or counteracts the earlier weighting relation | earlier profile retroactively false |

The relevant form is:

```text
warranted source occurrences and relations
+ qualitative weighting
+ declared modulators
+ temporal sequence and phase trace
→ candidate difference in trajectory form
```

Not:

```text
profile label
→ later outcome predicted
```

A profile can constrain or bias the continuation field without selecting one continuation. Even strong path dependence remains compatible with intervention, interruption, new events, changed Frames, altered resources, distance, integration, and Non-Capture.

```text
trajectory-form influence
≠ determinism
≠ statistical probability
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ teleology
```

For `P`, WP2 does not establish that `omega_theta_prominent` changes transition accessibility, Λ-centrality, attractor persistence, cost sedimentation, or continuation. It establishes only the conditions under which such a claim could later be tested.

#### C35-MODVAR-01 — modulator removal, substitution, reversal, redundancy, and changed access must be tested without treating modulators as causes by label

The modulator packet `m1–m3` must remain counterfactually vulnerable. At minimum, the following variants are required:

| Variation | Test question | Possible bounded implication |
|---|---|---|
| removal | Does the profile or trajectory-form difference persist without `m1`, `m2`, or `m3`? | alleged modulator may be non-constitutive or profile may collapse |
| substitution | Can another condition carry the same access, timing, or resource relation? | original modulator may be replaceable or misidentified |
| reversal | Does reversing timing, access, or resource direction alter the profile? | directional sensitivity or profile failure |
| redundancy | Do two modulators carry the same relevant work? | narrower packet or Claim Reduction |
| changed access | Does role-specific access change the weighting relation? | one aggregate profile may be overinclusive |
| target-condition isolation | Does the later difference remain when present `K/L` conditions are held apart from source modulators? | present-condition rival may outperform source-profile claim |

The test concerns relations, not names. A condition does not become causal merely because the record calls it a modulator. If removing `m2` changes nothing, the correct response may be redundancy, background relevance, an alternative mechanism, or insufficient evidence. If substituting another timing condition preserves the same relation, the profile may remain valid while the claim about `m2` narrows.

```text
modulator sensitivity
≠ causal proof

modulator removal effect
≠ causal monopoly

modulator redundancy
≠ source Configuration irrelevant
```

A material change to the source modulator packet creates a new profile-formation claim. It cannot be used silently to rescue the original `P/K/L` anchor.

#### C35-RIVALPROFILE-01 — alternative profiles, narrower relations, descriptive-but-inert profiles, and no-stable-profile remain live

A credible profile method must preserve serious rivals. For the same source `P`, at least the following remain available:

1. **Alternative weighting profile:** another occurrence relation better explains the source Configuration.
2. **Role-split profiles:** `role_r1` and `role_r2` require different descriptions.
3. **Phase-split profiles:** the proposed relation reverses or changes across the temporal window.
4. **Narrower source relation:** one smaller occurrence-and-modulator relation carries all warranted work.
5. **Single-occurrence sufficiency:** one operator occurrence explains the later difference without a profile.
6. **Descriptive but functionally inert profile:** the source compression is valid but adds no later-praxis discrimination.
7. **Present-target-condition account:** current conditions in `K` or `L` explain the difference more economically.
8. **No stable profile:** available sources do not support one relation topology across the declared coordinates.
9. **Non-Capture:** several non-equivalent profiles remain viable or the source is insufficient for responsible selection.

A rival profile must use declared criteria rather than merely replacing one attractive label with another. The comparison must preserve source scope, occurrence warrants, roles, phases, modulators, uncertainty, and Loss unless a new source claim is explicitly opened.

```text
several plausible profiles
≠ one hidden true type

alternative profile preferred
≠ original source occurrences invalid

no stable profile
≠ no praxis structure present
```

<a id="35-7-emergent-functional-profile"></a>

### 35.7 Emergent Functional Profile

An emergent functional profile is a source-side composite relation whose organizing form becomes visible only at the level of the whole Configuration or Composite while remaining reconstructible through its occurrence, relation, modulator, role, phase, uncertainty, and Loss traces. “Emergent” does not mean source-free, ontologically new, or operator-like.

#### C35-EMERGENTPROFILE-01 — an emergent functional profile has composite-level visibility and complete reconstructible source traces

The admissible form is:

```text
multiple warranted operator occurrences
+ source-supported relation topology
+ qualitative weighting
+ contextual modulators
+ bounded stability under role and phase variation
→ composite-level profile candidate
```

The profile is emergent only where no isolated occurrence reproduces the same organizing description, yet material changes to the contributing occurrences, relations, modulators, or temporal coordinates alter or defeat the candidate profile.

The Source Trace must preserve:

- the identity and PMS typing of every materially invoked occurrence;
- the canonical Δ–Ψ dependencies;
- support, opposition, sequence, threshold, persistence, accessibility, and countervailing relations;
- the role and phase distribution of relative load;
- the declared modulator relation paths;
- contradictory and missing evidence;
- the exact five-part Loss;
- the profile's stability and break conditions;
- alternative profiles and no-stable-profile.

```text
visible only at composite level
≠ detached from components

not reducible to one isolated occurrence
≠ unreconstructible

emergent functional profile
≠ new PMS operator
≠ new origin type
≠ person or group essence
```

The word `functional` at this stage refers to the source-side organizing work of the profile within `P`: it may organize access, thresholds, timing, persistence, transition relations, or continuation costs. It does not yet establish a contextual target function in `K`. That later claim requires an explicit `PROJECT_AS` occurrence, target-level PraxisPurchase, Source Trace, Contextual Boundedness, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, rival comparison, and the no-projection option.

```text
source-side emergent profile formation
≠ target-level profile projection

profile organizes P
≠ profile performs function in K
```

The pressure object therefore remains unadjudicated. WP2 does not establish that `P` possesses an emergent profile, that `omega_theta_prominent` is the correct description, that any of `m1–m3` is constitutive, that a trajectory-form difference exists, that `K` receives a bounded continuation-cost modulation function, or that `L` yields no-projection.

#### C35-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives the complete profile-formation architecture without projection, target-function, threshold, or route adjudication

WP2 hands forward:

- the independently warranted source Configuration/Composite `P`;
- the occurrence and relation traces with unchanged PMS dependencies;
- qualitative weighting dimensions and declared comparison bases;
- the `m1–m3` modulator packet and its required variation tests;
- bounded stability, role, phase, context, and profile-break conditions;
- the distinction between prominence and exclusivity;
- the conditions for trajectory-form influence without determinism or prediction;
- source-traceable emergent profile formation;
- alternative profiles, narrower source relations, functionally inert profiles, no-stable-profile, and Non-Capture;
- stable same-source `K/L` target coordinates;
- exact Loss, Claim Ceiling, Stop, governance, and authority boundaries.

WP2 does not hand forward:

- a selected weighting for `P`;
- an established effect for `m1–m3`;
- a selected stable or emergent profile;
- an established trajectory-form effect;
- a preferred rival profile;
- a crossed profile or function threshold;
- a profile type;
- a function in `K` or no-projection in `L`;
- a local `PROJECT_AS` result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-35 or RETYPE lock.

```text
profile-formation grammar complete
+ trajectory-form conditions explicit
+ source-traceable emergence explicit
+ rival and instability pressure preserved
≠ profile selected
≠ target function established
≠ projection executed
≠ Output Class selected
```

<a id="chapter-35-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 35.5–35.7 establish the bounded source-side architecture for modulating-profile formation, profile stability, prominence without exclusivity, trajectory-form influence without determinism, modulator variation, rival profiles, and source-traceable emergent functional profiles. They preserve the distinction between a source-side profile and a target-level projected function.

The stable `P/K/L` pressure object remains unexecuted. `omega_theta_prominent` remains a candidate description only. `m1–m3` remain unadjudicated contextual conditions. No stable or emergent profile, trajectory-form effect, target function, no-projection result, local `PROJECT_AS` result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-35 lock, or RETYPE lock has been selected.

The next controlled step is WP3, §§35.8–35.12: Profile versus Type, Profile Projection, Risks of Profile Inflation, Use of Add-On Stress Cases, and Failed Modulation Claim.

<a id="35-8-profile-versus-type"></a>

### 35.8 Profile versus Type

A modulating profile describes a bounded relation among already warranted operator occurrences, qualitative weighting dimensions, contextual modulators, roles, phases, and source conditions. A type classifies an object within a formal analytical grammar. These are not interchangeable claim positions.

#### C35-PROFILETYPE-01 — a configuration-bound profile must not become a formal type, person trait, group essence, diagnosis, maturity class, or moral category

The admissible grammatical form is:

```text
Configuration or Composite P
has, within declared Frame F and period P,
a source-supported modulating-profile candidate W/M.
```

The inadmissible rewrite is:

```text
profile description of P
→ hidden type of P
→ trait of persons, groups, institutions, cultures, or identities
```

A profile remains attached to the declared source Configuration or Composite. Its validity depends on the selected occurrence set, relation map, roles, phases, temporal window, modulators, evidence basis, uncertainty, and Loss. Change any of those materially and a new profile claim is required.

```text
configuration-bound profile
≠ formal PMS operator type
≠ derived operator type
≠ stable person essence
≠ group or institutional character
≠ culture type
≠ identity category
≠ maturity level
≠ diagnosis
≠ moral rank
```

The distinction is not merely terminological. Formal types possess grammar-level identity and declared dependencies. A modulating profile possesses only local descriptive scope. It may summarize a relation topology, but it cannot inherit the stability, portability, or authority of a formal type.

The same source may support different profile descriptions when roles, phases, or comparison bases differ. That does not imply that one description reveals the hidden true type. It indicates that the profile claim is relational and bounded.

```text
role_r1 profile description
≠ role_r2 profile description
≠ two person types

phase_1 prominence
≠ timeless origin type
```

A profile label must therefore be removable without damaging the underlying source record. The source occurrences and relations must remain reconstructible in ordinary PMS and source terms even if the profile label is rejected.

```text
profile label removed
+ occurrence and relation trace remains intelligible
→ description may be dispensable

profile label removed
+ source reconstruction collapses
→ hidden type or label substitution risk
```

No person-level inference follows from a configuration-level profile. A person may participate in several configurations with non-equivalent profiles. A group may contain internally contradictory role and phase relations. An institution may host multiple profiles or no stable profile at all. Chapter 35 therefore prohibits profile-to-person, profile-to-group, profile-to-culture, and profile-to-maturity conversion.

<a id="35-9-profile-projection"></a>

### 35.9 Profile Projection

A source-side profile claim and a target-level functional projection are separate operations and separate evidential burdens. Even a well-supported modulating profile in `P` does not automatically perform a function in `K`, and failure to project in `L` does not erase the source profile.

#### Projection reconstruction without the profile label

The same label-removal test applies to the target claim: the bounded target-function relation must remain reconstructible from the source occurrences, relation map, modulators, and target conditions even if the profile name is withheld. Otherwise the projection label is substituting for Constitutive Source Trace.

#### C35-PROJECTION-01 — profile projection requires a declared PROJECT_AS occurrence, a concrete target-praxis difference, complete Source Trace, Contextual Boundedness, exact Loss, and a live no-projection option

The minimum projection form is:

```yaml
source:
  object: operator_weighting_configuration_P
  origin_type: configuration_bound_relational_composite
  profile_candidate: declared_source_supported_profile_only
  source_trace: retained

projection:
  operation: PROJECT_AS
  target_context: later_continuation_field_K
  proposed_target_function: bounded_continuation_cost_modulation_function
  required_target_difference:
    - alternative_continuations_change_in_cost_or_accessibility
    - role_specific_exit_or_revision_conditions_change
    - later_path_selection_becomes_praxeologically_discriminating

constraints:
  origin_type_preserved: true
  profile_not_retyped_as_operator_or_type: true
  target_function_bounded: true
  no_projection_option_retained: true
  authority_inheritance: prohibited

loss:
  preserved: declared
  compressed: declared
  excluded: declared
  uncertain: declared
  irrecoverable: declared
```

The source-side claim must be complete before projection begins. `PROJECT_AS` may not repair an unstable profile, invent missing occurrence relations, resolve role variation by aggregation, or turn a rhetorically attractive label into a source object.

```text
source profile unestablished
→ target function cannot establish it retroactively

source profile established
→ target function still requires independent warrant
```

The proposed function in `K` must identify a concrete praxis difference. It is not enough to say that the profile is “relevant,” “shaping,” “dominant,” “structuring,” or “present.” The projection must specify what becomes differently accessible, costly, delay-prone, persistent, reversible, revisable, or selectable in the target context.

The same source packet must be tested in `L` without changing occurrences, relations, roles, period, modulators, uncertainty, or inherited Loss:

```text
same source profile candidate P
+ target context K
→ bounded target-function candidate

same source profile candidate P
+ target context L
→ no distinct profile function remains possible
```

`K` is not preselected as positive. `L` is not a failed copy of `K`. Present target conditions, a single occurrence, an alternative profile, a narrower source relation, or descriptive-but-functionally-inert status may outperform the proposed projection.

Counterfactual Source Trace requires that material changes in the source profile alter or defeat the target-function claim. If the function label remains unchanged after removing the alleged prominent occurrences, reversing role-specific weights, substituting modulators, changing the temporal window, or disrupting the relation map, the projection lacks traceable load.

```text
target function invariant under material source change
→ label without traceable load
```

Projection preserves the origin type and source history. A successful projection does not transform `P` into a new profile type, operator, person category, predictive model, or authority-bearing object.

#### C35-THRESHOLD-01 — profile formation and profile projection require qualitative, calibration-sensitive thresholds that cannot be replaced by counts, symbol frequency, label recurrence, diagram prominence, model centrality, or aesthetic fit

Two thresholds must remain distinct:

| Threshold | Governing question | Insufficient substitutes |
|---|---|---|
| profile-formation threshold | Does a bounded relation topology remain stable and discriminating enough to justify a profile description? | symbol count, label recurrence, visual prominence |
| profile-function threshold | Does the source-supported profile perform additional praxis work in the declared target context? | narrative fit, model centrality, rhetorical usefulness |

The thresholds are qualitative because the chapter provides no universal metric for operator weight, profile stability, modulator strength, or later-path effect. A later domain method may independently warrant measurement. Such measurement would remain domain-specific and could not rewrite STRATA's general criteria.

A profile-formation threshold requires at least:

- independently warranted operator occurrences;
- a stable enough relation topology under declared role, phase, and temporal variation;
- explicit weighting criteria and comparison bases;
- modulator declarations with Removal, Substitution, Reversal, and Redundancy pressure;
- break conditions capable of rejecting the profile;
- rival profiles and `no stable profile`.

A profile-function threshold additionally requires:

- an explicit `PROJECT_AS` occurrence;
- a declared target context and target function;
- a concrete later-praxis difference;
- Source Trace and Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- target-condition isolation;
- exact Loss;
- rival function and no-projection options.

```text
many operator symbols
+ repeated profile label
+ elegant diagram
+ schema-valid packet
≠ profile threshold crossed
≠ target-function threshold crossed
```

Threshold uncertainty must be reported rather than hidden. Calibration-sensitive or underdetermined evidence may require `admissible_but_provisional`, `partially_admissible`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, or `non_capture` after the full executed audit. These are not selected for `P/K/L` here.

<a id="35-10-risks-of-profile-inflation"></a>

### 35.10 Risks of Profile Inflation

Profile language is compact and therefore especially vulnerable to overproduction. A system can appear more precise while merely multiplying names. Chapter 35 treats this as a discriminative failure, not as harmless terminological richness.

#### C35-INFLATION-01 — profile inflation occurs when labels proliferate without stable criteria, become hidden types, absorb counterevidence, or substitute descriptive compactness for functional gain

Profile inflation includes at least the following patterns:

1. **Universal profiling:** every Configuration receives a named profile whether or not the label changes reconstruction.
2. **Micro-variation naming:** every small role, phase, or source difference creates a new profile without additional PraxisPurchase.
3. **Criteria drift:** the same label uses different weighting criteria across cases, or different labels use materially equivalent criteria.
4. **Hidden typing:** a local profile becomes a stable person, group, institution, culture, identity, or maturity category.
5. **Counterexample absorption:** contradictory phases or roles are redescribed as variants of the same profile rather than allowed to defeat it.
6. **Projection inflation:** a descriptive profile is treated as functional in every later context.
7. **Label invariance:** the label survives material changes to source occurrences, relations, modulators, or target context.
8. **Authority inflation:** profile legibility is converted into diagnosis, prediction, recommendation, sanction, or governance.

The lower boundary is profile naming without praxeological purchase:

```text
additional profile label
+ no changed source reconstruction
+ no changed rival discrimination
+ no changed target-function test
→ distinction without praxeological purchase
```

The upper boundary is profile abstraction without traceable load:

```text
profile label remains stable
while source relations, roles, phases, and modulators materially change
→ abstraction without traceable load
```

The anti-inflation test asks whether the profile label can be removed, merged, narrowed, split, or replaced without losing a warranted difference. Possible outcomes include:

- retain one bounded profile;
- split by role or phase;
- merge redundant labels;
- reduce to an occurrence-level or relation-level description;
- retain descriptive profile but deny functional projection;
- prefer an alternative profile;
- conclude `no stable profile`;
- stop or return Non-Capture.

A profile taxonomy is not required by STRATA. The chapter does not create canonical profile names, an exhaustive profile inventory, a score space, or a classification hierarchy.

```text
more profile labels
≠ more discrimination
≠ more coverage
≠ more authority
```

The same protection applies to machine-readable implementation. A field named `profile`, a complete relation map, successful schema validation, or automated clustering cannot determine that a profile is substantively warranted.

```text
formal clustering
≠ PMS weighting warrant

schema-valid profile object
≠ stable praxeological profile
```

<a id="35-11-use-of-add-on-stress-cases"></a>

### 35.11 Use of Add-On Stress Cases

Domain lenses and adjacent PMS ecosystem artifacts may expose Chapter 35 to difficult examples. They may show different role distributions, temporal forms, access conditions, asymmetry loads, Non-Event structures, stabilization burdens, or target functions. Their role is adversarial and illustrative, not architectural.

#### C35-ADDON-01 — add-on lenses are optional stress vectors and cannot define weighting criteria, profile types, STRATA rules, canonical results, or application authority

An add-on stress case may test whether:

- the same occurrence relation receives different weight under different Frames;
- role-specific profiles defeat an aggregate description;
- temporal reversal defeats apparent stability;
- a modulator is redundant or target-condition dependent;
- descriptive compactness produces no later-praxis difference;
- a profile label drifts toward person typing, diagnosis, or moral ranking;
- a cross-domain analogy is mistaken for projection.

It may not supply the governing rule by virtue of its domain vocabulary. AXIOM, STACK, EM, DISCIPLINE, MIP, ANTICIPATION, CONFLICT, CRITIQUE, EDEN, LOGIC, SEX, QC, or another artifact cannot authorize a STRATA weighting, profile, threshold, function, route, or Output Class merely because the example is vivid or formally developed.

```text
add-on example
→ optional test pressure

add-on vocabulary
≠ STRATA definition
≠ profile enum
≠ weighting criterion
≠ architectural dependency
≠ application authority
```

Every add-on case must be translated back into Chapter 35's own bounded declarations:

- source Configuration or Composite;
- warranted operator occurrences and relations;
- Frame, roles, phases, and temporal scope;
- qualitative weighting criteria;
- modulators and variation tests;
- profile stability and break conditions;
- optional target context and target function;
- rival profile, no-profile, and no-projection;
- exact Loss, Claim Ceiling, Stop, Non-Capture, and authority prohibition.

The add-on may expose a weakness in Chapter 35. It may not repair that weakness by donating its own authority. If the Chapter 35 criteria fail, the result must remain Claim Reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture rather than “validated by domain fit.”

Add-on cases are therefore deferred to later stress-test and case-production phases. During Block drafting, Chapter 35 assigns or prepares the pressure but does not create new test, case, or smoke YAML files.

<a id="35-12-failed-modulation-claim"></a>

### 35.12 Failed Modulation Claim

A modulation claim can fail at several distinct positions. The failure must be localized rather than compressed into a single negative label or displaced onto persons and groups.

#### C35-CFTEST-01 — the integrated profile counterfactual test varies occurrence load, role, phase, modulator, source relation, target context, target condition, and rival profile without converting sensitivity into causal or predictive proof

The integrated test packet is:

| Variation | Pressure question |
|---|---|
| occurrence-load variation | Does the profile survive removal or changed relative load of allegedly prominent occurrences? |
| role variation | Does one aggregate profile conceal materially different role-specific relations? |
| phase variation | Is stability limited, reversed, intermittent, or absent outside the selected phase? |
| modulator variation | Do Removal, Substitution, Reversal, or Redundancy change the profile or target function? |
| relation variation | Does disruption of support, opposition, sequence, threshold, or persistence relations alter the claim? |
| target-context variation | Does the same source profile perform different, no, or incompatible functions in `K/L`? |
| target-condition isolation | Can present target conditions explain the later difference without the source profile? |
| rival-profile variation | Does an alternative, narrower, role-split, phase-split, or functionally inert profile discriminate better? |

The profile claim must also remain vulnerable to `no stable profile`, `no projection`, descriptive occurrence-level reconstruction, and Non-Capture.

```text
counterfactual sensitivity
≠ causal necessity
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ causal percentage
≠ probability estimate
≠ prediction
```

If the source label changes under every minor variation, it may be too unstable to form a profile. If it survives every material variation, it may be too elastic to remain discriminating. Both pressures can defeat the claim.

```text
profile changes under immaterial variation
→ instability or calibration failure

profile survives material contradiction and reversal
→ inflation or hidden-type failure
```

The `P/K/L` pressure object remains unexecuted. WP3 defines the test; it does not select which variation succeeds, whether `omega_theta_prominent` is warranted, whether `m1–m3` carry load, or whether `K` or `L` supports a function result.

#### C35-FAILURE-01 — failed weighting, profile-formation, modulation, or projection claims must be localized while preserving valid source occurrences and distinct canonical routes

Failure locations include:

1. **Occurrence warrant failure:** one or more invoked operator occurrences are unsupported.
2. **Relation warrant failure:** support, opposition, sequence, threshold, persistence, or access relations are invented or underdetermined.
3. **Weighting failure:** no qualitative comparison basis or discriminating relative load is established.
4. **Modulator failure:** the alleged modulator is untraceable, redundant, target-condition dependent, or treated as an operator.
5. **Stability failure:** roles, phases, or temporal reversal defeat one bounded profile.
6. **Profile threshold failure:** the label adds no source-side discrimination.
7. **Type-integrity failure:** the profile becomes a new operator, formal type, person/group trait, or dependency reorder.
8. **Projection failure:** no additional target-praxis difference remains after rival and target-condition pressure.
9. **Authority failure:** profile description is converted into prediction, diagnosis, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority inheritance.

The failure route depends on the exact claim position:

```text
source profile too broad but narrower claim survives
→ claim_reduction_required remains available

continuation would require invented weights, hidden typing,
dependency change, prediction, or authority
→ mandatory_stop remains available

executed delimited transformation fails its required conditions
→ failed_transformation remains available

available evidence cannot select among non-equivalent profiles,
separate modulator from target conditions, or support bounded capture
→ non_capture remains available
```

These routes are not interchangeable. Stop is not Failure. Failure is not Non-Capture. Non-Capture does not protect a weak claim. Claim Reduction does not silently preserve the original scope.

A failed target projection does not automatically invalidate the source Configuration, its warranted operator occurrences, or a separately established descriptive profile. A failed profile claim does not erase the occurrence-level reconstruction. A failed weighting claim does not modify PMS Base.

```text
failed profile projection
≠ source occurrences false

failed profile formation
≠ source Configuration absent

failed weighting claim
≠ Δ–Ψ revised
```

No negative result authorizes person judgment. `mandatory_stop`, `failed_transformation`, and `non_capture` are method results, not diagnoses or classifications.

#### C35-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete Chapter-35 family architecture and Chapter-36 preparation boundary without pressure-object adjudication, RETYPE lock, or new case/test/smoke YAML production

WP4 receives:

- the complete source occurrence and relation packet for `P`;
- qualitative weighting and dependency-preservation rules;
- modulator declarations and full variation pressure;
- profile formation, stability, dominance/non-exclusivity, rival, and no-profile architecture;
- trajectory-form conditions without determinism or prediction;
- source-traceable emergent functional-profile conditions;
- profile/type and configuration/person boundaries;
- explicit profile-projection and dual-threshold requirements;
- profile-inflation and add-on non-authority failures;
- the integrated Counterfactual Profile Test;
- localized Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, source non-invalidation, exact Loss, Claim Ceiling, and authority prohibition;
- stable same-source `K/L` target coordinates.

WP4 does not receive:

- a selected substantive weighting for `P`;
- an established effect for `m1–m3`;
- a selected stable or emergent profile;
- an established trajectory-form effect;
- a crossed profile or function threshold;
- a function in `K` or no-projection in `L`;

- a new test, case, or smoke YAML.

```text
complete Chapter-35 family architecture
+ twenty-four duties represented
+ pressure object remains unadjudicated
→ WP4 lock audit may begin
```

<a id="chapter-35-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 35.8–35.12 complete the Chapter-35 family architecture. They separate profile from type, source description from target projection, profile-formation threshold from profile-function threshold, and bounded profile language from inflation, person typing, hidden prediction, add-on authority, and source-erasing failure.

The stable `P/K/L` pressure object remains unexecuted. `omega_theta_prominent` remains a candidate description only. `m1–m3` remain unadjudicated contextual conditions. No substantive weighting, stable or emergent profile, trajectory-form effect, threshold result, function in `K`, no-projection in `L`, local `PROJECT_AS` result, canonical Output Class, Chapter-35 lock, or RETYPE lock has been selected.

The next controlled step is WP4: the integrated Chapter-35 family-method lock audit and Chapter-36 Preparation handoff.

<a id="chapter-35-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 35 completion boundary

Chapter 35 is complete as a **family-method specification for operator weighting, contextual modulation, modulating-profile formation, optional trajectory-form relevance, source-traceable emergent functional profiles, and bounded profile projection**. The completion decision concerns the method only. It does not execute or classify the stable `P/K/L` pressure object.

```text
warranted operator occurrences and relations
+ qualitative configuration-bound weighting
+ contextual modulators
+ bounded profile-formation and profile-function thresholds
+ explicit source-side/target-side claim separation
+ Counterfactual Profile Test, exact Loss, Stop, and Non-Capture
→ complete Chapter-35 family method
```

The chapter-level methodological result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This result is not inherited by any unexecuted weighting, profile, modulation, trajectory-form, or projection claim.

#### Integrated method finding

The completed method preserves the canonical Δ–Ψ sequence, every invoked operator identity, and every dependency declared by PMS Base. Weighting concerns only the relative load, accessibility, visibility, threshold effect, or temporal effect of **already warranted operator occurrences** within a declared Configuration or Composite.

```text
operator weighting
≠ operator replacement
≠ dependency reordering
≠ operator independence
≠ numerical score
≠ causal percentage
```

A weighting claim must remain reconstructible through the source Frame, roles, phases, temporal scope, occurrence traces, relation map, evidence basis, uncertainty, prior disposition, and exact five-part Loss. Symbol frequency, word count, YAML field count, diagram prominence, graph centrality, model position, Reader grouping, or aesthetic compactness cannot supply weighting warrant.

A modulator remains a contextual condition that may affect access, threshold, timing, persistence, reversal, or stabilization. It does not enter the PMS operator grammar and does not become a fourth STRATA operation.

```text
modulator
≠ PMS operator
≠ derived operator
≠ COMPOSE
≠ DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS
≠ hidden domain primitive
```

Removal, Substitution, Reversal, Redundancy, Changed Access, and Target-Condition Isolation remain required pressures. A label such as `access_condition_m1` does not establish that the condition carries load.

#### Profile formation without type formation

A modulating profile is a bounded relation topology among warranted occurrences, qualitative weighting relations, and contextual modulators under declared role, phase, Frame, and temporal coordinates. It is not a list of operator symbols, a ranking table, a formal type, or a property of a person, group, institution, culture, identity, or maturity category.

```text
modulating profile
≠ operator type
≠ person or group trait
≠ diagnosis
≠ moral rank
≠ prediction model
```

Profile stability is always bounded. It may be role-specific, phase-specific, intermittent, reversed, contradicted, or absent outside the declared window. `no stable profile` remains a positive possibility. Prominence or dominance never entails exclusivity; non-prominent occurrences and canonical dependencies remain visible.

A compact profile label must remain removable without destroying the underlying source record. If the label becomes indispensable to reconstructing what should already be available in the source evidence, it has become a hidden type or label substitute.

#### Trajectory form and emergent profile boundary

Weighting may contribute to Path or Trajectory form only where source-supported differences appear in transition accessibility, warrantable Non-Event centrality, attractor persistence, cost sedimentation, continuation fields, or temporal reversal. Such influence remains bounded and non-predictive.

```text
trajectory-form influence
≠ determinism
≠ statistical probability
≠ causal sufficiency
≠ teleology
```

An emergent functional profile may become visible only at composite level while remaining fully traceable through its occurrences, relations, modulators, roles, phases, uncertainty, and Loss. Composite-level visibility does not authorize source-free novelty, a new origin type, a new operator, or a collective essence.

```text
emergent functional profile
≠ source-free novelty
≠ new PMS primitive
≠ new entity
≠ person or group essence
```

Source-side profile formation and target-side profile projection remain two distinct claims. A profile may be descriptively stable yet functionally inert in a later context.

#### Projection and dual-threshold discipline

A projected profile requires a new `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with its own target context, target function, Source Trace, Contextual Boundedness, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, rival comparison, no-projection option, result, and canonical mapping.

```text
profile formed in P
≠ profile projected in K

source profile established
≠ target function established
```

Two qualitative thresholds remain separate:

1. **Profile-Formation Threshold:** whether the source relation topology is stable and discriminating enough to warrant a bounded profile description.
2. **Profile-Function Threshold:** whether that source-supported profile performs additional praxeological work in the declared target context.

Neither threshold can be replaced by count, recurrence, symbol frequency, label repetition, visual prominence, schema validity, or model completeness. A later domain method may warrant measurement, but it cannot retroactively rewrite STRATA's general criteria.

#### Profile inflation, rivals, and counterfactual pressure

Profile inflation occurs where labels multiply without additional PraxisPurchase, criteria drift, counterexamples are absorbed, local profiles become person or group types, descriptive compactness becomes automatic projection, or the same label survives material source changes.

```text
additional profile label
+ no changed reconstruction or discrimination
→ distinction without praxeological purchase

profile label invariant under material source change
→ abstraction without traceable load
```

The integrated Counterfactual Profile Test retains variation in occurrence load, role, phase, modulator, relation, target context, present target conditions, and rival profile. It also retains:

- alternative weighting profiles;
- role-split and phase-split profiles;
- a narrower source relation;
- Single-Occurrence Sufficiency;
- descriptive but functionally inert status;
- Present-Target-Condition explanations;
- `no stable profile`;
- no-projection;
- Non-Capture.

A profile that changes under immaterial variation may be unstable or poorly calibrated. A profile that survives material contradiction, reversal, and source removal may be overelastic or a hidden type. Neither outcome proves causality or prediction.

#### Add-on and authority boundary

Add-on lenses and adjacent ecosystem artifacts remain optional stress vectors. They may expose difficult role, phase, access, asymmetry, Non-Event, temporal, or target-function cases. They may not define STRATA weighting criteria, profile enums, thresholds, routes, Output Classes, or authority.

```text
add-on stress vector
≠ STRATA rule authority
≠ profile validity
≠ application authority
```

The chapter introduces no truth, causal, normative, diagnostic, classificatory, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority inheritance claim.

```text
profile legibility
≠ empirical truth
≠ prediction
≠ diagnosis
≠ legitimacy
≠ recommendation
≠ sanction
≠ governance authority
```

#### Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and source preservation

Failure remains localized to the exact claim position: occurrence warrant, relation warrant, weighting, modulator, stability, profile-formation threshold, Type Integrity, projection, profile-function threshold, Loss, or authority boundary.

The canonical routes remain distinct:

- `claim_reduction_required` where a narrower role, phase, interval, source relation, profile, or descriptive claim survives;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation would require invented weighting, dependency change, hidden typing, prediction, causal monopoly, person classification, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority inheritance;
- `failed_transformation` where an executed delimited projection fails its declared conditions;
- `non_capture` where available sources cannot responsibly establish one bounded profile or distinguish profile effect from target conditions.

```text
failed profile projection
≠ source occurrences false

failed profile formation
≠ source Configuration absent

failed weighting claim
≠ PMS Base revised
```

A change of Frame, role set, phase, temporal window, occurrence packet, relation map, weighting criterion, modulator, target context, target function, evidence basis, or rival profile creates a new testable claim. It does not rescue the prior claim by relabeling.

#### Protected non-adjudication of `P/K/L`

The stable pressure architecture remains unexecuted:

```yaml
  origin_type: configuration_bound_relational_composite

```

Chapter 35 does not establish:

- an effect for `m1–m3`;
- one stable or emergent profile;
- a trajectory-form difference;
- either threshold as crossed;
- a function in `K`;
- no-projection in `L`;

- a canonical Output Class for the pressure object.

```text
Chapter 35 provisionally locked
≠ P assigned a profile
≠ omega_theta_prominent established
≠ modulator effect established
≠ trajectory-form effect established
≠ function established in K
≠ no-projection established in L
≠ RETYPE locked
```

#### Integrated completion decision

All §§35.1–35.12 are complete. All twenty-four assigned Chapter-35 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. The chapter preserves operator and dependency integrity, source/target separation, role and phase variation, bounded profile stability, dual thresholds, exact Loss, rival profiles, no-profile, no-projection, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, source non-invalidation, add-on non-authority, and the prohibition of person typing and authority inheritance.

```text
complete weighting-modulation-profile family specification
+ twenty-four-duty closure
+ integrated scientific and formal audit pass
+ protected non-adjudication of P/K/L

```

The chapter-level methodological class is `admissible_but_provisional`. It does not flow to `P/K/L` and does not preselect any later case result.

#### Chapter-36 handoff boundary

Chapter 36 receives five locked constraints from Chapter 35 and the preceding projection-family chapters:

1. one source object may support more than one bounded target-function candidate;
2. shared source does not imply shared target context, target level, Claim Scope, Loss, or compatibility status;
3. compatible and competing projections require explicit coordinates rather than label comparison;
4. comparative preference cannot transform a target function into the source object's true type;
5. no-projection, indeterminacy, non-comparability, non-translation, Stop, and Non-Capture remain positive outcomes.

Chapter 36 must compare projections without a universal ranking metric, forced integration, theory tribunal, person judgment, political legitimacy decision, or authority increase. It receives no preferred projection, no compatibility decision, no competition result, no canonical Output Class for a comparison pressure object, and no RETYPE lock.
<a id="chapter-36-competing-projections"></a>

## 36. Competing Projections

Chapter 36 applies the provisionally locked projection-family methods of Chapters 31–35 to a problem that those chapters deliberately leave open: the same independently warranted source object may support more than one plausible target-function claim. The existence of several candidates does not require that exactly one candidate disclose the source object's true identity. Nor does it authorize a synthetic super-label that absorbs all candidates. Each candidate remains a bounded `PROJECT_AS` claim with its own target coordinates, family-specific burden, Source Trace, Loss, alternatives, Claim Ceiling, and possible negative result.

The comparative task begins only after candidate formation. Chapter 29 owns the distinction between origin type and target function. Chapter 30 owns the generic `PROJECT_AS` procedure. Chapters 31–35 own the burdens of their respective projection families. This chapter does not weaken or replace those burdens. It asks whether complete or at least comparison-ready candidates are compatible, genuinely competing, context-dependent, non-comparable, unresolved, or better displaced by a narrower or no-projection account.

```text
multiple plausible target functions
≠ multiple origin types
≠ one hidden true type
≠ forced ranking
≠ forced integration
```

Two coordinated pressure scenes remain distinct throughout the chapter. In the compatibility scene, the same documented Trajectory `N` is considered as a bounded historical frame-function in `later_configuration_A` and as a bounded Macro-Event-function in `wider_path_B`. In the competition scene, the same source is considered within one continuation field `C` through a bounded frame-function candidate and a bounded dynamic attractor-function candidate. Neither compatibility nor competition is preselected. The first scene may prove non-comparable or only partially co-valid; the second may dissolve when target coordinates or source subsets are made explicit.

WP1 establishes the shared-source lock, target-coordinate grammar, compatibility and competition conditions, serious alternative set, and anti-integration boundary. It does not compare candidates through substantive criteria, select a preferred projection, decide co-validity or indeterminacy, map a comparison result to an Output Class, or execute either pressure scene.

<a id="36-1-multiple-plausible-functions"></a>

### 36.1 Multiple Plausible Functions

One independently warranted source object may support several bounded target-function candidates because target function is relational rather than intrinsic. A Trajectory can condition interpretation in one later Configuration, mark a transition in one wider Path, stabilize a transition form in another continuation field, or remain merely historical background elsewhere. These claims can coexist as candidates because they answer different target questions. Their plurality does not rewrite the source's origin type.

#### C36-BASIC-01 — one warranted source may support several bounded candidates without becoming several source types

The minimum comparative family form is:

```text
source object X with retained origin type T_o
+ candidate target context C_a and function F_a
+ candidate target context C_b and function F_b
→ several independently testable PROJECT_AS claims
```

For the stable pressure source:

```yaml
shared_source:
  object: documented_trajectory_N
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_N
  frame: declared_historical_practice_frame_N
  temporal_scope: period_N
  prior_disposition: retained
  uncertainty: retained
```

The source remains a Trajectory whether candidate `A`, `B`, `F`, or `G` later succeeds, fails, requires reduction, or remains non-captured. A successful frame-function does not turn `N` into `□`. A successful Macro-Event-function does not turn `N` into an Event origin type. A successful dynamic attractor-function does not turn `N` into `Α`.

```text
one source
+ several contextual functions
≠ several source identities

preferred target function
≠ source object's true type
```

Candidate plurality therefore creates several claims, not one multi-valued essence. Every candidate requires its own operation occurrence and record. A negative result for one candidate does not flow automatically to another, and a positive result for one candidate does not grant presumptive validity to the others.

#### C36-SOURCELOCK-01 — comparison requires stable source identity, disposition, uncertainty, Loss, and declared source subsets

A nominally shared source is not enough. Comparison requires a stable source packet across all candidates:

```yaml
shared_source_lock:
  reference_object: documented_trajectory_N
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_frame: declared_historical_practice_frame_N
  source_granularity: retained
  source_relative_level: retained
  temporal_scope: period_N
  source_basis: retained
  prior_disposition: retained
  uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

Each candidate may identify a family-specific subset of the stable source as load-bearing. A frame-function may depend especially on sedimented expectations and asymmetric interpretation costs. A Macro-Event-function may depend on boundary-bearing phases and changed later possibilities. A dynamic attractor-function may depend on recurrent transition form and later-path accessibility. Such subsets must be declared candidate by candidate.

```text
shared source label
≠ identical constitutive source trace

candidate-specific subset
≠ permission to rewrite the source
```

If a candidate changes the source period, deletes contrary phases, changes the Frame, replaces the relation map, or imports new evidence to improve target fit, it is a new projection claim and potentially a new comparison. It may not remain inside the original comparison under the appearance of stable source identity.

Source-side validity and candidate-side validity also remain distinct. A valid PATH Trajectory can support no admissible projection in the tested contexts. Conversely, one failed candidate cannot erase the source Trajectory or a separately warranted candidate.

#### C36-OCCURRENCECOMPARE-01 — rival projections compare one warranted occurrence packet before candidate-specific source load diverges

Where the shared source is PMS-typed or PMS-derived, comparison must remain anchored in the same already warranted operator occurrences and occurrence relations available through `source_reference` and `source_basis`. Candidate-specific subsets may assign different load-bearing relevance to those relations, but they may not infer new source typing from target fit or replace the shared relation map merely because a different target label appears preferable.

The comparison must therefore distinguish:

```text
same warranted occurrence packet
+ different declared load-bearing subset, relation emphasis, or target context
→ rival PROJECT_AS candidates remain comparable

changed occurrence packet, source period, Frame, or relation map
→ new source claim or new comparison required
```

Where an operator relation is material, the comparative sensitivity test varies the concrete occurrence or relation—not the abstract operator type—and records whether candidate `A`, candidate `B`, both, or neither must change. Operator symbols without occurrence identity, source support, and relation placement do not satisfy the comparative Source Trace burden.

<a id="36-2-compatibility"></a>

### 36.2 Compatibility

Compatibility is possible when candidate projections do not make mutually exclusive claims over the same target work. It is established through explicit coordinates, not through friendly vocabulary or the analyst's desire for a comprehensive interpretation.

#### C36-TARGETCOORD-01 — target coordinates must be explicit before compatibility or competition is asserted

Each candidate must declare at least:

```yaml
target_coordinates:
  target_context:
  target_level:
  target_object:
  target_function:
  claim_scope:
  validity_scope:
  temporal_scope:
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
```

The comparison must additionally state whether these coordinates are shared, overlapping, complementary, nested, or distinct. The phrase “both explain the same history” is insufficient because it hides the target object, target level, validity window, and the exact PraxisPurchase claimed.

The compatibility pressure scene is:

```yaml
compatibility_scene:
  candidate_A:
    source_object: documented_trajectory_N
    target_context: later_configuration_A
    target_level: relative_level_A
    target_object: interpretation_and_action_corridor_structure_A
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
    claim_scope: interpretation_and_action_corridor_conditioning
    validity_scope: declared_window_A
  candidate_B:
    source_object: documented_trajectory_N
    target_context: wider_path_B
    target_level: relative_level_B
    target_object: transition_and_segmentation_structure_B
    target_function: bounded_macro_event_function
    claim_scope: wider_path_transition_and_segmentation
    validity_scope: declared_window_B
  comparison_status: not_executed
```

The two candidates share a source but do not yet share target context, target level, target object, Claim Scope, or validity scope. Apparent tension may therefore dissolve at the coordinate level. Equally, distinct coordinates do not guarantee compatibility: one candidate might still presuppose a source boundary or historical interpretation that defeats the other.

#### C36-COMPAT-01 — compatibility concerns non-exclusive target work and does not require integration

Candidates are compatibility candidates where at least one of the following is warranted:

- they operate in different target contexts;
- they operate at different relative levels;
- they concern different target objects;
- their Claim Scopes are distinct or only partially overlapping;
- their validity scopes differ;
- they preserve complementary praxis dimensions without mutually exclusive source or target claims.

Compatibility does not mean that both candidates are substantively admissible. It means only that success of one would not, by itself, defeat the other. Each candidate must first satisfy its own family burden.

```text
compatible candidate coordinates
≠ both candidates admissible

co-validity
≠ one integrated super-function
```

A frame-function in `A` may condition later interpretation while a Macro-Event-function in `B` marks a wider transition. These functions can remain analytically separate even when both eventually prove admissible. Their different Loss profiles may be necessary: the frame-function may preserve interpretive and role-conditioned historical load, whereas the Macro-Event-function may preserve transition boundaries and wider-path segmentation. Integration could destroy precisely the differences that make the candidates useful.

<a id="36-3-competition"></a>

### 36.3 Competition

Competition is narrower than plurality. Two candidates compete only where they claim materially overlapping work over sufficiently shared target coordinates. Different words, operator associations, or family names do not create competition by themselves.

#### C36-COMPETE-01 — genuine competition requires shared target coordinates and overlapping explanatory work

The competition pressure scene is:

```yaml
competition_scene:
  shared_source: documented_trajectory_N
  shared_target_context: continuation_field_C
  shared_target_level: relative_level_C
  shared_target_object: later_continuation_structure_C
  shared_temporal_scope: declared_window_C
  candidate_F:
    target_function: bounded_frame_function
    proposed_work: conditions_relevance_and_action_corridors
  candidate_G:
    target_function: bounded_dynamic_attractor_function
    proposed_work: stabilizes_transition_form_or_sequence
  comparison_status: not_executed
```

Competition exists only if the proposed work materially overlaps. Candidate `F` might claim that historical load makes some interpretations and continuations credible while excluding others. Candidate `G` might claim that the same source-supported pattern lowers friction for one recurrent transition form. These may be competing explanations if each claims to account for the same continuation difference in `C`. They may instead prove complementary if one conditions legibility and the other stabilizes sequence under separate evidential and target burdens.

The comparison must therefore test:

```text
same source?
same source subset?
same target context?
same relative level?
same target object?
same temporal and validity scope?
materially overlapping PraxisPurchase?
mutually limiting or exclusive claims?
```

A candidate that has not satisfied its own family requirements cannot become preferable merely because the rival is weak. Comparative victory cannot repair missing historical load, missing recurrence mechanism, missing target difference, Type Integrity failure, or an excessive Claim Ceiling.

```text
rival candidate failure
≠ surviving candidate automatically admissible

competition
≠ winner-takes-source-identity
```

Competition remains claim-local. Candidate `F` may be stronger for one role or interval while candidate `G` remains viable for another. A narrower claim may survive where a comprehensive candidate fails. WP1 does not select among these possibilities.

<a id="36-4-projection-alternatives"></a>

### 36.4 Projection Alternatives

A comparison becomes self-sealing when it treats the supplied candidates as the only possible readings. Every strong candidate and every pairwise comparison must therefore preserve serious alternatives capable of narrowing, displacing, or defeating the proposed projections.

#### C36-ALTERNATIVES-01 — alternatives must include rival functions, contexts, narrower claims, source-only treatment, no-projection, and Non-Capture

The minimum alternative set is:

```yaml
projection_alternatives:
  alternative_target_functions:
    - family_appropriate_rival_function
  alternative_target_contexts:
    - narrower_or_different_context
  narrower_claims:
    - role_bounded_claim
    - phase_bounded_claim
    - shorter_validity_scope
  non_projection_accounts:
    - descriptive_historical_background_only
    - present_target_conditions_only
  terminal_possibilities:
    - no_projection
    - non_capture
```

Alternatives must be live rather than ceremonial. A present-target-conditions account may outperform both `F` and `G` if current structures carry the full explanatory load. A source-only description may be preferable if the Trajectory remains relevant history but performs no distinct contextual function. A narrower role- or time-bounded projection may preserve Source Trace where a broad candidate overreaches. Non-Capture remains necessary where the evidence cannot distinguish historical projection from present-condition effects or where candidates rely on non-comparable Loss profiles.

The no-projection option is not a claim that the source is irrelevant. It states only that no tested target function adds a warranted difference in the declared context.

```text
no-projection
≠ no historical relevance
≠ source reconstruction failed

Non-Capture
≠ permission to select the most elegant candidate
```

#### C36-NOFORCEDINTEGRATION-01 — compatibility, breadth, elegance, and readability do not authorize merger

Compatible candidates must not be merged merely because a combined label appears more complete. A merger would require its own declared source, target object, operation, function, Loss, alternatives, and counterfactual burden. It may constitute a new `COMPOSE`, a new `PROJECT_AS`, or an invalid label substitution depending on what is actually done.

```text
candidate A admissible
+ candidate B admissible
≠ integrated candidate AB admissible

joint readability
≠ joint function

comprehensiveness
≠ comparative superiority
```

Forced integration is especially dangerous where candidates preserve different source dimensions or incur different losses. A combined label can conceal non-translation, erase candidate-specific failure conditions, and exceed the Traceability Ceiling. WP1 therefore preserves candidate separation even where later comparison establishes co-validity.

The same prohibition applies to competition. The inability to select between `F` and `G` does not authorize a hybrid “frame-attractor” label. Indeterminacy, non-comparability, narrower claims, no-projection, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture remain preferable to an untested synthesis.

#### C36-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives stable comparison packets without preference, indeterminacy result, or Output Class

WP1 hands WP2 two explicit but unexecuted comparison packets:

```text
compatibility packet:
  stable source N
  candidate-specific source subsets to be declared
  explicit A/B target coordinates
  separate family burdens
  no forced integration

competition packet:
  stable source N
  explicit shared C coordinates
  frame-function candidate F
  dynamic attractor-function candidate G
  serious alternatives and no-projection
```

WP2 receives no comparative preference, no co-validity finding, no competition finding, no indeterminacy result, no non-comparability result, no no-projection result, no local `PROJECT_AS` result, and no canonical Output Class. It must compare only candidates that remain viable under their own family requirements and must use qualitative, non-compensatory criteria rather than a universal score.

<a id="chapter-36-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

WP1 establishes the Chapter-36 entry grammar: one stable source may support several independently testable target-function candidates; source identity and candidate-specific subsets must remain explicit; target coordinates precede compatibility or competition; compatibility does not imply integration; competition requires materially overlapping work; and serious alternatives include narrower claims, source-only treatment, no-projection, and Non-Capture.

WP1 does not establish:

- that candidate `A` and candidate `B` are compatible or co-valid;
