- that candidate `F` and candidate `G` genuinely compete after full coordinate and source-subset audit;
- that any candidate satisfies its family-specific projection burden;
- comparative preference, weaker-candidate preference, indeterminacy, or non-comparability;
- candidate-specific comparative Loss findings;
- no-projection in `A`, `B`, or `C`;
- a local comparison result or canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-36 or RETYPE lock.

```text
shared-source and target-coordinate grammar complete
+ compatibility/competition/alternative architecture complete
≠ comparison executed
≠ preference selected
≠ Output Class selected
```

The next controlled step is Chapter 36 WP2, §§36.5–36.7: Comparative Criteria, Discriminative Performance, and Projection Indeterminacy.

<a id="36-5-comparative-criteria"></a>

### 36.5 Comparative Criteria

Projection comparison begins only after each candidate remains viable under its own family method. Comparison cannot compensate for an incomplete candidate. A broad, elegant, parsimonious, or familiar label does not repair missing Source Trace, missing target work, Type Integrity failure, or an excessive Claim Ceiling.

#### C36-CRITERIA-01 — comparative criteria are qualitative, explicit, and non-compensatory

Every candidate comparison must declare the applicability and result status of at least the following criteria:

```yaml
comparative_criteria:
  praxis_purchase:
    question: what additional target-level praxis difference does the candidate discriminate?
  constitutive_source_trace:
    question: which stable source features and relations carry that difference?
  counterfactual_sensitivity:
    question: which material source or target changes would alter or defeat the candidate?
  exact_loss:
    question: what is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable?
  context_fit:
    question: does the function fit the declared target coordinates rather than a broader narrative?
  type_integrity:
    question: do origin type and contextual target function remain distinct?
  claim_ceiling:
    question: is the claim narrower than causal, universal, normative, or authority-bearing inference?
  parsimony:
    question: what assumptions, source subsets, and target conditions are additionally required?
```

No universal score, weighting formula, optimization rule, or ranking metric is introduced. The criteria are not mutually substitutable:

```text
high PraxisPurchase
+ low Source Trace
≠ admissible candidate

strong context fit
+ Type Integrity failure
≠ bounded projection

parsimony
≠ permission to omit constitutive structure
```

A candidate may remain locally strong on one criterion and fail on another. Comparative language must retain that profile rather than collapse it into one total value. Criteria that lack a justified common basis must be marked non-comparable or uncertain rather than assigned invented equivalence.

#### C36-LOSSCOMPARISON-01 — Loss remains candidate-specific and may resist aggregation

Each candidate retains the exact canonical Loss form:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Comparison asks not which candidate has “less loss” in the abstract, but which structures each candidate must preserve for its own claim and what becomes unavailable through its compression. In the compatibility scene, candidate `A` may preserve interpretive and role-conditioned historical load while candidate `B` preserves transition boundaries and wider-path segmentation. Those preservation tasks are not automatically commensurable.

```text
different Loss profiles
≠ one candidate defective automatically

more preserved detail
≠ better projection automatically

one aggregate Loss score
≠ exact candidate-specific Loss
```

A candidate that preserves irrelevant detail may be less discriminating; a narrower candidate may legitimately compress more while retaining every constitutive structure for its bounded target function. Conversely, parsimony cannot excuse irrecoverable loss of the source features that carry the claimed function. Where candidate Loss profiles serve different Claim Scopes and no justified common basis exists, non-comparability remains a positive result.

#### C36-WEAKERPREFERENCE-01 — a narrower candidate may be comparatively preferable without becoming the source's true type

Comparative preference may favor the less comprehensive candidate where it:

- reacts more clearly to material source changes;
- rejects relevant countercases rather than absorbing them;
- requires fewer unsupported assumptions;
- preserves a tighter Source Trace;
- fits a narrower target context or validity window;
- maintains a lower Claim Ceiling;
- leaves alternatives and no-projection genuinely live.

```text
weaker or narrower claim
+ better discrimination and traceability
→ comparative preference remains possible

comparative preference
≠ absolute truth
≠ source identity
≠ permanent family superiority
```

A broader candidate may still be preferable where the narrower claim omits constitutive relations or artificially divides one target function. WP2 therefore establishes the possibility of bounded preference, not a default rule favoring narrowness.

<a id="36-6-discriminative-performance"></a>

### 36.6 Discriminative Performance

A projection earns comparative standing by changing warranted classification across relevant cases and variations. Merely redescribing the same materials in richer vocabulary is not discriminative performance.

#### C36-DISCRIMINATION-01 — candidates must distinguish positive cases, reject countercases, respond to source variation, and disclose assumption burden

The minimum discriminative test has four parts:

1. **Positive-case discrimination** — identify a target case in which the claimed function adds a concrete praxis difference beyond source-only description.
2. **Countercase rejection** — identify a materially similar case that the candidate must reject, narrow, or classify differently.
3. **Source sensitivity** — vary load-bearing source features or candidate-specific source subsets and test whether the function changes.
4. **Added-assumption burden** — disclose present-target conditions, bridging premises, temporal continuities, and exclusions required by the candidate.

```yaml
discriminative_performance:
  positive_case_difference: required
  countercase_rejection: required
  material_source_variation: required
  added_assumptions: explicit
  result: not_adjudicated
```

A candidate is comparatively weak where it survives the removal of every supposedly constitutive source feature, absorbs its countercases by widening the label, or depends on unmarked present-target assumptions. A candidate is not strengthened merely because its vocabulary covers more scenes.

```text
coverage
≠ discrimination

countercase absorption
≠ robustness

label invariance under material source change
→ Traceability-Ceiling pressure
```

For the competition scene, candidate `F` must identify source-supported historical conditions that alter relevance or credible action corridors in `C`. Candidate `G` must identify a recurrent transition form whose later accessibility or friction changes in `C`. If both make the same classifications under all material variations, the comparison may be underdetermined, the proposed distinction may lack purchase, or one candidate may be a vocabulary substitution rather than a separate function.

#### C36-NOPROJECTION-01 — source-only or present-target accounts may outperform every projection candidate

No-projection is part of comparative performance, not an afterthought. It may be preferable where:

- current target conditions account for the full discriminating difference;
- the historical source remains relevant background but performs no distinct contextual function;
- every candidate is insensitive to material source variation;
- projected functions add assumptions without additional case discrimination;
- available evidence cannot separate source-carried function from present-condition effects.

```text
no-projection preferred
≠ source irrelevant
≠ source reconstruction failed
≠ history erased
```

A source-only description can therefore outperform both candidate projections while preserving the PATH finding. This is a comparative outcome at the claim level, not a global rejection of the source object or its family methods. WP2 does not select no-projection for `A`, `B`, or `C`; it makes the option operationally serious.

<a id="36-7-projection-indeterminacy"></a>

### 36.7 Projection Indeterminacy

Comparison need not end in a single preferred projection. Several disciplined local descriptions may remain available after all candidate-specific and comparative burdens have been applied.

#### C36-COVALIDITY-01 — co-validity and context-dependence remain distinct from integration or contradiction

Co-validity is possible where two candidates independently satisfy their family burdens and perform non-exclusive work under declared contexts, levels, scopes, or validity windows. Context-dependence applies where comparative standing changes with a justified change in target coordinates while source identity remains stable.

```text
candidate A admissible in context A
+ candidate B admissible in context B
→ co-validity may be warranted

co-validity
≠ integrated super-function
≠ identical target work
≠ mutual proof

context-dependent preference
≠ arbitrary analyst choice
```

In the compatibility scene, the frame-function candidate in `A` and Macro-Event-function candidate in `B` may eventually be co-valid because they answer different target questions. That result would preserve separate Records and Loss profiles. WP2 does not adjudicate it.

#### C36-INDETERMINACY-01 — indeterminacy, underdetermination, and non-comparability are not candidate failure

The following local comparison descriptions must remain distinguishable:

- **co-valid** — candidates independently remain admissible and non-exclusive;
- **context-dependent** — preference varies with justified target coordinates;
- **partially preferred** — one candidate is stronger only for a declared role, phase, interval, or praxis dimension;
- **underdetermined** — evidence does not discriminate among comparable candidates;
- **non-comparable** — no warranted common basis exists for target work, evidence, scope, or Loss;
- **non-capture** — available structures cannot support a bounded comparison or projection claim.

These are local descriptions, not new Output Classes. Final mapping occurs only after an executed audit and uses the ten canonical classes.

```text
indeterminacy
≠ failed transformation automatically

non-comparability
≠ contradiction

absence of one winner
≠ permission for forced integration
```

Indeterminacy must identify its location. It may arise from evidence limits, non-separable present conditions, candidate-specific source subsets, non-commensurable Loss, or equal discriminative performance. It must not conceal a candidate that already failed Type Integrity, Source Trace, or its family threshold.

#### C36-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives structured comparison findings without tribunal ranking or canonical result selection

WP2 hands WP3 the following architecture:

```text
candidate-specific family viability
→ qualitative non-compensatory criteria
→ exact candidate-specific Loss comparison
→ positive/countercase/source-sensitivity/assumption tests
→ weaker-candidate and no-projection options
→ co-validity, context-dependence, partial preference,
  underdetermination, and non-comparability descriptions
```

WP3 receives no selected compatibility result for `A/B`, no selected competition or preference for `F/G`, no no-projection result, no final non-comparability or Non-Capture result, no local `PROJECT_AS` result, and no canonical Output Class. It must next establish non-translation, non-contradiction, comparison-record and output discipline, tribunal prohibition, integrated counterfactual pressure, localized Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, and the WP4 handoff.

<a id="chapter-36-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

WP2 establishes qualitative, non-compensatory comparison criteria; exact candidate-specific Loss comparison; positive-case, countercase, source-sensitivity, and assumption-burden tests; the possibility of narrower-candidate preference and no-projection; and disciplined co-validity, context-dependence, partial preference, underdetermination, and non-comparability descriptions.

WP2 does not establish:

- compatibility or co-validity for candidates `A/B`;
- genuine competition or comparative preference for candidates `F/G`;
- any candidate's family-specific admissibility;
- a preferred weaker candidate;
- a selected no-projection result;
- a final indeterminacy, non-comparability, or Non-Capture result;
- non-translation or contradiction status;
- a local comparison result or canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-36 or RETYPE lock.

```text
comparative criteria and discriminative-performance architecture complete
+ indeterminacy descriptions available
≠ pressure scenes adjudicated
≠ preferred projection selected
≠ Output Class selected
```

The next controlled step is Chapter 36 WP3, §§36.8–36.10: Non-Translation, Projection Competition without Tribunal Logic, and Projection Comparison Record.

<a id="36-8-non-translation"></a>
## 36.8 Non-Translation

Projection comparison does not presuppose that every viable target-function claim can be translated into every other candidate without changing what is preserved, compressed, excluded, left uncertain, or rendered irrecoverable. A frame-function may preserve historical conditions of present legibility; a Macro-Event-function may preserve transition segmentation within a wider Path; a dynamic attractor-function may preserve a recurrent transition form. These candidates can share one independently warranted source object while making different parts of that source constitutive for different target work.

#### C36-NONTRANSLATION-01 — admissible projections may preserve non-equivalent dimensions and need not translate losslessly into one another

Non-translation is present where a candidate's target work cannot be restated in the other candidate's target grammar without materially changing at least one of the following:

- target context, target level, or target object;
- Claim Scope, validity scope, or temporal scope;
- candidate-specific Constitutive Source Trace;
- affected praxis dimensions;
- positive-case and countercase boundaries;
- exact five-part Loss;
- relevant alternatives or no-projection burden.

```text
projection F preserves historical framing work
projection G preserves recurrent transition-form work

F cannot be restated as G
without changing target work or constitutive Source Trace
→ F and G may be non-translatable
```

This finding does not imply that one candidate is deeper, truer, more comprehensive, or more authoritative. It establishes only that their warranted claims are not lossless substitutes.

```text
non-translation
≠ source-type difference
≠ absolute incommensurability
≠ theory superiority
≠ authority
```

Translation may remain partial. A comparison may identify a shared source segment, a shared target effect, or a limited correspondence while preserving untranslatable remainder. Partial translation must state what survives and what changes. It may not hide a changed target context, altered Claim Scope, or different Loss profile under a common label.

#### C36-NONCONTRADICTION-01 — non-translation is distinct from contradiction, competition, and vocabulary difference

Four conditions must be separated:

| Condition | Required structure |
|---|---|
| vocabulary difference | different terms with materially equivalent source, target, scope, and claim work |
| non-translation | target work or preserved dimensions cannot be transferred without material claim change |
| competition | candidates occupy sufficiently shared target coordinates and make materially overlapping claims |
| contradiction | the same delimited claim position is affirmed and denied under compatible evidence and scope conditions |

```text
different words
≠ non-translation automatically

non-translation
≠ contradiction automatically

competition
≠ contradiction automatically
```

Two non-translatable projections may be compatible because they operate in different target contexts. Two translatable formulations may compete because they make the same target claim with different labels. Two competing candidates may remain jointly underdetermined rather than contradictory. Contradiction requires a same-claim conflict; it cannot be inferred from semantic distance or different Loss profiles alone.

#### C36-NONCOMPARABILITY-01 — comparison may stop where no justified common basis exists

Non-comparability is a bounded result where target work, Claim Scope, evidence basis, temporal coordinates, or candidate-specific Loss lack a justified common comparison basis. It is not permission to abandon candidate-specific audits. Each candidate must first be assessed under its own family method as far as the source permits.

Potential sources of non-comparability include:

- non-equivalent source subsets with no warranted relation between them;
- different target levels whose praxis differences cannot be responsibly aligned;
- evidence standards that support different claim types but no shared comparison;
- Loss profiles serving genuinely different functions;
- incompatible temporal windows;
- present target conditions that cannot be separated from historical projection;
- materially different Claim Ceilings.

```text
no justified common comparison basis
→ non-comparability may be recorded

non-comparability
≠ candidate false
≠ contradiction
≠ hidden ranking
```

Where only part of the candidate claims is comparable, the comparison must be segmented. The comparable segment may receive a bounded finding; the remaining segment stays non-comparable, underdetermined, or non-captured. A total verdict may not be manufactured from a partial common basis.

### 36.8.1 Output discipline for local comparison descriptions

#### C36-OUTPUTDISCIPLINE-01 — local comparison descriptions map only after a complete executed audit

Terms such as `compatible`, `co-valid`, `context-dependent`, `competing`, `partially preferred`, `underdetermined`, `non-comparable`, `non-translatable`, `source-only account preferred`, and `no-projection preferred` remain local comparison descriptions. They are not new Output Classes and do not bypass the twelve-stage audit or the existing routing contract.

After a complete executed candidate-specific and comparative audit, each delimited claim may map only to one of the ten canonical classes:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

Possible mappings remain claim-specific. Non-translation may coexist with two bounded admissible claims. Non-comparability may require `non_capture` for the comparison claim while preserving candidate-specific findings. Forced translation, invented common metrics, or tribunal ranking may require `mandatory_stop`. A candidate that fails its own projection conditions may map to `failed_transformation` without deciding the remaining candidates.

```text
local comparison description
≠ canonical Output Class

comparison-level non_capture
≠ every candidate non_capture
```

No Output Class is selected for `A/B`, `F/G`, or any Chapter-36 pressure-scene claim in WP3.

<a id="36-9-projection-competition-without-tribunal-logic"></a>
## 36.9 Projection Competition without Tribunal Logic

Projection comparison evaluates bounded claims, not persons, groups, institutions, political orders, legal legitimacy, moral worth, or the absolute standing of theories. Even a warranted comparative preference concerns only which candidate better performs declared target work under the stated source, scope, evidence, Loss, and Claim Ceiling.

#### C36-NOTRIBUNAL-01 — comparative discipline must not become person, institution, politics, law, morality, or theory tribunal

The following transformations are prohibited:

```text
preferred projection
→ person or group classification

comparative weakness
→ moral inferiority

context fit
→ political or legal legitimacy

stronger discrimination
→ institutional authority

bounded claim preference
→ absolute theory rank
```

Comparison cannot authorize recommendation, sanction, diagnosis, enforcement, governance, or authority inheritance. It cannot declare that a person, group, institution, political arrangement, legal order, or theoretical tradition “is” the preferred target function. A target-function claim remains a contextual reconstruction of an origin-typed source object.

The comparison must also resist a subtler tribunal form: treating methodological completeness as semantic superiority. A schema-valid record, exact Loss declaration, completed audit, or successful smoke suite can demonstrate structural discipline. It cannot determine empirical truth, causal adequacy, normative validity, or application authority.

### 36.9.1 Integrated Counterfactual Projection Comparison Test

#### C36-CFTEST-01 — comparison varies coordinates, source subsets, target conditions, countercases, Loss, alternatives, and no-projection without proving causality or rank

The integrated test applies only after candidate-specific family burdens are explicit. It varies at least seven dimensions:

1. **Target-coordinate variation** — change target context, level, object, Claim Scope, validity scope, or temporal scope.
2. **Source-subset variation** — remove, narrow, substitute, or differently segment candidate-specific source structures while preserving the common source identity where warranted.
3. **Target-condition variation** — alter current conditions to test whether the claimed function is historical projection or present-condition effect.
4. **Countercase variation** — introduce cases that preserve surface resemblance but remove the proposed target function.
5. **Loss variation** — test whether different preservation/compression/exclusion choices alter the candidate or comparison.
6. **Alternative-function variation** — test rival target functions, narrower claims, source-only descriptions, and partial translation.
7. **No-projection variation** — test whether no target-function claim performs the declared work more parsimoniously and traceably.

```text
material variation changes one candidate but not another
→ comparative discrimination may increase

all candidates survive every material variation unchanged
→ overelasticity or weak discrimination pressure
```

This test does not establish causal necessity, causal sufficiency, causal percentage, prediction, universal ranking, or source identity. Its role is to expose where claims differ, where they depend on distinct assumptions, and where comparison must be reduced, stopped, or left unresolved.

For the Compatibility Scene, the test asks whether `A` and `B` retain independent target work under their distinct coordinates. For the Competition Scene, it asks whether `F` and `G` still overlap after source-subset, target-condition, countercase, and Loss variation. WP3 defines these questions but does not answer them.

### 36.9.2 Failure localization, Stop, and re-entry

#### C36-FAILURE-01 — candidate failure and comparison failure must be localized without erasing valid findings

Failure can occur at distinct positions:

1. shared-source identity;
2. candidate-specific source subset;
3. target-coordinate declaration;
4. candidate family admissibility;
5. comparison-basis formation;
6. discriminative-performance test;
7. translation or non-translation claim;
8. comparative preference;
9. Output mapping or authority boundary.

```text
candidate F fails
≠ candidate G succeeds automatically

comparison fails
≠ each candidate fails

non-translation claim fails
≠ candidates become identical

no bounded preference available
≠ invented tie-breaker permitted
```

Canonical routes remain distinct:

- `claim_reduction_required` where a narrower source subset, target scope, temporal window, or comparison claim remains supportable;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation requires invented common coordinates, source alteration, forced translation, forced integration, ranking metrics, tribunal judgment, or authority inheritance;
- `failed_transformation` where an executed candidate fails its own `PROJECT_AS` conditions;
- `non_capture` where no responsible comparison or bounded selection is possible with available sources.

A valid source reconstruction, a viable candidate-specific result, or a supported partial comparison remains preserved when another claim fails. Re-entry requires materially new source, target, evidence, Loss, or comparison coordinates and therefore creates a new testable claim.

<a id="36-10-projection-comparison-record"></a>
## 36.10 Projection Comparison Record

The Projection Comparison Record is a controlled comparative view over separate candidate `PROJECT_AS` Records. It does not merge those Records, create a new STRATA operation, expand the Transformation Record schema, or itself select a canonical Output Class.

### 36.10.1 Minimal comparative view

```yaml
projection_comparison_view:
  comparison_id:
  comparison_claim:
  shared_source:
    object:
    origin_type:
    source_frame:
    granularity:
    relative_level:
    temporal_scope:
    evidence_basis:
    prior_disposition:
    uncertainty:
    inherited_loss:
  candidates:
    - candidate_id:
      project_as_record_reference:
      family_method:
      candidate_source_subset:
      target_context:
      target_level:
      target_object:
      target_function:
      claim_scope:
      validity_scope:
      temporal_scope:
      affected_praxis_dimensions:
      constitutive_source_trace:
      counterfactual_sensitivity:
      loss:
        preserved:
        compressed:
        excluded:
        uncertain:
        irrecoverable:
      alternatives:
      no_projection_option:
      claim_ceiling:
      candidate_status:
  relation_assessment:
    shared_target_coordinates:
    compatibility_basis:
    competition_basis:
    translation_status:
    common_comparison_basis:
  comparative_assessment:
    criteria_applicability:
    positive_case_discrimination:
    countercase_rejection:
    source_sensitivity:
    target_condition_sensitivity:
    assumption_burden:
    loss_comparison:
    bounded_preference:
    unresolved_fields:
  governance:
    no_forced_integration:
    no_universal_score:
    no_source_type_replacement:
    no_tribunal_logic:
    authority_inheritance: prohibited
  local_description:
  canonical_output_class: not_selected_until_complete_executed_audit
  stop_condition:
  non_capture_status:
  reentry_condition:
```

This is a prose-level and reference-level view. Candidate Records remain individually responsible for their own operation declaration, target function, Source Trace, Loss, alternatives, governance, and result axes. The comparison view references rather than replaces them.

### 36.10.2 Record discipline

The view must preserve five separations:

```text
candidate record
≠ comparison record

candidate result
≠ comparison result

local comparison description
≠ canonical Output Class

non-translation
≠ contradiction

comparative preference
≠ source identity or authority
```

Where candidates use materially different source objects rather than declared subsets of one stable source, the comparison view must either open a new shared-source claim or record that comparison is unsupported. It may not manufacture shared identity through matching labels.

Where the comparison is segmented, each segment receives its own Claim Scope, local description, unresolved fields, Stop condition, and potential later Output mapping. One global result may not erase candidate-specific or segment-specific differences.

### 36.10.3 Chapter-36 WP3 completion boundary

<a id="chapter-36-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### C36-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete comparison architecture without pressure-scene adjudication or RETYPE lock

WP3 completes the chapter's substantive comparison method:

- non-translation and partial translation;
- distinction from contradiction, competition, and vocabulary difference;
- bounded non-comparability and segmented comparison;
- local-description-to-canonical-class discipline;
- non-tribunal and non-authority boundaries;
- integrated counterfactual comparison;
- localized Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, preservation, and re-entry;
- the Projection Comparison Record view.

WP3 does not establish:

- compatibility or co-validity for `A/B`;
- competition, contradiction, translation, non-translation, preference, or partial preference for `F/G`;
- family-specific admissibility for any pressure-scene candidate;
- a selected source-only or no-projection account;
- final indeterminacy, non-comparability, or Non-Capture;
- a local comparison result;
- a canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-36 or RETYPE lock.

```text
complete comparison architecture
+ twenty-four-duty substantive closure
≠ pressure scenes adjudicated
≠ preferred projection selected
≠ Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 36 provisionally locked
```

The next controlled step is Chapter 36 WP4: integrated family-method lock audit, provisional-lock decision, and Chapter-37 Preparation handoff. No new test, case, or smoke YAML is produced during this handoff.

<a id="chapter-36-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 36 completion boundary

Chapter 36 is complete as a **family-method specification for comparing several bounded projection candidates without forcing integration, ranking, source-type replacement, or tribunal judgment**. The completion decision concerns the comparison method only. It does not execute or classify either stable pressure scene.

```text
one stable origin-typed source
+ separately complete candidate PROJECT_AS claims
+ explicit target and comparison coordinates
+ non-compensatory criteria, exact Loss, rivals, and no-projection
+ translation, indeterminacy, Stop, and Non-Capture discipline
→ complete Chapter-36 comparison method
```

The chapter-level methodological result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This result is not inherited by candidate `A`, `B`, `F`, `G`, any source-only account, any no-projection route, or any later comparison Record.

#### Integrated method finding

The completed method preserves the origin type and independently warranted disposition of the shared source. Candidate plurality remains a plurality of bounded target-function claims rather than a plurality of source identities.

```text
multiple plausible projections
≠ multiple source types
≠ one hidden true type
≠ one candidate must win
```

A comparison begins only after each candidate has declared its own target context, target level, target object, target function, Claim Scope, validity scope, temporal scope, affected praxis dimensions, family-specific Source Trace, evidence basis, exact five-part Loss, alternatives, Stop conditions, and possible negative result. A comparison table cannot complete an incomplete candidate.

```text
comparison-ready fields
≠ candidate admissibility

candidate family-method compliance
≠ target-function warrant established
```

Shared-source language remains strict. Candidate-specific subsets may differ, but they must be declared against one stable source packet. A material change of source Frame, temporal scope, phase selection, relation topology, evidence basis, uncertainty, prior disposition, or inherited Loss creates a new source or comparison claim.

#### Compatibility and competition boundary

Compatibility is a bounded relation among candidates whose target contexts, relative levels, objects, scopes, validity windows, or praxis dimensions are different or complementary enough that co-validity remains possible without integration. Compatibility does not establish that either candidate is substantively admissible.

```text
compatible target coordinates
≠ compatible candidate results
≠ integrated super-function
```

Competition requires materially overlapping target work under the same or responsibly comparable source, target context, target level, target object, temporal scope, Claim Scope, and validity scope. Vocabulary difference, different family names, or one shared source label does not establish competition.

```text
different words
≠ competing claims

shared source label
≠ shared comparison basis
```

Where overlap is only partial, the comparison must be segmented. One global conclusion may not erase segment-specific compatibility, competition, non-comparability, unresolved fields, Stop conditions, or Loss differences.

#### Comparative criteria and discrimination

The completed method compares candidates through:

- PraxisPurchase;
- Constitutive Source Trace;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- exact candidate-specific Loss;
- Context Fit;
- Type Integrity;
- Claim Ceiling;
- parsimony and added-assumption burden.

These criteria are qualitative and non-compensatory. Elegance, comprehensiveness, formal completeness, coverage, Reader visibility, or a low apparent assumption count cannot compensate for missing Source Trace, Type Integrity, Contextual Boundedness, or a failed Claim Ceiling.

```text
high coverage
+ weak discrimination
≠ stronger projection

high PraxisPurchase
+ missing Type Integrity
≠ admissible candidate
```

Discriminative performance requires a candidate to distinguish positive cases, reject relevant countercases, react to material source changes, and expose its additional assumptions and present-target conditions. A candidate that absorbs all counterpressure or remains label-invariant under material change exceeds the Traceability Ceiling.

A narrower candidate may be comparatively preferable when it is more source-sensitive, parsimonious, countercase-discriminating, and tightly scoped. Such preference remains local and bounded.

```text
bounded comparative preference
≠ absolute truth
≠ family superiority
≠ source identity
```

A source-only, present-target-condition, or no-projection account may outperform all target-function candidates. This preserves the source reconstruction while denying that a distinct target function has been warranted.

#### Loss, translation, and non-comparability

Every candidate retains its own canonical Loss profile:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Different Loss profiles may reflect different legitimate target work. More preserved detail is not automatically better, and one aggregate Loss score is prohibited. Where candidate losses, target work, evidence, or Claim Scopes lack a justified common basis, non-comparability remains a positive result.

Full translation is available only where one candidate's target work, Source Trace, scope, evidence, and Loss can be transferred to another without material claim change. Partial translation must name the shared segment and retain the untranslatable remainder.

```text
non-translation
≠ contradiction
≠ source-type difference
≠ absolute incommensurability
```

Contradiction requires incompatible claims at the same delimited claim position under compatible evidence and scope conditions. Competition, non-translation, non-comparability, and vocabulary difference remain separate.

#### Indeterminacy and output discipline

The completed method retains bounded local descriptions including:

- co-valid;
- context-dependent;
- partially preferred;
- underdetermined;
- non-comparable;
- non-translatable;
- no-projection preferred.

These descriptions are not new Output Classes. Each executed, delimited claim is mapped only after a complete audit to exactly one of the ten canonical classes.

```text
local comparison description
≠ canonical Output Class

comparison-level non_capture
≠ every candidate non_capture
```

Indeterminacy is not hidden failure. It may preserve separately supported candidate claims, expose missing comparison evidence, or require a narrower segmented comparison. Non-Capture remains available where no responsible comparison or target-function claim can be established from the available sources.

#### Comparison Record and operation boundary

The Projection Comparison Record remains a bounded reference view over separate candidate `PROJECT_AS` Records. It records stable source identity, candidate-specific source subsets, target coordinates, family checks, exact Loss, comparison basis, translation status, discrimination, assumption burden, local descriptions, unresolved fields, Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry.

```text
candidate record
≠ comparison record

comparison record
≠ fourth STRATA operation
≠ merged candidate claim
≠ automatic result engine
```

The view cannot infer candidate validity, compatibility, competition, translation, preference, route, Output Class, semantic superiority, or empirical truth. It cannot repair a candidate that lacks its own operation-specific burden.

#### Non-tribunal and authority boundary

Chapter 36 compares bounded analytical claims. It does not compare the worth, legitimacy, maturity, dignity, or authority of persons, groups, institutions, political positions, legal positions, or theories as wholes.

```text
preferred projection
≠ person classification
≠ moral verdict
≠ political or legal legitimacy decision
≠ institutional rank
≠ absolute theory tribunal
```

Schema validity, audit completeness, smoke-test success, formal elegance, translation breadth, or machine-readable consistency cannot increase claim authority. No recommendation, sanction, governance route, or authority inheritance follows from a comparative result.

#### Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and preservation

Failure remains localized to the exact claim position: shared-source identity, candidate-specific Source Trace, target coordinates, family admissibility, comparison basis, discrimination, Loss comparison, translation claim, comparative preference, Output mapping, or authority boundary.

The canonical routes remain distinct:

- `claim_reduction_required` where a narrower candidate, segment, temporal window, source subset, target function, or source-only claim survives;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation would require invented commonality, compensatory scoring, source-type replacement, forced integration, tribunal logic, authority inflation, or hidden output selection;
- `failed_transformation` where an executed delimited projection or comparison claim fails its declared conditions;
- `non_capture` where available sources cannot responsibly establish or compare the relevant claims.

```text
failed comparison
≠ shared source false
≠ every candidate false

failed candidate
≠ rival candidate automatically admissible
```

A material change in source, target coordinates, candidate family, comparison segment, evidence, Loss, rival set, or no-projection account creates a new testable claim. It does not rescue the prior claim by relabeling.

#### Protected non-adjudication of the Chapter-36 pressure scenes

The compatibility scene remains unexecuted:

```yaml
compatibility_scene:
  shared_source: documented_trajectory_N
  candidate_A:
    target_context: later_configuration_A
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  candidate_B:
    target_context: wider_path_B
    target_function: bounded_macro_event_function
  status: unadjudicated
```

Chapter 36 does not establish that `A/B` are compatible, co-valid, context-dependent, non-comparable, partially translatable, or admissible.

The competition scene also remains unexecuted:

```yaml
competition_scene:
  shared_source: documented_trajectory_N
  shared_target_context: continuation_field_C
  candidate_F:
    target_function: bounded_frame_function
  candidate_G:
    target_function: bounded_dynamic_attractor_function
  status: unadjudicated
```

Chapter 36 does not establish competition, contradiction, translation, non-translation, comparative preference, source-only preference, no-projection, indeterminacy, Non-Capture, a local comparison result, or a canonical Output Class for `F/G`.

```text
Chapter 36 provisionally locked
≠ A/B compatibility established
≠ A/B co-validity established
≠ F/G competition established
≠ F/G translation status established
≠ preferred projection selected
≠ no-projection selected
≠ comparison Output Class selected
≠ RETYPE locked
```

#### Integrated completion decision

All §§36.1–36.10 are complete. All twenty-four assigned Chapter-36 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. The chapter preserves source identity, target-coordinate explicitness, candidate-family burdens, compatibility/competition separation, qualitative non-compensatory criteria, exact Loss, discrimination, narrower-candidate preference, no-projection, indeterminacy, non-comparability, non-translation, segmented comparison, output discipline, tribunal prohibition, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, source preservation, and authority non-inheritance.

```text
complete competing-projections comparison specification
+ twenty-four-duty closure
+ integrated scientific and formal audit pass
+ protected non-adjudication of A/B and F/G
→ Chapter 36 provisionally locked
```

The chapter-level methodological class is `admissible_but_provisional`. It does not flow to either pressure scene and does not preselect any later case result.

#### Chapter-37 handoff boundary

Chapter 37 receives six locked constraints from Chapter 36 and the preceding projection chapters:

1. a valid projection remains a complete bounded `PROJECT_AS` claim with typed source, target function, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, and Claim Ceiling;
2. structural similarity, formal correspondence, symbolic mapping, or executable translation does not establish semantic preservation;
3. analogy may remain useful and terminate legitimately without being upgraded to projection;
4. label substitution lacks new PraxisPurchase, constitutive Source Trace, material source sensitivity, and bounded target function;
5. cross-domain residuals, target-only features, unmapped source structures, and failed semantic preservation remain visible;
6. `analogy_only`, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain available without theory, person, or domain tribunal logic.

Chapter 37 must separate functional projection, structural analogy, and label substitution through non-overlapping minimum requirements. It receives no cross-domain projection result, analogy status, semantic-preservation finding, label-substitution finding, canonical Output Class for a pressure object, new PMS primitive, authority increase, or RETYPE lock.

## 37. Projection, Structural Analogy, and Label Substitution

Chapter 37 establishes a boundary that RETYPE cannot leave implicit. A source and a target may share sequence, branching, recurrence, position, topology, vocabulary, executable form, or analytical convenience without thereby sharing origin type, praxeological semantics, or contextual function. Conversely, a mapping can be substantively useful even when it does not satisfy `PROJECT_AS`. The method therefore needs three non-overlapping claim forms: valid functional projection, bounded structural analogy, and label substitution.

Chapter 29 owns the generic distinction between origin type and target function. Chapter 30 owns the complete `PROJECT_AS` procedure. Chapters 31–35 own family-specific projection burdens, and Chapter 36 owns comparison among complete or comparison-ready projection candidates. Chapter 37 does not reopen those definitions. It determines which mapping claim is being made and what minimum burden that claim must carry before any substantive result is considered.

```text
valid functional projection
≠ structural analogy
≠ label substitution

formal correspondence
≠ semantic preservation

useful mapping
≠ valid PROJECT_AS automatically
```

The chapter uses two stable but distinct pressure packets. Mapping candidate `M` relates a documented historical Trajectory `S` to an executable technical state-transition trace `T`. It may preserve sequence, branch, and rollback form while leaving expectation, Non-Event, role asymmetry, binding, responsibility, and exit-cost semantics unmapped. Label candidate `L` assigns `dynamic attractor` to a repeated workflow trace `U` without a declared target context, Source Trace, exact Loss, alternatives, or tested function. `M` and `L` must not be collapsed: a potentially useful analogy is not the same failure position as an unsupported label.

WP1 establishes the triad, the complete projection burden, the stable source lock, the analogy boundary, the cross-domain coordinate packet, semantic-preservation pressure, and residual discipline. It does not select analogy status for `M`, establish semantic preservation or target function, classify `L` as label substitution, route either packet, or map an Output Class.

<a id="37-1-why-the-distinction-matters"></a>

### 37.1 Why the Distinction Matters

The distinction matters because the same visible correspondence can support radically different claims. A sequence-to-sequence mapping may merely organize comparison. It may preserve one bounded relation while excluding most source semantics. It may support a target function after a complete `PROJECT_AS` audit. Or it may be no more than a familiar label attached to recurrence. Treating these as one continuum would let rhetorical strength or technical elegance replace the actual transformation burden.

#### C37-TRIAD-01 — functional projection, structural analogy, and label substitution have non-overlapping minimum requirements

The minimum triad is:

| Claim form | Minimum assertion | Required burden | Does not establish |
|---|---|---|---|
| Valid functional projection | An origin-typed source object performs a bounded target function in a declared context | Complete `PROJECT_AS` burden, family-specific conditions, PraxisPurchase, Constitutive Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, alternatives, Claim Ceiling | source-type replacement, semantic identity, authority |
| Structural analogy | Declared source and target features are similar in a bounded and useful respect | explicit relata, comparison dimension, mapping direction, preserved relation, residuals, incompatibilities, uncertainty, scope | origin-type identity, semantic preservation, target function |
| Label substitution | A PMS-derived label is assigned without the burden needed for projection or even a delimited analogy | no independent positive burden; identified through missing function, discrimination, trace, sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, or context | PraxisPurchase, explanation, type, function, warrant |

These are not three ranks on one scale. Analogy is not an incomplete projection by definition, and label substitution is not merely a weak analogy. A bounded analogy may be the correct terminal claim because it preserves a real comparison while refusing unsupported semantic or functional transfer. Label substitution lacks that disciplined relational burden.

```text
projection burden satisfied
→ bounded target-function claim may be considered

bounded similarity burden satisfied
+ target-function burden unestablished
→ analogy remains possible

familiar PMS label
+ no new discrimination or traceable function
→ substitution pressure
```

The triad also prevents two opposite errors. The first is **projection inflation**: every formal resemblance is promoted to `PROJECT_AS`. The second is **analogy suppression**: a useful but semantically limited mapping is declared worthless because it cannot carry a target function. STRATA permits neither move.

A mapping-status claim remains local. The same source-target pair can support a structural analogy along one declared dimension and fail a functional projection along another. Such segmentation requires separate Claim Scopes. It does not permit the analogy to lend its warrant automatically to the projection.

```text
same relata
≠ same claim

analogy support
≠ projection support

projection failure
≠ analogy failure automatically
```

The triad is also non-authoritative. A valid projection does not rank domains. A useful analogy does not establish model superiority. A failed label claim does not imply that the target domain, source domain, or source object is false or inferior.

<a id="37-2-valid-functional-projection"></a>

### 37.2 Valid Functional Projection

A valid functional projection is the strongest of the three claim forms because it asserts more than resemblance. It states that an already typed source object makes a bounded, source-dependent praxeological difference in a declared target context. That claim must remain vulnerable to source variation, target variation, rivals, no-projection, exact Loss, and the relevant family method.

#### C37-PROJECTION-01 — valid projection remains a complete bounded PROJECT_AS claim

The minimum form is unchanged:

```text
origin-typed source object X
+ retained source reference and prior disposition
+ declared target context C
+ bounded target function F
+ PraxisPurchase
+ Constitutive Source Trace
+ Counterfactual Sensitivity
+ exact Loss
+ serious alternatives and no-projection
+ Claim Ceiling
→ candidate PROJECT_AS(X, F, C)
```

Every term is required. A mapping cannot become projection merely because the target representation executes, the source and target graphs are isomorphic, the terminology is familiar, or the mapping is useful for visualization. A concrete target function must identify what difference the source object makes to reconstruction of later or otherwise situated praxis.

For a cross-domain candidate, the target-function statement must be more specific than “helps understanding,” “corresponds to,” “models,” or “looks like.” It must identify a bounded contextual role and the praxis dimension affected. Where the target domain contains no praxeological object or function corresponding to the claim, the mapping may remain formal or analogical; vocabulary cannot create the missing function.

```text
formal fit
+ executable consistency
+ broad translation
≠ target function

source term reused in target
≠ source-dependent praxis difference
```

The complete burden remains non-compensatory. Strong formal correspondence cannot compensate for missing Type Integrity. High explanatory appeal cannot compensate for missing Source Trace. A narrow target function cannot compensate for erased Loss, and an exact Loss declaration cannot create PraxisPurchase.

A valid projection also preserves origin type. If `S` is a Trajectory, it remains a Trajectory. A later technical or analytical function cannot convert it into an operator type, technical state-machine type, or new cross-domain primitive.

```text
Trajectory S projected as bounded target function
≠ S becomes target-domain type
≠ target object becomes Trajectory by label
≠ new PMS primitive formed
```

#### C37-SOURCELOCK-01 — source identity, mapping subset, prior disposition, uncertainty, inherited Loss, and unmapped source residuals remain explicit

The Chapter-37 source packet is fixed:

```yaml
source_packet:
  object: documented_transition_trajectory_S
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_S
  domain: historical_praxis_domain_S
  frame: declared_practice_frame_S
  temporal_scope: period_S
  source_features:
    - ordered_transition_phases
    - branch_and_reversal_structure
    - warranted_non_events
    - role_and_asymmetry_relations
    - binding_and_exit_costs
  prior_disposition: retained
  uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

A candidate may use an explicit mapping subset, such as ordered phases, branch points, and reversals. It may not silently redefine the Source Object as only those features. The source-only remainder remains part of the source packet and must be visible in the mapping assessment.

```yaml
candidate_mapping_subset:
  included:
    - ordered_transition_phases
    - branch_and_reversal_structure
  not_claimed_as_mapped:
    - warranted_non_events
    - role_and_asymmetry_relations
    - binding_and_exit_costs
```

This distinction protects source continuity. A subset can delimit what a mapping claims to carry; it cannot delete features that challenge semantic preservation. If `M` later requires removal of Non-Events, role relations, or exit costs to look exact, the exclusion must appear in Loss and residual declarations. If the source period, Frame, relations, evidence, or prior disposition changes, a new mapping or projection claim is required.

The inherited five-field Loss remains exact:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Cross-domain target-only features do not create a sixth Loss field. They are declared separately as target residuals. This prevents source Loss from being overloaded with properties the source never possessed.

```text
unmapped source feature
→ source-side residual and Loss relevance

target-only feature
→ target residual

mapping residual
≠ sixth Loss field
```

A failed mapping does not alter the source's prior disposition. The locked PATH Trajectory remains independently warranted unless a separate source-side claim fails.

#### C37-OCCURRENCETRACE-01 — projection and analogy retain occurrence-level source relations without deriving them from the mapped label

Where the source packet is PMS-typed or PMS-derived, the mapping assessment must identify the concrete, already warranted occurrence relations that the candidate claims to preserve, compress, exclude, or leave uncertain. Terms such as Non-Event, Asymmetry, recurrence, Binding, or Recontextualization count as PMS anchors only when the linked source Record supports the corresponding occurrence and relation in its declared Frame and temporal position.

```text
PMS label retained in the mapping
≠ occurrence-level relation preserved

occurrence-level relation preserved under a declared comparison dimension
≠ contextual target function established automatically
```

The label-removal pressure test remains decisive: if ordinary source- and target-domain language preserves the same discrimination while the PMS-derived label can be removed without changing Source Trace, sensitivity, or Loss, the stronger projection claim loses support. If removing or materially changing a warranted occurrence relation alters the candidate, that response supports claim sensitivity but still does not prove semantic identity or target function.

<a id="37-3-structural-analogy"></a>

### 37.3 Structural Analogy

Structural analogy is a bounded relation of similarity between declared source and target structures. It can support comparison, contrast, pedagogy, representation, hypothesis formation, or exposure of differences. Its legitimate use depends on precision about what is similar and what remains outside the analogy.

#### C37-ANALOGY-01 — structural analogy establishes bounded similarity, not type identity, semantic preservation, or target function

The minimum analogy form is:

```text
source object X with feature relation R_x
+ target object Y with feature relation R_y
+ declared comparison dimension D
+ warranted similarity between R_x and R_y under D
+ explicit residuals, incompatibilities, uncertainty, and scope
→ structural analogy candidate
```

An analogy claim must state its comparison dimension. “Both are trajectories,” “both have attractors,” or “both are systems” is too broad unless the terms are used only descriptively and the relevant relation is specified. A valid bounded statement may instead be:

```text
ordered historical transition phases in S
are structurally comparable to
ordered executable states in T
with respect to sequence and branch representation
```

That statement does not assert that executable states have expectations, commitments, roles, responsibility, Non-Events, or exit costs. It does not assert that `T` is a PMS Trajectory or that `S` is a technical workflow.

```text
similar sequence relation
≠ same semantic role

similar graph position
≠ same praxeological function

useful analogy
≠ origin-type identity
≠ semantic preservation
≠ target function
```

Analogy is not exempt from traceability. Its source and target relata, comparison dimension, mapping direction, preserved relation, residuals, counterexamples, and Claim Ceiling must be explicit. What it does not require is the stronger claim that the source object performs a contextual target function. That burden belongs only to `PROJECT_AS`.

An analogy can reveal non-equivalence. For example, mapping historical reversals to rollback transitions may make clear that a technical rollback is rule-governed and executable whereas a historical reversal may remain contested, incomplete, and conditioned by expectation and cost. The analogy is useful precisely because the mismatch becomes visible.

```text
analogy reveals difference
≠ analogy failed
```

#### C37-SEMANTIC-01 — positional or formal similarity remains distinct from comparable praxeological function and semantic preservation

Semantic preservation is stronger than matching labels or relations. It asks whether the mapped elements retain sufficiently comparable role, dependence, constraint, consequence, and praxeological load for the delimited claim. The question is not whether two diagrams can be aligned, but what their aligned elements do within their respective domains.

A source branch point may represent practical alternatives under uncertainty, unequal access, prior commitment, or anticipated cost. A target branch node may represent a deterministic conditional in executable code. Both occupy a branching position, but the reasons, constraints, and consequences of branching differ.

```text
same topology
≠ same dependence structure

same sequence
≠ same temporality

same recurrence
≠ same expectation or friction

same transition label
≠ same praxeological consequence
```

The assessment therefore distinguishes at least three layers:

1. **Positional correspondence** — elements occupy similar positions in a declared structure.
2. **Formal relational correspondence** — relations such as sequence, branch, reversal, or recurrence can be represented under a common formalism.
3. **Praxeological semantic preservation** — the mapped relation performs sufficiently comparable practical work under a bounded Claim Scope.

The first two may support analogy without the third. The third cannot be inferred from the first two. It requires domain-grounded warrant and remains beyond automatic formal-model adjudication.

Semantic preservation is also claim-relative rather than total. A mapping may preserve branch-order comparison while not preserving responsibility, expectation, or exit-cost semantics. Any later claim of partial preservation must identify the preserved relation and the unmapped remainder; WP1 does not select such a status for `M`.

<a id="37-4-cross-domain-projection"></a>

### 37.4 Cross-Domain Projection

Cross-domain assessment begins by declaring the domains, relata, direction, target context, and proposed correspondence. Without these coordinates, the phrase “PMS applies to the technical workflow” is neither a projection nor a disciplined analogy. It is only an underdeclared mapping assertion.

#### C37-CROSSDOMAIN-01 — source domain, target domain, target object, target context, mapping direction, and proposed correspondence are mandatory

The stable mapping candidate is:

```yaml
mapping_candidate_M:
  source_object: documented_transition_trajectory_S
  source_domain: historical_praxis_domain_S
  source_origin_type: Trajectory
  target_object: executable_state_transition_trace_T
  target_domain: technical_workflow_domain_T
  target_context: technical_transition_comparison_context_T
  mapping_direction: source_S_to_target_T
  proposed_mapping:
    source_transition_phases: target_state_sequence
    source_branch_points: target_branch_nodes
    source_reversals: target_rollback_transitions
  claimed_gain:
    - compare_transition_form
    - expose_sequence_and_branch_differences
  semantic_preservation_status: unestablished
  target_function_status: unestablished
  initial_status: analogy_candidate_only
```

`initial_status` is a preparation label, not an adjudicated mapping result or Output Class. WP1 does not establish that `M` is analogy-only, partially analogous, semantically preserving, functionally projectable, or non-captured.

The mapping direction is material. Mapping `S → T` asks which source relations can be represented or function within the technical target. Reverse mapping `T → S` would be a distinct claim because technical execution constraints, determinism, failure states, or machine-specific semantics may not transfer back into historical praxis. Direction may change residuals, Loss, Claim Scope, and target-function burden.

```text
S → T mapping
≠ T → S mapping

bidirectional notation
≠ bidirectional semantic preservation
```

The target context must also be concrete. A technical workflow may be used for visualization, execution, validation, simulation, comparison, control, or pedagogy. These are different target contexts and can support different claims. A change in target context creates a new testable mapping or projection claim.

The label-substitution packet remains distinct:

```yaml
label_substitution_candidate_L:
  target_object: repeated_workflow_trace_U
  assigned_label: dynamic_attractor
  target_context: undeclared
  source_trace: absent
  target_function: metaphorical_only
  counterfactual_sensitivity: untested
  loss: undeclared
  serious_alternatives: absent
  initial_status: substitution_pressure_only
```

WP1 retains `L` only as substitution pressure. It does not yet execute the substitution-marker audit or select `failed_transformation`, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, analogy-only, or another class.

#### C37-RESIDUALS-01 — source-only residuals, target-only features, artificial fitting risks, and incompatibilities remain visible

A cross-domain mapping must carry a two-sided residual ledger in addition to the canonical source Loss:

```yaml
mapping_residuals:
  source_only:
    - warranted_non_events
    - role_and_asymmetry_relations
    - binding_and_exit_costs
    - expectation_and_responsibility_semantics
  target_only:
    - executable_state_semantics
    - deterministic_transition_guards
    - machine_specific_failure_conditions
    - compilation_or_runtime_constraints
  incompatibilities:
    - contested_historical_phase_boundaries_vs_declared_state_boundaries
    - practical_uncertainty_vs_rule_encoded_branching
    - irreversible_social_cost_vs_technical_rollback
  artificial_fitting_risks:
    - redescribing_target_guards_as_commitments
    - redescribing_repetition_as_attractor_function
    - deleting_source_residuals_to_improve_graph_fit
```

Source-only residuals show what the mapping does not carry. Target-only features show what cannot be inherited from the source. Incompatibilities identify relations that are not merely absent but differently constituted. Artificial fitting identifies changes made only to make the target look PMS-compatible.

```text
residual disclosed
≠ defect automatically

residual erased
→ Traceability-Ceiling pressure

new target vocabulary chosen only to match PMS
→ artificial-fitting pressure
```

Residual visibility prevents two false completions. First, a formally neat mapping cannot claim total semantic preservation by omitting source features with no target equivalent. Second, a technical target cannot be explained entirely through the source when its executable constraints and target-only structures perform independent work.

The cross-domain claim remains bounded by the same authority ceiling:

```text
mapping breadth
≠ PMS completeness
≠ model superiority
≠ domain rank
≠ application authority
```

#### C37-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives stable M/L packets without mapping-status, semantic, substitution, or Output-Class adjudication

WP1 hands WP2:

- the non-overlapping projection/analogy/substitution triad;
- the complete generic `PROJECT_AS` burden;
- stable Source Object `S`, explicit candidate subset, prior disposition, uncertainty, inherited Loss, and source residuals;
- mapping candidate `M` with declared domains, object, context, direction, correspondence, claimed gain, and unresolved semantic/function status;
- label candidate `L` as a separate underdeclared pressure packet;
- the positional/formal/praxeological semantic distinction;
- source-only, target-only, incompatibility, and artificial-fitting declarations;
- live no-projection, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture routes;
- unchanged inventory, schema, and non-authority boundaries.

WP1 does not hand WP2:

- an adjudicated analogy status for `M`;
- semantic-preservation or target-function establishment;
- a determination that `L` is label substitution;
- a selected partial analogy or formal-only result;
- a local route or canonical Output Class;
- a new operation, primitive, Rule, audit stage, schema field, Loss field, score, similarity metric, domain hierarchy, or authority;
- Chapter-37 or RETYPE lock.

```text
Chapter 37 WP1 complete
+ triad and cross-domain entry architecture established
+ M/L packets stable
+ residual and semantic-preservation burdens explicit
≠ M analogy status selected
≠ semantic preservation established
≠ target function established
≠ L substitution result selected
≠ Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 37 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-37-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

All §§37.1–37.4 are complete. The eight assigned WP1 Pressure Duties occur exactly once. The chapter now possesses a stable mapping-entry architecture but no executed mapping-status audit.

The next controlled step is WP2, §§37.5–37.8: symbolic, formal, and executable mapping; executable-correspondence limits; analogy as a legitimate terminal status; partial analogy; label substitution and its operational signs; and the cross-domain counterfactual mapping test.
<a id="37-5-symbolic-formal-and-executable-mapping"></a>

### 37.5 Symbolic, Formal, and Executable Mapping

Cross-domain work must distinguish four increasingly demanding claim forms. They may coexist, but none inherits the warrant of the next:

```text
symbolic mapping
→ notation or label correspondence

formal mapping
→ declared relation-preserving correspondence

executable mapping
→ target-side implementation, simulation, compilation, or workflow execution

functional projection
→ bounded source-dependent target function under PROJECT_AS
```

#### C37-SYMBOLIC-01 — symbolic, formal, and executable mapping remain distinct from praxeological functional projection

A **symbolic mapping** associates signs, labels, nodes, or fields. It may support indexing, visualization, or communication. It does not establish that the associated relata have the same origin type, semantic role, or function.

A **formal mapping** declares which relations are intended to correspond and under which constraints. It may preserve order, adjacency, branching, reversal, recurrence, or other explicitly named structure. It does not establish that those relations carry comparable praxeological load.

An **executable mapping** additionally shows that a target-side representation can run, compile, simulate, validate, or move through a declared workflow. This is evidence for target-side technical consistency. It is not evidence that the source semantics have been preserved or that the source object performs a bounded target function.

A valid functional projection remains a separate `PROJECT_AS` claim. It requires the complete burden specified in Chapters 29–30 and the family-specific burdens relevant to the source object.

```text
same symbol
≠ same relation

same relation schema
≠ same semantic role

successful execution
≠ source-dependent target function
```

For mapping candidate `M`, the declared phase/sequence, branch/node, and reversal/rollback correspondences may support symbolic or formal assessment. Even a technically executable implementation would not by itself establish comparable expectation, responsibility, binding, asymmetry, Non-Event, or exit-cost semantics.

The mapping layer must therefore disclose its strongest warranted status rather than adopting the vocabulary of the strongest imaginable status. A formal mapping must not be described as projection merely because it is useful. An executable mapping must not be described as semantic preservation merely because it runs.

#### C37-EXECUTABLE-01 — execution, compilation, simulation, or workflow success cannot serve as semantic or empirical proof

Executable success is target-relative. It can depend on target-only conditions such as deterministic guards, runtime constraints, state declarations, compilation rules, or machine-specific failure handling. Those conditions may explain why the technical artifact works even when source-side praxis semantics are absent.

```text
implementation success
→ target-side consistency evidence

implementation success
≠ empirical validation of the source account
≠ semantic equivalence across domains
≠ causal equivalence
≠ PROJECT_AS admissibility
```

The converse also holds. A mapping can fail to compile or execute because of target-language limitations while the declared structural analogy remains intelligible. Technical failure therefore does not automatically refute every bounded correspondence claim.

```text
executable failure
≠ structural analogy false automatically

executable success
≠ structural analogy complete automatically
```

The formal model may validate that symbolic, formal, executable, and projection statuses are declared separately. It may not infer semantic preservation, analogy usefulness, empirical truth, or target-function warrant from implementation status.

<a id="37-6-analogy-as-a-legitimate-terminal-status"></a>

### 37.6 Analogy as a Legitimate Terminal Status

An analogy need not be treated as an unfinished projection. It can be the strongest responsible result when a bounded correspondence has clear comparative value but semantic preservation or target-function warrant is absent, unnecessary, or unavailable.

#### C37-ANALOGYSTATUS-01 — analogy is a legitimate terminal mapping status and may end in `analogy_only` without upgrade pressure

A terminal analogy claim must identify:

- the source and target relata;
- the declared comparison dimension;
- the mapping direction;
- the relation or relations actually preserved;
- the practical or analytical gain of comparison;
- source-only residuals;
- target-only features and incompatibilities;
- uncertainty, countercases, and Claim Ceiling;
- why no stronger target-function claim is made.

After a complete audit, such a claim may map to the existing canonical class `analogy_only`. The class records useful bounded resemblance without a warranted functional projection. It is not a provisional promise that `PROJECT_AS` will eventually succeed.

```text
analogy_only
≠ failed transformation by default
≠ incomplete PROJECT_AS by definition
≠ request for stronger vocabulary
```

Analogy can create genuine PraxisPurchase by:

- exposing a sequence or branch difference;
- testing whether a formal model suppresses source residuals;
- revealing target-only constraints;
- clarifying which structures are and are not transferable;
- generating a countercase against an overbroad projection;
- improving comparison without asserting identity.

This purchase remains bounded. It does not establish a target function, shared origin type, semantic equivalence, causal identity, model superiority, or application authority.

For `M`, `analogy_only` remains available but is not selected in WP2. The mapping still requires the counterfactual and residual audit below. Formal usefulness, executable success, or an intuitively persuasive correspondence cannot preselect the class.

A no-projection outcome and an analogy-only outcome are also distinct. No-projection rejects or withholds a target-function claim. Analogy-only positively warrants a bounded resemblance. They may coexist at different claim positions:

```text
no warranted target function
+ warranted bounded correspondence
→ analogy_only remains possible
```

<a id="37-7-partial-analogy"></a>

### 37.7 Partial Analogy

Cross-domain correspondences often preserve one relation while failing to preserve others. Such cases must be reduced to the exact relation carried rather than described as globally similar or globally dissimilar.

#### C37-PARTIAL-01 — partial analogy must declare the preserved relation, unmapped remainder, uncertainty, and Claim Ceiling

A partial-analogy claim requires at least:

```yaml
partial_analogy_claim:
  source_object:
  target_object:
  mapping_direction:
  preserved_relation:
  comparison_dimension:
  source_only_remainder:
  target_only_remainder:
  incompatibilities:
  uncertainty:
  countercases:
  claim_ceiling:
  target_function_status:
```

The preserved relation must be specific enough to be removed, reversed, or replaced in a counterfactual test. “Broad similarity” is not a sufficient preserved relation. The unmapped remainder must remain visible even when it is inconvenient to the analogy.

For candidate `M`, possible relation-level claims include:

| Candidate correspondence | Possible bounded gain | Unmapped or incompatible remainder |
|---|---|---|
| transition phases ↔ state sequence | compare declared ordering | contested historical boundaries, practical uncertainty, expectation |
| branch points ↔ branch nodes | compare branching representations | historical availability, blocked alternatives, source-supported counterfactual status |
| reversals ↔ rollback transitions | compare return or reversal form | irreversible social cost, responsibility, binding, machine-defined rollback |

The table records pressure candidates only. It does not adjudicate any preserved relation.

Partial analogy is not a percentage, score, weighted average, or similarity metric. A single well-supported correspondence can be sufficient for a narrow claim; many superficial correspondences can remain insufficient.

```text
three correspondences
≠ stronger analogy automatically

one constitutive correspondence
≠ weak analogy automatically

partial analogy
≠ fifty-percent semantic preservation
```

Where the declared claim is broader than the preserved relation, Claim Reduction remains available. Reduction must name the retained comparison and the excluded remainder. It must not hide the failed broader projection or silently reset the Source Object.

Reverse mapping remains independently testable. A relation preserved from `S → T` may not remain preserved from `T → S`, because target execution semantics can become constitutive in the reverse direction.

<a id="37-8-label-substitution-and-counterfactual-mapping-test"></a>

### 37.8 Label Substitution and Counterfactual Mapping Test

Label substitution occurs when a PMS or STRATA-derived term supplies rhetorical compression without the burden required for the claim it appears to make. The problem is not terminology itself. The problem is that the label stands in for missing target coordinates, function, discrimination, Source Trace, sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, or type discipline.

#### C37-SUBSTITUTION-01 — label substitution is identified through missing functional and discriminative warrant, not through unfamiliarity or domain distance

A label-substitution claim is under pressure when one or more of the following remain absent:

- a declared target context;
- a bounded target function;
- new praxeological discrimination supplied by the label;
- constitutive Source Trace;
- material-source and target sensitivity;
- exact five-part Loss;
- serious alternative descriptions;
- a no-label or no-projection comparison;
- preserved origin type and dependency structure.

No single missing field automatically proves the final route in every case. The omissions identify where the claim must be reduced, repaired, stopped, failed, or left uncaptured. A familiar label cannot compensate for the missing burden.

```text
PMS vocabulary present
≠ PMS relation established

rhetorical coherence
≠ praxeological purchase

label explains by repetition
≠ source-dependent discrimination
```

Candidate `L` assigns `dynamic_attractor` to repeated workflow trace `U` while target context, Source Trace, target function, sensitivity, Loss, and alternatives remain absent or metaphorical. WP2 therefore retains `L` as a concentrated substitution-pressure packet. It does not yet select `failed_transformation`, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, analogy-only, or another canonical result.

#### C37-SIGNS-01 — substitution markers include label invariance, undefined context, metaphor-only function, missing Loss or rivals, and rhetorical strength without test load

Operational markers include:

1. **Label invariance:** the label survives material changes in source relations, target conditions, recurrence pattern, or countercases.
2. **Undefined target context:** the same label is applied across visualization, execution, control, comparison, and explanation without a new claim.
3. **Metaphor-only function:** the label evokes similarity but no target-level praxis difference can be stated.
4. **Missing Loss:** no account identifies what the label compresses, excludes, leaves uncertain, or cannot recover.
5. **Missing rivals:** ordinary recurrence, state-machine persistence, queue behavior, cyclic control, or no special function are not compared.
6. **Rhetorical strength without test load:** stronger language appears without stronger sensitivity, evidence, or Claim Ceiling.
7. **Vocabulary accumulation:** additional PMS terms are added to rescue the original label without new discrimination.

```text
label stable under material variation
→ substitution pressure

more PMS vocabulary
+ unchanged test burden
→ no warrant increase
```

These markers are diagnostics, not automatic semantic adjudication. The final route depends on the delimited claim, the attempted correction, Type Integrity, source preservation, and the full audit.

#### C37-COUNTERFACTUAL-01 — the mapping test varies source, target, direction, label, countercases, and no-projection without converting sensitivity into proof

The Chapter-37 Counterfactual Mapping Test varies one dimension at a time:

| Variation | Required question |
|---|---|
| Source-feature removal | Does the claimed correspondence or function change when a purportedly constitutive source feature is removed? |
| Source-feature replacement | Does a materially different source relation receive the same mapping and label? |
| Target-feature removal | Does the claimed target function survive without a target-only execution condition? |
| Target-context change | Does a comparison claim remain unchanged across visualization, simulation, execution, control, or pedagogy? |
| Mapping-direction reversal | Does `T → S` preserve the same relation, residuals, and Claim Ceiling as `S → T`? |
| Label removal or replacement | Does discrimination remain when the PMS-derived label is removed or replaced by ordinary domain language? |
| Countercase insertion | Does a relevant non-matching case narrow, reverse, or defeat the mapping? |
