| No-projection comparison | Can a formal-only, analogy-only, source-only, or target-only account explain the gain with fewer assumptions? |

For `M`, removal of source Non-Events, role/asymmetry relations, or binding/exit costs tests whether the proposed mapping depends only on sequence geometry. Removal of target execution guards tests whether executability rather than source correspondence performs the claimed work. Reverse mapping tests whether technical determinism is being imported into historical praxis.

For `L`, changing recurrence while retaining the label tests label invariance. Removing the label tests whether any distinct target function remains. Comparing ordinary workflow descriptions tests whether `dynamic_attractor` adds discrimination or only rhetorical intensity.

Counterfactual response is necessary evidence for claim sensitivity, but it is not semantic or causal proof:

```text
mapping changes under material variation
≠ semantic preservation proved
≠ causal equivalence proved

mapping survives all material variation
→ elasticity or substitution pressure
```

No universal similarity threshold, numeric score, translation percentage, or automated semantic engine follows from the test.

#### C37-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives mapping-status discipline, residuals, substitution markers, and unresolved fields without drift, translation, Failure, or class adjudication

WP2 hands WP3:

- the symbolic/formal/executable/projection separation;
- the execution-success and execution-failure boundaries;
- analogy as a legitimate terminal status with `analogy_only` available but unselected;
- partial-analogy requirements and relation-level Claim Reduction pressure;
- stable `S/M/L` packets and the two-sided residual ledger;
- explicit label-substitution burdens and operational markers;
- the integrated Counterfactual Mapping Test;
- live no-projection, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture routes;
- unchanged operation, Output-Class, Rule, audit-stage, schema, Loss, and authority inventories.

WP2 does not hand WP3:

- an adjudicated symbolic, formal, executable, analogy, partial-analogy, projection, or Non-Capture status for `M`;
- semantic preservation or a target function for `M`;
- a final label-substitution finding for `L`;
- a preferred relation subset or reverse-mapping result;
- analogy-drift or translation-breadth findings;
- a local route or canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-37 or RETYPE lock.

```text
Chapter 37 WP2 complete
+ mapping-status and substitution-test architecture established
+ sixteen cumulative duties represented exactly once
+ M/L pressure packets retained
≠ M or L adjudicated
≠ analogy_only selected
≠ Failure, Stop, Reduction, or Non-Capture selected
≠ Chapter 37 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-37-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

All §§37.5–37.8 are complete. The eight assigned WP2 Pressure Duties occur exactly once, bringing cumulative Chapter-37 completion to sixteen of twenty-four duties. WP3 remains responsible for analogy drift, translation breadth, the integrated stress test, canonical output mapping, localized failure, Non-Capture, the non-authority ceiling, and the WP4 handoff.

The next controlled step is WP3, §§37.9–37.12.

<a id="37-9-analogy-drift"></a>

### 37.9 Analogy Drift

Analogy drift occurs when a bounded resemblance acquires claims that were not warranted by the original analogy. The drift is not simply an analogy becoming more detailed. It is a change in Claim Type: a declared correspondence is converted into semantic identity, target function, source type, primitive status, causal equivalence, or authority without a separately testable claim.

#### C37-DRIFT-01 — analogy drift begins when resemblance is converted into identity, function, type, primitive, or authority without a new claim

The minimum drift sequence is:

```text
bounded resemblance under dimension D
→ semantic-preservation assertion
→ target-function assertion
→ source- or target-type identity
→ primitive or authority claim
```

No arrow is automatic. Each transition requires a new claim with its own scope, evidence, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, and Claim Ceiling. A prior analogy may motivate the next question; it cannot supply the missing warrant.

```text
useful resemblance
≠ semantic preservation

semantic preservation
≠ target function

target function
≠ source-type replacement

repeated use
≠ primitive status

broad uptake
≠ authority
```

Analogy drift can occur in either mapping direction. In `S → T`, historical praxis may be redescribed as if it obeyed deterministic workflow guards. In `T → S`, executable technical states may be redescribed as commitments, expectations, or institutional constraints merely because they occupy similar graph positions. Reverse drift is not repaired by noting that both directions use the same notation.

The principal drift markers are:

1. **Scope widening:** a relation-specific analogy becomes a whole-object equivalence.
2. **Semantic hardening:** positional or formal correspondence is treated as shared practical meaning.
3. **Function inheritance:** usefulness for comparison is converted into a target function.
4. **Type migration:** the target is assigned the source's origin type, or the source is redescribed through the target's type.
5. **Residual suppression:** source-only or target-only features disappear from later restatements.
6. **Countercase absorption:** failed correspondences are reclassified as further evidence of the analogy.
7. **Label hardening:** a provisional shorthand becomes the name of a stable object or family.
8. **Primitive inflation:** a mapping-specific construct is treated as a new PMS element or fourth STRATA operation.
9. **Authority inheritance:** analytical convenience becomes a recommendation, legitimacy claim, governance rule, or application authority.
10. **Failure rescue by relocation:** a failed projection is moved to another domain, level, Frame, or wording without retaining the original failure.

A drift audit must compare the current claim with the earliest bounded declaration. The relevant question is not only whether the later wording is stronger, but whether its additional semantic, functional, typological, or authoritative load is independently traceable.

```text
same label across expanding scopes
+ disappearing residuals
+ no new counterfactual burden
→ analogy-drift pressure
```

Drift does not imply that the original analogy was invalid. A narrow correspondence can remain admissible even when a later identity or projection claim fails. The failed extension must be localized rather than pushed backward into every prior relation.

For mapping candidate `M`, the drift question remains open. The currently declared phase/sequence, branch/node, and reversal/rollback correspondences have not been adjudicated as analogies. They therefore cannot yet drift into semantic preservation or target function as an established matter. For label candidate `L`, the presence of the phrase `dynamic_attractor` creates substitution pressure, but WP3 does not preselect whether the exact claim is reducible, stoppable, failed, or uncaptured.

<a id="37-10-translation-breadth"></a>

### 37.10 Translation Breadth

Translation breadth concerns how much of a source representation can be expressed, compressed, rendered, queried, simulated, or compared in a target representation. It is an important engineering and analytical property. It is not a measure of semantic superiority, completeness, truth, or valid RETYPE.

#### C37-TRANSLATION-01 — translation breadth, expression capacity, and compression remain distinct from semantic superiority, completeness, and valid RETYPE

At least five dimensions must remain separate:

| Dimension | What may be established | What does not follow |
|---|---|---|
| Symbol coverage | many source signs receive target expressions | relation preservation |
| Relation coverage | declared relations can be represented | comparable praxeological role |
| Execution coverage | target procedures can run or simulate | source validity or semantic identity |
| Compression capacity | many source distinctions can be encoded compactly | preservation without Loss |
| Bidirectional renderability | expressions can be generated in both directions | symmetric semantic preservation |

```text
broad expression capacity
≠ broad semantic preservation

translation success
≠ valid PROJECT_AS

compression ratio
≠ PraxisPurchase

bidirectional rendering
≠ bidirectional meaning
```

A broad translator may preserve notation while flattening practical distinctions. It may encode sequence, branch, recurrence, and rollback while excluding expectation, Non-Event, role asymmetry, responsibility, binding, and exit cost. Conversely, a narrow mapping may preserve one constitutive relation with high traceability and low Claim Ceiling. Breadth therefore cannot compensate for weak semantics or hidden Loss.

Translation breadth can increase three burdens rather than reduce them:

- **Residual burden:** more target expressions can expose more source-only and target-only features.
- **Loss burden:** compression may preserve formal availability while weakening practical load or distinction.
- **Claim-boundary burden:** a broad representation creates more opportunities for unsupported identity, function, and authority claims.

```text
more translated material
≠ less Loss automatically

more target coverage
≠ fewer residuals automatically

wider vocabulary
≠ wider warranted function
```

Breadth must be evaluated relative to a declared purpose. A representation that supports comparison of branch geometry may be adequate for that narrow purpose and inadequate for semantic reconstruction. A mapping that supports technical execution may be highly useful for implementation while remaining irrelevant to a source-dependent praxeological function.

No universal translation percentage is admissible. A count of mapped fields, symbols, relations, tests, or executable paths cannot determine semantic preservation. Nor does a low field count imply weak analogy where the preserved relation is constitutive and the Claim Ceiling is narrow.

The five canonical Loss fields remain unchanged:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Translation breadth may inform each field, but it does not add a sixth field and does not overwrite the separate mapping-residual ledger. Target-only execution semantics, deterministic guards, runtime constraints, or failure modes remain target declarations. They cannot be reimported into source Loss as if the source had always contained them.

For `M`, neither broad nor narrow translation has been established. For `L`, vocabulary breadth is not at issue: adding further PMS-derived labels would not supply the missing context, function, Source Trace, sensitivity, Loss, or rivals.

<a id="37-11-integrated-analogy-stress-test"></a>

### 37.11 Integrated Analogy Stress Test

The integrated test joins the earlier mapping-status, semantic-role, residual, substitution, and counterfactual checks into one ordered audit. Its purpose is not to calculate similarity. It determines which bounded Claim Type, if any, survives source, target, semantic, residual, countercase, reverse-mapping, label, and authority pressure.

#### C37-STRESSTEST-01 — the integrated test varies source and target structure, semantic role, residuals, artificial fitting, countercases, reverse mapping, and label sensitivity

The test proceeds through nine non-compensatory stations:

| Station | Required pressure | Failure cannot be compensated by |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Source lock | preserve source reference, origin type, prior disposition, Frame, time, uncertainty, inherited Loss | target elegance |
| 2. Mapping subset | declare the exact source features and relations used | whole-object labels |
| 3. Target coordinates | declare target domain, object, context, direction, and proposed function or comparison dimension | executable success |
| 4. Correspondence level | separate symbolic, positional, formal, executable, semantic, and functional claims | vocabulary familiarity |
| 5. Semantic-role pressure | test comparable practical load, dependency, consequence, and use | graph isomorphism |
| 6. Residual pressure | retain source-only, target-only, incompatible, uncertain, and irrecoverable structure | breadth or compression |
| 7. Counterpressure | vary source, target, direction, role, countercases, alternatives, and no-projection | rhetorical coherence |
| 8. Drift and substitution | test label invariance, scope widening, semantic hardening, type migration, and authority inheritance | repeated use |
| 9. Output boundary | map only the delimited surviving claim to a canonical class | desire for one global result |

The stations are ordered for control, not for score accumulation. Passing a later station does not repair a failure at source identity, Type Integrity, or constitutive trace. A formally rich mapping cannot compensate for a rewritten source object. A useful target tool cannot compensate for an undeclared target function when a projection is claimed.

The integrated variation matrix is:

```yaml
analogy_stress_test:
  source_variation:
    - remove_candidate_feature
    - replace_candidate_relation
    - narrow_source_subset
    - restore_excluded_source_feature
  target_variation:
    - remove_target_only_feature
    - change_target_context
    - replace_target_object
    - isolate_present_target_conditions
  mapping_variation:
    - symbolic_only
    - formal_relation_only
    - executable_only
    - reverse_direction
  semantic_variation:
    - positional_similarity
    - comparable_praxeological_role
    - semantic_preservation_claim
    - target_function_claim
  residual_variation:
    - source_only_remainder
    - target_only_remainder
    - incompatibility
    - irrecoverable_difference
  rival_variation:
    - narrower_analogy
    - ordinary_domain_description
    - alternative_mapping
    - no_projection
    - non_capture
  label_variation:
    - remove_pms_label
    - replace_with_domain_language
    - add_countercase
    - test_label_invariance
```

The matrix does not create schema fields or an automated semantic engine. It is an analytical protocol for controlled prose, records, and later cases.

For candidate `M`, the test must distinguish at least four possible local outcomes without selecting among them here:

1. a bounded relation-specific analogy survives while semantic preservation remains unestablished;
2. a narrower analogy survives after Claim Reduction;
3. a fully specified `PROJECT_AS` candidate becomes testable but still requires independent target-function adjudication;
4. responsible cross-domain comparison cannot be established and Non-Capture remains necessary.

For candidate `L`, the test must distinguish:

1. ordinary workflow terminology preserves all discrimination, leaving the PMS label functionally inert;
2. a narrow analogy can be stated after removing the unsupported functional implication;
3. continuation would require Label Substitution, type migration, invented Source Trace, or erased Loss;
4. the available evidence is insufficient to decide among materially non-equivalent reconstructions.

These are test routes, not results.

Counterfactual response remains evidence of sensitivity only:

```text
claim changes under material source or target variation
→ sensitivity evidence

claim survives every material variation unchanged
→ elasticity, overbreadth, or substitution pressure

sensitivity evidence
≠ semantic identity
≠ causal equivalence
≠ model superiority
```

The test must preserve failed claims. A later narrower analogy, different mapping direction, new target context, or revised label is a new testable claim. It does not erase the disposition of the prior claim.

<a id="37-12-output-failure-non-capture-and-authority-boundaries"></a>

### 37.12 Output, Failure, Non-Capture, and Authority Boundaries

Chapter 37 closes its substantive drafting by separating local mapping descriptions from canonical outputs and by localizing every failure position. The chapter does not force a single global result when different delimited claims occupy different positions.

#### C37-OUTPUT-01 — analogy-only outcomes map to the existing analogy_only class while Reduction, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain distinct

After a complete executed audit, the following bounded mappings remain available:

| Delimited result | Canonical route available | Boundary |
|---|---|---|
| useful bounded analogy, no warranted target function | `analogy_only` | does not establish `PROJECT_AS` |
| broader claim fails, narrower resemblance survives | `claim_reduction_required` | failed broader claim remains recorded |
| continuation requires source erasure, type jump, primitive creation, authority inheritance, or invented semantics | `mandatory_stop` | Stop is a positive boundary result |
| executed delimited projection or mapping claim fails its required conditions | `failed_transformation` | failure is localized to that claim |
| responsible semantic or functional comparison cannot be established | `non_capture` | does not protect a stronger weak claim |

Other canonical classes remain available where their existing conditions are met. Chapter 37 creates no eleventh class and no mapping-status hierarchy.

```text
analogy_only
≠ weak admissible projection
≠ failed transformation automatically

claim_reduction_required
≠ analogy_only automatically

mandatory_stop
≠ failed transformation

non_capture
≠ uncertainty shield for label substitution
```

A valid functional projection, if later established, maps under the ordinary canonical output discipline. Chapter 37 does not create a special “cross-domain admissible” class.

#### C37-FAILURE-01 — failure is localized at the exact source, mapping, semantic, functional, substitution, drift, or authority position

Failure locations include:

1. **Source Identity Failure** — the mapping changes the reference object, origin type, Frame, time, or prior disposition without a new claim.
2. **Mapping-Subset Failure** — the declared subset omits constitutive source features while retaining whole-object language.
3. **Target-Context Failure** — the target domain, object, context, level, or direction remains undefined or shifts during testing.
4. **Correspondence Failure** — the claimed symbolic, formal, executable, or relational preservation does not hold.
5. **Semantic-Preservation Failure** — source and target roles are positionally similar but not comparably praxeological.
6. **Target-Function Failure** — no source-dependent target praxis difference can be established.
7. **Sensitivity Failure** — the claim is invariant under material variation or changes under immaterial variation.
8. **Loss and Residual Failure** — source-only, target-only, incompatible, uncertain, or irrecoverable structure is hidden.
9. **Substitution Failure** — the label supplies no new discrimination beyond ordinary domain language.
10. **Drift Failure** — analogy is upgraded to identity, type, function, primitive, or authority without a new claim.
11. **Authority-Boundary Failure** — mapping validity is converted into person, group, domain, theory, governance, recommendation, sanction, or application authority.

A failure at one position does not automatically invalidate every other position. Formal correspondence can remain valid after semantic-preservation failure. A bounded analogy can remain valid after target-function failure. The source object can remain valid after every mapping claim fails.

```text
failed projection
≠ source object false

failed semantic preservation
≠ formal relation absent automatically

failed label claim
≠ target domain invalid

failed cross-domain mapping
≠ either domain inferior
```

A prohibited continuation after a known failure triggers Stop pressure. Rewording, changing level, changing direction, changing domain, or adding vocabulary does not reset the earlier disposition.

#### C37-NONCAPTURE-01 — cross-domain Non-Capture remains available where semantic or functional comparison cannot be responsibly established

Non-Capture is required when the available source and target descriptions do not support a responsible decision among materially non-equivalent mapping claims. Typical conditions include:

- domain-specific semantics lack a warranted comparison basis;
- source and target boundaries are too unstable for a shared mapping subset;
- source-only and target-only residuals dominate the proposed correspondence;
- reverse mapping produces materially different claims that cannot be resolved;
- countercases support several incompatible narrow analogies;
- evidence is insufficient to distinguish analogy, no-projection, and substitution without invention.

```text
non_capture
≠ no resemblance exists
≠ no practical use exists
≠ domains are incommensurable in every respect
≠ permission to retain an overstrong label
```

Non-Capture must state what remains unresolved and which stronger claims are therefore unavailable. It preserves re-entry if new evidence, clearer target coordinates, or a narrower mapping claim later becomes available. The new claim must still be audited independently.

#### C37-NOAUTHORITY-01 — translation, analogy, executability, and label familiarity generate no person, domain, theory, governance, or application authority

No mapping status authorizes:

- classification or diagnosis of persons or groups;
- ranking of cultures, institutions, disciplines, or domains;
- political or legal legitimacy judgments;
- automatic recommendations, sanctions, or interventions;
- claims that PMS or STRATA is complete because many domains can be expressed through it;
- claims that a target model is superior because it compiles or executes;
- inheritance of authority from source terminology, formal schemas, passed smoke tests, or repository lock status.

```text
translation breadth
≠ model completeness
≠ theory superiority

useful analogy
≠ domain rank

executable mapping
≠ application authority

familiar PMS label
≠ legitimacy

schema-valid record
+ passed smoke tests
≠ semantic truth
≠ governance authority
```

The formal model may validate declared fields, closed inventories, status separation, Loss structure, routes, and prohibited authority inheritance. It cannot decide whether semantic preservation or substantive analogy usefulness is true.

#### C37-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete triad architecture without pressure-object adjudication, RETYPE lock, or new test production

WP3 hands WP4:

- the non-overlapping projection / analogy / substitution triad;
- stable `S/M/L` source, target, mapping, and residual packets;
- symbolic, formal, executable, semantic, and functional claim separation;
- terminal and partial analogy discipline;
- substitution markers and Counterfactual Mapping Test;
- analogy-drift markers and new-claim requirement;
- translation-breadth and compression boundaries;
- the integrated nine-station Analogy Stress Test;
- canonical output mapping without new classes;
- localized Failure, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, and Non-Capture routes;
- source preservation and non-authority boundaries;
- unchanged operation, Rule, audit-stage, Loss, schema, and governance inventories.

WP3 does not hand WP4:

- an adjudicated mapping, analogy, partial-analogy, projection, or Non-Capture status for `M`;
- semantic preservation or target function for `M`;
- a final label-substitution result for `L`;
- a selected translation breadth or analogy-drift finding;
- a preferred mapping subset or reverse-mapping result;
- a local route or canonical Output Class for either pressure object;
- Chapter-37 or RETYPE lock;
- any new test, case, or smoke YAML.

```text
Chapter 37 WP3 complete
+ complete triad, drift, translation, stress-test, output, failure and authority architecture
+ twenty-four cumulative duties represented exactly once
+ M/L pressure packets retained
≠ M or L adjudicated
≠ analogy_only, Reduction, Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture selected
≠ Chapter 37 or RETYPE locked
```

#### WP3 completion boundary

Chapter 37 remains open until WP4 completes the integrated family-method audit, confirms reference and formal-model synchronization, tests the completion gate, decides provisional lock, and prepares Chapter 38 without adjudicating `M` or `L`.

<a id="chapter-37-integrated-family-method-lock-audit"></a>

#### Integrated Chapter-37 family-method lock audit

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 37 now owns a complete and non-overlapping method for distinguishing valid functional projection, structural analogy, and label substitution without converting any of them into a new source type, operation, primitive, semantic engine, score, or authority route.

The chapter preserves four ordered burdens:

1. identify the claim form before evaluating its strength;
2. retain the origin-typed source, mapping subset, target coordinates, direction, residuals, uncertainty, and exact Loss;
3. separate symbolic, formal, executable, semantic, and functional claims;
4. map only a fully audited bounded result to one of the existing ten Output Classes.

```text
projection burden complete
≠ semantic preservation established automatically

analogy burden complete
≠ target function established automatically

substitution markers present
≠ canonical Output Class selected automatically
```

The audit also confirms that formal correspondence, execution success, translation breadth, bidirectional rendering, familiar PMS vocabulary, and repository validity cannot compensate for missing PraxisPurchase, Constitutive Source Trace, material sensitivity, Contextual Boundedness, exact Loss, or Type Integrity.

#### Locked triad and cross-domain boundary

A valid functional projection remains a bounded `PROJECT_AS` claim. It preserves origin type, declares a target context and function, carries constitutive source load, changes a target-side praxis distinction, remains materially sensitive, declares exact Loss, and retains serious alternatives and no-projection.

A structural analogy remains a bounded relation of resemblance. It may preserve position, relation, sequence, branch, reversal, or other declared structure while leaving semantic preservation or target function unestablished. It may terminate legitimately as `analogy_only` after complete audit.

Label substitution remains a failure pressure where a PMS-derived label supplies no additional discrimination, bounded function, Source Trace, material sensitivity, exact Loss, or serious rival test.

```text
valid functional projection
≠ structural analogy
≠ label substitution

analogy_only
≠ weak PROJECT_AS
≠ failed_transformation automatically
```

Cross-domain mappings must retain source-only residuals, target-only structure, incompatibilities, uncertainty, and irrecoverable semantic difference. Target-only structure does not become a source property, and mapping residuals do not create a sixth Loss field.

#### Drift, translation, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture closure

Analogy drift is stopped whenever resemblance is silently hardened into semantics, function, type identity, primitive status, model completeness, theory rank, or authority. Every such increase is a new testable claim.

Translation breadth remains expression, relation, execution, compression, or rendering capacity. It is not semantic completeness, exhaustive capture, target-function warrant, or model superiority.

Failure remains localized to the exact position: source identity, mapping subset, target coordinates, correspondence, semantic preservation, target function, sensitivity, residual/Loss declaration, substitution, drift, or authority boundary.

The canonical routes remain distinct:

- `analogy_only` for a useful bounded resemblance without warranted target function;
- `claim_reduction_required` where a narrower relation survives;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation requires source erasure, type jump, primitive inflation, label rescue, or authority inheritance;
- `failed_transformation` where an executed delimited claim fails its own requirements;
- `non_capture` where responsible semantic or functional adjudication is unavailable without invention.

```text
failed mapping claim
≠ source object false
≠ target domain invalid
≠ every narrower analogy false
```

A changed source, mapping subset, target context, direction, semantic burden, function, evidence packet, residual ledger, or Claim Ceiling creates a new claim. It does not erase a prior failure.

#### Protected non-adjudication of the Chapter-37 pressure packets

The mapping packet remains unexecuted:

```yaml
mapping_candidate_M:
  source_object: documented_transition_trajectory_S
  target_object: executable_state_transition_trace_T
  mapping_direction: source_S_to_target_T
  semantic_preservation_status: unestablished
  target_function_status: unestablished
  result: unadjudicated
```

Chapter 37 does not establish symbolic-only, formal-only, executable-only, analogy, partial-analogy, valid-projection, no-projection, or Non-Capture status for `M`. It does not select a preferred mapping subset, reverse-mapping result, translation-breadth result, analogy-drift result, local route, or canonical Output Class.

The label packet also remains unexecuted:

```yaml
label_substitution_candidate_L:
  target_object: repeated_workflow_trace_U
  assigned_label: dynamic_attractor
  target_context: undeclared
  source_trace: absent
  result: unadjudicated
```

Chapter 37 does not select label substitution, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, or another route for `L`.

```text
Chapter 37 provisionally locked
≠ M analogy status established
≠ M semantic preservation established
≠ M target function established
≠ L substitution result established
≠ pressure-object Output Class selected
≠ RETYPE locked
```

#### Integrated completion decision

All §§37.1–37.12 are complete. All twenty-four assigned Chapter-37 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. The chapter preserves the complete projection/analogy/substitution triad, stable source and target coordinates, mapping direction, semantic-preservation burden, source-only and target-only residuals, symbolic/formal/executable separation, terminal and partial analogy, label-substitution markers, qualitative counterfactual variation, analogy-drift control, translation-breadth limits, exact Loss, localized Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, source preservation, and non-authority.

```text
complete projection-analogy-substitution family specification
+ twenty-four-duty closure
+ integrated scientific and formal audit pass
+ protected non-adjudication of M and L
→ Chapter 37 provisionally locked
```

The chapter-level methodological class is `admissible_but_provisional`. It does not flow to either pressure packet and does not preselect any later case result.

#### Chapter-38 handoff boundary

Chapter 38 receives seven locked constraints from Chapter 37 and the preceding RETYPE chapters:

1. origin type and bounded target function remain distinct under every projection;
2. symbolic, formal, executable, analogical, semantic, and functional claims remain separately declared;
3. a target-context omission cannot be repaired by metaphor, level language, label familiarity, or broader scope;
4. local, component, configuration, composite, higher-level, and person claims may relate only through explicit Frame, granularity, relative-level, and operation declarations;
5. a failed claim remains failed at its original position, and relocation creates a new independently testable claim;
6. every projection retains exact Loss, source continuity, alternatives, Stop, and Non-Capture;
7. no projection, profile, macrofunction, analogy, label, formal record, or successful validation creates a new PMS primitive, person type, recommendation, governance route, or authority.

Chapter 38 must classify invalid type jumps, missing target context, metaphor presented as formal projection, unmarked level and granularity mixing, projection rescue, macrofunction-to-person transfer, new-primitive creation, scope inflation, temporal flattening, and projection without Loss. It must also protect genuine declared cross-level relations, separate dual operations, bounded metaphor, and Claim Reduction from overclassification.

Chapter 38 receives no error result for a pressure object, no invalid-projection Output Class beyond the existing inventory, no new operation, no schema expansion, no person judgment, and no RETYPE lock.
## 38. Invalid Type Jumps and Unmarked Level Mixing

Chapter 38 classifies hard RETYPE errors before any substantive preference among projections is considered. Its object is not an unusual vocabulary choice, a surprising target function, or the mere presence of several levels. Its object is a broken claim architecture: origin type is replaced, target coordinates are omitted, a metaphor is silently treated as formal `PROJECT_AS`, or a later claim inherits warrant that belongs to neither its source nor its declared target.

Chapter 29 owns the generic separation of origin type and target function. Chapter 30 owns the complete `PROJECT_AS` procedure. Chapter 37 owns the distinction among valid projection, structural analogy, and label substitution. Chapter 38 does not reopen those definitions. It identifies the exact trigger at which a proposed projection becomes formally invalid, incomplete, ambiguous, reducible, stoppable, failed, or unavailable for responsible capture.

The stable source packet is `documented_trajectory_V`. Its origin type, source reference, Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, prior disposition, uncertainty, dependencies, and inherited five-part Loss remain fixed throughout the chapter. A bounded baseline candidate projects that Trajectory as a historical frame-function in `later_coordination_context_C`; the baseline exists only as a discriminating control and is not adjudicated. WP1 contrasts that packet with three entry pressures: origin-type replacement, missing target context, and metaphor/formal-claim confusion.

```text
unusual target function
≠ invalid type jump

missing context
≠ negative target-function result

bounded metaphor
≠ formal PROJECT_AS automatically
```

WP1 establishes the type/context/metaphor error architecture. It does not classify any pressure variant, select Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, or Non-Capture, assign a canonical Output Class, or lock Chapter 38 or RETYPE.

<a id="38-1-invalid-type-jump"></a>

### 38.1 Invalid Type Jump

An invalid type jump occurs when a claim does more than assign a bounded contextual function. It replaces the already warranted origin type of the source object without an independently warranted typing claim. The error therefore lies neither in novelty nor in cross-level movement as such. It lies in the unsupported conversion of one claim kind into another.

#### C38-INVALIDTYPE-01 — invalid type jump is unsupported origin-type replacement, not an unusual target function

Let `V` be warranted as a Trajectory. A later analysis may test whether `V` functions as a historical frame in context `C`. That target function may be unusual, narrow, or domain-specific without being a type jump. The type jump appears only when the functional statement is rewritten as an origin-type identity:

```text
Trajectory V
+ functions as bounded historical frame in C
→ PROJECT_AS candidate

Trajectory V
+ is declared to be a Frame operator
→ origin-type replacement pressure
```

The distinction is structural:

| Claim | Claim form | Immediate burden |
|---|---|---|
| `V functions as F in C` | bounded target-function candidate | complete `PROJECT_AS` warrant |
| `V is type F` | origin-type revision or replacement | independent typing warrant and continuity test |
| `V resembles F` | analogy or metaphor candidate | bounded comparison burden |
| `V is called F` | label claim | discrimination, context, trace, and anti-substitution burden |

A target function can therefore fail without producing a type jump, and an origin-type replacement can be invalid even where the proposed function would have been useful. The two claims occupy different positions and must be audited separately.

```text
function candidate rejected
≠ source origin type revised

origin-type replacement detected
≠ target function impossible in every context
```

The error trigger is exact: the source object's existing type, identity, dependencies, or prior disposition is overwritten, absorbed, or treated as dispensable because a contextual role appears analytically salient. A phrase such as “the Trajectory is the Frame” is not classified by grammar alone. The audit asks what the claim is made to do. If it asserts identity, operator status, dependency change, or type inheritance, origin-type replacement pressure is present. If it is shorthand for a bounded contextual role, the claim must be restored to explicit `PROJECT_AS` form and tested there.

An invalid type jump is also non-compensatory. Explanatory elegance, repeated successful use, target salience, broad adoption, formal validity, or a complete record cannot compensate for lost Type Integrity.

```text
high PraxisPurchase
+ origin type overwritten
≠ admissible projection

strong target fit
+ source identity erased
≠ bounded RETYPE
```

The stable pressure variant remains unexecuted:

```yaml
T1_origin_type_replacement:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_V
  warranted_origin_type: Trajectory
  pressure_claim: documented_trajectory_V_is_Frame_operator
  trigger_under_test: unsupported_origin_type_replacement
  result: unadjudicated
```

This packet does not yet establish `invalid_type_jump`, Mandatory Stop, Failure, Claim Reduction, or another route. It fixes only the claim position to be tested.

#### C38-OCCURRENCEJUMP-01 — a warranted operator-typed occurrence may carry a source claim without becoming operator identity, primitive, or person type

A concrete PMS occurrence can be claim-bearing inside `V` without licensing a jump to the abstract operator type. For example, a source Record may warrant a role-specific `Ω` occurrence relation or a supported `Λ` Non-Event within the Trajectory. Those occurrences may enter the Constitutive Source Trace of a bounded target-function candidate. They do not convert the Trajectory, its target object, or a person occupying a role into `Ω`, `Λ`, a new primitive, or a stable person type.

```text
warranted operator-typed occurrence in source object V
→ possible load-bearing source relation for PROJECT_AS

source occurrence performs claim-bearing work
≠ V becomes the abstract operator type
≠ target function becomes a PMS primitive
≠ person or group inherits the analytical type
```

The occurrence, its Frame, relation, temporal placement, uncertainty, and source support must remain reconstructible. If only the operator symbol survives while the occurrence relation disappears, the claim exhibits source-trace or type-jump pressure rather than stronger PMS grounding.

<a id="38-2-origin-type-equals-target-function"></a>

### 38.2 Origin Type Equals Target Function

The most common type jump compresses two grammatically similar but analytically non-equivalent statements:

```text
X functions as T in context C
≠ X is T as origin type
```

The first is a contextual function claim. The second is an identity or typing claim. `PROJECT_AS` authorizes only the first form and preserves the source object's origin type throughout.

#### C38-ORIGINFUNCTION-01 — “functions as T” remains distinct from “is T as origin type”

For the stable source packet:

```text
documented_trajectory_V
→ origin type: Trajectory

documented_trajectory_V
+ later_coordination_context_C
+ bounded_historical_frame_function
→ target-function candidate only
```

Even if the target function is later admitted, the result remains:

```text
Trajectory V performs bounded frame-function in C
≠ V is a Frame operator
≠ Frame becomes a second origin type of V
≠ V enters Δ–Ψ as a new operator occurrence
```

The distinction protects several forms of continuity at once:

1. **Reference continuity** — the claim continues to concern the same documented source object.
2. **Type continuity** — `Trajectory` remains the warranted source type unless a separate type-revision claim is executed.
3. **Dependency continuity** — the source object's formation conditions and PMS dependencies remain visible.
4. **Disposition continuity** — earlier admissibility, uncertainty, failure, or provisional status remains attached to the original claim.
5. **Loss continuity** — inherited compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability are not reset by the target function.

#### C38-TYPEINTEGRITY-01 — error detection retains source type, disposition, dependencies, reference, and exact Source Trace

Finding a type error must not destroy the source-side record. The invalid statement is isolated while valid source claims remain available:

```yaml
source_integrity_after_error_detection:
  object: documented_trajectory_V
  origin_type: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_V
  frame: declared_practice_frame_V
  granularity: trajectory_granularity
  relative_level: source_level_L1
  temporal_scope: period_V
  prior_disposition: retained
  dependencies: retained
  uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
```

This preservation is not leniency toward the invalid claim. It is claim localization. A failed origin-function collapse does not make the documented Trajectory nonexistent, erase its formation history, or preclude a narrower future projection.

```text
invalid “V is Frame” claim
≠ Trajectory V invalid
≠ every frame-function candidate invalid
≠ source history erased
```

Restoration can therefore produce a new testable formulation:

```text
invalid identity claim:
V is Frame

restored bounded candidate:
V functions as historical frame in C
```

But restoration is not retroactive rescue. The original identity claim keeps its original disposition. The bounded candidate is a new claim with explicit target coordinates, a new `PROJECT_AS` burden, exact Loss, alternatives, and independent output routing.

The same rule prevents reverse inheritance. A valid target function does not prove the source was “really” the target type all along, and a source type does not guarantee that every similarly named target function is available.

```text
valid bounded function
≠ retrospective type identity

stable origin type
≠ automatic target function
```

<a id="38-3-missing-target-context"></a>

### 38.3 Missing Target Context

A formal projection cannot be adjudicated when its target is only a label, an unspecified “higher level,” or a generic promise of explanatory usefulness. Missing target context is a completeness error: the claim has not yet identified where, at what level, over which object and time, and within what Claim Scope the proposed function is supposed to operate.

#### C38-MISSINGCONTEXT-01 — formal projection requires context, object, level, Frame, temporal scope, and claim scope

The minimum target-coordinate packet is:

```yaml
target_coordinates:
  target_context:
  target_object:
  target_level:
  target_frame:
  target_granularity:
  temporal_scope:
  target_function:
  claim_scope:
  validity_scope:
```

Not every family requires the same substantive values, but every formal claim must declare enough coordinates to make its PraxisPurchase, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, rivals, and failure conditions testable.

A phrase such as

```text
documented_trajectory_V is an attractor
```

leaves unresolved:

- whether `attractor` names an origin type, analogy, metaphor, or target function;
- which later or parallel praxis context is affected;
- what target object is being reconstructed;
- which relative level and granularity are involved;
- which Frame bounds the claim;
- whether the claim concerns `period_V`, a later interval, or all time;
- which concrete target-side difference is asserted;
- which source components carry that difference;
- what would count against the claim.

The stable pressure variant therefore remains:

```yaml
T2_missing_target_context:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_V
  pressure_expression: documented_trajectory_V_is_an_attractor
  target_context: undeclared
  target_object: undeclared
  target_level: undeclared
  target_frame: undeclared
  temporal_scope: undeclared
  claim_scope: undeclared
  formal_status: unresolved
  result: unadjudicated
```

Missing context must remain separate from a negative substantive result:

```text
target context absent
→ projection not yet adjudicable

target context declared
+ function test fails
→ substantive projection failure pressure
```

The first says that the claim is incomplete. The second says that a complete bounded claim was tested and failed. Collapsing them would misstate both the evidence and the available route.

Context completion can repair only the missing-coordinate defect. It does not establish the proposed function. Once coordinates are supplied, the claim still faces Type Integrity, PraxisPurchase, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, serious alternatives, Claim Ceiling, Stop, and Non-Capture.

```text
context completed
≠ target function established
≠ claim admissible
≠ prior ambiguity erased
```

The target packet must also be stable across evaluation. If the analyst changes context, target object, level, Frame, temporal scope, or function after counterpressure, the result is a new claim rather than completion of the old one.

#### C38-TRIGGERSEPARATION-01 — type replacement, context omission, and metaphor confusion have distinct triggers

WP1 fixes three non-interchangeable entry triggers:

| Trigger | Exact defect | What may remain valid |
|---|---|---|
| Origin-type replacement | source type is overwritten by target role | source object, source type, possibly narrower function |
| Missing target context | formal target coordinates are absent | source claim, metaphor, analogy, later complete projection candidate |
| Metaphor/formal confusion | rhetorical or analogical language is treated as executed `PROJECT_AS` | bounded metaphor or analogy at lower Claim Ceiling |

Several triggers may co-occur, but one must not hide another. For example, “V is an attractor” may omit context and also imply type replacement. The audit records both pressures rather than selecting whichever route seems rhetorically strongest.

```text
multiple defects present
≠ one undifferentiated failure code
```

Conversely, the presence of one ambiguous phrase does not prove all three defects. Classification depends on the intended and operational claim form, not on isolated vocabulary.

<a id="38-4-metaphorical-projection"></a>

### 38.4 Metaphorical Projection

Metaphor can compress a difficult relation, orient comparison, or draw attention to a bounded feature. It is not prohibited. The error appears when metaphorical language silently acquires the status of formal projection, semantic preservation, source type, canonical output, or authority.

#### C38-METAPHOR-01 — bounded metaphor remains permissible without automatic PROJECT_AS, type, semantic, or output status

The confusion control is:

```yaml
bounded_metaphor:
  expression: history_acts_like_a_frame_here
  intended_use: explanatory_shorthand_candidate
  formal_project_as_claim: absent
  origin_type_revision: absent
  semantic_identity_claim: absent
  canonical_output_selected: false
```

Under this control, the expression may be useful if its scope and non-literal status remain clear. It does not by itself establish:

- a `PROJECT_AS` occurrence;
- a target context or function;
- Semantic Preservation;
- a Frame operator occurrence;
- a new source type;
- an Output Class;
- empirical truth, causality, legitimacy, recommendation, or authority.

```text
“acts like”
≠ “functions as under complete PROJECT_AS”
≠ “is as origin type”
```

Metaphor, analogy, and projection may concern the same relata but carry different burdens:

| Form | Minimum responsible statement | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|
| Bounded metaphor | a feature is illuminated through non-literal comparison | orientation or shorthand |
| Structural analogy | a declared relation is similar under bounded dimensions | `analogy_only` may become available after audit |
| Functional projection | the source performs a bounded target function | full `PROJECT_AS` claim |

A metaphor may be converted into a formal candidate only by opening a new claim and supplying the missing coordinates and burdens. The phrase itself contributes no inherited warrant.

```text
metaphor clarified into projection candidate
→ new PROJECT_AS claim

metaphor repeated often
≠ projection executed
```

The inverse protection is equally important. Formal insufficiency does not make a bounded metaphor false or useless. A phrase may remain at a lower Claim Ceiling when it improves orientation without claiming type identity or target function.

#### C38-AMBIGUITY-01 — genuinely ambiguous natural-language status requires review, reduction, or Non-Capture rather than invented formalization

Natural language may not disclose whether a speaker intends metaphor, analogy, bounded function, or type identity. STRATA must not infer the strongest or weakest form merely to make routing easy. The review asks:

1. Is a literal origin-type identity asserted?
2. Is a target context and concrete function declared?
3. Is only one comparison dimension intended?
4. Is the phrase explicitly non-literal or provisional?
5. Does the claim change when the label is removed?
6. Can a narrower metaphor or analogy preserve the useful content?
7. Would formal classification require inventing unstated coordinates or intention?

Possible bounded dispositions remain open:

```text
strong formal claim stated but overbroad
→ Claim Reduction may remain possible

formal claim explicit and requirements fail
→ Failure or Stop pressure may arise

intended claim form cannot be responsibly determined
→ Non-Capture may be required
```

These are routing possibilities, not WP1 results. Ambiguity cannot be used to shield an overstrong claim, but neither may the analyst manufacture an identity claim from a metaphor or a formal projection from a suggestive phrase.

```text
ambiguity
≠ permission for type jump
≠ automatic failed_transformation
≠ automatic analogy_only
```

WP1 therefore preserves a **claim-form checkpoint** before formal invalidity routing. The checkpoint does not decide substantive truth. It determines whether the available statement is sufficiently specified to enter the relevant audit without invention.

#### C38-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives stable type/context/metaphor architecture without pressure-object error or route selection

WP2 receives:

- the stable `documented_trajectory_V` source packet;
- the unadjudicated bounded baseline candidate;
- `T1` origin-type-replacement and `T2` missing-context pressure packets;
- the bounded-metaphor confusion control;
- strict separation of origin type, target function, analogy, metaphor, and label;
- target-coordinate completeness requirements;
- exact trigger separation;
- source-reference, prior-disposition, dependency, uncertainty, and Loss preservation;
- interpretive-review, Claim-Reduction, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture availability;
- the rule that every repaired, relocated, re-leveled, or newly contextualized claim is independently testable.

WP2 does not receive:

- an adjudicated invalid type jump;
- an adjudicated missing-context error;
- a metaphor, analogy, or projection result;
- a selected route or canonical Output Class;
- a changed origin type;
- a person-level attribution;
- a new primitive or operation;
- Chapter-38 or RETYPE lock.

```text
Chapter 38 WP1 complete
+ type/function/context/metaphor architecture established
+ source integrity and trigger separation retained
+ ambiguity discipline explicit
≠ T1 or T2 adjudicated
≠ bounded metaphor classified
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 38 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-38-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP1 completion boundary

All §§38.1–38.4 are complete. The eight assigned WP1 Pressure Duties occur exactly once. The chapter remains open. The next controlled step is WP2: unmarked level mixing, granularity mixing, projection as rescue operation, macrofunction attributed to a person, and new primitive by projection.
<a id="38-5-unmarked-level-mixing"></a>

### 38.5 Unmarked Level Mixing

Relative levels are analytical coordinates. Their coexistence in one account is not an error. The error appears when a claim transfers identity, evidence, function, or consequence across levels without declaring the relation that is supposed to carry the transfer.

#### C38-LEVELMIX-01 — declared cross-level relation remains distinct from identity collapse, evidential inheritance, and part/whole conflation

A local occurrence may contribute to a configuration-level function. A configuration-level function may condition later local possibilities. Neither relation authorizes immediate identity:

```text
local occurrence contributes to configuration function
≠ local occurrence is the configuration function

configuration function conditions local continuation
≠ every local occurrence instantiates the same function
```

Three level-mixing triggers must remain separate:

| Trigger | Invalid transfer | Required repair burden |
|---|---|---|
| Identity collapse | part and whole, source and target, or local and higher-level object are treated as the same object | restore object identity and declare the cross-level relation |
| Evidential inheritance | evidence for one level is treated as sufficient evidence for another | provide level-specific evidence and the relation carrying the inference |
| Functional inheritance | a function warranted at one level is assigned to every element at another | test the lower- or higher-level function independently |

The stable pressure variant is:

```yaml
T3_unmarked_level_mixing:
  source_object: local_transition_occurrence_o1
  source_level: occurrence_level_L1
  pressure_claim: local_transition_occurrence_o1_entails_configuration_function
  target_level: configuration_level_L2
  relation_between_levels: undeclared
  evidence_transfer: assumed
  object_identity_status: unresolved
  result: unadjudicated
```

The phrase “the local transition entails the configuration function” is not rejected because it crosses levels. It is incomplete because the relation is absent. The audit must ask whether the proposed bridge is constitutive, enabling, constraining, evidential, projective, or merely descriptive; which other occurrences and relations are required; what countercases weaken the bridge; and which Loss follows from the level change.

```text
cross-level analysis
+ declared relation
+ stable source and target objects
+ level-specific evidence
→ testable bounded claim

cross-level vocabulary
+ undeclared relation
+ inherited evidence
→ level-mixing pressure
```

The relation must be material. Merely naming it “emergence,” “macro,” “systemic,” or “higher-level” does not establish how the source supports the target. Likewise, the target cannot retroactively make every source occurrence constitutive.

```text
higher-level label
≠ cross-level relation

target function established
≠ every component necessary
```

Level mixing may also occur in the reverse direction. A configuration-level result cannot be used as automatic proof of a local intention, motive, occurrence state, or operator role. The higher-level claim and the local claim require separate warrants.

```text
configuration-level function present
≠ local intention established
≠ local occurrence type established
```

The pressure remains unadjudicated. WP2 fixes the trigger and the repair burden; it does not select `unmarked_level_mixing`, Claim Reduction, Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, or a canonical Output Class for `T3`.

<a id="38-6-granularity-mixing"></a>

### 38.6 Granularity Mixing

Granularity concerns resolution within a reference object or analytical field. Fine and coarse descriptions may both be valid. Granularity mixing arises when information, counterevidence, or function is transferred directly between them without an explicit reconstruction, aggregation, decomposition, or projection relation.

#### C38-GRANULARITYMIX-01 — multi-granular analysis remains distinct from direct transfer between fine evidence and coarse function

A coarse function may depend on fine-grained evidence, but fine detail does not prove the coarse function by accumulation alone:

```text
fine-grained evidence supports reconstruction
≠ fine-grained evidence automatically proves macrofunction

coarse function remains plausible
≠ local countercases become irrelevant
```

The stable pressure variant is:

```yaml
T4_unmarked_granularity_mixing:
  source_packet:
    object: documented_trajectory_V
    fine_granularity_evidence:
      - event_detail_e1
      - event_detail_e2
      - local_non_event_n1
  pressure_claim: fine_event_detail_directly_proves_macrofunction
  coarse_target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  aggregation_or_relation_rule: undeclared
  countercase_handling: undeclared
  loss_status: undeclared_for_granularity_transfer
  result: unadjudicated
```

Granularity mixing has at least four distinct forms:

1. **Direct proof transfer** — fine observations are treated as self-sufficient proof of a coarse function.
2. **Countercase suppression** — coarse salience is used to dismiss local exceptions or missingness.
3. **Resolution privilege** — finer detail is presumed truer, or coarser structure is presumed more explanatory, without claim-specific warrant.
4. **Reference drift** — analysis changes resolution and thereby changes the reference object while presenting the result as continuous.

```text
finer resolution
≠ higher truth

coarser resolution
≠ higher explanatory authority

many fine details
≠ warranted coarse function
```

A responsible multi-granular claim must declare:

- the stable reference object;
- the fine and coarse granularities;
- the relation or operation connecting them;
- which detail is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable;
- how local countercases affect the coarse claim;
- whether the coarse function adds PraxisPurchase beyond descriptive aggregation;
- what would change if the granularity boundary moved.

A `DECOMPOSE` record may open a coarse occurrence without granting finer detail truth priority. A `COMPOSE` record may form a composite without granting the composite an automatic target function. A `PROJECT_AS` record may assign a bounded function without treating every fine component as that function. These operations may be chained, but their records and Loss remain separate.

```text
DECOMPOSE fine reconstruction
≠ coarse claim invalid automatically

COMPOSE coarse object
≠ target function established automatically

PROJECT_AS coarse function
≠ fine components inherit function automatically
```

The local `T4` claim remains unexecuted. WP2 neither rejects the fine evidence nor establishes the macrofunction. It establishes only that the direct inference requires a declared relation, independent target-function warrant, countercase handling, and exact Loss.

<a id="38-7-projection-as-rescue-operation"></a>

### 38.7 Projection as Rescue Operation

A failed claim cannot be immunized by moving it to another context, level, Frame, granularity, time, or target function while presenting the relocation as if it answered the original objection. `PROJECT_AS` may open a new bounded claim after failure, but it cannot revise the disposition of the earlier claim without a separate re-evaluation of that earlier claim.

#### C38-RESCUE-01 — relocation, relabeling, reframing, or re-leveling preserves the original failed disposition

The stable pressure variant is:

```yaml
T5_projection_rescue:
  original_claim:
    statement: documented_trajectory_V_is_Frame_operator
    claim_position: origin_type_identity
    disposition: failed_or_under_failure_pressure
  rescue_attempt:
    statement: documented_trajectory_V_is_valid_at_configuration_level_L2
    operation: PROJECT_AS_claimed
    target_context: changed_or_new
    failure_continuity: omitted
    new_record: absent
  result: unadjudicated
```

A valid new projection may coexist with an earlier failed typing claim:

```text
original claim:
V is Frame as origin type
→ original disposition retained

new claim:
V functions as bounded historical frame in context C
→ independently testable PROJECT_AS candidate
```

What is prohibited is the inference:

```text
claim failed at source position
+ claim relocated to another level
→ original objection defeated
```

The original failure remains attached to the original claim position. A new target context may change what is being asked; it does not show that the earlier answer was correct. This is the RETYPE-specific form of the general rule:

```text
new transformation = new testable claim
```

Rescue pressure is present when one or more of the following occur:

- the original failure record disappears;
- the same wording is moved to a new level without target coordinates;
- a new label replaces the failed claim without new discrimination;
- new evidence is cited but not linked to the new target function;
- Loss is reset or omitted;
- the new claim inherits the original source warrant but not the original counterevidence;
- repeated relocation prevents any stable failure condition from being reached.

```text
new context
≠ prior failure erased

new label
≠ new warrant

new level
≠ objection answered
```

#### C38-NEWCLAIM-01 — every post-failure projection requires a new independently testable operation record and Loss declaration

A post-failure projection must contain at least:

```yaml
post_failure_projection_claim:
  prior_claim_reference:
  prior_claim_disposition:
  preserved_prior_failure: true
  operation: PROJECT_AS
  source_object:
  origin_type:
  target_context:
  target_object:
  target_level:
  target_frame:
  target_granularity:
  temporal_scope:
  target_function:
  claim_scope:
  source_trace:
  counterfactual_variations:
  serious_alternatives:
  loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:
  result:
```

This is not a new schema requirement. It is a controlled view over fields and relations already owned by the Shared Transformation Record and the existing `PROJECT_AS` procedure.

The new claim must be able to fail independently. If every counterexample triggers another relocation, contextual rewrite, or level change, Mandatory Stop pressure increases because the procedure no longer exposes a stable testable claim.

```text
independent new record
+ retained prior failure
+ stable target coordinates
→ legitimate re-entry candidate

serial relocation after counterpressure
+ no stable failure condition
→ anti-immunization and Stop pressure
```

WP2 does not adjudicate `T5`. It establishes failure continuity and new-claim burden while leaving Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture open.

<a id="38-8-macrofunction-attributed-to-a-person"></a>

### 38.8 Macrofunction Attributed to a Person

Configuration-, composite-, path-, trajectory-, or context-level functions describe bounded relations among objects, occurrences, roles, conditions, and consequences. They are not personal essences. A macrofunction-to-person jump occurs when a higher-level analytical function is rewritten as a stable property, diagnosis, rank, identity, motive, or moral quality of a person or group.

#### C38-PERSONTYPE-01 — configuration and composite functions may not become person or group essence, diagnosis, rank, or stable trait

The stable pressure variant is:

```yaml
T6_person_level_jump:
  source_claim:
    object: configuration_composite_Q
    function: bounded_asymmetry_access_function
    context: institutional_access_field_H
  pressure_claim:
    target: person_p
    attributed_property: asymmetry
    scope: global_or_stable_person_property
    person_level_evidence: absent
    relation_from_configuration_to_person: undeclared
  result: unadjudicated
```

The non-equivalence is categorical:

```text
configuration performs asymmetry-function in H
≠ person p is asymmetry

composite stabilizes binding-function
≠ group has binding essence

trajectory functions as historical frame
≠ actor possesses frame-type personality
```

A person may participate in, reproduce, resist, benefit from, or be constrained by a configuration. Each is a separate bounded relation claim. None authorizes a global person type.

```text
participates in function
≠ embodies function

occupies role in composite
≠ owns composite property

benefits from asymmetry
≠ person essence established
```

The prohibition applies equally to positive and negative attribution. STRATA may not infer maturity, pathology, legitimacy, virtue, blame, competence, danger, loyalty, ideology, or stable identity from a macrofunction. It also may not use group membership as a shortcut for individual attribution.

A responsible person-related claim, where independently warranted and within project scope, would need its own reference object, evidence, relation, context, temporal scope, countercases, uncertainty, Claim Ceiling, and Loss. Yet STRATA's authority boundary remains structural: the framework does not perform personality typing, diagnosis, moral ranking, sanction, or automatic action recommendation.

```text
structural participation record
≠ person diagnosis
≠ moral judgment
≠ sanction recommendation
```

Macrofunction-to-person transfer is non-compensatory. Large datasets, repeated labels, institutional authority, consensus, model confidence, or schema validity cannot repair the category error.

```text
many observations
+ configuration-level function
≠ stable person trait

formal record complete
+ person-level type jump
≠ admissible attribution
```

WP2 retains `T6` as an unadjudicated pressure packet. No person judgment, route, Output Class, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority follows.

<a id="38-9-new-primitive-by-projection"></a>

### 38.9 New Primitive by Projection

A successful local projection may establish a bounded contextual function. It cannot add a PMS operator, modify Δ–Ψ, create a fourth STRATA operation, or convert an emergent profile or macrofunction into a new primitive.

#### C38-NEWPRIMITIVE-01 — target functions, profiles, and macrofunctions do not become PMS operators, STRATA operations, or Δ–Ψ revisions

The stable pressure variant is:

```yaml
T7_new_primitive:
  source_claim:
    object: operator_weighting_configuration_P
    proposed_profile: omega_theta_prominent_candidate
  pressure_claim:
    statement: successful_profile_projection_adds_operator_to_Delta_Psi
    proposed_new_operator: profile_operator_P
    proposed_dependency_change: append_to_Delta_Psi
    proposed_strata_operation: none_declared
  result: unadjudicated
```

The authority order is decisive:

```text
PMS.yaml
→ unchanged PMS Base

local target function
→ bounded derived function only
```

Therefore:

```text
successful PROJECT_AS
≠ new PMS operator

emergent functional profile
≠ operator type

higher-level macrofunction
≠ fourth STRATA operation

repeated analytical usefulness
≠ Δ–Ψ revision
```

A derived object or function may be named for local reconstruction, provided the name remains subordinate to its source, context, function, Loss, uncertainty, and Claim Ceiling. Naming does not grant primitive status. A primitive claim would require authority outside STRATA's mandate and cannot be inherited from local analytical success.

Primitive inflation can appear through:

- insertion of a profile term into the Δ–Ψ sequence;
- reordering or deletion of dependencies because a target function appears salient;
- treating a recurring macrofunction as a universal operator;
- treating a formal-model field or convenience label as ontology;
- adding a fourth core operation to handle a difficult case;
- claiming that local empirical uptake authorizes PMS Base revision.

```text
model field
≠ ontology

convenience label
≠ primitive

local empirical support
≠ authority to revise PMS Base
```

The prohibition does not deny legitimate derivative language. It fixes the Claim Ceiling:

```text
source-traceable derived function
+ bounded context
+ retained origin type
→ potentially admissible local analytical object

source-traceable derived function
+ primitive or operator status asserted
→ Type Integrity and authority-boundary pressure
```

#### C38-DUALRECORD-01 — genuine dual operations or cross-level relations remain protected when records, coordinates, outputs, and Loss are separate

Not every two-stage or multi-level analysis is invalid mixing or primitive inflation. A genuine dual record may contain, for example:

```yaml
genuine_dual_record:
  record_A:
    operation: COMPOSE
    source_objects:
      - occurrence_a
      - occurrence_b
    output_object: composite_Q
    loss:
      preserved: declared
      compressed: declared
      excluded: declared
      uncertain: declared
      irrecoverable: declared

  record_B:
    operation: PROJECT_AS
    source_object: composite_Q
    origin_type: derived_relational_composite
    target_context: context_H
    target_function: bounded_access_function
    loss:
      preserved: declared
      compressed: declared
      excluded: declared
      uncertain: declared
      irrecoverable: declared

  relation_between_records: record_B_uses_output_of_record_A
  outputs_kept_separate: true
  result: unadjudicated
```

This structure is not a fourth operation, not an operator revision, and not level mixing merely because two levels or operations are present. Its validity depends on the independent validity of each record and the declared relation between them.

```text
multiple declared records
≠ one mixed operation

cross-level chain
≠ unmarked level mixing automatically

output of COMPOSE used by PROJECT_AS
≠ composite becomes operator primitive
```

The confusion control protects legitimate complexity from overclassification while preserving all burdens. If the records are merged, Loss is collapsed, output identity changes silently, or one record inherits the other's result, the protection no longer applies.

#### C38-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives the complete level, granularity, rescue, person, and primitive architecture without route selection

WP3 receives:

- `T3` level-mixing pressure with identity, evidential, and functional inheritance triggers separated;
- `T4` granularity-mixing pressure with fine/coarse relation, countercase, and Loss burdens explicit;
- `T5` rescue pressure with prior failure preserved and every relocated claim treated as new;
- the minimum post-failure projection view using existing record fields;
- `T6` person-level pressure with essence, diagnosis, rank, trait, recommendation, sanction, and authority prohibited;
- `T7` primitive pressure with PMS Base, Δ–Ψ, operation inventory, and dependency order protected;
- the genuine dual-record confusion control;
- open Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture routes;
- exact source continuity, target coordinates, Counterfactual Sensitivity, and five-part Loss.

WP3 does not receive:

- an adjudicated error for `T3–T7`;
- a selected repair, relation, level, granularity, person attribution, or primitive;
- a selected route or canonical Output Class;
- a new operation, Rule, audit stage, schema field, Loss field, type score, level metric, person taxonomy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority mechanism;
- Chapter-38 or RETYPE lock.

```text
Chapter 38 WP2 complete
+ level and granularity transfer triggers separated
+ prior-failure and new-claim continuity established
+ person and primitive boundaries explicit
+ genuine dual-record protection retained
≠ T3-T7 adjudicated
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 38 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-38-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP2 completion boundary

All §§38.5–38.9 are complete. The eight assigned WP2 Pressure Duties occur exactly once, bringing cumulative Chapter-38 completion to sixteen of twenty-four duties. Chapter 38 remains open. The next controlled step is WP3: scope inflation, temporal flattening, projection without Loss, the Invalid Projection Record, localized failure, Claim Reduction, and Output-boundary discipline.

<a id="38-10-scope-inflation"></a>

### 38.10 Scope Inflation

A bounded projection is defined not only by its target function but by the coordinates within which that function is claimed to hold. Scope inflation occurs when one or more coordinates widen without new source-dependent warrant, a new target-function test, and a revised Loss declaration.

#### C38-SCOPEINFLATION-01 — context, object, population, level, temporal, and similarity-class widening must be tested separately

The stable pressure variant is `T8`:

```yaml
T8_scope_time_loss_inflation:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_V
  origin_type: Trajectory
  bounded_candidate:
    target_context: later_coordination_context_C
    target_object: coordination_configuration_C
    target_level: configuration_level_L2
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
    population_scope: declared_participants_in_C
    temporal_scope: bounded_later_period_C
    similarity_class: explicitly_delimited_cases
    loss_status: declared
  inflated_claim:
    context_scope: all_later_scenes
    object_scope: all_related_configurations
    population_scope: all_group_members
    level_scope: all_relative_levels
    temporal_scope: timeless
    similarity_class: all_similar_objects
    loss_status: none_claimed
  result: unadjudicated
```

The dimensions must not be collapsed into one generic “too broad” code. Each answers a different question:

| Scope dimension | Bounded question | Inflation trigger |
|---|---|---|
| Context | In which declared target setting does the function matter? | transfer to undeclared settings |
| Object | Which target object carries the function? | transfer to neighboring or merely associated objects |
| Population | For whom or which participants is the claim warranted? | transfer to every member, subgroup, or observer |
| Relative level | At which level is the function claimed? | transfer to lower or higher levels without a declared relation |
| Temporal | During which interval does the function hold? | extension before formation or after dissolution |
| Similarity class | Which cases are comparable under the declared relation? | transfer to all formally or rhetorically similar objects |

```text
local function in context C
≠ function in every later context

configuration-level function
≠ function at every component level

function for declared participants
≠ property of every group member

one warranted analogue class
≠ all similar-looking objects
```

Scope extension is not prohibited. It is a new testable claim. A widened claim must declare:

- the added context, object, population, level, period, or similarity-class coordinates;
- whether the Source Object and candidate-specific Source Trace remain the same;
- which additional source features carry the widened target work;
- new countercases introduced by the extension;
- the revised Claim Ceiling;
- a new exact five-part Loss;
- whether the original bounded result remains independently valid if the extension fails.

```text
bounded projection P_C
+ proposed extension to C2
→ new PROJECT_AS claim P_C2

P_C2 fails
≠ P_C automatically fails

P_C succeeds
≠ P_C2 automatically succeeds
```

The similarity-class dimension requires particular restraint. Formal resemblance, common vocabulary, shared institutional category, or repeated use of the same diagram does not establish that the same target function carries across cases.

```text
same label
≠ same source load

same topology
≠ same target praxis difference

same organization category
≠ same bounded function
```

Scope inflation may coexist with missing context, level mixing, person-level transfer, temporal flattening, or Loss failure. These defects must remain separately visible. A global claim is not repaired merely by adding one context label if population, level, time, or similarity-class scope remains unbounded.

WP3 does not adjudicate the bounded baseline or the inflated `T8` claim. It establishes separate widening tests and preserves narrower-claim continuity.

<a id="38-11-temporal-flattening"></a>

### 38.11 Temporal Flattening

RETYPE operates on temporally formed objects and bounded functions. Temporal flattening occurs when formation, revision, interruption, phase change, or dissolution is removed and the resulting object or function is treated as timeless, permanent, or essential.

#### C38-TEMPORALFLATTENING-01 — historically formed trajectories, bindings, profiles, and functions may not become timeless essences

The error is not simply a long duration. It is the loss of temporal dependence.

```text
historically persistent
≠ timeless

repeated across several periods
≠ invariant essence

stable within a declared window
≠ permanent type
```

The minimum temporal trace distinguishes:

```yaml
temporal_trace:
  formation_conditions:
  onset_or_entry:
  relevant_phases:
  revisions_or_reversals:
  interruption_or_non_event_structure:
  persistence_conditions:
  dissolution_or_exit_conditions:
  observation_window:
  excluded_periods:
  temporal_uncertainty:
```

A projection must remain sensitive to at least four kinds of temporal variation:

1. **Pre-formation variation** — the target function must not be retrojected into periods before the warranting structure existed.
2. **Phase variation** — prominence, relation, binding, profile, or function may differ across phases.
3. **Interruption and reversal** — a pause, breach, reversal, exit, or changed condition may weaken or terminate the claim.
4. **Post-dissolution variation** — historical residues may persist, but persistence must not be confused with the continued existence of the original function.

```text
Trajectory V formed during period_V
≠ V was always present

binding function operated during phase_B
≠ every later relation is the same binding

profile stable in window_W
≠ profile is a person or group essence

historical residue remains
≠ original function remains unchanged
```

Temporal flattening can also occur through presentism. A current target condition may be projected backward as the explanation of the whole source trajectory, or a historically formed source object may be treated as if it were only a current state.

```text
present condition supports current function
≠ present condition explains all prior formation

current state resembles earlier phase
≠ temporal identity established
```

The same discipline applies to future extension. A currently bounded function cannot be asserted for all later scenes without a new continuation claim and revised uncertainty.

```text
function remains possible
≠ function will persist

historical regularity
≠ prediction
```

Temporal claims retain uncertainty and Loss. Missing dates, disputed phase boundaries, unavailable counterfactual periods, and irreversible compression must remain visible. Formal chronology or a clean timeline does not establish causal order, necessity, permanence, or person identity.

Temporal flattening is therefore distinct from:
