- Scope Inflation: a claim may be temporally bounded but overgeneralized across contexts or populations;
- Origin-Type Replacement: a trajectory may remain a Trajectory while still being falsely treated as timeless;
- Person-Level Type Jump: temporal flattening may prepare a person-type claim but is not identical to it;
- Granularity Mixing: coarse periodization may be legitimate if its compression and excluded phase detail are declared.

WP3 does not select temporal-flattening status for `T8`. It establishes the temporal-dependence burden and preserves historically bounded alternatives.

<a id="38-12-projection-without-loss"></a>

### 38.12 Projection without Loss

Every projection changes visibility. It foregrounds a bounded target function, backgrounds other source relations, compresses some structure, and may exclude or render some distinctions irrecoverable. A claim of lossless projection is therefore incompatible with RETYPE discipline.

#### C38-PROJECTIONLOSS-01 — every projection requires exact five-part Loss plus explicit foregrounding and backgrounding

The canonical Loss structure remains exactly:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Foregrounding and backgrounding are required descriptions of visibility change. They are not sixth and seventh Loss fields:

```yaml
visibility_change:
  foregrounded:
  backgrounded:
```

```text
foregrounding/backgrounding
≠ additional Loss fields

complete formal mapping
≠ lossless projection

all source elements represented
≠ all source functions preserved
```

For the bounded baseline candidate, the Loss declaration must answer at least:

- which Trajectory relations remain constitutive to the historical frame-function;
- which phase, branch, reversal, expectation, asymmetry, binding, or non-event distinctions are compressed;
- which source structures are excluded from the target claim;
- which source or target relations remain uncertain;
- which distinctions cannot be reconstructed after projection;
- what the target function foregrounds;
- what valid source information is backgrounded but not denied.

A projection-without-Loss claim may appear in several forms:

1. **Total preservation claim** — every source distinction is said to survive unchanged.
2. **Neutral-view claim** — the target function is presented as perspective-free.
3. **No-compression claim** — source complexity is claimed to transfer without reduction.
4. **No-exclusion claim** — no relevant source relation is acknowledged as outside the target function.
5. **Reversibility claim** — the complete source is claimed to be recoverable from the projection without remainder.
6. **Loss omission** — the record simply leaves Loss or visibility change undeclared.

```text
projection selects a target function
→ perspective and visibility change occur

perspective declared
≠ arbitrary subjectivity

Loss declared
≠ projection automatically admissible
```

Loss is candidate-specific. Two projections of the same Source Object can preserve different relations and exclude different structures. Their Loss profiles must not be merged merely because they share a source label.

```text
same source object
+ different target functions
→ separate Loss profiles
```

Loss declaration is non-compensatory. High PraxisPurchase, elegant formalization, broad coverage, executable mapping, or strong Context Fit cannot repair absent or fictitiously empty Loss.

```text
strong target fit
+ undeclared Loss
≠ complete PROJECT_AS
```

The exact route still depends on the executed claim. Missing details that can be supplied may require completion or Claim Reduction; a delimited executed claim that asserts losslessness may fail; continuation that requires source erasure or an impossible total-preservation claim may require Stop; unavailable responsible reconstruction may require Non-Capture. No route is selected in WP3.

<a id="38-13-invalid-projection-record"></a>

### 38.13 Invalid Projection Record

The Invalid Projection Record is a controlled diagnostic view over an existing or proposed transformation claim. It does not replace the Shared Transformation Record, add a schema, create an operation, or preselect an Output Class.

#### C38-INVALIDRECORD-01 — the minimal invalid-projection view must expose the exact trigger, retained valid material, repair boundary, and open route

A minimum view is:

```yaml
invalid_projection_view:
  claim_reference:
  source_object:
  origin_type:
  prior_disposition:
  proposed_operation: PROJECT_AS

  claimed_target_coordinates:
    target_context:
    target_object:
    target_level:
    target_frame:
    target_granularity:
    temporal_scope:
    target_function:
    claim_scope:
    validity_scope:

  local_findings:
    invalid_type_jump: not_assessed
    origin_function_collapse: not_assessed
    missing_target_context: not_assessed
    metaphor_formal_claim_confusion: not_assessed
    unmarked_level_mixing: not_assessed
    unmarked_granularity_mixing: not_assessed
    projection_rescue: not_assessed
    person_level_type_jump: not_assessed
    new_primitive_by_projection: not_assessed
    scope_inflation: not_assessed
    temporal_flattening: not_assessed
    projection_without_loss: not_assessed

  retained_valid_material:
    source_object_status:
    source_trace_status:
    valid_relation_or_operation_records:
    bounded_metaphor_or_analogy_status:
    narrower_claim_candidate:

  loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:

  visibility_change:
    foregrounded:
    backgrounded:

  failure_continuity:
    earlier_failed_claim_reference:
    earlier_disposition_preserved:
    new_claim_reference_if_any:

  repair_boundary:
    context_completion_possible:
    narrower_scope_possible:
    explicit_level_or_granularity_relation_possible:
    lower_claim_ceiling_possible:
    repair_would_create_new_claim:

  route_status:
    claim_reduction_required: open
    mandatory_stop: open
    failed_transformation: open
    non_capture: open
    selected_output_class: not_selected
```

The local findings are diagnostic states, not a new closed enum and not Output Classes. A deployment may represent them through existing notes, audit findings, or extension-safe annotations; Chapter 38 does not require a schema change.

```text
invalid projection view
≠ fourth operation
≠ replacement transformation record
≠ thirteenth audit stage
≠ eleventh Output Class
```

The view must preserve the original claim wording or record reference. Otherwise the error can be hidden by rewriting the claim during audit. It must also preserve valid source-side findings and any separately valid relation, metaphor, analogy, or narrower function.

#### C38-FAILURELOCAL-01 — every invalidity trigger is localized and valid source, relation, metaphor, analogy, or narrower claims remain independently reviewable

Failure localization asks:

| Failure position | What may fail | What does not automatically fail |
|---|---|---|
| Origin Type | identity or type assignment | Source Object and bounded function candidate |
| Target Coordinates | completeness of formal projection | bounded metaphor or source-only description |
| Level Relation | cross-level transfer | valid evidence at either level |
| Granularity Relation | fine/coarse entailment | fine reconstruction or coarse description separately |
| Failure Continuity | rescue attempt | a genuinely new, separately recorded claim |
| Person Boundary | essence or trait attribution | bounded participation or role relation |
| Primitive Boundary | PMS or operation expansion | local derived function or profile |
| Scope | widened claim | original bounded claim |
| Temporality | timeless extension | period-bound historical claim |
| Loss | completeness of projection record | independently warranted source material |

```text
invalid stronger claim
≠ every component claim invalid

one local finding
≠ all local findings established

repairable omission
≠ substantive success
```

Several findings can coexist. Their co-occurrence must not be collapsed into a single total-rejection statement if a bounded source, relation, analogy, metaphor, or reduced function survives.

#### C38-REDUCTION-01 — Claim Reduction remains available only where a narrower claim is independently warrantable and the invalid stronger claim is not preserved

Reduction may be appropriate when:

- a missing context can be completed but only for a narrower target setting;
- a global population or similarity-class claim can be reduced to declared participants or cases;
- an unmarked level or granularity transfer can be replaced by an explicit bounded relation;
- a timeless claim can be reduced to a documented period or phase;
- a type identity can be reduced to a bounded function, analogy, or metaphor;
- an overstrong semantic or functional claim can be reduced to source-only relevance;
- Loss can be declared only for a narrower projection.

```text
strong claim invalid
+ narrower claim independently warranted
→ claim_reduction_required remains possible

strong claim invalid
+ same claim merely reworded
≠ Claim Reduction

reduced claim
≠ original error erased
```

Reduction is not permitted where the surviving wording still depends on person typing, primitive creation, source erasure, repeated rescue, authority inheritance, or an undeclared target context. It also does not authorize indefinite narrowing until every possible countercase disappears. Every reduced claim needs a stable failure condition and exact Loss.

#### C38-OUTPUTBOUNDARY-01 — local error codes remain distinct from canonical Output Classes and Reduction, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain non-equivalent

Local findings such as:

```text
invalid_type_jump
missing_target_context
unmarked_level_mixing
scope_inflation
temporal_flattening
projection_without_loss
```

are descriptions of error location. They do not determine the canonical result by themselves.

```text
local error location
≠ canonical route automatically
```

The route distinction is:

- `claim_reduction_required` — an independently warrantable narrower claim survives and the stronger claim is explicitly reduced;
- `mandatory_stop` — continuation requires type replacement, person typing, primitive creation, repeated relocation, source erasure, authority inheritance, or another prohibited move;
- `failed_transformation` — an executed, sufficiently delimited projection fails one or more non-compensatory requirements;
- `non_capture` — the intended claim form, level relation, semantic status, or responsible route cannot be determined without invention.

```text
missing field
≠ failed_transformation automatically

invalidity trigger
≠ mandatory_stop automatically

ambiguity
≠ non_capture automatically

non_capture
≠ protection for an overstrong claim
```

The valid baseline candidate and `T1–T8` remain unadjudicated. No route or Output Class is selected.

#### C38-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete error taxonomy and Chapter-39 preparation boundary without pressure-object adjudication or new test production

WP4 receives:

- all twelve local invalidity findings from origin-type jump through projection without Loss;
- separate tests for context, object, population, level, temporal, and similarity-class widening;
- temporal formation, phase, interruption, dissolution, residue, and future-extension controls;
- exact five-part Loss and explicit foregrounding/backgrounding without schema expansion;
- the minimal Invalid Projection Record/view over existing claims and records;
- failure localization and valid-material preservation;
- Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture as separate open routes;
- the valid baseline, `T1–T8`, bounded-metaphor, genuine-dual-record, and partial-continuity controls;
- the Chapter-39 preparation boundary: lower/upper RETYPE limits, functional gain, Source Trace, Type/Context boundary, elasticity, Stop, Non-Capture, and local admissibility test remain later work.

WP4 does not receive:

- an adjudicated pressure-object error;
- a repaired or preferred claim;
- a person or group judgment;
- a new primitive, operator, operation, Rule, audit stage, schema field, Loss field, error score, natural-language classifier, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority mechanism;
- a selected canonical Output Class;
- Chapter-38 or RETYPE lock;
- any new test, case, or smoke YAML.

```text
Chapter 38 WP3 complete
+ complete invalid-projection taxonomy
+ scope, temporality, Loss, record, localization, reduction, and output boundaries explicit
+ Chapter-39 preparation boundary protected
≠ valid baseline or T1-T8 adjudicated
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 38 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-38-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### WP3 completion boundary

All §§38.10–38.13 are complete. The eight assigned WP3 Pressure Duties occur exactly once, bringing cumulative Chapter-38 completion to twenty-four of twenty-four duties. Chapter 38 remains open pending the integrated WP4 lock audit and Chapter-39 Preparation Gate. The valid baseline, `T1–T8`, bounded-metaphor, genuine-dual-record, and partial-continuity controls remain fully unadjudicated.


<a id="chapter-38-integrated-family-method-lock-audit"></a>

#### Integrated Chapter-38 family-method lock audit

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 38 now owns a complete local taxonomy for invalid projection structures without turning natural-language ambiguity, multiple analytical levels, multiple granularities, or every incomplete record into one undifferentiated failure. The chapter locates errors at the exact claim position and preserves independently valid source objects, relations, metaphors, analogies, operation records, and narrower claims for separate review.

The method retains four ordered disciplines:

1. preserve Source Object, origin type, reference, prior disposition, dependencies, uncertainty, and inherited Loss before testing the proposed target claim;
2. declare target context, object, level, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, target function, claim scope, and validity scope before substantive adjudication;
3. test type, level, granularity, failure continuity, person boundary, primitive boundary, scope, temporality, and Loss independently;
4. map only the executed bounded claim to one of the existing canonical Output Classes after failure localization and retained-valid-material review.

```text
complete invalidity taxonomy
≠ every variant invalid
≠ natural-language classifier
≠ automatic output routing
```

The audit confirms that a strange target function is not itself a type jump, that two levels or granularities may be related through declared records, that a bounded metaphor may remain informal, and that a stronger claim may fail while an independently warranted narrower claim survives.

#### Locked invalidity and confusion-control boundary

The following local findings remain distinct:

- invalid type jump and origin-function collapse;
- missing target context and metaphor/formal-claim confusion;
- unmarked level mixing and unmarked granularity mixing;
- projection rescue and failure-preserving re-entry;
- macrofunction-to-person transfer and new-primitive creation;
- scope inflation and temporal flattening;
- projection without exact Loss and visibility-change declaration.

```text
local error finding
≠ canonical Output Class
≠ total source invalidation
```

The three confusion controls remain equally protected:

1. bounded metaphor is not automatically a formal `PROJECT_AS` occurrence;
2. a genuine dual record is not level or granularity mixing when operations, coordinates, outputs, relations, and Loss remain separate;
3. partial continuity may support Claim Reduction when the invalid stronger claim is explicitly abandoned.

The Invalid Projection Record remains a diagnostic view over an existing or proposed claim. It neither replaces the Shared Transformation Record nor creates a fourth operation, a thirteenth audit stage, a new schema requirement, a sixth Loss field, or an eleventh Output Class.

#### Failure continuity, Loss, route, and authority closure

Every changed context, object, level, granularity, temporal scope, target function, claim scope, evidence packet, or operation creates a new independently testable claim. It does not erase the original disposition.

```text
new projection claim
≠ rescue of earlier failed claim

reduced claim
≠ original error erased
```

The canonical five-part Loss remains exact:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Foregrounding and backgrounding remain required visibility-change descriptions, not additional Loss fields. High target fit, broad coverage, formal elegance, executability, or apparent usefulness cannot compensate for absent Type Integrity, target coordinates, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Contextual Boundedness, or exact Loss.

The local routes remain non-equivalent:

- `claim_reduction_required` where an independently warrantable narrower claim survives;
- `mandatory_stop` where continuation requires type replacement, person typing, primitive creation, repeated relocation, source erasure, or authority inheritance;
- `failed_transformation` where an executed and sufficiently delimited projection fails a non-compensatory requirement;
- `non_capture` where claim form, level relation, semantic status, or responsible route cannot be determined without invention.

```text
mandatory_stop
≠ failed_transformation
≠ non_capture

non_capture
≠ protection for an overstrong claim
```

No local finding establishes empirical truth, motive, diagnosis, blame, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, application authority, or a revision of PMS Base or Δ–Ψ.

#### Protected non-adjudication of the Chapter-38 pressure architecture

The bounded baseline remains unexecuted:

```yaml
valid_baseline_candidate:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_V
  origin_type: Trajectory
  operation: PROJECT_AS
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C
  target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  result: unadjudicated
```

The eight invalidity-pressure variants remain unadjudicated:

```yaml
pressure_results:
  T1_origin_type_replacement: not_selected
  T2_missing_target_context: not_selected
  T3_unmarked_level_mixing: not_selected
  T4_unmarked_granularity_mixing: not_selected
  T5_projection_rescue: not_selected
  T6_person_level_jump: not_selected
  T7_new_primitive: not_selected
  T8_scope_time_loss_inflation: not_selected
```

The bounded-metaphor, genuine-dual-record, and partial-continuity controls also remain unexecuted. Chapter 38 does not select a repaired or reduced claim, a local route, or a canonical Output Class for any pressure object.

```text
Chapter 38 provisionally locked
≠ valid baseline established
≠ T1-T8 error result selected
≠ confusion control validated
≠ repaired or reduced claim selected
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ RETYPE locked
```

#### Integrated Chapter-38 completion decision

All §§38.1–38.13 are complete. All twenty-four assigned Chapter-38 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. The chapter preserves origin-type/target-function separation, complete target-coordinate burden, bounded metaphor, declared cross-level and multi-granular relations, failure-preserving re-entry, person and primitive prohibitions, separate scope dimensions, temporal formation and dissolution, exact five-part Loss, foregrounding/backgrounding, Invalid Projection Record discipline, failure localization, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, source preservation, and non-authority.

```text
complete invalid-projection family specification
+ twenty-four-duty closure
+ integrated scientific and formal audit pass
+ protected non-adjudication of baseline, T1-T8, and confusion controls
→ Chapter 38 provisionally locked
```

The chapter-level methodological class is `admissible_but_provisional`. It does not flow to any pressure object and does not preselect a later case result.

#### Chapter-39 handoff boundary

Chapter 39 receives the provisionally locked `PROJECT_AS` procedure and family methods of Chapters 29–38 together with the complete Chapter-38 invalidity taxonomy. It must apply the local RETYPE Admissibility Band without redefining Chapter 6 or pre-empting integrated LIMITS.

Chapter 39 receives eight locked constraints:

1. the lower RETYPE boundary is crossed only by additional praxeological discrimination, not by renaming, compression, elegance, or domain familiarity;
2. the upper RETYPE boundary requires constitutive Source Trace and material source dependence, not citation, target fit, or retrospective narrative;
3. Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, exact Loss, and Counterfactual Sensitivity remain non-compensatory;
4. analogy-only, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain positive classified outcomes rather than defects to be hidden;
5. no projection may be narrowed indefinitely to absorb every objection or survive opposite source structures unchanged;
6. no-projection and source-only description remain serious alternatives;
7. local RETYPE limits do not replace later integrated LIMITS or create a truth tribunal;
8. no successful local gate increases truth, rank, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority.

Chapter 39 must establish the lower and upper boundaries, Functional Gain, Constitutive Source Trace, Type and Context boundaries, Counterfactual and Alternative Projection Tests, Analogy Boundary, Projection Elasticity, RETYPE Stop, RETYPE Non-Capture, and the local Admissibility Test. It receives no permission to adjudicate Chapter-38 pressure packets, create a new operation, Rule, audit stage, schema, Loss field, Output Class, score, ranking, person classification, or application authority.

<a id="chapter-38-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 38 completion boundary

Chapter 38 is provisionally locked at the family-method level. The next controlled step is Chapter 39 WP1: §§39.1–39.4, covering Lower RETYPE Boundary, Renaming without Purchase, Projection without Functional Gain, and Upper RETYPE Boundary.

## 39. RETYPE Boundary Conditions

Chapter 39 applies the Admissibility Band locally to `PROJECT_AS`. It asks neither whether a source object is interesting nor whether a target vocabulary is elegant. It asks whether a proposed contextual function lies between two independent limits: below it, the claim adds no praxeologically relevant discrimination; above it, the claim exceeds the constitutive source load that can still be reconstructed and tested.

```text
lower RETYPE boundary
→ minimum additional praxeological discrimination

upper RETYPE boundary
→ maximum abstraction compatible with traceable source load
```

The two boundaries are not mirror images. A claim may fail below the floor because it merely renames an already available description. A different claim may fail above the ceiling because it fits the target scene while no longer depending on the source structure it cites. Passing one boundary does not compensate for failing the other.

The stable source packet is `documented_trajectory_W`. Its origin type, reference, Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited five-part Loss remain fixed throughout the chapter. WP1 retains four candidate packets without adjudication: bounded function candidate `A`, renaming-only pressure `B`, source-only sufficiency pressure `C`, and upper-boundary pressure `D`.

```text
interesting source
≠ target function established

useful target label
≠ Functional Gain

strong target fit
≠ Constitutive Source Trace
```

WP1 establishes the lower and upper RETYPE boundaries. It does not select a candidate result, a local route, or a canonical Output Class.

<a id="39-1-lower-retype-boundary"></a>

### 39.1 Lower RETYPE Boundary

The lower RETYPE boundary is the point below which a proposed target function fails to add a practical distinction beyond what is already available from the source object, an ordinary target-domain description, or a source-only reconstruction. The boundary therefore protects RETYPE from becoming a vocabulary-expansion procedure.

#### C39-LOWERBOUNDARY-01 — the lower RETYPE boundary requires additional praxeological discrimination, not terminological novelty

A candidate crosses the lower boundary only if the target function changes at least one bounded reconstruction-relevant dimension in a way that the source-only account does not already supply. Relevant dimensions may include:

- credible interpretation sets;
- available or blocked action corridors;
- role-specific expectations;
- coordination, breach, revision, or continuation costs;
- access to alternatives;
- the practical significance of a later transition or non-transition.

The candidate need not change every dimension. It must, however, make a concrete difference that is attributable to the proposed target function rather than to a new label, a more compact diagram, or the analyst's preference for one vocabulary.

```text
source object remains relevant in target scene
≠ source object performs additional target function

new description
≠ new praxeological discrimination
```

The lower boundary is claim-specific. It does not ask whether `documented_trajectory_W` matters in general. It asks whether the proposed `bounded_historical_frame_function` in `later_coordination_context_C1` contributes work that would be lost if the contextual function were removed while the source history remained available as background relevance.

A minimum lower-boundary test therefore compares at least three reconstructions:

1. the target scene with the proposed function;
2. the same target scene with the source retained but no new target function assigned;
3. the target scene described in ordinary domain terms without PMS-derived functional vocabulary.

If these reconstructions leave the same practical distinctions, the candidate remains below the floor. If the functional reconstruction changes a bounded target-side difference that the two rivals cannot recover without reintroducing the same relation, the candidate may proceed to the remaining RETYPE gates.

The floor is not numerical. No count of affected actors, repeated references, represented relations, model nodes, or pages establishes Functional Gain. A small but decisive role-specific difference may cross the floor, while a broad and visually prominent redescription may remain below it.

```text
large coverage
≠ Functional Gain

small bounded difference
≠ trivial difference
```

The lower boundary also preserves source validity. Failure to cross it means only that the additional target-function claim is unnecessary or insufficiently discriminating. It does not invalidate `documented_trajectory_W`, its PATH reconstruction, its inherited Loss, or its relevance as historical background.

<a id="39-2-renaming-without-purchase"></a>

### 39.2 Renaming without Purchase

Renaming without Purchase is the clearest lower-boundary failure. It occurs when a new functional label is applied but the reconstruction, practical alternatives, expectations, costs, and countercase discrimination remain unchanged.

#### C39-RENAMING-01 — a new label that leaves reconstruction and discrimination unchanged does not cross the lower boundary

The stable pressure packet is:

```yaml
renaming_only_B:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  assigned_function_label: historical_frame_function
  changed_reconstruction: none_declared
  changed_praxis_corridor: none_declared
  changed_cost_or_expectation_structure: none_declared
  discriminative_gain: unestablished
  result: unadjudicated
```

The packet is deliberately incomplete as a substantive claim. It isolates the question whether the label performs any work beyond restating that the past remains relevant.

A renaming claim may appear sophisticated because it:

- uses a controlled PMS or STRATA term;
- compresses several observations into one phrase;
- improves navigation across a document;
- fits an existing schema or diagram;
- permits an executable representation;
- aligns with a familiar theoretical vocabulary.

These may be legitimate editorial, formal, or pedagogical gains. They are not, by themselves, praxeological purchase.

#### C39-PURCHASETRACE-01 — PraxisPurchase remains distinct from descriptive convenience, rhetorical clarity, navigation, and target fit

```text
clearer prose
≠ changed praxis reconstruction

better navigation
≠ target function

formal completeness
≠ additional discrimination

strong target fit
≠ PraxisPurchase
```

The label-removal pressure is therefore direct: remove `historical_frame_function` while preserving the source packet and all ordinary descriptive content. If the same target conclusions, action corridors, role expectations, costs, and countercases remain available without loss, the label has not established Functional Gain.

Label removal does not demand that every technical term be dispensable. A warranted term may encode a relation that would otherwise require a longer but equivalent reconstruction. The question is not whether the word itself is necessary. The question is whether the relation it names is independently specified and makes a practical difference. A compact term can be retained if its content survives paraphrase and counterfactual pressure.

```text
label removable
+ relation remains explicit and discriminating
→ label may remain convenient shorthand

label removed
+ no distinct relation remains
→ renaming-without-purchase pressure
```

Renaming without Purchase is not automatically Label Substitution in the Chapter-37 sense. The former locates a lower-boundary failure of Functional Gain; the latter concerns unsupported importation of a label and its implied relations. A claim may exhibit both, but each finding must be recorded at its own position.

Likewise, lower-boundary failure does not yet determine the terminal route. Depending on the executed claim and surviving material, later audit may support a narrower claim, source-only description, analogy-only, Failure, or another canonical result. WP1 selects none of them.

<a id="39-3-projection-without-functional-gain"></a>

### 39.3 Projection without Functional Gain

Projection without Functional Gain is broader than renaming. A claim may contain a declared target context, function, Source Reference, and exact Loss yet still fail to change the target reconstruction in a way that requires the proposed function.

#### C39-FUNCTIONALGAIN-01 — a retained target function requires a concrete target-side praxis difference, with source-only sufficiency kept as a serious rival

The bounded candidate remains:

```yaml
bounded_function_candidate_A:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  operation: PROJECT_AS
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  target_object: coordination_configuration_C1
  target_level: configuration_level_L2
  target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  proposed_functional_gain:
    - changes_credible_interpretation_set
    - changes_role_specific_coordination_cost
  source_trace_status: declared_candidate
  counterfactual_status: untested
  alternatives_status: open
  result: unadjudicated
```

Candidate `A` proposes two target-side differences. WP1 does not establish either one. It specifies what later execution must show: which interpretations cease to be credible, for which roles, under which target conditions, and how the relevant coordination cost differs because `W` performs the proposed bounded function.

The source-only rival remains equally explicit:

```yaml
source_only_sufficiency_C:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  source_description: documented_trajectory_W_remains_background_relevant
  additional_target_function_needed: unestablished
  result: unadjudicated
```

The rival matters because many target scenes can acknowledge history without assigning it a distinct contextual function. Historical information may remain evidentially relevant, narratively important, or interpretively available while current rules, resource distributions, role relations, or immediate target conditions carry the full practical load.

```text
history is relevant
≠ history performs bounded frame-function

source cited in reconstruction
≠ source changes target action corridor
```

Functional Gain must therefore be specified as a difference between bounded reconstructions, not as a statement that the source “matters.” A useful claim answers:

- what target-side distinction changes;
- where the change appears;
- for whom or for which role relation it matters without becoming a person type;
- over what period it holds;
- what source-only or present-condition account cannot recover;
- what would remain unchanged if the function were absent.

The claim may be narrow. For example, `W` might alter only one role's credible interpretation set during one coordination phase. Such a bounded difference may cross the floor if it is independently traceable and testable. Conversely, a claim that `W` broadly “shapes the whole system” may remain below the floor when it supplies no discriminating target consequence.

```text
bounded role-specific gain
→ may cross lower boundary

broad rhetorical influence
→ may remain below lower boundary
```

Functional Gain does not establish admissibility by itself. It is only the floor condition. A candidate that crosses the floor must still preserve origin type and reference, remain contextually bounded, provide Constitutive Source Trace, survive counterfactual and alternative pressure, declare exact Loss, respect Claim Ceiling, and retain Stop and Non-Capture. These later burdens are not optional extensions; they prevent a useful target description from exceeding what its source can warrant.

<a id="39-4-upper-retype-boundary"></a>

### 39.4 Upper RETYPE Boundary

The upper RETYPE boundary is the point above which a target-function claim no longer remains reconstructibly dependent on the constitutive source structure it invokes. The claim may fit the target scene, explain a salient difference, or provide broad coverage and still exceed the ceiling if the source contributes only a citation, theme, historical mention, or retrospective narrative.

#### C39-UPPERBOUNDARY-01 — the upper RETYPE boundary is crossed when abstraction exceeds traceable constitutive source load

The stable pressure packet is:

```yaml
upper_boundary_pressure_D:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  proposed_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  target_fit: apparently_strong
  constitutive_source_features: undeclared
  source_variation_changes_function: unestablished
  source_trace: citation_only_candidate
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
  result: unadjudicated
```

Candidate `D` is intentionally plausible at the target side. Its weakness lies elsewhere: the proposed function has not yet shown which features of `W` carry the target work or how material variation in those features would change the function.

The upper boundary therefore separates two questions:

```text
Does the function fit the target scene?
≠
Does the function remain constitutively traceable to the declared source?
```

Target fit can be generated by present conditions, generic institutional features, another source trajectory, an alternative function, or a narrative selected after the outcome is known. Constitutive Source Trace requires more than historical adjacency. It requires a reconstructible path from specific source features and relations to the bounded target difference, together with conditions under which changing or removing that source load would alter the claim.

WP1 defines the ceiling but does not yet execute the full Source Trace or Counterfactual Projection Test; those belong to WP2. At this stage the minimum principle is fixed:

```text
citation
≠ constitutive load

thematic resemblance
≠ source dependence

retrospective coherence
≠ Source Trace
```

A projection may also exceed the upper boundary by abstracting away the very Loss that makes its source dependence intelligible. Exact Loss must remain attached to every candidate:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Substantial declared Loss does not automatically defeat a projection. What matters is whether the surviving target function is carried by what remains preserved and whether the Claim Ceiling matches the compression and exclusions. A falsely empty or undeclared Loss obscures the source load and pushes the claim toward the Traceability Ceiling.

#### C39-NONCOMPENSATION-01 — lower- or upper-boundary failure cannot be repaired by elegance, coverage, executability, or formal completeness

```text
formal elegance
+ no Functional Gain
≠ lower-boundary pass

broad coverage
+ no Constitutive Source Trace
≠ upper-boundary pass

executable model
+ citation-only source relation
≠ admissible PROJECT_AS

schema-complete record
+ fictitiously empty Loss
≠ traceable function
```

The two boundaries therefore form a non-compensatory local band:

```text
below floor:
no additional praxeological discrimination

within candidate band:
additional bounded discrimination
+ source-traceable load remains possible

above ceiling:
target abstraction exceeds reconstructible source dependence
```

#### C39-BASELINEPRESSURE-01 — the stable `W/A–D` packets and exact Loss remain fixed and unadjudicated

WP1 preserves:

- `documented_trajectory_W` as the unchanged source packet;
- `A` as the bounded functional candidate;
- `B` as renaming-only pressure;
- `C` as source-only sufficiency pressure;
- `D` as target-fit-without-trace pressure;
- the inherited five-part Loss for every comparison;
- open alternatives, no-projection, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture.

No candidate crosses or fails either boundary in WP1. No route and no canonical Output Class is selected.

#### C39-WP1-HANDOFF-01 — WP2 receives the complete lower/upper boundary architecture without candidate or route selection

WP2 receives four locked tasks:

1. identify the constitutive source features, dependencies, and reconstruction path required for a retained target function;
2. separate Source Trace from citation, target fit, and thematic resemblance;
3. preserve Type Integrity and complete Context Boundary as independent non-compensatory gates;
4. execute the qualitative Counterfactual Projection Test across material and irrelevant source variation, opposite source structures, target conditions, context, time, and function candidate.

```text
Chapter 39 WP1 complete
+ lower and upper RETYPE boundaries established
+ Functional Gain separated from renaming and source-only relevance
+ target fit separated from constitutive source load
+ W/A-D packets and exact Loss retained
≠ candidate boundary result selected
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 39 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-39-wp1-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 39 WP1 completion boundary

Chapter 39 WP1 is complete through §§39.1–39.4. The next controlled step is WP2: Function without Source Trace, Type Integrity Boundary, Context Boundary, and the Counterfactual Projection Test.

<a id="39-5-function-without-source-trace"></a>

### 39.5 Function without Source Trace

A target function may be intelligible, practically salient, and well fitted to a later scene while remaining insufficiently connected to the source object invoked to support it. Chapter 39 treats that condition as an upper-boundary pressure: the proposed function may describe the target, but it has not yet shown why this source, with these constitutive features and dependencies, carries the claimed work.

#### C39-SOURCETRACE-01 — every retained target function requires constitutive source features, dependencies, and a reconstructible path to the bounded target difference

Constitutive Source Trace is not a demand for exhaustive historical reproduction. It is a claim-bounded reconstruction contract. At minimum it must identify:

```yaml
constitutive_source_trace_contract:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_W
  origin_type: Trajectory
  constitutive_features: required_but_unselected
  constitutive_relations: required_but_unselected
  dependency_path: required_but_unselected
  reconstruction_steps: required_but_unselected
  target_difference_carried: required_but_unselected
  material_source_variations: required_but_unselected
  irrelevant_source_variations: required_but_unselected
  uncertainty: retained
  inherited_loss:
    preserved: retained
    compressed: retained
    excluded: retained
    uncertain: retained
    irrecoverable: retained
  result: unadjudicated
```

The contract separates four burdens that must not be collapsed:

1. **Feature burden** — which source features are constitutive for the target function rather than merely present in the source record;
2. **Relation burden** — which ordering, dependency, role, cost, expectation, branch, persistence, or transition relations carry the function;
3. **Reconstruction burden** — how those features and relations connect to the bounded target-side difference;
4. **Sensitivity burden** — which material variation would alter, narrow, or defeat the function and which irrelevant variation should not.

A source feature is constitutive only relative to the declared target function. It is not transformed into a universal cause, permanent essence, or privileged historical explanation. Different target functions may depend on different source subsets, but each subset must remain explicitly related to the stable source object and inherited Loss.

```text
source feature appears in W
≠ source feature carries target function

source feature carries one bounded function
≠ source feature carries every later function
```

For candidate `A`, WP2 does not select constitutive features. It preserves the burden that any later retained historical-frame function must show why the proposed changes in credible interpretation and role-specific coordination cost depend on a reconstructible structure of `W`, rather than on current conditions, generic background knowledge, or retrospective description alone.

#### C39-OCCURRENCECEILING-01 — operator vocabulary without reconstructible occurrence load remains above the Traceability Ceiling

Where candidate `A` invokes PMS-derived source structure, the trace must remain reconstructible at occurrence level through the existing source reference, constitutive features, constitutive relations, material variations, and exact Loss. A retained symbol such as `Λ`, `Ω`, `Θ`, or `Ψ` does not preserve traceable load when the concrete occurrence, Frame, temporal relation, or supported dependency can no longer be identified.

```text
operator label retained
+ occurrence relation unreconstructible
→ abstraction without traceable load pressure

concrete occurrence relation retained
+ target difference changes under material variation
→ Traceability Ceiling may remain satisfied, subject to the full gate
```

This is a narrow ceiling example, not an operator inventory requirement. Only occurrence relations material to the tested function must be foregrounded, and the abstract PMS definitions are never varied by the counterfactual test.

#### C39-TRACEVSCITATION-01 — Source Trace remains distinct from citation, historical mention, thematic resemblance, narrative plausibility, and target fit

A citation can establish that a source was consulted. A historical mention can establish temporal adjacency. Thematic resemblance can establish a shared vocabulary or motif. Narrative plausibility can establish coherence. Target fit can establish that a function describes the later scene well. None of these, separately or together, establishes constitutive source load.

```text
source cited
≠ source constitutive

history mentioned
≠ historical structure functionally loaded

theme repeated
≠ dependency preserved

target description fits
≠ source-dependent projection established
```

The contrast between `A` and `D` remains deliberately sharp:

```yaml
trace_pressure_comparison:
  candidate_A:
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
    target_difference: declared_candidate
    source_trace_status: declared_candidate
    constitutive_features: unselected
    result: unadjudicated

  candidate_D:
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
    target_fit: apparently_strong
    source_trace: citation_only_candidate
    constitutive_features: undeclared
    source_variation_changes_function: unestablished
    result: unadjudicated
```

The same target label therefore does not imply the same warrant. A later audit may find that `A` possesses a sufficient trace, that `D` does not, that both require reduction, or that neither supports projection. WP2 selects none of these outcomes.

Function without Source Trace is also distinct from a lower-boundary failure. A candidate may add genuine Functional Gain yet exceed the ceiling because the gain cannot be reconstructibly attributed to its declared source. Conversely, a source-trace packet can be carefully documented while the target function adds no PraxisPurchase. The gates remain independent and non-compensatory.

```text
Functional Gain without Source Trace
→ upper-boundary pressure

Source Trace without Functional Gain
→ lower-boundary pressure remains possible
```

<a id="39-6-type-integrity-boundary"></a>

### 39.6 Type Integrity Boundary

RETYPE permits an origin-typed source object to perform a bounded contextual function. It does not permit the target function to overwrite source identity, revise Δ–Ψ, create a fourth operation, or convert a relational function into a person or group type.

#### C39-TYPEBOUNDARY-01 — every retained RETYPE claim preserves origin type, source reference, declared level relations, and the prohibition of new primitives or person types

For the stable source packet:

```yaml
type_integrity_lock:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  origin_type_before_projection: Trajectory
  origin_type_after_projection: Trajectory
  source_reference: locked_PATH_trajectory_record_W
  source_level: source_level_L1
  target_level: configuration_level_L2
  cross_level_relation: must_be_declared
  proposed_target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  new_pms_operator_created: false
  new_strata_operation_created: false
  person_or_group_type_created: false
  result: unadjudicated
```

The proposed function is therefore written as a contextual relation:

```text
Trajectory W
+ target context C1
→ candidate bounded historical-frame function
```

It is not written as:

```text
Trajectory W is a Frame operator
W becomes a new origin type
configuration C1 becomes a person or group trait
```

Type Integrity carries four separate protections.

**Origin-Type Integrity** preserves `Trajectory` as the source type. A successful target function does not retype the source ontology.

**Reference Integrity** preserves `documented_trajectory_W` as the same reference object across the claim. A convenient subset may support one function, but it may not silently replace the source object.

**Level-Relation Integrity** requires an explicit relation between source level `L1` and target level `L2`. Cross-level function is allowed; unmarked identity or evidential inheritance is not.

**Primitive and Person Boundary** prevents local functions, profiles, labels, or repeated analytical usefulness from becoming PMS primitives, STRATA operations, diagnoses, traits, essences, ranks, or authority-bearing classifications.

```text
origin type retained
≠ target function denied

target function retained
≠ origin type revised

cross-level relation declared
≠ levels collapsed

function affects role relation
≠ person owns the function
```

Type Integrity is non-compensatory. Strong PraxisPurchase, detailed Source Trace, elegant formalization, or broad target fit cannot repair an origin-type overwrite, unmarked level collapse, primitive inflation, or person typing. A narrower valid claim may remain possible, but it must be stated and tested as a new bounded claim rather than treated as an automatic repair.

<a id="39-7-context-boundary"></a>

### 39.7 Context Boundary

A target function exists only within declared target coordinates. Context is not a decorative label appended after interpretation; it defines where the function is supposed to make a bounded practical difference and where the claim ceases to apply.

#### C39-CONTEXTBOUNDARY-01 — a retained target function requires bounded context, object, level, Frame, granularity, time, claim scope, and validity scope

The complete target packet is:

```yaml
context_boundary_contract:
  target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  target_object: coordination_configuration_C1
  target_level: configuration_level_L2
  target_frame: required_and_fixed_for_test
  target_granularity: required_and_fixed_for_test
  temporal_scope: required_and_fixed_for_test
  target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
  claim_scope: required_and_bounded
  validity_scope: required_and_bounded
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
    - credible_interpretation_set_candidate
    - role_specific_coordination_cost_candidate
  present_target_conditions: must_be_declared_separately
  result: unadjudicated
```

Each coordinate answers a different question:

- **Target Context:** in which practical setting is the function claimed?
- **Target Object:** what receives or exhibits the function?
- **Target Level:** at which relative level is the function located?
- **Target Frame:** under which analytical boundary is the claim made?
- **Target Granularity:** at what resolution is the function discriminated?
- **Temporal Scope:** during which period or phase does the function apply?
- **Claim Scope:** exactly what difference is asserted?
- **Validity Scope:** under which bounded conditions may the assertion remain warranted?

These coordinates cannot substitute for one another. A narrow time window does not repair an undeclared target object. A declared level does not repair a missing Frame. A familiar function name does not repair missing validity conditions.

```text
context declared
≠ function established

context fit
≠ Source Trace

narrow wording
≠ bounded claim automatically
```

Material changes to target context, object, level, Frame, granularity, time, function, claim scope, or validity scope create a new testable claim. They may be legitimate, but they do not retroactively answer an objection to the earlier claim.

The Context Boundary also separates target context from present target conditions. Current rules, resources, role distributions, or immediate constraints may explain the observed target difference without requiring historical projection. They must therefore be declared as rival load rather than folded into the function attributed to `W`.

```text
present target conditions support target difference
≠ historical source carries that difference

target context specifies where claim is tested
≠ target context explains why claim is true
```

The stable `C1` coordinates remain fixed for WP2. No narrower context, alternate level, revised Frame, or changed time window is selected.

<a id="39-8-counterfactual-projection-test"></a>

### 39.8 Counterfactual Projection Test

The Counterfactual Projection Test examines whether a proposed target function reacts appropriately to changes in its declared source and target coordinates. It is a qualitative discrimination discipline. It does not establish causal necessity, causal sufficiency, probability, prediction, or authority.

#### C39-CFTEST-01 — material and irrelevant source features, target conditions, context, time, and function candidate must be varied separately

The test uses distinct variation axes:

```yaml
counterfactual_projection_test:
  stable_source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  stable_target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  stable_target_function_candidate: bounded_historical_frame_function

  variations:
    material_source_feature_change:
      expected_pressure: target_function_changes_narrows_or_fails
    irrelevant_source_feature_change:
      expected_pressure: bounded_function_remains_stable
    source_relation_change:
      expected_pressure: reconstruction_path_retested
    source_removal:
      expected_pressure: source_dependent_function_must_not_survive_unchanged
    opposite_source_structure:
      expected_pressure: same_function_must_not_be_preserved_without_revision
    present_target_condition_change:
      expected_pressure: source_load_and_current_load_separated
    target_context_change:
      expected_pressure: new_project_as_claim_required
    temporal_scope_change:
      expected_pressure: new_or_reduced_claim_required
    target_function_change:
      expected_pressure: new_function_claim_required

  causal_probability_prediction_inference: prohibited
  result: unadjudicated
```

The test seeks a differentiated response profile:

```text
material source change
→ function should change, narrow, or fail where source load is constitutive

irrelevant source change
→ bounded function should remain stable
```

A claim that changes under every minor source variation may be poorly calibrated. A claim that survives every material source variation unchanged may be detached from its declared source. WP2 records both pressures without assigning a score or threshold.

Target-side variations are equally important. If present conditions alone reproduce the proposed PraxisPurchase, the source-only or no-projection rival remains strong. If the function appears only after changing the target context, level, Frame, granularity, or time window, the revised formulation is a new claim rather than evidence for the original one.

#### C39-OPPOSITESOURCE-01 — a source-dependent projection is rejected when opposite source structures or source removal leave the claimed function unchanged

The strongest upper-boundary pressure occurs when the function is invariant under removal or reversal of its alleged constitutive source load.

```text
W present
→ bounded historical-frame function F claimed

W removed
→ same F, same scope, same target difference

or

opposite source structure W_opposite
→ same F, same scope, same target difference
```

Where this pattern holds after adequate target-condition control, the function may still describe the target scene, but it no longer qualifies as a source-dependent projection from `W`.

```text
function survives source removal unchanged
→ Source Trace fails for that projection claim

Source Trace fails
≠ target description meaningless
≠ source object invalid
≠ another bounded claim impossible
```

Opposite-source testing does not require imaginary histories detached from evidence. It uses source-supported contrasts, documented alternatives, or explicitly bounded hypothetical variation where the evidential basis permits them. Where responsible opposition cannot be constructed, the limitation remains visible and later provisionality or Non-Capture must remain possible.

#### C39-FAILURELOCAL-01 — lower-, upper-, type-, context-, trace-, sensitivity-, and Loss failures remain localized without erasing valid source material

The local gate distinguishes at least seven failure positions:

| Failure position | What may fail | What remains separately reviewable |
|---|---|---|
| Lower boundary | additional Functional Gain | source relevance and ordinary target description |
| Upper boundary | abstraction within traceable source load | target-side usefulness or narrower function |
| Type Integrity | origin-type, reference, level, primitive, or person boundary | source object and separately stated contextual claim |
| Context Boundary | target coordinates or validity scope | source-only, analogy, or newly bounded claim |
| Source Trace | constitutive feature/relation path | citation, historical relevance, or target fit |
| Counterfactual Sensitivity | material/irrelevant variation response | independently warranted source facts |
| Loss | preservation, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, or irrecoverability accounting | valid material explicitly retained outside the failed claim |

```text
one gate fails
≠ all gates fail automatically

projection claim fails
≠ documented_trajectory_W erased

source-dependent function fails
≠ source relevance denied
```

The exact terminal route remains open. A localized failure may later support Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, `failed_transformation`, `non_capture`, source-only description, `analogy_only`, or another canonical result depending on the complete WP3 gate. WP2 does not select among them.

#### C39-WP2-HANDOFF-01 — WP3 receives the complete non-compensatory gate and counterfactual burden without candidate or route selection

WP3 receives:

- the lower and upper RETYPE boundaries;
- Functional Gain and source-only sufficiency pressure;
- the complete Constitutive Source Trace contract;
- the distinction between trace, citation, thematic resemblance, narrative plausibility, and target fit;
- Type Integrity and Context Boundary as independent non-compensatory gates;
- material, irrelevant, opposite-source, source-removal, target-condition, context, time, and function variations;
- exact inherited Loss and failure-localization requirements;
- stable `W/A–F` packets with all results open.

WP3 must add the Alternative Projection Test, Analogy Boundary, Projection Elasticity, RETYPE Stop, RETYPE Non-Capture, terminal routing discipline, and the complete local RETYPE Admissibility Test. It receives no selected function, boundary result, route, Output Class, Chapter-39 lock, RETYPE lock, or permission to create new test production.

```text
Chapter 39 WP1-WP2 complete
+ lower and upper boundaries established
+ Source Trace, Type Integrity, and Context Boundary specified
+ Counterfactual Projection Test complete as method
+ failure localization preserved
≠ W/A-F pressure packet adjudicated
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 39 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-39-wp2-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 39 WP2 completion boundary

Chapter 39 WP2 is complete through §§39.5–39.8. The next controlled step is WP3: Alternative Projection Test, Analogy Boundary, Projection Elasticity, RETYPE Stop Conditions, RETYPE Non-Capture, and the RETYPE Admissibility Test.

<a id="39-9-alternative-projection-test"></a>

### 39.9 Alternative Projection Test

A RETYPE claim is not tested adequately while its proposed function is treated as the only available reconstruction. The Alternative Projection Test therefore compares the bounded candidate against serious, non-equivalent alternatives under stable source and target coordinates. The purpose is not to force one winner. It is to expose whether the proposed function performs distinctive praxeological work or merely occupies an attractive label position.

#### C39-ALTERNATIVES-01 — every retained projection must face alternative functions, narrower claims, source-only description, analogy-only, and no-projection

The minimum alternative set is:

```yaml
alternative_projection_test:
  stable_source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  stable_target_context: later_coordination_context_C1
  primary_candidate:
    target_function: bounded_historical_frame_function
    result: unadjudicated
  alternatives:
    rival_function:
      target_function: bounded_dynamic_attractor_function
      result: unadjudicated
    narrower_claim:
      target_function: narrower_role_and_period_bound_function
      result: unadjudicated
    source_only_description:
      additional_target_function: not_required_candidate
      result: unadjudicated
    analogy_only:
      target_object: formal_transition_model_M
      result: unadjudicated
    no_projection:
      source_relevance_retained: true
      target_function_selected: false
      result: unadjudicated
  comparison_score: prohibited
  automatic_best_candidate_selection: prohibited
```

Each alternative must be tested under its own claim burden. A rival function is not defeated merely because it uses different vocabulary. A narrower claim is not automatically preferable merely because it says less. Source-only description is not a failure merely because it declines a new function. Analogy-only is not a weak projection. No-projection does not erase the source object.

The comparison asks:

- Which candidate changes the reconstruction of credible interpretations, action corridors, role expectations, coordination costs, or continuation possibilities?
- Which source features and relations are constitutive for that difference?
- Which countercases does the candidate reject rather than absorb?
- Which assumptions and present target conditions carry the result?
- What exact Loss accompanies each candidate?
- Does a narrower formulation preserve the warranted difference with less unsupported load?

```text
alternative function available
≠ primary candidate failed

primary candidate plausible
≠ alternatives discharged

source-only sufficiency
≠ source irrelevance

no-projection
≠ no praxis structure
```

Where two candidates remain equally source-traceable and discriminating, provisionality or Non-Capture must remain available. Chapter 39 does not invent a ranking score to break substantive indeterminacy.

<a id="39-10-analogy-boundary"></a>

### 39.10 Analogy Boundary

The Analogy Boundary protects useful structural correspondence from forced promotion into functional projection. Candidate `E` may preserve sequence, branching, reversal, or transition relations between `W` and `M` while failing to preserve the praxeological semantics required for a target function.

#### C39-ANALOGYBOUNDARY-01 — `analogy_only` remains available where structural usefulness survives but semantic preservation or target function does not

The boundary distinguishes:

```text
formal correspondence
≠ semantic preservation

semantic preservation
≠ target function

useful analogy
≠ failed transformation automatically
```

A bounded analogy claim must declare:

```yaml
analogy_boundary_contract:
  source_object: documented_trajectory_W
  target_object: formal_transition_model_M
  comparison_dimension:
    - sequence
    - branching
    - reversal
  preserved_relation: must_be_declared
  source_only_residuals: must_be_declared
  target_only_residuals: must_be_declared
  incompatibilities: must_be_declared
  semantic_preservation_status: unestablished
  target_function_status: unestablished
  analogy_only_status: open
  result: unadjudicated
```

If structural usefulness survives but semantic preservation or target function does not, `analogy_only` is a legitimate terminal route after full audit. If only a narrower relation survives, Claim Reduction may remain necessary. If no warranted relation survives, the analogy claim may fail. If responsible semantic comparison cannot be completed without invention, Non-Capture remains available.

The boundary also blocks reverse inflation:

```text
projection candidate fails
≠ analogy_only selected automatically

formal mapping succeeds
≠ PROJECT_AS established

analogy label repeated
≠ semantic identity established
```

`E` remains unadjudicated. No preserved relation, semantic equivalence, target function, route, or Output Class is selected in WP3.

<a id="39-11-projection-elasticity"></a>

### 39.11 Projection Elasticity

A bounded projection must possess stable failure conditions. Projection Elasticity occurs when objections are absorbed by repeatedly changing the function, context, level, period, source subset, or claim scope while preserving the appearance that one original claim has survived.

#### C39-ELASTICITY-01 — objection absorption, function proliferation, endless narrowing, and context relocation require new claims and may defeat the original projection

The principal elasticity patterns are:

1. **Objection absorption** — every countercase is redescribed as another manifestation of the function.
2. **Function proliferation** — a new target function is introduced after each failed test.
3. **Endless narrowing** — the claim is repeatedly reduced without surrendering the broader original disposition.
4. **Context relocation** — the claim moves to another context, level, Frame, granularity, role, or period after objection.
5. **Source-subset migration** — constitutive source features change after each counterexample.
6. **Loss rewriting** — excluded or irrecoverable material is reclassified merely to preserve the claim.
7. **Output drift** — Failure, Stop, Claim Reduction, provisionality, or analogy are exchanged rhetorically without a new audit.

```text
new context
= new testable claim

new function
= new testable claim

new source subset
= new testable claim

new claim
≠ original claim repaired
```

A valid Claim Reduction is finite and disposition-preserving: the stronger claim is marked as reduced, and the narrower claim is independently tested. Endless narrowing that leaves the stronger claim rhetorically active is anti-immunization failure.

The elasticity check asks whether the claim has stable rejection conditions. A projection that survives source removal, opposite source structures, target-condition substitution, every countercase, and every context relocation unchanged is not thereby robust. It may be detached from both source and target.

```text
survives every material variation unchanged
→ overelasticity pressure

changes under every irrelevant variation
→ poor calibration pressure

stable failure conditions
≠ causal proof
```

No elasticity result is selected for `A–F` in WP3.

<a id="39-12-retype-stop-conditions"></a>

### 39.12 RETYPE Stop Conditions

Stop is a positive boundary result. It prevents further transformation when continuation would require prohibited invention, erasure, type replacement, arbitrary relocation, or authority inheritance. Not every lower- or upper-boundary failure requires Mandatory Stop.

#### C39-STOP-01 — Mandatory Stop is local to prohibited continuation and does not replace ordinary failure, reduction, analogy, or no-projection

Mandatory-Stop pressure arises where continuation would require at least one of the following:

- inventing constitutive source features or relations;
- erasing a prior failed disposition;
- replacing the source origin type with the target function;
- collapsing undeclared levels or granularities;
- selecting an arbitrary target context solely to save the claim;
- proliferating functions or source subsets without independent warrant;
- suppressing material Loss, uncertainty, countercases, or residuals;
- converting a macrofunction into a person or group property;
- creating a PMS primitive, fourth STRATA operation, sanction, recommendation, governance, or authority claim;
- continuing after stable anti-immunization conditions have been violated.

Mandatory Stop is not required where a delimited executed claim simply fails its test, where a narrower claim remains independently warrantable, where analogy-only is the correct result, or where source-only/no-projection is sufficient.

```text
lower-boundary failure
≠ mandatory_stop automatically

upper-boundary failure
≠ mandatory_stop automatically

prohibited continuation required
→ mandatory_stop remains available
```

Stop applies to the prohibited continuation, not to all future analysis of the source object. A new claim may later be tested only if it preserves the earlier disposition and supplies genuinely new coordinates and warrant.

<a id="39-13-retype-non-capture"></a>

### 39.13 RETYPE Non-Capture

RETYPE Non-Capture is required where the available evidence and claim structure do not permit responsible adjudication of a function, comparison, semantic relation, or boundary result without invention. It is not a refuge for weak claims and not a synonym for uncertainty alone.

#### C39-NONCAPTURE-01 — Non-Capture remains available where responsible function or comparison adjudication is unavailable without invention

Non-Capture pressure may arise when:

- two or more non-equivalent functions remain equally grounded and no responsible discriminant is available;
- source boundaries or constitutive relations are materially indeterminate;
- a necessary counterfactual contrast cannot be constructed from evidence without fabrication;
- semantic preservation across domains cannot be responsibly assessed;
- target coordinates cannot be stabilized without arbitrary selection;
- candidate-specific Loss cannot be compared on a common warranted basis;
- the distinction between source-only relevance, analogy, and target function cannot be resolved with the available record.

```text
non_capture
≠ no source structure exists
≠ no analogy exists
≠ every candidate failed
≠ permission to retain the strongest wording
```

Non-Capture must state exactly what cannot be captured and what valid material remains. It may coexist with preserved source reconstruction, bounded analogy, or separately admissible subclaims. It may not be used to conceal Label Substitution, Type Integrity failure, source erasure, or authority inflation.

<a id="39-14-retype-admissibility-test"></a>

### 39.14 RETYPE Admissibility Test

The RETYPE Admissibility Test integrates the local boundary architecture into one ordered, non-compensatory gate. It does not add a thirteenth audit stage, a score, a weighting system, or an automatic truth selector. It operationalizes the existing Admissibility Band for a delimited `PROJECT_AS` claim.

#### C39-TERMINALROUTES-01 — admissibility, bounded or provisional results, analogy-only, Claim Reduction, Stop, Failure, and Non-Capture remain distinct

The local descriptions produced by the gate are not new Output Classes. After full execution, a delimited claim may map only to an existing canonical class. The following distinctions remain mandatory:

```text
admissible
≠ admissible_with_bounded_claim
≠ admissible_but_provisional

analogy_only
≠ failed_transformation

claim_reduction_required
≠ mandatory_stop

failed_transformation
≠ non_capture
```

`partially_admissible` and `resolution_neutral` remain available only under their established canonical meanings. Chapter 39 does not redefine them.

#### C39-ADMISSIBILITYTEST-01 — the complete local RETYPE gate is non-compensatory and contains no score, weighting, automatic truth inference, or authority transfer

The gate is:

```yaml
retype_admissibility_test:
  source_lock:
    reference_and_origin_type_preserved: required
    prior_disposition_and_inherited_loss_preserved: required

  lower_boundary:
    additional_praxeological_discrimination: required
    renaming_and_source_only_rivals_tested: required

  upper_boundary:
    constitutive_source_trace: required
    source_removal_and_opposite_source_pressure: required

  integrity:
    type_reference_level_and_person_boundaries: required
    complete_target_coordinates: required

  sensitivity:
    material_and_irrelevant_variations_separated: required
    present_target_conditions_separated: required
    stable_failure_conditions: required

  alternatives:
    rival_functions: required
    narrower_claim: required
    source_only: required
    analogy_only: required
    no_projection: required

  loss:
    preserved: required
    compressed: required
    excluded: required
    uncertain: required
    irrecoverable: required

  terminal_boundary:
    stop_conditions_checked: required
    non_capture_checked: required
    canonical_output_only: required

  score_or_weighting: prohibited
  automatic_empirical_semantic_causal_normative_or_authority_inference: prohibited
  result: unadjudicated
```

Failure at one gate is localized and cannot be compensated by performance elsewhere. A precise target difference does not repair missing Source Trace. Strong Source Trace does not repair absent Functional Gain. Elegant formalization does not repair Type Integrity. Narrow wording does not repair unstable context. Broad evidence does not repair fictitiously empty Loss.

The gate asks, in order:

1. Is the same source object, origin type, reference, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss retained?
2. Does the proposed function add bounded praxeological discrimination beyond renaming, source-only relevance, and ordinary target description?
3. Is that difference reconstructibly dependent on declared constitutive source features and relations?
4. Are Type Integrity, Context Boundary, level relation, Claim Ceiling, and exact Loss intact?
5. Does the claim respond discriminatorily to material, irrelevant, opposite-source, source-removal, target-condition, context, time, and function variation?
6. Do serious alternative functions, narrower claims, source-only description, analogy-only, and no-projection remain testable?
7. Does the claim possess stable failure conditions rather than elastic rescue routes?
8. Is continuation admissible, must the claim be reduced, stopped, failed, or left in Non-Capture?

No machine-readable completion of these fields proves empirical truth, semantic validity, causal explanation, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or authority.

#### C39-HANDOFF-01 — WP4 receives the complete local-boundary architecture without pressure-object adjudication or new test production

WP4 receives:

- the lower and upper RETYPE boundaries;
- Functional Gain, Source Trace, Type Integrity, Context Boundary, Counterfactual Sensitivity, exact Loss, and Claim Ceiling;
- Alternative Projection and Analogy Boundary tests;
- Projection Elasticity and stable failure conditions;
- RETYPE Stop and Non-Capture conditions;
- the complete non-compensatory RETYPE Admissibility Test;
- stable `W/A–F` packets with all candidate, route, and Output-Class results open.

WP4 must perform the integrated Chapter-39 lock audit and prepare Chapter 40. It receives no permission to adjudicate `W/A–F` merely because the family method is complete, no RETYPE lock, and no permission to create new test, case, or smoke YAML production.

```text
Chapter 39 WP1-WP3 complete
+ twenty-four Pressure Duties represented exactly once
+ complete local RETYPE gate specified
+ W/A-F packets and exact Loss retained
≠ candidate boundary result selected
≠ route or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 39 or RETYPE locked
```

<a id="chapter-39-wp3-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 39 WP3 completion boundary

Chapter 39 WP3 is complete through §§39.9–39.14. The next controlled step is WP4: integrated Chapter-39 family-method lock audit, provisional-lock decision, and Chapter-40 Preparation Gate.

<a id="chapter-39-integrated-family-method-lock-audit"></a>

#### Integrated Chapter-39 local-boundary lock audit

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 39 now supplies a complete local RETYPE admissibility gate without converting the Admissibility Band into a score, a universal semantic selector, or a substitute for the family methods of Chapters 29–38. The chapter tests one delimited `PROJECT_AS` claim between two independent limits:

```text
lower RETYPE boundary
→ minimum additional praxeological discrimination

upper RETYPE boundary
→ maximum abstraction compatible with constitutive Source Trace
```

The limits remain independent and non-compensatory. A function label that adds no bounded target-side PraxisPurchase remains below the lower boundary even when it is elegant, familiar, executable, or formally complete. A target description that appears powerful remains above the upper boundary when it no longer depends reconstructibly on the declared Source Object.

```text
Functional Gain
+ missing Constitutive Source Trace
≠ admissible projection

Constitutive Source Trace
+ no additional Functional Gain
≠ admissible projection
```

The audit also confirms that local RETYPE passage requires the same source reference, origin type, prior disposition, uncertainty, inherited five-part Loss, declared target coordinates, stable Claim Ceiling, serious alternatives, Counterfactual Sensitivity, and visible failure conditions. Passage at one gate cannot repair failure at another.

#### Locked lower/upper-boundary and integrity architecture

The provisionally locked method preserves eight ordered burdens:

1. retain the same source object, origin type, reference, prior disposition, uncertainty, and inherited Loss;
2. identify a concrete target-side difference beyond renaming, source-only relevance, and ordinary target description;
3. reconstruct that difference through declared constitutive source features, relations, and dependencies;
4. preserve Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, explicit level relations, and the prohibition of person typing or primitive creation;
5. declare stable target context, object, level, Frame, granularity, temporal scope, target function, claim scope, and validity scope;
6. distinguish material from irrelevant Source variation and test Source Removal, opposite Source structure, present Target Conditions, context, time, and function alternatives;
7. retain rival functions, narrower claims, source-only description, analogy-only, no-projection, Stop, and Non-Capture;
8. map only the fully executed delimited claim to one existing canonical Output Class.

```text
local RETYPE gate complete
≠ candidate adjudicated
≠ integrated LIMITS completed
≠ automatic route selected
```

Source citation, historical mention, thematic resemblance, narrative plausibility, target fit, formal correspondence, executability, and schema completeness remain insufficient substitutes for Constitutive Source Trace. Conversely, source relevance alone does not establish a target function.

#### Alternative, analogy, elasticity, Stop, and Non-Capture closure

The following alternatives remain constitutive rather than optional decorations:

- a different bounded target function;
- a narrower context-, level-, role-, phase-, time-, or function-specific claim;
- source-only description;
- structural analogy with possible `analogy_only` result;
- no-projection;
- Non-Capture where responsible distinction is unavailable without invention.

Projection Elasticity remains prohibited. A changed context, function, source subset, temporal window, evidence packet, or target coordinate creates a new testable claim and does not repair the earlier disposition.

```text
new claim
≠ original claim rescued

endless narrowing
≠ Claim Reduction

context relocation
≠ objection answered
```

Mandatory Stop is reserved for prohibited continuation: invented source load, source erasure, origin-type replacement, unmarked level or granularity collapse, arbitrary context relocation, function proliferation, person typing, primitive creation, anti-immunization violation, or authority inheritance. A delimited claim may instead fail, be reduced, remain analogy-only, yield source-only/no-projection, or enter Non-Capture under its own burden.

The terminal routes remain non-equivalent:

```text
admissible
≠ admissible_with_bounded_claim
≠ admissible_but_provisional

analogy_only
≠ failed_transformation

claim_reduction_required
≠ mandatory_stop

failed_transformation
≠ non_capture
```

No local passage establishes empirical truth, causal sufficiency, prediction, semantic identity, person judgment, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, governance, or application authority.

#### Protected non-adjudication of the Chapter-39 pressure architecture

The stable Source Object remains:

```yaml
source_packet:
```
